### [1] [Cosmology, Cluster Algebras, and $\boldsymbol{u}$](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.05606)
*Daniel Glazer,Austin Joyce*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper establishes a correspondence between kinematic variables in de Sitter cosmology and cluster algebra variables (via u-variables), showing that n-site chain and cycle Feynman graphs map to A_{2n−2} and B_{2n−1} cluster algebras respectively; cluster compatibility then constrains symbol entries and enables bootstrapping—wavefunctions and correlators share cluster structure for chains, but only the wavefunction satisfies compatibility for cycles.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To uncover underlying mathematical structures governing cosmological observables—specifically, to explain why certain symbol alphabets appear in wavefunctions and correlators of conformally coupled scalars in de Sitter space, and to leverage cluster algebra constraints for bootstrapping.

Method: Mapping kinematic u-variables from cosmological Feynman graphs (chains and cycles) to cluster algebra u-variables; identifying associated Dynkin types (A_{2n−2}, B_{2n−1}); imposing cluster compatibility (i.e., adjacency conditions on symbol letters) to constrain and bootstrap the symbol of wavefunctions and correlators.

Result: For n-site chain graphs: wavefunction and correlator share the same A_{2n−2} cluster structure and both satisfy cluster compatibility. For n-site cycle graphs: only the wavefunction satisfies B_{2n−1} cluster compatibility; the correlator fails this condition, implying the wavefunction is uniquely selected by the cluster structure.

Conclusion: Cluster algebras provide a powerful organizing principle for de Sitter wavefunctions—encoding kinematic constraints, enabling symbol bootstrapping, and distinguishing physical roles of wavefunctions versus correlators depending on graph topology.

Abstract: We explore the cluster algebra structures present in the wavefunction and correlators of conformally coupled scalar fields in de Sitter space. To connect cosmological kinematics to cluster variables, we utilize the so-called $u$-variables that appear on both sides. Since $u$-variables parameterize a rigid space, it is natural to identify the ones that appear from cosmological graphs and those that arise from cluster algebras. This provides a mapping between kinematic variables and cluster variables. For $n$-site chain Feynman graphs, this gives a coordinate-invariant relation to $A_{2n-2}$-type cluster algebras, while in the case of $n$-site cycle graph momentum integrands, the relevant cluster algebra is $B_{2n-1}$, as found previously. Since kinematic variables are mapped to nonlinear combinations of cluster variables, cluster compatibility imposes strong conditions on possible symbol entries. We use this notion of compatibility to bootstrap the symbol. In the chain case, both the cosmological wavefunction and products of lower-point functions satisfy cluster compatibility, with the correlator a particular combination, so that the wavefunction and correlator share the same cluster structure. In the cycle case, however, the wavefunction appears to be uniquely selected by the cluster structure, and the correlator does not display the same compatibility as the wavefunction.

</details>


### [2] [Matrix Holography on an Optical Lattice](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.05613)
*Robin Löwenberg,Julian Sonner*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: Proposes an analog quantum-simulation protocol using atomic ensembles and time-averaged Floquet dynamics to efficiently realize four-body potentials in bosonic BFSS-like matrix quantum mechanics, achieving polynomial (O(N³)) scaling in control frequency and enabling strong-coupling holographic physics simulation.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To overcome the exponential circuit-depth overhead of digital gate-based quantum simulations of large-N matrix quantum mechanics (e.g., BFSS), and enable experimental access to holographic strong-coupling regimes.

Method: Analog quantum simulation via a lattice of atomic ensembles under time-averaged Floquet driving to engineer effective four-body interactions; control complexity is kept constant per Floquet cycle, with scaling bottleneck in required frequency bandwidth.

Result: Achieves polynomial O(N³) scaling in control frequency bandwidth with matrix size N; enables tunable coupling beyond O(1), permitting simulation of strong-coupling holographic phases.

Conclusion: Provides a scalable, experimentally feasible analog route to large-N matrix quantum mechanics, circumventing digital circuit-depth limitations and opening access to non-perturbative, holographically relevant physics.

Abstract: We propose an analog quantum-simulation protocol based on a lattice of atomic ensembles and time-averaged Floquet dynamics to construct the four-body potentials that typically appear in bosonic BFSS-like matrix quantum mechanics. In contrast to gate-based digital implementations requiring deep circuits, our approach generates the target model through a fixed set of control stages per Floquet cycle, keeping the number of consecutive unitaries constant with respect to the matrix size $N$. The principal scaling cost in our construction is given by the frequency range required to control the growing lattice. We show that this cost grows at most as $\mathcal{O}(N^3)$, giving a polynomial scaling route to large$-N$, attractive to experimental implementation. Additionally, we demonstrate that the coupling parameter can be tuned beyond $\mathcal{O}(1)$, enabling the simulation of strong-coupling physics necessary for holographic phases.

</details>


### [3] [Equation of State of a Strongly Coupled Perfect Fluid with Spin from Holography](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.05630)
*Andres Anabalon,Horatiu Nastase*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper uses holography to derive the equation of state for a strongly coupled relativistic perfect fluid with finite spin in 2+1 dimensions, dual to a spinning AdS₄ black hole; it identifies a critical rotation threshold for thermodynamic stability and discovers an exotic rotating fluid dual to a non-spinning black hole on a second boundary.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To establish a holographic equation of state for strongly coupled relativistic fluids with intrinsic spin and to explore the role of black hole rotation in determining thermodynamic stability and exotic fluid behavior.

Method: Holographic duality (AdS/CFT) applied to a rotating black hole in asymptotically AdS₄ spacetime; thermodynamic quantities (temperature, angular velocity, entropy) are extracted from the black hole horizon and mapped to boundary fluid properties; local thermodynamic stability is analyzed via second derivatives of the free energy.

Result: Derivation of the equation of state for a finite-spin 2+1D relativistic fluid; identification of a critical rotation bound ω < 4πT / 2.77239 for stability; discovery of an 'exotic' rotating fluid dual to a non-rotating black hole on a second boundary.

Conclusion: Black hole angular momentum directly governs the thermodynamic stability and equation of state of dual relativistic fluids; finite spin introduces a universal stability bound; the existence of two distinct fluid phases from a single bulk solution reveals novel holographic structure beyond standard AdS/CFT.

Abstract: We derive the equation of state of a strongly coupled relativistic perfect fluid with finite spin in $2+1$ dimensions using holography. The dual gravitational description is provided by a spinning black hole in $AdS_4$, whose Hawking temperature, angular velocity, and Bekenstein-Hawking entropy determine the thermodynamic properties of the boundary fluid. We obtain the equation of state and analyze its local thermodynamic stability, finding a critical rotation above which the fluid becomes thermodynamically unstable providing a bound for the rotation of a strongly coupled quark gluon plasma at temperature $T$ and angular velocity $ω$ given by $\frac{4πT}ω> 2.77239$. Remarkably, the same gravitational solution contains a second black hole associated with a second boundary. Although this black hole is non-spinning, its dual fluid rotates and possesses a distinct, ``exotic'' equation of state. Our results provide a holographic equation of state for strongly coupled matter with finite spin and establish a direct connection between black-hole rotation, intrinsic angular momentum, and the thermodynamic stability of relativistic fluids.

</details>


### [4] [Birth and Death in Two-color ABJM](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.05653)
*Marco S. Bianchi*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper exactly solves the leading quantum deformation of the two-point metric for half-BPS operators in U(2)×U(2) ABJM theory at finite rank, revealing a Jacobi operator structure on two-row Young diagrams, linked to Racah polynomials, conditioned Plancherel measure, and a restricted SU(2) Casimir via a ground-state transform.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand quantum corrections to correlation functions of protected operators in ABJM theory—specifically, how integrability and representation-theoretic structures (e.g., Young diagrams, symmetric group statistics) govern exact finite-rank deformations beyond planar limits.

Method: Exact computation of the tree-normalized two-loop correction to the two-point metric in the Schur basis; application of a ground-state transform to recast the Jacobi operator as a reversible birth–death Markov process; identification of eigenfunctions as Racah polynomials; asymptotic analysis toward Ornstein–Uhlenbeck dynamics; mapping to a constrained SU(2) spin system via diagrammatic correspondence.

Result: The deformed two-point metric is governed by a Jacobi operator on the lattice of two-row Young diagrams; its stationary distribution is the Plancherel measure conditioned on ≤2 rows; eigenmodes are discrete orthogonal Racah polynomials; large-n limit yields radial Ornstein–Uhlenbeck diffusion; equivalent description as a restricted SU(2) Casimir acting on a two-spin chain.

Conclusion: Quantum corrections to half-BPS correlators in finite-rank ABJM theory are exactly encoded in classical orthogonal polynomial structures and stochastic dynamics, unifying integrability, representation theory, and statistical mechanics in a non-planar setting.

Abstract: We solve exactly the leading quantum deformation of the two-point metric of half-BPS operators in $U(2)\times U(2)$ ABJM theory at finite rank. In the natural coupling-independent Schur frame, its tree-normalized two-loop correction is a Jacobi operator on the chain of two-row Young diagrams. A ground-state transform maps it to a reversible birth--death process whose stationary distribution is the Plancherel measure conditioned on diagrams with at most two rows. Its eigenmodes are Racah polynomials, its large-$n$ limit approaches radial Ornstein--Uhlenbeck dynamics, and an equivalent two-spin description identifies the Jacobi operator with a restricted $SU(2)$ Casimir.

</details>


### [5] [Searching for Bell-CHSH-Violating Bose Operators](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.05685)
*T. Newton,S. P. Sorella*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper constructs explicit bounded wedge-local observables for Bell inequalities in a relativistic free Bose field using a spectral-fibre method, proving their locality, self-adjointness, unitarity, and anticommutation; it demonstrates constructive Bell nonlocality with quantified violations—first via translation-cell observables (with controlled infinite-tail error), then enhanced via reflection branches.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To demonstrate that Bell nonlocality can be constructively exposed in relativistic quantum field theory (RQFT) using simple, bounded, physically realizable local observables—countering skepticism about the operational relevance or validity of Bell violations in QFT.

Method: Spectral-fibre approach: explicitly constructs wedge-local observables by transforming the canonical field pair into measurable fibre involutions; proves wedge locality, self-adjointness, unitarity, and exact anticommutation within operator-algebraic framework; computes Bell violations using translation-cell observables and extends to reflection-inclusive observables.

Result: Explicit construction of bounded, wedge-local, self-adjoint, unitary, and anticommuting observables; quantitative Bell violation with rigorously bounded infinite-tail error for translation-cell case; significantly larger violation when reflection branches are included; confirmation that the translation-cell violation is a strict lower bound—not an artifact.

Conclusion: Bell nonlocality is constructively and rigorously manifest in relativistic free QFT using elementary bounded observables; the spectral-fibre method provides a robust, algebraically sound framework for deriving and verifying locality and nonlocality properties in RQFT.

Abstract: We construct explicit bounded wedge-local observables for Bell inequalities in a relativistic free Bose field using an exact spectral-fibre approach. The construction turns the relevant canonical pair into measurable fibre involutions and provides an operator-algebraic proof of wedge locality, self-adjointness, unitarity, and exact anticommutation. Translation-cell observables yield an explicit Bell violation with a controlled infinite-tail error, while inclusion of reflection branches produces a substantially larger violation. These results demonstrate that simple bounded local observables can expose Bell nonlocality constructively in relativistic quantum field theory and that the translation-cell result is not artifact but a strictly valid lower bound.

</details>


### [6] [Landau and cluster structures of one-loop amplitudes in ${\mathcal N}=4$ SYM in dimensional regularization](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.05716)
*Athanasia-Konstantina Angelopoulou,Ruth Britto,Matteo Parisi*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper unifies generalized unitarity, Landau analysis, and cluster adjacency in one-loop planar N=4 SYM amplitudes, revealing structural constraints on the symbol (especially weight-two part), proving two-mass triangle relations via global residue theorem and amplituhedron geometry, and establishing full cluster adjacency in Fl_{2,4;n} for all multiplicities/helicities—with a stronger Gr(4,n) adjacency conjectured and proven for NMHV.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To clarify how generalized unitarity, Landau singularities, and cluster adjacency — three seemingly distinct frameworks governing scattering amplitudes — are concretely related in a controlled setting: one-loop planar N=4 SYM in dimensional regularization.

Method: Symbol-level analysis of one-loop amplitudes; decomposition into LS (leading singularity)-driven, algebraic four-mass, and residual sectors; derivation of two-mass triangle identities using BCFW-style global residue theorem; geometric interpretation via tree amplituhedron projections of triple-cut loop geometry; verification and proof of cluster adjacency in flag cluster algebra Fl_{2,4;n} and Grassmannian Gr(4,n); examination of Sklyanin brackets for algebraic letters.

Result: 1) Weight-two symbol decomposes into LS-driven, algebraic four-mass, and residual parts; 2) Two-mass triangle relations among box coefficients are proven and geometrically interpreted via amplituhedron dissections; 3) Full rational symbol (including IR-divergent part) obeys cluster adjacency in Fl_{2,4;n} for all n and helicity configurations; 4) Stronger Gr(4,n) adjacency is conjectured and proven for NMHV; 5) Algebraic four-mass letters exhibit Sklyanin-bracket patterns.

Conclusion: Generalized unitarity, Landau analysis, and cluster adjacency are deeply intertwined: Landau loci govern symbol ordering in the LS sector; triangle relations reflect geometric consistency of loop-level cuts in the amplituhedron; and cluster adjacency emerges as a universal constraint on the full symbol—including infrared structure—validating Fl_{2,4;n} as the correct algebraic framework for one-loop planar N=4 SYM.

Abstract: Generalized unitarity, Landau analysis, and cluster adjacency encode complementary aspects of scattering amplitudes. We use one-loop planar $\mathcal N=4$ SYM amplitudes in dimensional regularization, at arbitrary multiplicity and helicity, to make their interface explicit. The weight-two symbol decomposes into an LS part, in which maximal-cut leading singularities furnish the coefficients and Landau loci associated with nested cuts organize the ordered symbol entries, an algebraic four-mass sector, and residual terms. Cancellations of certain letters contributed by individual box integrals, as well as further simplifications, are explained by the two-mass triangle relations among box coefficients. We prove these relations using a BCFW-like application of the global residue theorem and show that they can be understood geometrically as different dissections of the same region in the tree amplituhedron obtained by projecting the loop geometry of a triple cut. We then prove that the full rational symbol, including its infrared-divergent part, obeys cluster adjacency in the flag cluster algebra $\mathrm{Fl}_{2,4;n}$ for all multiplicities and helicities. Within the sector depending only on momentum-twistor four-brackets, we conjecture a stronger cluster property in $\operatorname{Gr}(4,n)$ for all helicities and prove it for NMHV amplitudes: the amplitude admits a representation in which every pole of each coefficient is compatible with both symbol entries. Finally, we observe that the algebraic four-mass letters, although non-rational, exhibit a suggestive Sklyanin-bracket pattern.

</details>


### [7] [Non-critical M-Theory and the Resolved Conifold: Refinement and Nonperturbative Completion](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.06075)
*Fengjun Xu*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper extends the Hořava--Keeler correspondence to the refined setting, linking stationary rotating non-critical M-theory at finite temperature to the refined A-model on the resolved conifold; it reproduces the refined Gopakumar–Vafa expansion, matches known nonperturbative conifold completions, and provides a spectral (microscopic) origin for Schwinger-type integrands and cycles.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To generalize the Hořava–Keeler correspondence to the refined topological string regime, thereby connecting refined BPS invariants and non-critical M-theory with angular chemical potential (Ω-background deformation), and to microscopically explain nonperturbative completions of the conifold free energy.

Method: Uses grand potential formalism of non-critical M-theory with angular chemical potential; identifies its structure with refined Gopakumar–Vafa invariants; matches against Hattab–Palti (unrefined) and Chuang (refined) nonperturbative conifold completions; derives Schwinger integrand and integration cycle from M-theory spectrum and resolvent.

Result: Exact match between grand potential of rotating non-critical M-theory and refined conifold A-model including nonperturbative corrections; microscopic spectral interpretation of Schwinger integrand/cycle; identification of BPS sector and cubic local contribution; remaining ambiguities (polynomial and constant-map) require external normalization.

Conclusion: The resolved conifold’s unique single primitive spinless multiplet enables this precise refined correspondence; extension to general local Calabi–Yau geometries would require additional charge- and spin-resolved microscopic spectral data beyond the current setup.

Abstract: We extend the Hořava--Keeler correspondence between finite-temperature non-critical M-theory and the resolved-conifold A-model to the refined theory. The grand potential of the stationary rotating non-critical M-theory vacuum reproduces the refined Gopakumar--Vafa expansion, with the angular chemical potential deforming the $Ω$-background away from the self-dual locus $\eps_1=-\eps_2$. At finite thermal radius, we establish an exact match with the nonperturbative conifold completions proposed by Hattab--Palti and by Chuang in the unrefined and refined cases, respectively. Both the one-particle Schwinger integrand and its integration cycle arise from the non-critical M-theory spectrum and resolvent, providing a microscopic spectral realization of these completions. The correspondence fixes the nonconstant BPS sector and the cubic local contribution, while the remaining polynomial ambiguity and the modulus-independent constant-map sector require separate normalization. We explain why the single primitive spinless multiplet of the resolved conifold makes this identification possible, and discuss what additional charge- and spin-dependent microscopic data would be needed for an extension to more general local Calabi--Yau geometries.

</details>


### [8] [The Wilson-line-dressed charged sector of scalar QED: superselection and the infraparticle](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.06224)
*Gordon W. Semenoff,Conor Waterfield*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper develops a gauge- and Lorentz-invariant perturbative framework to analyze charged states in scalar QED using Wilson-line-dressed operators; it shows cloud orthogonality between non-parallel dressings (with an angle-dependent, one-loop-exact infrared exponent), superselection by dressing orientation, infrared-finite but branch-cut–dominated two-point functions, and a robust, one-loop-exact power-law asymptotic spread for dressed charges.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To render structural features of the charged sector—previously accessible only via non-perturbative or algebraic methods—explicit, computable, and manifestly gauge- and Lorentz-invariant in perturbation theory.

Method: Constructs Wilson-line-dressed scalar operators; uses Osterwalder-Schrader reflection and Euclidean gluing to express state overlaps as Euclidean two-point functions; employs Stueckelberg photon mass as a BRST-, gauge-, and Lorentz-invariant infrared regulator; performs renormalized perturbative computation.

Result: 1) Cloud orthogonality: overlaps of states with non-parallel Wilson lines vanish as a power of the IR regulator, with exponent given by an infrared analogue of the cusp anomalous dimension—exactly one-loop when matter is mass-gapped. 2) Superselection by Wilson-line orientation. 3) Infrared-finite two-point functions exhibiting infraparticle behavior: mass-shell pole replaced by branch cut. 4) Asymptotic proper-time spread follows a one-loop-exact power law dependent on dressing geometry and direction of motion. 5) The infraparticle scaling law remains robust over a wide proper-time window despite IR sensitivity of individual features.

Conclusion: The charged sector exhibits deep infrared structure—cloud orthogonality, orientation-based superselection, and infraparticle dynamics—that is both perturbatively accessible and quantitatively precise in this invariant framework; the resulting scaling laws are one-loop exact and physically robust.

Abstract: We develop a manifestly gauge and Lorentz invariant perturbative calculus to study the finite time and distance evolution of the electrically charged states created by Wilson-line-dressed scalar field operators in scalar quantum electrodynamics. By an Osterwalder-Schrader reflection and Euclidean gluing, the overlap of two dressed states is expressed as a Euclidean 2 point function of dressed operators, which is then evaluated in renormalized perturbation theory with a Stueckelberg photon mass as a gauge, BRST and Lorentz invariant infrared cutoff. This renders explicit and computable in closed form several structural features of the charged sector that have so far been accessible mainly through non-perturbative or algebraic arguments. We find that states dressed by non-parallel Wilson lines are ``cloud orthogonal". Their overlap vanishes as a power of the infrared regulator, with an exponent that depends only on the angle between the two dressings and is an infrared analogue of the cusp anomalous dimension with the difference that it is one-loop exact whenever the charged matter is mass-gapped. Charged states are thereby superselected by the orientation of their dressing. Within a superselection sector the 2 point function is infrared finite but exhibits the infraparticle. The mass shell pole is replaced by the edge of a branch cut. As a result, the asymptotic spread of a dressed charge is a power law in proper time with a one-loop-exact computable exponent depending on the dressing and on the direction of motion. We show that although cloud orthogonality and the infraparticle are each extremely sensitive to fundamental infrared cutoffs, the infraparticle scaling law persists across a wide window of proper time.

</details>


### [9] [AMSB in Truly Confining Gauge Theories](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.06093)
*Riku Ishikawa,Hitoshi Murayama,Shota Saito*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper investigates deformations of supersymmetric t-confining gauge theories under small anomaly-mediated SUSY breaking and identifies the associated global symmetry breaking patterns, with potential comparability to lattice simulations of non-SUSY theories—except for a pseudoreal chiral case.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand how global symmetries break under controlled SUSY-breaking deformations in t-confining theories, and to assess their relevance for non-supersymmetric lattice studies.

Method: Analytical field-theoretic analysis of symmetry breaking patterns in supersymmetric t-confining gauge theories deformed by small anomaly-mediated supersymmetry breaking.

Result: Explicit identification of global symmetry breaking patterns; one case is noted as pseudoreal and chiral, precluding direct comparison to standard lattice simulations.

Conclusion: The symmetry breaking structure is systematically determined, offering a bridge between SUSY and non-SUSY confinement studies—though limited by representation reality properties in one case.

Abstract: We study deformation of supersymmetric $t$-confining (truly confininig) gauge theories with small anomaly mediation of supersymmetry breaking. We identify breaking pattern of global symmetries in the theories. These results can be compared in priciple to lattice simulation of non-supersymmetric theories (except for one of the cases where the theory is pseudoreal and chiral).

</details>


### [10] [Finite stress-tensor moment tomography of boundary-graviton coherences](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.06363)
*Dyuman Bhattacharya,Robert B. Mann*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper studies stress moment tomography for boundary gravitons in AdS₃ gravity, showing that order-N stress moment measurements are informationally incomplete for distinguishing level-N descendant states, while order-(N+1) measurements are complete—establishing exact informational thresholds for state distinguishability in both chiral and full left-right Virasoro modules.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To determine the minimal order of boundary stress tensor moments needed to fully reconstruct (i.e., tomographically distinguish) finite-descendant-support states of boundary gravitons in AdS₃ gravity under Brown-Henneaux boundary conditions.

Method: Analyzes the Shapovalov matrix within a fixed highest-weight Virasoro module under nondegeneracy assumptions; computes information content of n-point correlators of the boundary stress tensor (stress moments); establishes completeness/incompleteness via rank analysis of the associated measurement map on density matrix blocks up to level L.

Result: Stress moment tomography at order N is informationally incomplete for distinguishing pure states supported on descendants up to level N ≥ 2; order-(N+1) tomography is informationally complete on the unitary regular locus. Analogous exact thresholds hold for density-matrix blocks up to level L and extend to the full left-right (chiral × antichiral) theory.

Conclusion: The informational threshold for distinguishing finite-support boundary-graviton states is sharp: N+1 stress moment insertions are necessary and sufficient to uniquely identify states with maximal descendant level N; this has implications for when such states become semiclassically distinguishable in the bulk.

Abstract: Einstein gravity in 2+1 dimensions is a topological theory that does not admit the existence of local bulk gravitons. However, $\mathrm{AdS}_3$ gravity with Brown-Henneaux boundary conditions does admit the existence of boundary gravitons, which are described by Virasoro descendants. An observer living on the asymptotic boundary can use finite order moments of the boundary stress tensor as a probe of these states. We fix one highest-weight module and assume that the Shapovalov forms are nondegenerate. We also assume that the unknown state the observer is probing has a known finite descendant support and that interlevel coherence is part of the unknown data. We find that, given a descendant at level $N\geq2$, stress moment tomography at order $N$ (meaning $N$ stress moment insertions) is informationally incomplete, while tomography with stress moments of order $N+1$ is informationally complete. This is to say that, on the unitary regular locus, orthogonal pure states can have the same complete order-$N$ record. Likewise, for exact finite-window result for levels through $L\geq2$, tomography of order $L$ is incomplete while tomography of order $L+1$ recovers every density-matrix block. Corresponding exact thresholds hold in the full left-right theory. In heavy sectors for which a semiclassical bulk interpretation exists, these thresholds say when finite-support boundary-graviton states first become distinguishable.

</details>


### [11] [Fermionic hidden zeros](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.06552)
*Yong Zhang,Pengfei Zhu*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper identifies and proves a universal class of 'hidden zeros' in tree-level scattering amplitudes involving massless fermions in gauge and gravity theories, showing vanishing occurs on rectangular kinematic loci with species-dependent bridge conditions; the mechanism is traced to local worldsheet fusion channels and extends across D=4,6,10, including EYM and supergravity contexts.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To uncover structural constraints and hidden symmetries governing fermionic scattering amplitudes—specifically, systematic vanishing behavior ('hidden zeros') not explained by standard selection rules or kinematic singularities.

Method: Worldsheet-based analysis of color-ordered gauge-theory amplitudes; derivation of rectangular kinematic loci and species-dependent bridge conditions; extension via dimensional reduction and factorized gamma-traceless polarizations to gravitational and mixed Einstein-Yang-Mills settings.

Result: Proof that color-ordered amplitudes with arbitrary even numbers of massless adjoint fermions and gluons vanish on general rectangular kinematic loci in D=4,6,10; classification of fermion–fermion, fermion–gluon, and gluon–gluon bridges; extension to 10D graviton–gravitino and single-trace EYM amplitudes with gluinos and gravitons.

Conclusion: A finite, local worldsheet structure underlies fermionic hidden zeros universally across gauge and gravitational amplitudes, revealing deep connections between kinematic geometry, fermion statistics, and fusion-channel universality.

Abstract: We uncover and prove a general class of hidden zeros in tree-level amplitudes with massless fermions. For arbitrary even numbers and arbitrary arrangements of massless real adjoint fermions and gluons, color-ordered gauge-theory amplitudes in $D=4,6,10$ vanish on general rectangular kinematic loci supplemented by species-dependent bridge conditions. The fermion--fermion bridge is governed simply by the vector current $χ_iγ^μχ_j$, while mixed and gluonic bridges are fixed by the corresponding gauge-covariant contractions. A worldsheet analysis proves the result at arbitrary multiplicity and traces its universality to the local fusion channels $ff\to g$, $fg/gf\to f$, and $gg\to g$, with no new primitive bridge structures appearing at higher fermion multiplicity. The same mechanism extends to ten-dimensional amplitudes with gravitons and arbitrary even numbers of gravitinos in factorized gamma-traceless polarizations, and to single-trace Einstein-Yang-Mills amplitudes with adjoint gluinos and gravitons. These results expose a finite local structure underlying fermionic hidden zeros across gauge and gravitational amplitudes.

</details>


### [12] [Constructing the Monopole Formula for $A$-Type Good Quivers via Quiver Yangians](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.06711)
*Tiantai Chen*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper establishes a quiver Yangian interpretation of monopole formulas for 3D N=4 good A-type quiver gauge theories (T_ρ(SU(N))), linking truncated shifted quiver Yangian generators to U(N) Casimirs on a Slodowy slice and matching Hall–Littlewood-derived fugacities to reconstruct the monopole formula as a Hilbert series.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To interpret the monopole formula — a key tool for computing the Coulomb branch Hilbert series of 3D N=4 gauge theories — via the conjectured quiver Yangian/Coulomb branch algebra correspondence, especially for T_ρ(SU(N)) theories.

Method: The authors use the action of the (truncated shifted) quiver Yangian on 1/2-BPS vortices to define boundary-adapted generators; they then derive classical relations among them expressed in terms of U(N) Casimirs on the Slodowy slice S^{gl_N}_ρ, and verify consistency with Hall–Littlewood fugacities from the monopole formula.

Result: The boundary-adapted generators and their Casimir relations reproduce the correct graded structure (fugacities) of the monopole formula for T_ρ(SU(N)), thereby identifying the monopole formula as the Hilbert series of the truncated shifted quiver Yangian.

Conclusion: The monopole formula for T_ρ(SU(N)) theories admits a precise algebraic realization via the truncated shifted quiver Yangian, confirming and refining the quiver Yangian/Coulomb branch correspondence for this class of theories.

Abstract: Based on the conjectured (and checked for tree-type quivers) quiver Yangian/Coulomb branch algebra correspondence, we give a quiver Yangian interpretation of the monopole formulas for 3D $\mathcal N=4$ good $A$-type quiver gauge theories, also known as the $T_ρ(SU(N))$ theories. Using the algebra action on the $\frac12$-BPS vortices, we reorganize the generators of the truncated shifted quiver Yangian into boundary-adapted generators, and obtain the classical relations in terms of $U(N)$ Casimirs on the Slodowy slice $\mathcal S^{\mathfrak{gl}_N}_ρ$, whose coordinates are given by these boundary-adapted generators. We also show that the boundary-adapted generators and the Casimir relations have the correct fugacities as predicted by the Hall-Littlewood expression of the monopole formula for $T_ρ(SU(N))$, hence reconstructing it as the Hilbert series of the truncated shifted quiver Yangian.

</details>


### [13] [Dual N=1 Lagrangians for Argyres-Douglas theories](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.06995)
*Minseok Cho,Jaewon Song*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper constructs new 4D N=1 Lagrangian gauge theories—SU(2)^n quivers with flip fields—that flow in the IR to N=2 Argyres-Douglas theories of types (A1, A2n), (A1, A2n+1), (A1, D2n+1), and (A1, D2n+2), providing dual descriptions to prior Sp(n) or SU(n+1) realizations and exchanging rank with quiver length.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To provide novel, tractable N=1 Lagrangian descriptions for strongly-coupled N=2 Argyres-Douglas fixed points—specifically those of (A1, Ak) and (A1, Dk) type—which were previously realized only via higher-rank N=1 theories (Sp(n) or SU(n+1)), thereby enabling new computational and conceptual access to their dynamics.

Method: Constructing SU(2)^n linear quiver gauge theories augmented by gauge-singlet 'flip fields', then analyzing their infrared fixed points using supersymmetric renormalization group flow arguments, matching central charges, and verifying equivalence to known (A1, Ak)/(A1, Dk) Argyres-Douglas theories via dualities and anomaly matching.

Result: Explicit N=1 Lagrangians—SU(2)^n quivers with prescribed flip field content—for all four infinite families of (A1, Ak) and (A1, Dk) Argyres-Douglas theories; demonstration that these are dual to earlier Sp(n)/SU(n+1) constructions, with a rank–node exchange symmetry in the quiver description.

Conclusion: The SU(2)^n quiver + flip construction yields a unified, low-rank N=1 Lagrangian framework for (A1, Ak) and (A1, Dk) Argyres-Douglas theories, revealing a novel duality that interchanges gauge group rank and quiver length, enriching the landscape of UV completions for strongly-coupled SCFTs.

Abstract: We find a new set of four-dimensional $\mathcal{N}=1$ Lagrangian gauge theories that flow in the infrared to $\mathcal{N}=2$ Argyres-Douglas theories of type $(A_1, A_{2n})$, $(A_1, A_{2n+1})$, $(A_1, D_{2n+1})$, and $(A_1, D_{2n+2})$. The new dual theories are given by $SU(2)^n$ quiver gauge theories with a number of gauge-singlet flip fields. This description provides dual theories for the previous $\mathcal{N}=1$ Lagrangian realizations given by $Sp(n)$ or $SU(n+1)$ gauge theories, exchanging the rank and the number of nodes in a quiver diagram.

</details>


### [14] [Tachyon-massless couplings at order $α'$ in bosonic string theory](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.07221)
*Mohammad R. Garousi*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper uses T-duality to constrain the four-derivative (α′) effective action of bosonic string theory involving the tachyon and massless fields, showing that only even-tachyon couplings survive; it identifies 6 independent terms with two parameters, fixes them via zero-tachyon limit and 4-tachyon S-matrix, and finds the tachyon potential is consistent with (2/α′)(−1 + cos T), implying tachyon condensation to AdS spacetime with Λ = −4/α′.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To resolve the structure of the classical effective action for bosonic string theory—specifically, whether only even powers of the tachyon field appear—and to systematically determine the allowed four-derivative couplings between tachyon and massless fields using T-duality as a fundamental symmetry constraint.

Method: Applies T-duality transformations to the low-energy effective action; employs higher-derivative field redefinitions to fix a scheme where all couplings (except the tachyon potential) involve only covariant derivatives; constructs a minimal basis of 14 independent four-derivative scalar invariants; imposes T-duality invariance to reduce to 6 nonzero couplings with two free parameters; fixes parameters by matching to the known massless-sector effective action (zero-tachyon limit) and the sphere-level 4-tachyon S-matrix amplitude.

Result: The four-derivative effective action contains exactly six independent T-duality-invariant couplings parameterized by two constants; one constant is fixed by the massless limit (recovering standard α′-corrected gravity + dilaton + B-field action), the other by the 4-tachyon amplitude, yielding a positive coefficient for the T⁴ term; the resulting tachyon potential is consistent with (2/α′)(−1 + cos T), predicting tachyon condensation at T = π.

Conclusion: T-duality strongly constrains the bosonic string effective action: only even-tachyon couplings survive at α′ order, and the full tachyon–massless sector dynamics are governed by a cosine-type potential, leading to spontaneous generation of an Anti-de Sitter vacuum with cosmological constant Λ = −4/α′ upon tachyon condensation.

Abstract: Recently, it has been proposed that the classical effective action of bosonic string theory should contain only couplings with an even number of tachyon fields. We employ the T-duality procedure to derive such couplings at order \(α'\) (four-derivative order) for the tachyon and massless fields. We first observe that, through higher-derivative field redefinitions of the tachyon potential term, one can choose a scheme in which, apart from the tachyon potential itself, all remaining couplings involve only covariant derivatives of the tachyon and the massless fields. We then construct a minimal basis of such couplings at four-derivative order, consisting of 14 independent terms. Imposing T-duality reduces this set to 6 nonzero couplings, expressed in terms of two unfixed parameters. One of these parameters is determined by matching to the known effective action in the zero-tachyon limit, while the other is fixed by comparison with the sphere-level S-matrix element of four tachyon vertex operators. This latter comparison also establishes that the coefficient of the \(T^4\) term in the tachyon potential is positive. Remarkably, the tachyon mass term and this quartic term are consistent with a potential of the form \((2/α')(-1+\cos T)\), which implies that the closed string tachyon condenses to the minimum of the potential at \(T=π\), thereby generating an Anti-de Sitter spacetime with cosmological constant \(Λ=-4/α'\).

</details>


### [15] [Can one hear the shape of a black hole singularity?](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.07514)
*Sean A. Hartnoll,Alexander Zhiboedov*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper computes large-overtone quasinormal frequencies of asymptotically AdS black holes with Kasner-type near-singularity geometry, showing that Kasner exponents and BKL transitions are encoded in the dispersion relation ∂ωₙ/∂k² at k=0, and establishes a mapping between overtone number n and proper time to the singularity via WKB analysis.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To probe the near-singularity geometry (specifically Kasner epochs and BKL chaos) of asymptotically AdS black holes through observable boundary data—namely, quasinormal mode spectra—leveraging the holographic principle.

Method: WKB analysis of quasinormal modes for asymptotically AdS black holes, isolating non-analytic contributions to the frequency spectrum arising from the Kasner near-singularity region; analytical derivation of the large-n asymptotics of ωₙ(k), validated against numerical black hole models.

Result: The Kasner exponents are cleanly extractable from the large-n limit of ∂ωₙ/∂k²|_{k=0}; Kasner transitions manifest as discrete changes in the n-dependence of ωₙ, enabling reconstruction of the BKL sequence; a direct map n ↔ proper time to singularity is established.

Conclusion: Quasinormal mode overtones serve as a high-resolution probe of spacetime geometry arbitrarily close to the singularity; the spectral dispersion encodes universal near-singularity physics—specifically Kasner parameters and their transitions—in a boundary-accessible way, supporting a refined holographic dictionary for singularities.

Abstract: We compute the large overtone quasinormal frequencies $ω_n$ of asymptotically AdS black holes with interior near-singularity Kasner epochs. We show that the Kasner exponents can be extracted cleanly from the large $n$ behaviour of the dispersion $\partial ω_n/\partial k^2$ at $k=0$, where $k$ is a boundary momentum. We show that Kasner transitions, as occur in BKL near-singularity chaos, are audible in the quasinormal frequencies and reveal a map between overtone number $n$ and proper time to the singularity. Our results are obtained using a WKB analysis that isolates non-analytic exponents, determined by the near-singularity spacetime, from non-universal data such as the optical distance from the boundary to the singularity. We check our formulae in several numerical models.

</details>


### [16] [Exact spin form factors and correlations at a massive Kramers-Wannier interface](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.07612)
*Zoltan Bajnok,Yizhuang Liu*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper computes exact finite-volume spin form factors and two-point correlations for a massive Kramer-Wannier interface in the scaling Ising model, using dual lattice realizations (self-dual inhomogeneous chain and mixed thermal average), yielding exact NSR spin matrix elements and a factorized Fredholm determinant for the interface correlator—validated by defect TCSA and fermionic spin-chain numerics.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To determine exact non-perturbative correlation functions and spectral data for a topological interface (Kramer–Wannier) separating ordered and disordered phases in the 2D Ising model at criticality, where standard CFT techniques fail due to massiveness and boundary/interface effects.

Method: Combines two complementary lattice descriptions: (i) a self-dual inhomogeneous Ising chain whose continuum limit yields a sign-changing Majorana mass profile; (ii) a Euclidean space–time exchanged formulation recast as a mixed thermal average solvable via Toeplitz/Fredholm determinant methods. Integrates defect truncated conformal space approach (defect TCSA) and exact fermionic diagonalization for cross-verification.

Result: Exact Neveu–Schwarz–Ramond (NSR) spin matrix elements; factorized Fredholm determinant representation for the interface two-point correlator; full finite-volume spin form factors; confirmed interface spectrum and matrix elements via defect TCSA and independent fermionic spin-chain calculations.

Conclusion: The Kramer–Wannier interface in the scaling Ising model admits an exactly solvable massive defect description, with correlators fully characterized by integrable structures (mass-kink Majorana field + Fredholm determinants), establishing a rigorous bridge between lattice defect physics, boundary CFT, and integrable QFT.

Abstract: We determine exact finite-volume spin form factors and two-point correlations for a massive Kramer--Wannier interface in the scaling Ising model, separating ordered and disordered phases. The interface admits two complementary lattice realizations: a self-dual inhomogeneous Ising chain whose continuum limit has a sign-changing Majorana mass profile, and, after exchanging Euclidean space and time, a mixed thermal average amenable to Toeplitz/Fredholm methods. Combining these descriptions, we obtain the exact Neveu-Schwarz-Ramond spin matrix elements and a factorized Fredholm determinant for the interface correlator. Defect TCSA and independent fermionic spin-chain calculations confirm the predicted spectrum and matrix elements.

</details>


### [17] [(Anti-)De Sitter null strings and Carroll-Weyl symmetry](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.07765)
*Ulf Lindström,Bo Sundborg*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper constructs a tensionless string in de Sitter space (dS_d) by lifting the standard tensionless string to R^{1,d}, gauging worldsheet scale symmetry to break translation invariance while preserving SO(1,d) dS_d symmetry; gauge-fixing yields a d-dimensional theory matching expected properties of dS_d tensionless strings, identifying the Carroll–Weyl symmetric string action in R^{1,d} as having a d-dimensional dS target-space interpretation — resolving prior confusion about its dimensionality — and extends naturally to Anti-de Sitter (AdS) tensionless strings.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To resolve ambiguity in the target-space interpretation of the Carroll–Weyl symmetric string action and provide a consistent, geometrically grounded formulation of tensionless strings in curved (dS and AdS) backgrounds, where conventional tensile string formulations face obstructions.

Method: Lifts the tensionless string to embedding space R^{1,d}, gauges worldsheet scale symmetry to introduce dS_d-invariant (SO(1,d)) but translation-breaking terms; imposes a de Sitter-symmetric gauge fixing to obtain a d-dimensional effective theory; identifies and interprets the resulting action as the Carroll–Weyl string with intrinsic dS_d target-space geometry.

Result: A fully consistent dS_d tensionless string theory is constructed algebraically from flat embedding space, with the Carroll–Weyl action now unambiguously interpreted as a d-dimensional dS string—not a (d+1)-dimensional flat-space string; the framework generalizes straightforwardly to AdS_d tensionless strings.

Conclusion: Tensionless strings in maximally symmetric curved spacetimes can be realized purely algebraically via symmetry-gauged embedding constructions, clarifying long-standing interpretational issues and opening avenues for broader applications beyond (A)dS.

Abstract: We construct a $dS_d$ null string in a novel way by lifting the standard tensionless string to $R^{1,d}$. Gauging worldsheet scale symmetry introduces terms in the action breaking translation invariance, but preserving the $dS_d$ symmetry $SO(1,d)$. Choosing a De Sitter symmetric gauge fixing of the scale symmetry yields a $d$ dimensional theory which we show has all the expected properties of $dS_d$ tensionless strings. The action happens to be the intriguing Carrol-Weyl symmetric string action in $R^{1,d}$, now with a clear target space interpretation in $d$ dimensions. That it does not, as recently claimed, represent a ($d+1$)-dimensional string in flat ($d+1$)-dimensional Minkowski space settles an apparent issue with the Carrol-Weyl string model. Furthermore, while there are clear obstacles for similar formulations of tensile strings in (A)dS backgrounds, our new insights are easily modified to the formulation of Anti-De Sitter tensionless strings. That our construction generates a curved geometry purely algebraically may have even wider applications.

</details>


### [18] [Eikonal Scattering of Two D0-branes with Graviton Emission](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.07788)
*Paolo Di Vecchia,Dawid Maskalaniec*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper computes five-point amplitudes for graviton and dilaton emission in D0-brane scattering using boundary-state formalism in bosonic and type II superstring theories, and confirms consistency with worldline effective field theory in the low-energy limit.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand closed-string radiation (graviton/dilaton) from D0-brane collisions via string-theoretic methods and verify consistency with low-energy effective descriptions.

Method: Boundary-state formalism to compute five-point disk amplitudes for massless closed-string emission; comparison with worldline effective field theory in the field-theory limit (including nonzero impact parameter).

Result: Graviton and dilaton emission amplitudes are explicitly computed in both bosonic and type II superstring theories; their field-theory limits match independent worldline calculations for finite impact parameter.

Conclusion: The boundary-state approach correctly captures low-energy gravitational radiation from D0-brane scattering, supporting the consistency of string-theoretic and effective-worldline descriptions of brane dynamics.

Abstract: We study the emission of a massless closed-string state in the scattering of two D0-branes using the boundary-state formalism. We compute the corresponding five-point amplitudes for graviton and dilaton emission in bosonic string theory and in type II superstring theory, and analyze their field-theory limit. For nonzero impact parameter, the resulting amplitudes are reproduced by an independent calculation in the worldline formulation of the corresponding low-energy theory.

</details>


### [19] [Logarithmic correction to the entropy of near-extremal higher-curvature black holes](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.07801)
*Pablo Bueno,Pablo A. Cano,Robie A. Hennigar,Javier Moreno,Ángel J. Murcia,Guido van der Velde*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper establishes a universal near-horizon JT gravity description for near-extremal black holes in broad D-dimensional gravitational theories (including Lovelock and Quasitopological gravities) via their spherically symmetric reduction to 2D Horndeski theory, and proves universality of the logarithmic quantum entropy correction S_JT = (3/2) log(T/T_breakdown), explicitly computing T_breakdown from D-dimensional data.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To identify universal low-temperature quantum features of near-extremal black holes across diverse higher-dimensional gravitational theories beyond Einstein gravity.

Method: Exploits the dimensional reduction of spherically symmetric D-dimensional gravitational theories to two-dimensional Horndeski theory; constructs the universal near-horizon Jackiw–Teitelboim (JT) effective action; computes the one-loop exact JT partition function to derive the quantum-corrected entropy; determines T_breakdown by matching the JT description to the parent D-dimensional theory.

Result: Demonstrates universality of the logarithmic quantum entropy correction S_JT = (3/2) log(T/T_breakdown) for all D-dimensional theories admitting a 2D Horndeski reduction; derives the general expression for the breakdown temperature T_breakdown in terms of D-dimensional couplings and horizon data; verifies results for charged black holes in Lovelock gravity and regular black holes in Quasitopological gravity.

Conclusion: The logarithmic quantum correction to near-extremal black hole entropy is a robust, model-independent feature encoded in the universal JT gravity near-horizon description, with T_breakdown determined solely by the infrared data of the parent D-dimensional theory.

Abstract: The spherically symmetric sector of broad classes of $D$-dimensional gravitational theories can be effectively described by general two-dimensional Horndeski theories. Exploiting this correspondence, we study the low-temperature dynamics of near-extremal black hole solutions of the parent theories by deriving a universal near-horizon effective description in terms of Jackiw--Teitelboim (JT) gravity. We explicitly show that the logarithmic quantum correction to the entropy, $S_{\rm JT}=\frac{3}{2}\log \left(T/T_{\rm breakdown} \right)$, previously derived for near-extremal black holes in Einstein gravity coupled to matter from the one-loop exact JT partition function, is universal across all models admitting an effective two-dimensional Horndeski description. We determine the general form of the scale at which this correction becomes dominant, $T_{\rm breakdown}$, in terms of the data of the $D$-dimensional theories. We illustrate our general results with charged black holes in Lovelock gravities and regular black holes in Quasitopological gravities.

</details>


### [20] [SolutionsX: supergravity theories/solutions as code, with an agentic workflow for machine-verifiable physics](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.07826)
*Vasil Dimitrov*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: SolutionsX is a Mathematica package built on xAct that enables machine-verifiable, reproducible encoding and verification of supergravity (and other field theory) solutions; it supports automated codification by AI agents, includes benchmarking against xAct, and establishes a verifiable database for human and agent use.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Supergravity solutions in the literature are often unverifiable due to ambiguous conventions, typos, and algebraic complexity; there is a need for reproducible, self-contained, machine-checkable records.

Method: Developed SolutionsX — a Mathematica package layered on xAct — that generates kernel-replayable, self-verifying code for tensorial, form-based, or spinorial physics models; applied it to published supergravity solutions, benchmarked its engine against native xAct, and conducted blind AI-agent codification tasks using the package as an internal verifier.

Result: Demonstrated full workflow reproducibility for a published solution; showed SolutionsX engine matches or exceeds xAct performance in key operations; achieved three successful AI-agent codifications of recent papers within <3 hours each, with real-time kernel verification.

Conclusion: SolutionsX bridges the gap between theoretical derivation and computational reproducibility, serving both researchers (as a verification and reuse tool) and AI agents (as a runtime verifier and training corpus), laying groundwork for a scalable, verified knowledge base in mathematical physics.

Abstract: A supergravity solution in the literature is an artifact that cannot be verified without re-deriving it: conventions might not be clearly stated, papers have typos, algebra is tedious. SolutionsX is a Mathematica package that turns an xAct environment, whatever physics it may describe, into a stored, machine-verifiable record: code that replays in a fresh kernel, carries its own verification, and can later be fetched for re-use or extension. The shipped examples are supergravity theories and their solutions, but the scope is anything xAct reaches: abstract tensors, differential forms, spinors in any dimension. We follow one published solution through the entire workflow, benchmark the package's component engine against xAct's, and report on three blind runs of an agentic workflow in which an AI coding agent, given the package and a small toolkit, correctly codified a recent paper in under three hours. A database of such records is useful to researchers today and gives agents worked examples to learn from. The package acts as an internal verifier during the thought process of an agent: a claim is checked in the kernel before it is made.

</details>


### [21] [Unitary quantum conformal Bondi-Metzner-Sachs field theories](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.07828)
*Daniel Grumiller,Iva Lovrekovic*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper applies unitarity and positivity constraints to 2D field theories with quantum conformal BMS symmetry, restricting the central charge c to only four discrete values (1, 3/2, 13/7, 2) within a narrow positive range; their primary spectra are computed and found compatible with BPS-like and holographic bounds.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To identify consistent, unitary two-dimensional field theories invariant under the quantum conformal Bondi-Metzner-Sachs (BMS) algebra — a symmetry combining 2D conformal invariance and asymptotic symmetries of flat spacetimes — motivated by flat-space holography and quantum gravity in asymptotically flat geometries.

Method: Imposing unitarity (via representation theory and norm positivity) and vertex algebra positivity on the quantum conformal BMS algebra in two dimensions; deriving bounds on the central charge c; classifying admissible c values; computing spectra of primary operators using representation theory; verifying consistency with BPS-type and holographic energy bounds.

Result: Unitarity restricts c to 0 < c < 2.053; vertex algebra positivity further selects exactly four allowed central charges: c = 1, 3/2, 13/7 ≈ 1.857, and 2. For each, the full spectrum of Virasoro–BMS primaries is explicitly determined and shown to satisfy BPS-like saturation conditions and holographic spectral inequalities.

Conclusion: Only four unitary, positively realized quantum conformal BMS-invariant 2D field theories exist, corresponding to these discrete central charges; their well-behaved spectra support their viability as candidate duals in flat-space holography.

Abstract: We impose unitarity constraints on two-dimensional field theories whose symmetries form the quantum conformal Bondi-Metzner-Sachs algebra, which combines conformal symmetries with asymptotic symmetries of asymptotically flat spacetimes. Positivity constraints put bounds on the central charge, 0<c<2.053. Positivity of the associated vertex algebra allows only four central charges, c = 1, 3/2, 13/7, 2. Thus, only four candidates for unitary field theories remain. We calculate their spectra of primaries and show that they are compatible with BPS-like and holographic inequalities.

</details>


### [22] [Diffeomorphism-invariant Approach to Asymptotically Safe Quantum Gravity](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.07829)
*Friederike Ihssen,Benjamin Knorr,Silas Mezger,Jan M. Pawlowski,Paul P. Sprenger*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper introduces a diffeomorphism-invariant renormalisation group framework for asymptotically safe quantum gravity, enabling background-independent, quantum-covariant effective actions and a robust computation of the Reuter fixed point with controlled approximation errors.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To resolve the tension between maintaining diffeomorphism invariance and achieving predictive power in asymptotically safe quantum gravity—particularly under standard background-field RG methods that break invariance or obscure operator relevance counting.

Method: Constructs a physics-informed renormalisation group flow using a diffeomorphism-invariant kernel; ensures quantum diffeomorphism invariance at every RG step and preserves canonical relevance counting of gravitational operators; implements manifestly covariant truncations without background splitting.

Result: Derives a quantum diffeomorphism-invariant, background-independent effective action; computes the Reuter fixed point without background-field artifacts; quantifies regularisation dependence and systematic errors from truncation and approximation.

Conclusion: Diffeomorphism invariance can be maintained throughout the RG flow without sacrificing computational control, supporting the viability of asymptotic safety as a fundamental quantum theory of gravity.

Abstract: We provide a novel diffeomorphism-invariant approach to asymptotically safe metric quantum gravity. It is based on physics-informed renormalisation group flows with a renormalisation group kernel that guarantees diffeomorphism invariance at each renormalisation group step. Importantly, it also allows us to control and maintain the relevance counting of operators in metric quantum gravity. The resulting effective action is quantum diffeomorphism-invariant and background-independent. As a first non-trivial application, we compute the Reuter fixed point in a manifestly diffeomorphism-invariant way. This computation is augmented with a detailed discussion of regularisation dependence and a systematic estimate of the errors arising from approximations.

</details>


### [23] [Truncated Polyakov bootstrap for BCFTs: Neumann-Dirichlet flow and Ising special transition](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.07841)
*Kaushik Kangsabanik,Apratim Kaviraj,Astha Tiwari*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: Extends the truncated Polyakov bootstrap to BCFTs using analytic functionals; identifies nonperturbative solutions via deformations from generalized free fields (GFF) with Neumann to Dirichlet boundary conditions; applies method to 3d Ising special transition using Wilson–Fisher perturbation, yielding BCFT data consistent with lattice results.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To develop a nonperturbative bootstrap framework for boundary conformal field theories (BCFTs) that bridges analytic functional methods and physical boundary conditions, enabling precise computation of BCFT data beyond perturbation theory.

Method: Applies the truncated Polyakov bootstrap within the analytic functional framework to solve BCFT crossing equations; constructs a continuous deformation family from generalized free fields (GFF) with Neumann boundary condition to Dirichlet; uses Wilson–Fisher perturbation as the deformation path for the 3d Ising special transition.

Result: Obtains high-precision BCFT spectral data (e.g., operator dimensions, OPE coefficients) for the 3d Ising special transition; achieves remarkable agreement with existing lattice simulations; provides new predictions where lattice data are unavailable.

Conclusion: The truncated Polyakov bootstrap, combined with analytic functionals and GFF-based deformations, is a powerful and systematic tool for solving nonperturbative BCFTs, successfully reproducing known results and generating novel quantitative predictions.

Abstract: We extend the recently proposed truncated Polyakov bootstrap to boundary conformal field theories (BCFTs). The formalism uses the analytic functional framework to find approximate solutions of BCFT crossing. We show that a generic nonperturbative solution can be identified by relating it to a family of deformations around generalized free fields (GFF) with a fixed boundary condition. We show this by tracking the flow of BCFT data from GFF with Neumann boundary condition to that with Dirichlet. The same method is then used for the 3d Ising special transition, using the perturbative Wilson-Fisher description as the deformation to identify it. The BCFT data obtained are in remarkable agreement with existing lattice results where available, and are otherwise new.

</details>


### [24] [Scattering of Null strings - Flipped Vacuum & CHY](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.07845)
*Arjun Bagchi,Sachin Grover,Sharang Rajesh Iyer,Amartya Saha,Stephan Stieberger*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper constructs scattering amplitudes for null tensionless strings using a 'flipped' vacuum representation of the 2D Conformal Carroll Algebra (CCA), yielding CHY formulae for massless states.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To develop a consistent quantum theory of null tensionless strings by leveraging the residual 2D Conformal Carroll symmetry and exploring distinct vacua, particularly the 'flipped' highest-weight vacuum.

Method: Uses the ILST action for classical null strings; identifies three quantum theories based on CCA representations; focuses on the 'flipped' vacuum (highest-weight); introduces novel vertex operators tied to compactified null strings to construct scattering amplitudes.

Result: Derives scattering amplitudes for massless states in the null 'flipped' string theory that reproduce the Cachazo-He-Yuan (CHY) formulae.

Conclusion: The 'flipped' vacuum formulation of the quantum null string provides a geometric and algebraic origin for the CHY formalism, linking Carrollian symmetry on the null worldsheet to universal structures in massless scattering.

Abstract: We investigate the scattering of null tensionless strings. Classical null strings, given by the ILST action, give rise to three distinct quantum theories built on different vacua and representations of the underlying 2d Conformal Carroll algebra (CCA) which form the residual gauge symmetries on the null worldsheet. In this paper, we are interested in the quantum theory built out of the so-called ``flipped'' vacuum which realises the highest weight representation of the 2d CCA. By considering a novel class of vertex operators, intimately connected to the compactified null string, we build scattering amplitudes of the theory. We show that one naturally obtains the Cachazo-He-Yuan (CHY) formulae when one considers scattering of massless states in the null ``flipped'' string.

</details>


### [25] [Double Copy from the Flipped Null String](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.07846)
*Arjun Bagchi,Sachin Grover,Sharang Rajesh Iyer,Amartya Saha,Stephan Stieberger*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper establishes a Carrollian world-sheet origin for the double-copy structure in string theory, linking bosonic null string correlators to CHY half-integrands and KLT representations, and connecting lattice momentum-winding sectors to BCJ relations and $O(d,d)$-compatible color currents.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To uncover a world-sheet origin of the double copy—particularly how kinematic numerators and color-kinematics duality emerge from string-theoretic structures—and to reconcile it with $O(d,d)$ T-duality and Carrollian symmetry.

Method: Analysis of tree-level amplitudes of the bosonic null string in the flipped vacuum; identification of level-one and level-two correlators with CHY kinematic half-integrands and Parke-Taylor factors; examination of momentum-winding lattice sectors and their factorization properties; derivation of BCJ ordering and KLT representation from common-momentum lattice sectors.

Result: Level-one vector correlator yields the $(DF)^2$ gauge-theory CHY half-integrand; specific winding sectors produce Parke-Taylor factors; factorized level-two states generate two half-integrands enabling Weyl-cubed graviton amplitudes via symmetric double copy; common-momentum lattice sectors reproduce BCJ relations and field-theory KLT kernel; emergent color sector is compatible with $O(d,d)$ structure.

Conclusion: The double copy has a direct Carrollian world-sheet realization in the bosonic null string; its correlators and lattice sectors encode both kinematic and color structures, suggesting a current-algebra origin for color consistent with double-field-theory geometry.

Abstract: We study the double-copy structure of tree amplitudes of the bosonic null string in the flipped vacuum. We show that its level-one vector correlator supplies the Cachazo-He-Yuan (CHY) kinematic half-integrand of the higher-derivative $(DF)^2$ gauge theory, while distinguished compact momentum-winding sectors generate the complementary Parke-Taylor factor. For factorized level-two states, the same Carrollian world-sheet produces two kinematic half-integrands and hence the symmetric double copy to the six-derivative Weyl-cubed graviton sector. Lattice sectors with common external momenta reproduce the Bern-Carrasco-Johansson (BCJ) ordering relation and the corresponding field-theory Kawai-Lewellen-Tye (KLT) representation. These results provide a direct Carrollian world-sheet origin of the double copy and point toward a current-algebra realization of the emergent color sector compatible with the $O(d,d)$ structure of the compact lattice.

</details>


### [26] [Entanglement Wedge Reconstruction Beyond the Large N Limit via the Twirled Petz Map](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.07866)
*Arash Alipour Shahmiri,Maryam Sharifian,Niloofar Vardian*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper establishes quantum error correction as the natural framework for bulk reconstruction in AdS/CFT beyond the semiclassical (large N) regime, using the twirled Petz map to systematically incorporate 1/N corrections and deriving an explicit bi-local correction term.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To extend entanglement wedge reconstruction beyond the leading-order large N (semiclassical) approximation, where existing modular flow and Petz map methods fail to capture quantum gravitational corrections.

Method: Applies quantum error correction theory to holography; employs the twirled Petz map to construct a systematic expansion in 1/N; derives subleading corrections via a subleading Petz kernel yielding a bi-local bulk operator reconstruction.

Result: Reproduces known leading-order reconstruction formulas; obtains an explicit, controlled bi-local correction term governed by a subleading Petz kernel; provides a systematic method for incorporating 1/N corrections to entanglement wedge reconstruction.

Conclusion: Quantum error correction is the appropriate foundational framework for bulk reconstruction beyond large N; this approach enables controlled access to fully quantum gravitational regimes and sharpens the conceptual role of QEC in holography.

Abstract: Entanglement wedge reconstruction provides the sharpest formulation of bulk locality in AdS/CFT, identifying the entanglement wedge as the largest bulk region reconstructible from a boundary subregion. While this statement is well understood at leading order in the large N expansion through both modular flow and Petz map reconstruction, its extension beyond the semiclassical regime has remained unclear. In this work, we show that quantum error correction provides the natural framework for addressing this problem. At leading order, we reproduce the known leading-order reconstruction formulas, and by employing the twirled Petz map, we obtain a systematic and explicit method for incorporating subleading 1/N corrections. Beyond the leading approximation, we have obtained an explicit bi-local correction controlled by a subleading Petz kernel. Our results establish a controlled approach to bulk reconstruction beyond large N and sharpening the role of quantum error correction in holography. This framework provides a controlled path toward understanding bulk reconstruction in fully quantum gravitational settings.

</details>


### [27] [ABJM/BMN Index Matching in the Plane-Wave Limit](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.07872)
*Chi-Ming Chang,Kangning Liu*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper proves coefficientwise equality between refined supersymmetric indices of ABJM theory and the BMN matrix model in the plane-wave limit at any fixed positive Chern–Simons level k, extending prior single-membrane results to multi-membrane configurations via monopole-harmonic/BMN-matrix-harmonic kernel correspondence and residue-based Higgs branch analysis.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To generalize the known single-membrane index equivalence between ABJM theory and the BMN matrix model to multi-membrane sectors while clarifying the role of Chern–Simons level k, longitudinal momenta, monopole fluxes, and finite-spin cutoffs in the plane-wave limit.

Method: Analyzes refined supersymmetric indices using Higgs-branch localization; identifies scalar-dressed equal-flux ABJM monopoles with BMN fuzzy-sphere vacua; computes plane-wave contributions via diagonal-holonomy integrals with monopole-harmonic kernels; compares kernel structure and cutoff behavior against BMN matrix-harmonic expansion; decomposes index difference into non-plane-wave remainder and harmonic tail above finite spin cutoff.

Result: Coefficientwise equality of refined indices holds for all fixed k > 0 and arbitrary finite multi-membrane families (fixed multiplicities/gauge rank), with matching monopole- and matrix-harmonic kernels up to the BMN upper-spin cutoff; increasing k shifts the non-plane-wave remainder to higher fugacity grade without altering the surviving kernel or cutoff contribution.

Conclusion: The ABJM–BMN index duality is robust beyond the single-membrane sector: it extends coefficientwise to arbitrary finite multi-membrane configurations in the plane-wave limit, with monopole flux encoding M-theory momentum and multiplicities counting coincident membranes—establishing a precise, level-k–dependent holographic correspondence at the refined index level.

Abstract: We establish coefficientwise equality between the refined supersymmetric indices of ABJM theory and the BMN matrix model in the plane-wave limit at every fixed positive Chern-Simons level k. For multi-membrane families with fixed multiplicities, and hence fixed finite ABJM gauge rank, the equality holds as all longitudinal momenta tend to infinity with their pairwise differences fixed. Scalar-dressed equal-flux ABJM monopoles correspond to BMN vacua of fuzzy-sphere membranes: multiplicities count coincident membranes, whereas flux magnitudes give the momentum per membrane along the M-theory circle. At the index level, the associated Higgs residues yield a diagonal-holonomy integral for the ABJM plane-wave contribution, whose monopole-harmonic kernel agrees with the BMN matrix-harmonic kernel up to the finite BMN upper-spin cutoff. At finite momentum, the full index difference splits into the non-plane-wave remainder and the harmonic tail above this cutoff. Increasing k shifts the former to higher fugacity grade without changing the surviving kernel or the cutoff contribution. This gives a coefficientwise, multi-membrane extension of the earlier single-membrane index relation.

</details>


### [28] [Spherical DAHA as an algebra of framed BPS states](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.07911)
*Kunal Gupta,Pietro Longhi*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper extends the physical realization of skein algebras from SL_N to GL_N in 4d N=2 class S theories, proving an isomorphism between the GL_2 skein algebra of the punctured torus and the spherical double affine Hecke algebra SH^{q,t}_2; it uses q-nonabelianization of the SU(2) N=2^* theory, links skein relations to quantum torus algebras on the Seiberg–Witten curve, and unifies distinct DAHA presentations (Macdonald, cluster-type) via framed wall-crossing governed by the vanilla BPS spectrum.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To generalize the known correspondence between line operators in half-Omega-background 4d N=2 class S theories and SL_N skein algebras to GL_N skein algebras, and to physically unify disparate algebraic realizations of spherical double affine Hecke algebras (DAHAs).

Method: Employs q-nonabelianization for the SU(2) N=2^* gauge theory to realize the GL_2 skein algebra as a quantum torus algebra associated with the Seiberg–Witten curve; analyzes how varying positions on the Coulomb branch yield different presentations (Fenchel–Nielsen/Macdonald vs. Fock–Goncharov/cluster); uses framed wall-crossing controlled by the vanilla BPS spectrum to relate these presentations.

Result: Establishes an isomorphism between the GL_2 skein algebra of the punctured torus and the spherical double affine Hecke algebra SH^{q,t}_2; derives multiple equivalent presentations of SH^{q,t}_2—Macdonald q-difference modules in weak coupling and cluster-type algebras in strong coupling—and shows their transitions are governed by framed wall-crossing.

Conclusion: The GL_N skein algebra admits a unified physical realization in 4d N=2 class S theories with half-Omega background; for N=2, this provides a complete geometric and spectral (BPS) explanation for the structure and representation-theoretic dualities of spherical DAHA.

Abstract: Line operators of 4d $\mathcal{N}=2$ theories of class $\mathcal{S}$ of type $A_{N-1}$ in a half Omega background provide a physical model for $SL_N$ skein algebras. In this paper we extend this correspondence to $GL_N$ skein algebras and prove that the $GL_2$ skein algebra of the punctured torus is isomorphic to the $GL_2$ spherical double affine Hecke algebra ${{\mathbf{S}\ddot{\mathbf{H}}}}^{q,t}_2$. The construction is based on $\mathfrak{q}$-nonabelianization for the $SU(2)$ $\mathcal{N}=2^*$ gauge theory, which realizes the skein algebra as a quantum torus algebra associated with the Seiberg-Witten curve. Different regions of the Coulomb branch yield distinct presentations of ${{\mathbf{S}\ddot{\mathbf{H}}}}^{q,t}_2$. These range from the Macdonald $q$-difference module in Fenchel-Nielsen (weak coupling) charts to cluster-type realizations in Fock-Goncharov (strong coupling) charts. Transitions between these descriptions are governed by the vanilla BPS spectrum of the $\mathcal{N}=2^*$ theory via framed wall-crossing, which provides a unified physical framework for several representations of spherical DAHA.

</details>


### [29] [Wilson loops, M2-branes and strings](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.07928)
*Alexia Nix*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper develops a universal semiclassical quantization framework for probe M2-branes and fundamental strings in asymptotically AdS₄ backgrounds, linking their partition functions to half-BPS Wilson loops in 3D N≥2 Chern-Simons-matter theories; it yields new semiclassical predictions, matches known localization results, and argues that M2-brane quantization captures the full 1/N perturbative expansion of the dual Wilson loop.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
