Motivation: To unify and generalize the semiclassical treatment of probe branes and strings in AdS₄ holography, and to clarify the precise holographic ensemble and perturbative structure (e.g., full 1/N series) underlying Wilson loop expectation values in 3D Chern-Simons-matter theories.

Method: Application of semiclassical quantization techniques to probe M2-branes and fundamental strings in asymptotically AdS₄ spacetimes, combined with holographic duality to half-BPS Wilson loops in 3D N≥2 Chern-Simons-matter theories; cross-checking with supersymmetric localization results where available.

Result: A universal semiclassical quantization framework is established; novel predictions for Wilson loop behavior are obtained; agreement with prior localization results is demonstrated; and it is shown that the correct holographic ensemble implies M2-brane semiclassical quantization reproduces the full perturbative 1/N expansion (in gauge group rank N) of the dual Wilson loop.

Conclusion: The semiclassical quantization of probe M2-branes—not just strings—provides the complete perturbative (in 1/N) description of dual half-BPS Wilson loops in 3D Chern-Simons-matter theories, underscoring the centrality of the M2-brane ensemble in this AdS₄/CFT₃ context.

Abstract: We establish a universal approach for studying the semiclassical quantization of both probe M2-branes and fundamental strings in asymptotically AdS$_{4}$ backgrounds. On the field theory side, their partition functions are dual to the vacuum expectation value of half-BPS Wilson loops of a class of three-dimensional $\mathcal{N}\geq 2$ Chern-Simons-matter theories. Utilizing this universal approach we arrive at novel predictions for the semiclassical behaviour of the Wilson loop or upon availability match our results with the previously obtained expressions via supersymmetric localization. We also show that the correct choice of holographic ensemble suggests that the semiclassical quantization of probe M2-branes yields the full perturbative answer in the rank of the gauge group $N$ for the dual Wilson loop. This is a proceedings contribution to the ''Athens Workshop in Theoretical Physics: 10th Anniversary'', held at the National and Kapodistrian University of Athens on December 17-19 2025.

</details>


### [30] [Fortuitous ABJM Operators as Black Holes](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.08052)
*Connor Behan,Leonardo Pipolo de Gioia*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper studies BPS states in ABJM theory by diagonalizing the anomalous dimension matrix, identifying finitely-BPS operator trajectories, linking them to horizonless geometries vs. black holes, confirming large-N integrability predictions, and contrasting spectral gaps with N=4 SYM.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To distinguish holographically dual operators—those corresponding to horizonless geometries versus black holes—via precise BPS characterization across varying N, leveraging ABJM's computational tractability compared to N=4 SYM.

Method: Diagonalization of the anomalous dimension matrix in U(N)_k × U(N)_{-k} ABJM theory with N treated as a formal parameter; identification and analysis of 'fortuitous' BPS operator trajectories (BPS only at finitely many N); large-N asymptotics and comparison with integrability predictions and cohomological classification.

Result: 16 finitely-BPS operator trajectories (plus descendants) identified in ABJM; their large-N behavior matches integrability predictions; many exhibit graviton-dressing restrictions analogous to black hole microstates; spectral gap to first non-BPS state differs qualitatively from N=4 SYM as expected holographically.

Conclusion: ABJM provides a solvable setting to probe the holographic operator-state correspondence beyond cohomology, revealing structural distinctions between horizonless and black-hole duals via finite-BPS trajectories, dressing constraints, and spectral geometry.

Abstract: Given a superconformal gauge theory at a fixed loop order, BPS states can be studied in two ways --- by enumerating cohomology classes of a nilpotent supercharge or by diagonalizing the anomalous dimension matrix. The second one allows for a deeper look at how holographic theories distinguish between operators based on whether they are dual to horizonless geometries or black holes. To this end, we carry out the diagonalization in $\mathrm{U}(N)_k \times \mathrm{U}(N)_{-k}$ ABJM theory with $N$ as a formal parameter. This makes it possible to define trajectories of operators and focus on the fortuitous ones which are characterized by being BPS at only finitely many values of $N$. In contrast to $\mathcal{N} = 4$ Super Yang-Mills, where it has been numerically prohibitive to study more than one of these, ABJM gives us access to 16 such trajectories along with their descendants. We show that they take an especially simple form at large $N$ where they agree with the predictions of integrability. We also show that many of them, as with black holes, can only be dressed by certain types of gravitons and comment on how this exercise in cohomology also has implications for properties of the full operators. Finally, we show that the gap to the first non-BPS state in ABJM theory qualitatively differs from that of $\mathcal{N} = 4$ Super Yang-Mills in the expected way.

</details>


### [31] [Bogomol'nyi equations for Kruglov strings](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.08111)
*I. Praseyto,U. Ubaydillah,H. S. Ramadhan*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper constructs Bogomol'nyi (BPS) equations for Abelian gauge–Higgs vortices with Kruglov nonlinear electrodynamics—parameterized by exponent σ—replacing the Maxwell sector. Using the stressless method, it derives first-order BPS equations without pre-assuming the Higgs potential, revealing a σ-dependent algebraic coupling between gauge and Higgs fields. A constitutive map Φ(Y;σ) is introduced and analyzed: monotonic/unbounded for σ > 1/2, bounded with finite maximum for 0 < σ < 1/2, and marginally bounded at σ = 1/2—yielding explicit bounds on the nonlinearity parameter β. Closed-form BPS potentials and field equations are obtained for six representative σ values; numerical vortex profiles confirm that the BPS string tension μ_BPS = 2πn is topological and universal across σ and β.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To generalize the BPS vortex framework beyond Maxwell electrodynamics to a broader class of nonlinear electrodynamics (Kruglov family), and to systematically determine under which conditions well-behaved, first-order BPS solutions exist without ad hoc assumptions about the Higgs potential.

Method: Stressless method: derivation of first-order Bogomol'nyi equations from vanishing spatial stress tensor; introduction and analysis of a constitutive map Φ(Y;σ) to decouple and solve the coupled gauge–Higgs system; classification of monotonicity and boundedness of Φ(Y;σ) across σ ∈ (0, ∞); explicit construction of BPS potentials and gauge-field equations for selected σ values; numerical computation of vortex profiles.

Result: Existence and structure of BPS vortices depend critically on σ: strict monotonicity and unboundedness of Φ(Y;σ) for σ > 1/2 ensures a smooth Bogomol'nyi branch; for 0 < σ < 1/2, boundedness imposes upper bounds on β; closed-form BPS potentials and field equations derived for six σ values (including linear, quadratic, cubic cases); all solutions yield identical topological string tension μ_BPS = 2πn, independent of σ and β.

Conclusion: Kruglov nonlinear electrodynamics admits a consistent BPS vortex framework only when the constitutive map Φ(Y;σ) supports an invertible, smooth branch—achieved for all σ > 0 but with distinct constraints on β depending on σ. The universality of μ_BPS = 2πn confirms topological robustness of the BPS energy bound across this entire family of nonlinear theories.

Abstract: We construct the Bogomol'nyi equations for Abelian gauge--Higgs vortices in which the Maxwell gauge sector is replaced by Kruglov nonlinear electrodynamics, a power-law family that interpolates between Maxwell theory, Born--Infeld electrodynamics, and exponential electrodynamics, characterized by a dimensionless exponent $σ$. Using the stressless method, we derive a pair of first-order equations directly from the vanishing of the spatial stress tensor, without assuming the Higgs potential \textit{a priori}. For generic $σ$, the gauge and Higgs sectors are coupled through an implicit algebraic relation. We therefore introduce a constitutive map $Φ(Y;σ)$ and analyze its monotonicity and range to determine the conditions for a smooth admissible Bogomol'nyi branch. For $σ>1/2$, the constitutive map is strictly monotonic and unbounded, whereas for $0<σ<1/2$ it possesses a finite maximum; the marginal case $σ=1/2$ is bounded. These properties yield explicit bounds on the nonlinear parameter $β$ for the latter cases. We further obtain closed-form constitutive relations, BPS potentials, and gauge-field equations for six representative values of $σ$, spanning linear, quadratic, and cubic algebraic structures. The corresponding vortex profiles are then computed numerically. The resulting BPS string tension is purely topological, $μ_{\rm BPS}=2πn$, independent of both $σ$ and $β$.

</details>


### [32] [Scalar Casimir Effect on a Two-Dimensional Sphere with a Wu--Yang Magnetic Monopole](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.08378)
*Jun-Cheng Zhang,Xiao-Yin Pan,Juhao Wu,Qing-hai Wang*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper studies how a Wu-Yang magnetic monopole at the center of a 2D sphere alters the Casimir effect for a charged complex scalar field, finding that sufficiently strong monopole strength can reverse the Casimir pressure from attractive to repulsive—a topologically induced repulsive vacuum stress.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To explore how nontrivial topology (via a Wu-Yang monopole) affects quantum vacuum energy—specifically whether magnetic monopoles can induce repulsive Casimir forces, potentially relevant for gravity and cosmology.

Method: Analytic solution of the Klein-Gordon equation for a charged scalar field on S² in the Wu-Yang monopole background; use of a generalized Abel-Plana formula for multivalued functions to compute the renormalized Casimir energy and pressure.

Result: The Casimir pressure changes sign from attractive to repulsive beyond a critical monopole strength; this reversal is robust across various curvature coupling constants (ξ).

Conclusion: Wu-Yang monopoles—through their topological structure—can generate repulsive vacuum stress, offering a geometric-topological mechanism for counteracting gravitational attraction in quantum field-theoretic settings.

Abstract: We investigate the Casimir effect of a complex scalar field on a two-dimensional sphere threaded by a fixed Wu--Yang magnetic monopole at the center. In this background the charged scalar field is a section of a nontrivial complex line bundle over $S^2$, reflecting the monopole's nontrivial topology. We solve the Klein--Gordon equation analytically and compute the Casimir energy using a generalized Abel--Plana formula for multivalued functions. We find that the monopole can reverse the sign of the Casimir pressure, turning an attractive force into a repulsive one when the monopole strength is sufficiently large. This behavior persists across a broad range of curvature couplings and suggests that Wu--Yang monopoles may provide a source of repulsive vacuum stress, with possible implications for gravitational and cosmological settings.

</details>


### [33] [Covariant Phase Space and Carroll-Weyl $χ$ Symmetry of Carroll Non-BPS D$_p$-branes](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.08874)
*Limin Zeng*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper studies the covariant phase space of Carroll non-BPS Dp-branes, analyzing constraint structure, degrees of freedom, and symmetry properties under the Carroll limit; it distinguishes generic and tachyon-vacuum sectors, identifies rank conditions, and shows that Carroll–Weyl χ-symmetry fails to become first-class except in a highly restricted global vacuum.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand the geometric and algebraic structure of Carrollian limits of non-BPS Dp-branes, particularly how canonical constraints, symplectic structure, and residual symmetries behave in the ultra-relativistic (infinite speed-of-light) limit.

Method: Covariant phase space analysis; canonical contraction procedure applied to the Hamiltonian constraint system; classification of constraint types (first- vs. second-class); counting of local phase-space degrees of freedom; analysis of Poisson brackets and closure properties; examination of Carroll–Weyl χ-transformations and their generator status.

Result: The electric-like and magnetic-like sectors exhibit distinct Hamiltonian constraint closures; the generic non-BPS sector has D−1 local degrees of freedom, while the tachyon-vacuum sector has D−2 only after imposing second-class background conditions; rank conditions for the electric-like sector are identified and strengthened in the vacuum; Carroll–Weyl χ-symmetry preserves the symplectic form and admits a charge but is obstructed from being first-class by constraints, unlike in null string theory.

Conclusion: Carroll non-BPS Dp-branes feature sector-dependent constraint algebras and reduced physical degrees of freedom in the vacuum; the failure of χ-symmetry to lift to a first-class gauge generator highlights a fundamental distinction from null string theory, underscoring the rigidity of Carrollian brane dynamics outside highly symmetric backgrounds.

Abstract: We analyze the covariant phase space of Klusoň's canonical Carroll non-BPS D\(_p\)-brane actions. The Carroll limit is formulated as a contraction of the canonical phase space: the canonical one-form is invariant under the scaling of conjugate pairs, while the leading Hamiltonian constraint differs between the electric-like and magnetic-like sectors. This difference controls the weak closure of the electric-like constraint algebra and the stronger closure of the magnetic-like Hamiltonian brackets. We separate the generic non-BPS sector, which carries \(D-1\) local phase-space degrees of freedom, from the tachyon-vacuum sector, which carries \(D-2\) only after imposing second-class background conditions. We also identify the rank condition for the generic electric-like sector and its strengthening in the vacuum sector. Finally, we show that the Carroll--Weyl \(χ\) transformation preserves the symplectic form and admits a charge, but is obstructed by the constraints and cannot be promoted to a first-class gauge generator except in a highly restricted global vacuum sector. This contrasts with null string theory, where a restricted \(χ\)-symmetry can be completed to an additional first-class constraint.

</details>


### [34] [Gravitational decoherence from soft-graviton $S$-matrix](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.08584)
*Hiroki Matsui*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper derives a gauge-invariant, nonnegative leading-soft decoherence exponent for matter superpositions due to gravitational interactions, using Weinberg’s soft theorem and coherent radiation state overlaps; it shows vacuum decoherence vanishes in the IR limit, identifies the dominant nonrelativistic term as quadrupolar, and analyzes how graviton squeezing (including inflation-motivated two-mode states) modulates decoherence—phase averaging yields enhancement scaling with GW energy density and t⁴.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To rigorously quantify gravitational decoherence of matter superpositions using fundamental infrared (soft) structure of quantum gravity—specifically, the leading soft sector of the graviton S-matrix—while ensuring gauge invariance, physical interpretability, and extension beyond vacuum (e.g., squeezed or cosmological backgrounds).

Method: Applies Weinberg's soft graviton theorem and computes overlaps of coherent soft graviton radiation states associated with distinct matter branches; performs exact polarization sum and angular integration at leading-soft order; imposes four-momentum conservation to ensure branch distinguishability; extends to single- and two-mode squeezed graviton states (including inflationary two-mode case); analyzes phase-averaged decoherence for relic backgrounds with flat energy-density spectra.

Result: Derives a gauge-invariant, nonnegative leading-soft decoherence exponent; shows vacuum coherence loss vanishes as IR cutoff → 0; identifies purely quadrupolar nonrelativistic decoherence term; demonstrates squeezing can enhance or suppress decoherence depending on relative phase; finds phase-averaged relic background yields decoherence ∝ Ω_GW × t⁴ under sharp IR cutoff identified with inverse observation time.

Conclusion: Gravitational decoherence of matter superpositions is fundamentally governed by soft-graviton dynamics, is intrinsically tied to infrared quantum information, and exhibits rich dependence on background graviton state—especially in nonvacuum settings like inflationary or relic gravitational-wave backgrounds—providing a bridge between scattering amplitudes, IR physics, and quantum measurement in gravity.

Abstract: We revisit gravitational decoherence of matter superpositions using the leading soft sector of the graviton $S$-matrix. Starting from Weinberg's soft theorem and the overlap of coherent radiation states, we derive, for branch configurations with the same total four-momentum, a gauge-invariant, nonnegative leading-soft decoherence exponent and evaluate the polarization sum and angular integrals exactly at this order. In the vacuum, coherence between branches with distinguishable soft radiation vanishes as the infrared cutoff is removed. The leading nonrelativistic term is purely quadrupolar as a consequence of energy--momentum conservation. We extend the analysis to single- and two-mode squeezed graviton states, including the two-mode case motivated by inflation. Squeezing can enhance or suppress decoherence depending on the relative phase, while phase averaging gives an enhancement governed by the graviton occupation. For a phase-averaged relic background with an approximately flat energy-density spectrum over the relevant soft frequency band, the sharp-cutoff identification of the infrared scale with the inverse observation time gives a contribution that grows with the gravitational-wave energy density and with the fourth power of the observation time. These results connect soft-graviton scattering, infrared quantum information, and decoherence in nonvacuum gravitational backgrounds.

</details>


### [35] [Symplectic Chern-Simons theories dual to Abelian rank-$0$ theories](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.08648)
*Tadashi Okazaki,Douglas J. Smith*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: Proposes a duality between 3d N=4 USp(2n) symplectic Chern-Simons theory at half-integer level and an Abelian rank-0 theory T_{ℓ,n}; verifies matching of vacua counts under real mass deformations and supersymmetric indices; analyzes ℓ=1 case in detail.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To establish and test a novel 3d N=4 duality between a non-Abelian symplectic Chern-Simons theory with fundamental chiral matter and an Abelian rank-0 theory, motivated by structural constraints (pseudo-real representation, half-integral level, absence of non-Abelian flavor symmetry, vanishing Higgs/Coulomb branches).

Method: Uses supersymmetric localization: computes and compares supersymmetric vacua counting under real mass deformations for all (n,ℓ), and matches supersymmetric indices (e.g., topological and flavored indices) in selected cases; performs detailed analysis of the ℓ=1 dual pair, including monopole superpotential and tadpole level matrix structure.

Result: Confirms duality for all positive integers (n,ℓ) via exact match of vacuum degeneracies under real masses; provides evidence via index agreement in multiple instances; fully characterizes the ℓ=1 dual as a U(1)^n gauge theory with tadpole level matrix and bare monopole superpotential.

Conclusion: The proposed duality is robust across parameter space; it enriches the landscape of 3d N=4 dualities, links symplectic CS-matter theories to Abelian rank-0 models, and highlights the role of monopole superpotentials and half-integral levels in preserving N=4 SUSY and branchless moduli spaces.

Abstract: We propose that the 3d symplectic Chern-Simons theory $USp(2n)$ at level $k=n+\frac12+\ell$ with a single chiral multiplet in the fundamental representation is dual, for every pair of positive integers $(n,\ell)$, to the Abelian rank-$0$ theory $\mathcal{T}_{\ell,n}$ of Creutzig, Garner and Kim. The fundamental is pseudo-real, so the level is half-integral, there is no non-Abelian flavor symmetry, and the supersymmetry is enhanced to $\mathcal{N}=4$. Both sides have vanishing Higgs and Coulomb branches. We match the counting of supersymmetric vacua under real mass deformations for all $(n,\ell)$, and the supersymmetric indices in a number of cases. We then study in detail the case $\ell=1$, the minimal $USp(2n)$ Chern-Simons theory at level $n+\frac32$, whose dual is a $U(1)^{n}$ theory with a tadpole level matrix and a bare monopole superpotential.

</details>


### [36] [Mass for Particles from Extra Dimensions](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.08697)
*F. E. A. de Souza,M. O. Tahim,R. I. de Oliveira Junior,I. M. Macêdo*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper explores how mass arises for particles in a codimension-two braneworld with a hedgehog geometry, showing that angular motion in the compact extra dimension generates an effective 4D mass (Kaluza-Klein spectrum), and that spin-curvature coupling in supersymmetric spinning-particle models induces radial localization dependent on KK level.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand geometric mechanisms of mass generation in flux-stabilized codimension-two braneworlds, particularly how bulk geometry and particle spin influence the effective 4D mass spectrum.

Method: Canonical quantization of a massless particle in a codimension-two hedgehog background; extension to N = 1 and N = 2 spinning-particle models to incorporate spin-curvature coupling; analysis of effective potentials and KK mode localization.

Result: A dynamically generated Kaluza-Klein mass spectrum from angular momentum quantization; absence of radial confinement in bosonic sector; spin-curvature coupling introduces level-dependent radial localization in fermionic/spinning sectors.

Conclusion: Geometric mass generation in codimension-two braneworlds admits a consistent particle-mechanics description, where bulk curvature, compactification topology, and spin jointly determine the 4D mass spectrum and localization properties of KK modes.

Abstract: We investigate a geometric mechanism for mass generation in a codimension-two hedgehog braneworld. A massless particle propagating in the bulk acquires an effective four-dimensional mass through its motion along the compact angular extra dimension, yielding a dynamically determined Kaluza-Klein spectrum after canonical quantization. While the bosonic sector exhibits no radial confinement, extending the analysis to N = 1, 2 spinning-particle models shows that the spin-curvature coupling modifies the effective potential and introduces a level-dependent localization pattern for the Kaluza-Klein modes. The results establish a particle-mechanics realization of geometric mass generation in flux-supported codimension-two braneworlds and highlight the interplay between bulk geometry, spin, and the effective four-dimensional mass spectrum.

</details>


### [37] [Splitting Dynamics of Multiply Quantized Vortices in Holographic Superfluid of Finite Temperature](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.08831)
*Shanquan Lan,Xin Li,Jiexiong Mo,Yu Tian,Yu-Kun Yan,Hongbao Zhang*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper investigates the splitting dynamics of high-winding-number vortices (n = 5–8) in a 2D holographic superfluid at finite temperature, revealing three novel phenomena: deviation from the 2n−3 unstable mode count, temperature-driven jump-like transitions in dominant instability modes, and hierarchical sub-splitting patterns within a single topological configuration.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand how multiply quantized vortices destabilize and split in strongly coupled superfluids beyond weak-coupling limits—particularly whether universal linear stability rules (e.g., 2n−3 unstable modes) persist at higher winding numbers and finite temperature.

Method: Combined linear quasinormal mode analysis with fully nonlinear real-time holographic numerical simulations in a 2D finite-temperature superfluid model dual to a black hole background in AdS/CFT.

Result: For n=8, only 2n−4 unstable modes exist (missing p=2(n−1) mode); dominant unstable mode transitions become discontinuous (e.g., n=7 jumps 2→4→7; n=8 jumps 2→8); and single splitting channels (e.g., l=4 for n=8) host temperature-dependent sub-patterns with distinct topology.

Conclusion: The linear stability paradigm for quantized vortices breaks down at high n and finite T, exhibiting emergent complexity—mode-count deviations, non-sequential dominance shifts, and nested topological splitting—highlighting the role of strong coupling and thermal effects in holographic superfluids.

Abstract: We study the splitting dynamics of multiply quantized vortices with winding numbers $n=5,6,7$ and $8$ in a two-dimensional holographic superfluid at finite temperature, by combining linear perturbation analysis of quasinormal modes with fully nonlinear real-time numerical simulations. Three new physical phenomena are revealed. First, the number of unstable modes no longer strictly follows the $2n-3$ formula as $n$ increases. For the vortex with $n=8$, the unstable mode with $p=2(n-1)$ is absent throughout the entire temperature range, so that only $2n-4$ unstable modes exist. Second, the transition of the dominant unstable mode with increasing temperature exhibits new characteristics. For vortices with $n\le 6$, the dominant mode changes sequentially as $p=2,3,\dots,n$, whereas for $n\ge 7$ jump-like transitions occur-for instance, for $n=7$ the dominant mode jumps from $p=2$ to $p=4$ at $T=0.325T_c$ and then directly to $p=7$ at $T=0.359T_c$, and for $n=8$ it jumps directly from $p=2$ to $p=8$ at $T=0.302T_c$. Third, a single splitting pattern of high-winding-number vortices can contain multiple sub-splitting patterns with distinct topological structures, as exemplified by the $l=4$ pattern of the $n=8$ vortex, which exhibits three sub-patterns at low, intermediate and high temperatures. The nonlinear simulations confirm the predictions of the linear stability analysis, and the implications of our results for cold-atom experiments are discussed.

</details>


### [38] [Quantum spacetime and gravity from the IKKT matrix model: an invitation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.08955)
*Harold C. Steinacker*

Abstract: This is a concise and compact introduction to a framework for spacetime, gravity, and fundamental physics based on the IKKT matrix model, focusing on conceptual and structural aspects. The approach rests on nontrivial vacua or matrix backgrounds, which serve as 3+1-dimensional noncommutative spacetime. We discuss a specific class of backgrounds describing expanding homogeneous and isotropic FLRW spacetime, which provide a solution of the one-loop effective action with finitely many dof per volume. Perturbations of the background give rise to fields propagating on spacetime governed by a weakly-coupled gauge theory, as well as a nonstandard description of (quantum) gravity leading to some IR modifications. Maximal supersymmetry of the model is essential to ensure effective locality.

In particular, this enables the construction of 5d conformal matter SCFTs with non-simply laced flavor symmetry. Along the way, we discuss their circle reduction in relation to 4d theories of class-S type.

Abstract: We study the existence of supersymmetric M5-brane instantons in asymptotically AdS$_4 \times S^7/\mathbf{Z}_k$ geometries of M-theory, dual to the ABJM theory. The supersymmetric embeddings of these instantons are determined by the M5-brane $κ$-symmetry, which forces them to localise to the equivariant fixed points in the asymptotically AdS$_4$ geometry, and wrap $(S^3\times S^3)/\mathbf{Z}_k $. At the quantum level we find that the one-loop partition function of these instantons vanishes, suggesting that the bulk partition function is instead to be expanded in terms of supergravity modes and M2-brane instantons, in line with known results for the $S^3$ partition function of the dual ABJM theory.

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper analyzes and compares the specific dissipation Q⁻¹(ω) across several linear viscoelastic models—Becker, Lomnitz, Lambert, generalized Becker (Q_ν⁻¹), and extended Jeffreys–Lomnitz (Q_α⁻¹)—deriving new closed-form asymptotics and limits, and showing convergence to Maxwell-model behavior at high frequencies.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To systematically compare and unify the asymptotic frequency-domain dissipation behavior of classical and generalized linear viscoelastic models, particularly clarifying their low- and high-frequency limits and parameter-dependent transitions to the Maxwell model.

Result: Exact asymptotics of Q⁻¹(ω) for Becker and Lomnitz models at both extremes; new closed-form Q_ν⁻¹(ω) valid for rational ν ∈ (0,1], interpolating between Maxwell (ν→0) and Becker (ν=1); two new closed-form Q_α⁻¹(ω) expressions covering α ∈ (0,1] and α ∈ (−∞,1], recovering Lomnitz (α→0) and Maxwell (α=1); universal agreement of Q_α⁻¹(ω) with Maxwell dissipation as ω→+∞.

Conclusion: The Maxwell model serves as the universal high-frequency limit for all considered models; the generalized Becker and extended Jeffreys–Lomnitz models provide continuous, parameterized bridges between classical models, with rigorous closed-form dissipation expressions enabling precise modeling of frequency-dependent attenuation in viscoelastic media.

Abstract: We compare the specific dissipation $Q^{-1}\left( ω\right) $ for the Becker, Lomnitz, and Lambert models in linear viscoelasticity. Graphically, the specific dissipation of these models behaves similarly as $ω\rightarrow 0^{+}$ and $ω\rightarrow +\infty$. Furthermore, we obtain analytic formulas for the asymptotic behavior of $Q^{-1}\left( ω\right) $ in the Becker and Lomnitz models as $ω\rightarrow 0^{+}$ and $ω\rightarrow +\infty$, where the latter limit coincides with the specific dissipation of the Maxwell model. We also derive a novel closed-form expression for the specific dissipation in the generalized Becker model, $Q_{ν}^{-1}\left( ω\right) $, when the parameter $ν\in \left( 0,1\right] $ is rational. When $ν\rightarrow 0$, we recover the specific dissipation of the Maxwell model, whereas when $ν=1$, we recover that of the original Becker model. In addition, we derive two new closed-form expressions for the specific dissipation for the extended Jeffreys--Lomnitz model $Q_{α}^{-1}\left( ω\right) $, the first being valid for $α\in \left( 0,1\right] $, and the second for $α\in \left( -\infty ,1\right] $. When $α\rightarrow 0$, we recover the specific dissipation of the Lomnitz model, whereas when $α=1$, we recover that of the Maxwell model. Finally, we conclude that the asymptotic behavior of $Q_{α}^{-1}\left( ω\right) $ as $ω\rightarrow +\infty $ coincides with the specific dissipation of the Maxwell model. Keywords:

### [42] [Information Loss under Gauge Reduction of Discrete Electromagnetic Fields](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.05473)
*Jean-Pierre Magnot*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper decomposes relative entropy and Fisher information in Whitney-discretized electromagnetism under gauge reduction, showing exact splitting into physical and nonnegative gauge contributions via Schur complements and conditional statistics; it establishes the physical divergence as the minimum over Gaussian auxiliary extensions and gives an information-geometric interpretation of gauge reduction without ascribing physical meaning to gauge variables.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To provide an information-geometric foundation for gauge reduction in discrete field theories—specifically, to understand how relative entropy and Fisher information behave when eliminating unphysical gauge degrees of freedom in a principled, statistical manner.

Method: Uses discrete Hodge decomposition on Whitney discretizations to separate gauge (exact), harmonic, and coexact components; analyzes Gaussian distributions on the auxiliary potential space; applies tools from multivariate Gaussian statistics—including Schur complements, conditional expectations, and covariance partitioning—to decompose relative entropy and Fisher information; derives minimization properties and equality conditions.

Result: Relative entropy splits exactly into physical marginal relative entropy plus a nonnegative conditional gauge term expressible via Schur complements and physical–gauge correlations; physical relative entropy equals the minimum over all Gaussian auxiliary extensions; infinitesimal Fisher information admits an analogous decomposition; a two-variable model demonstrates non-uniqueness of auxiliary potentials yielding identical physical statistics.

Conclusion: Gauge reduction in discrete electromagnetism admits a clean, intrinsic information-geometric interpretation: physical divergences are optimal (minimal) abstractions of auxiliary ones, and gauge variables contribute only nonnegatively to uncertainty—justifying their elimination without physical attribution.

Abstract: We study relative entropy under gauge reduction in Whitney-discretized electromagnetism. The discrete Hodge decomposition separates exact gauge directions from harmonic and coexact physical degrees of freedom. For Gaussian distributions on the auxiliary potential space, relative entropy splits exactly into the relative entropy of the physical marginals and a nonnegative conditional gauge contribution. We express this contribution through Schur complements, conditional means, and physical--gauge correlations, and characterize equality between auxiliary and physical relative entropies. The physical divergence is also shown to be the minimum over all Gaussian auxiliary extensions. At the infinitesimal level, Fisher information admits an analogous decomposition. A two-variable model illustrates how statistically distinct auxiliary potentials may represent identical physical statistics. The construction provides an information-geometric interpretation of discrete gauge reduction without assigning physical significance to the eliminated gauge variables.

</details>


### [43] [Distance Energies and Negative Type of Flat Tori](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.05622)
*Ye Zhou*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper studies distance energies on flat tori, establishes a Fourier signature of the cut locus, proves Haar measure is never a local maximizer for positive distance powers, shows all d≥2 flat tori have zero supremal negative type and generalized roundness, and solves global maximization for orthogonal rectangular and regular hexagonal tori with phase transitions in extremal measures depending on α.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand extremal probability measures for distance energies on flat tori, characterize geometric-analytic properties (e.g., negative type, generalized roundness) via Fourier analysis of the distance function, and classify maximizers across different torus geometries and distance exponents α.

Method: Fourier analysis on flat tori; asymptotic evaluation of Fourier coefficients of ρ_Λ^α near facet normals of the dual lattice; geometric characterization of cut locus and Voronoi structure; variational arguments and symmetry-based extremal measure classification; case analysis for orthogonal rectangular and hexagonal lattices.

Result: A quantitative Fourier signature linking Voronoi facets to positivity and asymptotics of ρ_Λ^α’s Fourier coefficients; Haar measure fails to be a local maximizer for any α > 0; all d ≥ 2 flat tori have supremal negative type = generalized roundness = 0; full classification of global maximizers: two-torsion subgroup measures (1 ≤ α < 2), balanced couplings on same subgroup (α = 2), diametral pairs (α > 2) for orthogonal rectangular tori; uniform measures on order-3 cyclic subgroup for all α ≥ 1 on regular hexagonal torus.

Conclusion: The geometry of the cut locus governs the Fourier behavior of distance powers, fundamentally constraining extremality — Haar measure is universally non-extremal for α > 0, and global maximizers are highly structured, lattice-symmetric, and exhibit sharp phase transitions dependent on both α and torus geometry.

Abstract: Let \(T_Λ=\mathbb R^d/Λ\), \(d\geq2\), be a flat torus with quotient metric \(ρ_Λ\), and consider the distance energies \(I_α(μ)=\iint ρ_Λ(x,y)^α\,dμ(x)\,dμ(y)\) of Borel probability measures \(μ\). We prove a quantitative Fourier signature of the cut locus: for every Voronoi facet and every \(α>0\), there is a sequence of dual-lattice frequencies approaching the facet normal along which the Fourier coefficients of \(ρ_Λ^α\) are positive, with an explicit leading asymptotic determined by the facet. As direct consequences, Haar measure is not a local maximizer for any positive distance power, even among smooth densities, and every flat torus of dimension at least two has supremal negative type and generalized roundness zero. We then solve the global maximization problem for two classes of flat tori. On an orthogonal rectangular torus, the maximizers undergo a transition at \(α=2\): for \(1\leqα<2\) they are the translated uniform measures on the two-torsion subgroup; at \(α=2\) all balanced couplings on translates of that subgroup are extremal; and for \(α>2\) only equally weighted diametral pairs remain. On the regular hexagonal torus, the maximizers are precisely the uniform measures on translates of a distinguished cyclic subgroup of order three for every \(α\geq1\).

</details>

Main category: math-ph

  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To establish rigorous, algebraic gauge-fixing independence of perturbative asymptotics—crucial for consistency and predictability of quantum gauge theories—in a simplified finite-dimensional setting.

Abstract: This paper provides an algebraic proof of the gauge-fixing independence of perturbative asymptotic expansions in a finite-dimensional model of non-Abelian gauge theory. We develop an algebraic framework using the BRST formalism and perturbation theory. Our main theorem shows that under appropriate transversality conditions, normalized expectation values of observables remain unchanged under smooth deformations of the gauge-fixing conditions that preserve these assumptions.

</details>


### [45] [Renormalization Group on Wiener Space: Spectral Theory and Universality](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.06183)
*André L. P. Considera,Alexei A. Mailybaev*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper develops a renormalization group (RG) formalism on Wiener space, linearizes the RG operator around the Wiener measure, constructs its spectral theory using Hida distributions, identifies universal correction terms to Donsker’s invariance principle via Wick polynomials of white noise, and recovers the irrelevant/marginal/relevant classification from spectral data.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To establish a rigorous RG framework for stochastic processes—specifically Brownian motion—and uncover universal structure beyond convergence in Donsker's theorem, including leading corrections and perturbative classifications akin to quantum field theory.

Method: Introduces an RG operator R acting on path-space probability measures; linearizes it around Wiener measure to obtain operator L; constructs generalized eigenvectors as Hida distributions (not ordinary measures); uses white noise analysis (Hida calculus) to define spectral theory; identifies explicit eigenpairs involving Wick-ordered integrals of white noise; applies spectral decomposition to analyze universality of corrections to scaling limits.

Result: Establishes a spectral gap and diagonal-concentration property for L; constructs explicit eigen-distributions U_n = ∫₀¹ :Ẇ(t)ⁿ: dt with eigenvalues λ_n = 2^{1−n/2}; proves universality of all leading corrections to Brownian limit in Donsker’s principle; recovers power-counting classification (irrelevant/marginal/relevant) purely from spectral exponents of L.

Conclusion: The Wiener measure is a hyperbolic fixed point of a path-space RG flow, whose linearized dynamics encode both universal asymptotics of random walks and perturbative renormalizability—bridging stochastic analysis, probability theory, and constructive QFT.

Abstract: We develop a rigorous renormalization group (RG) formalism on Wiener space. We introduce an RG operator $\mathcal R$ acting on probability measures over continuous paths, whose fixed point is the Wiener measure. We linearize $\mathcal{R}$ around the Brownian fixed point and analyze the spectral structure of the resulting operator $\mathscr{L}$. Its eigenvectors are too singular to be realized as honest measures on path space, and we therefore develop a generalized spectral theory within the framework of white noise analysis (Hida calculus). The eigenvectors of $\mathscr{L}$ are realized as Hida distributions, satisfying $\mathscr{L} U=λU$ in the weak sense. This analysis yields structural results such as a spectral gap and a diagonal-concentration property of the eigenvectors. Moreover, we identify a family $\{\mathfrak{U}_n\}_{n \geq 0}$ of eigenvectors with eigenvalues $λ_n = 2^{1-n/2}$, consisting of Wick polynomials of white noise formally expressed as $\mathfrak{U}_n = \int_0^1 {:}\dot W(t)^n{:}\, dt$, and rigorously constructed as Hida distributions. We then use this spectral structure to uncover a finer, second layer of universality in the Donsker invariance principle: not only is the convergence of random walks towards the Brownian scaling limit universal, but the entire hierarchy of leading corrections to the Brownian limit is universal as well, governed by the eigenpairs $(λ_n, \mathfrak{U}_n)$. We also show, at a formal level, that the framework developed here extends to Gibbs-type perturbations of the Brownian fixed point, in the spirit of self-interacting quantum field theories. In particular, we recover the standard irrelevant/marginal/relevant classification, usually obtained in the physics literature by power-counting, solely from the spectral analysis of $\mathscr{L}$.

</details>


### [46] [Condensation and Collapse in the Mean-Field Limit of Rotating 2D Bose Gases with Two-Body and Three-Body Interactions](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.06466)
*Deke Li,Yuan Li,Qingxuan Wang*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper studies the mean-field collapse regime of N interacting bosons in a rotating 2D harmonic trap with attractive two-body and repulsive three-body interactions. It proves that, as the two-body coupling approaches criticality and three-body coupling vanishes, the many-body ground state energy and wavefunction asymptotically match those of a rotating cubic-quintic NLS functional, with universal blow-up governed by the Gagliardo–Nirenberg optimizer and precise energy expansion.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To rigorously justify the emergence of a rotating cubic-quintic nonlinear Schrödinger (NLS) description—and its universal blow-up behavior—in the collapse regime of a dilute, rotating Bose gas with competing two- and three-body interactions.

Method: Mean-field analysis combined with rigorous many-body quantum mechanics: derivation of the effective NLS functional via scaling limits; spectral and variational analysis of the NLS ground state near collapse; quantitative energy expansion using Gagliardo–Nirenberg optimality; proof of complete Bose–Einstein condensation via quantum de Finetti and localization techniques.

Result: The many-body ground state energy converges to the NLS energy with matching asymptotic expansion $E^{\mathrm{NLS}} = (1 - \zeta/4 + o(1)) \mathcal{Q}_{\mathrm{pot}} \ell_n^2$; the many-body ground states exhibit complete BEC onto the universal Gagliardo–Nirenberg optimizer profile; the blow-up rate and shape are independent of rotation up to leading order.

Conclusion: The rotating cubic-quintic NLS functional provides a mathematically exact effective description of the collapse regime for this interacting Bose system; universality of the blow-up profile and precise energy asymptotics are fully established from first principles.

Abstract: We consider a system of $N$ interacting bosons in a rotating harmonic trap in $\mathbb{R}^2$, where the two-body interaction is attractive and scaled as $N^{2α}U(N^αx)$ with $0<α<1/12$, and the three-body interaction is repulsive and scaled as $N^{4β}W(N^βx,N^βy)$ with $0<β<1/24$. In the mean-field limit, the ground state energy is effectively described by a rotating cubic-quintic nonlinear Schrödinger functional. We analyze the collapse regime where the two-body coupling $a$ approaches the critical value $a_*$ and the three-body coupling $b$ tends to zero. The NLS ground states blow up with a universal profile given by the optimizer of the Gagliardo-Nirenberg inequality, and the energy satisfies $E^{\mathrm{NLS}} = (1-ζ/4+o(1))\mathcal{Q}{\rm{pot}}\ell_n^2$ with $ζ\geqslant0$ determined by the relative rates of $a_n\to a_*$ and $b_n\searrow0$. From the many-body theory, we rigorously justify this effective description: the quantum ground state energy converges to the NLS energy with the same asymptotic expansion in the collapse regime, and the many-body ground states exhibit complete Bose-Einstein condensation onto the universal blow-up profile.

</details>

Proof method of theorems in this paper are different from those of theory of analytic numbers. The second goal of this paper is to show that special values and integral presentations of this function are closely related to unification of the Debye functions, the moment generating function for the negative binom distribution, the generating functions for the Apostol-type numbers of higher-order, and the Frobenius-Euler numbers, the Bernoulli numbers, and the Stirling numbers etc. Moreover, we give recurrence relations, Maclaurin's series expansion, and approximation formula for unification of the Debye functions. Result: Successful derivation of both particular and general solutions for several second-order nonlinear ODEs from classical mechanics and nonlinear oscillatory systems.

Abstract: This work presents a generalization of the commutative factorization framework for a broad class of second-order nonlinear ordinary differential equations. The main objective was to extend the commutative factorization procedure in order to use the Riccati-Bernoulli equation and the Bäcklund transformation. This provides a systematic route for constructing both particular and general solutions of the original second-order nonlinear equation. The theoretical development is illustrated through several nonlinear models of physical and mathematical interest, including equations arising in classical mechanics and nonlinear oscillatory systems. The results demonstrate that commutative factorization constitutes an effective analytical tool for solving nonlinear differential equations.

</details>


### [49] [A Note on Extended States on the Bethe Lattice](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.06987)
*Vojkan Jaksic,Yoram Last,Simone Warzel*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper provides a new proof for the existence of absolutely continuous spectrum in the weakly disordered Anderson model on the Bethe lattice, using a cyclicity criterion and Hellinger affinity; it also extends the framework to Z^d via a Schur complement formulation.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To establish a new, conceptually transparent proof of absolutely continuous spectrum for the weakly disordered Anderson model on the Bethe lattice—bypassing traditional expansions—and to generalize the approach to Z^d.
