Abstract: We give a new proof of the existence of absolutely continuous spectrum for the weakly disordered Anderson model on the Bethe lattice. The argument follows a general cyclicity criterion for Anderson-type Hamiltonians and reduces the problem to showing that the spectral measures of two independent copies of the rooted tree are not mutually singular. This is detected by their Hellinger affinity, and the required weak-disorder estimate follows from a short harmonic and compactness argument. We also formulate an analogous quantitative cyclicity criterion for the Anderson model on $\mathbb{Z}^d$, expressed through the Schur complement of the Poisson transform of a $2\times2$ matrix spectral measure.

</details>


### [50] [Renormalization group bounds on Araki relative entropy](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.07277)
*Edoardo D'Angelo*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper establishes a multiscale Bakry-Émery criterion to bound the Araki relative entropy for perturbations of equilibrium states in relativistic quantum field theory (QFT), leveraging stochastic positivity, Feynman–Kac representations, abstract Wiener space structure of the Euclidean free field, and a Polchinski-type equation; it applies the criterion to prove the bound for the ultraviolet-finite Lorentzian massive Sine-Gordon model.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To obtain rigorous, operator-algebraic bounds on the Araki relative entropy for physically relevant interacting QFT models—particularly where standard perturbative or lattice methods fail—by bridging probabilistic methods (stochastic analysis, Gaussian measures) with algebraic QFT.

Method: Combines stochastic positivity to map relativistic bosonic field perturbations to Euclidean massive free field perturbations; uses Feynman–Kac formula to express Araki relative entropy probabilistically; exploits abstract Wiener space structure to formulate a Polchinski equation; extends Bauerschmidt–Bodineau–Dagallier’s multiscale analysis to derive a probabilistic (hence operator-algebraic) bound via a multiscale Bakry–Émery curvature criterion.

Result: Derives a general multiscale Bakry–Émery criterion yielding an upper bound on Araki relative entropy for affiliated perturbations; proves that the Lorentzian massive Sine-Gordon model in its UV-finite regime satisfies this bound.

Conclusion: The multiscale Bakry–Émery framework successfully bridges probabilistic renormalization group methods and algebraic QFT, enabling non-perturbative, operator-algebraic control of relative entropy—a key quantity for quantum information and stability in QFT.

Abstract: This paper derives a multiscale Bakry-Émery criterion for an upper bound on the Araki relative entropy of perturbations of an equilibrium state in relativistic QFT. Using stochastic positivity, affiliated perturbations of the relativistic bosonic field can be put in correspondence with perturbations of the massive Euclidean free field. This gives a Feynman-Kac formula, which in turns is used to derive a probabilistic expression for the Araki relative entropy. The description of the massive Euclidean free field as an abstract Wiener space provides the infinite-dimensional framework to derive a Polchinski equation. Extending the methods of Bauerschmidt, Bodineau, and Dagallier to this context yields a probabilistic bound on the Araki relative entropy, which can be formulated entirely in operator-algebraic terms. Using the criterion, it is shown that the Lorentzian massive Sine-Gordon model in the ultraviolet finite regime satisfies the relative entropy bound.

</details>


### [51] [Big Crunch on Julia and Anti-Julia Sets in the Integrable Limit](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.07308)
*Masatomi Iizawa,Satoru Saito,Yasuhito Narita,Yasuyuki Maeda,Hiromitsu Harada,Akira Shudo*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper introduces the 'anti-Julia set'—the closure of divergences of periodic equations—and studies its interplay with the classical Julia set under a deformation parameter $a$ interpolating between integrable ($a \to 0$) and chaotic regimes; it reveals a topological annihilation ('Big Crunch') at the integrable limit, where Julia and anti-Julia sets collide and vanish, and analytically and numerically characterizes the transition.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To explain why Julia sets—hallmarks of chaos—do not appear in integrable systems, by introducing a dual structure (anti-Julia set) and analyzing their coevolution across an integrability-breaking deformation.

Method: Analytical asymptotic analysis and numerical visualization of Julia and anti-Julia sets for a complex map parametrized by a real deformation parameter $a$, distinguishing pre-sets (before closure) and closed sets, and tracking their topological evolution as $a \to 0$.

Result: For $0 < a \leq 1/2$, Julia and anti-Julia sets coincide but their pre-sets remain disjoint; at $a \to 0$, they undergo critical collision and annihilation ('Big Crunch'); for $1/2 < a < 1$, the Julia set lies within the boundary of the pre-anti-Julia set; asymptotics near $a = 0$ are rigorously characterized.

Conclusion: The absence of Julia sets in integrable systems arises from the topological annihilation of dual fractal structures at the integrability threshold—a phenomenon captured by the anti-Julia set framework and its limiting behavior.

Abstract: The Julia set is defined by the closure of repelling periodic points in chaotic systems. Why does this structure not appear in integrable systems? In this paper, we address this question by demonstrating the existence of the closure of divergences of the periodic equations, which we designate as the "anti-Julia set." We also call the sets before taking the closures the pre-Julia and pre-anti-Julia sets, respectively. We illustrate the transition mechanism by considering a complex map that interpolates between integrable and non-integrable dynamics, by introducing a real deformation parameter $a$. For $0<a\le 1/2$, we show that the Julia set and the anti-Julia set coincide in the complex plane, although the pre-Julia and pre-anti-Julia sets remain completely disjoint. At the integrable limit $a\to 0$, these two dual structures undergo a critical collision and subsequent annihilation, reminiscent of a cosmological Big Crunch. When $1/2<a<1$, on the other hand, the boundary of the pre-anti-Julia set includes the Julia set. We analytically characterize these phenomena, focusing in particular on the asymptotic behavior of the pre-anti-Julia set as it approaches the integrable limit, and provide numerical visualizations that elucidate the underlying mechanisms of this Big Crunch phenomenon.

</details>


### [52] [Towards Heterogeneous Exascale CFD with a Single Fortran Code Base: OpenMP Target Acceleration of the High-Order Unstructured Solver UCNS3D](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.07387)
*Panagiotis Tsoutsanis,Michal Uminski,Christopher Day,David Henty*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper presents a single-source OpenMP target modernization of UCNS3D, a high-order unstructured finite-volume CFD solver, enabling portable, production-scale CPU/GPU acceleration while preserving numerical fidelity, code base, and workflows.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To avoid code fragmentation and reproducibility loss from rewriting mature high-order CFD solvers for accelerators, and to enable portable, standards-based heterogeneous acceleration without sacrificing numerical fidelity or software sustainability.

Method: Modernized UCNS3D using OpenMP target directives—retaining Fortran source, numerical formulation, and workflows—while enabling full explicit time-advancement path (reconstruction, gradients, fluxes, boundaries, halo exchange, update); employed persistent data regions, flat arrays, compile-time temporaries, explicit local algebra, target-callable routines, and GPU-aware MPI with device-resident buffers to handle irregular stencils and complex data structures.

Result: Verification shows CPU/GPU dissipation histories agree to machine precision on LUMI and match published reference data; single-node ARCHER2 GPU portability confirmed; end-to-end benchmarks (Taylor-Green, LM1021, CRM) show 1.27–1.67× CPU speedup, 2.71–4.05× same-node GPU speedup, and 84–101% strong-scaling efficiency.

Conclusion: Standards-based OpenMP target provides portable, production-ready acceleration for high-order unstructured CFD without compromising numerical accuracy, CPU performance, or software maintainability.

Abstract: Heterogeneous exascale systems are reshaping computational fluid dynamics, yet rewriting mature high-order solvers for accelerators can fragment code bases and weaken reproducibility. We present a single-source OpenMP target modernisation of UCNS3D, a high-order unstructured finite-volume solver for compressible flows. The common CPU/GPU implementation preserves its numerical formulation, Fortran code base, and workflows. Rather than porting isolated kernels, we enable the complete explicit time-advancement path: high-order reconstruction, gradient evaluation, inviscid and viscous fluxes, boundary treatment, halo exchange, and solution update. The implementation uses persistent target data regions, flat run-time arrays, compile-time sizing of temporary storage, explicit local algebra, target-callable routines, and GPU-aware MPI with device-resident buffers. These choices address the irregular stencils, complex data structures, and substantial temporary storage of high-order unstructured CFD. Verification using the compressible Taylor-Green vortex shows CPU and GPU dissipation histories agreeing to machine precision on LUMI and with published reference data. A single-node run on the ARCHER2 GPU platform provides an independent portability check. End-to-end performance is evaluated on fully populated nodes using Taylor-Green vortex, LM1021 sonic-boom, and NASA high-lift CRM benchmarks. Relative to the previous production implementation, the refactored CPU path is 1.27-1.67 times faster, while GPU offload delivers same-node speed-ups of 2.71-4.05 and 84-101 percent strong-scaling efficiency. The results show that standards-based OpenMP provides portable, production-scale acceleration without sacrificing numerical fidelity, CPU performance, or software sustainability.

</details>


### [53] [Möbius Invariant Dimers and Miquel Dynamics](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.07485)
*Niklas C. Affolter,Luis Mühlhoff*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper introduces a Möbius-invariant correspondence between circle patterns and the dimer model, extending prior Euclidean-invariant results; it proves Miquel dynamics preserve the partition function, links convex t-embeddings to positive face weights, reveals a hidden PO(3,3) symmetry via Lorentz lift, and applies the framework to isoradial graphs, Doyle spirals, punctured tori, and isothermic patterns.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To extend the known Euclidean-invariant correspondence between circle patterns and the dimer model to one respecting the natural conformal symmetry group PO(3,1) of circle patterns, and to uncover deeper geometric and algebraic structures (e.g., hidden PO(3,3) symmetry) governing integrability and positivity.

Method: Constructs a Möbius-invariant mapping from circle patterns to dimer models using t-embeddings and their Lorentz lifts; analyzes Miquel dynamics via discrete holomorphicity and cluster algebra-type transformations; derives positivity conditions from convexity of t-embeddings in hyperbolic space; leverages conformal geometry and representation theory of orthogonal groups.

Result: A PO(3,1)-invariant dimer correspondence for circle patterns; proof that Miquel dynamics preserve the dimer partition function; convexity of the t-embedding implies positivity of face weights; discovery of an extended symmetry group PO(3,3) arising from the Lorentz lift; unified treatment of isoradial graphs, Doyle spirals, once-punctured torus embeddings, and isothermic patterns under this framework.

Conclusion: The Möbius-invariant dimer correspondence provides a unifying geometric framework for integrable circle pattern dynamics, revealing deep connections among discrete conformal geometry, statistical mechanics, and orthogonal group symmetries—particularly through the t-embedding formalism and its Lorentzian interpretation.

Abstract: As is already known, there is a correspondence between circle patterns and the dimer model, which is invariant under Euclidean transformations. We establish a new correspondence that is invariant under the group of Möbius transformations PO(3,1), which is the natural symmetry group of circle patterns. We show that Miquel dynamics preserve the partition function, and that convexity of the t-embedding guarantees positivity of the face weights. We also show that the correspondence has a somewhat hidden symmetry group PO(3,3), which relates to the Lorentz lift of the t-embedding. Finally, we consider isoradial graphs, Doyle spirals, the once-punctured torus and isothermic circle patterns.

</details>


### [54] [Entropic repulsion to the middle layer](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.07578)
*Max Mihailescu,Ron Peled*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper studies discrete gradient Gibbs measures for bounded-height $\nabla\varphi$ models with even, convex interaction $W$ on $\mathbb{Z}^d$, establishing uniqueness (with exponential decay of correlations) under various dimension–temperature–interaction conditions, while also constructing counterexamples to uniqueness and proving universal magnetization bounds and delocalization.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand how entropic repulsion and bounded spin values affect phase behavior and uniqueness of Gibbs measures in $\nabla\varphi$ interface models—particularly extending beyond the standard quadratic case and resolving open questions from Bricmont–El Mellouki–Fröhlich (1986).

Method: Uses cluster expansion, Pirogov–Sinai theory (for comparison), contour arguments, and refined entropy estimates; adapts techniques for bounded height spaces, leverages convexity and monotonicity of $W''$, and constructs explicit non-uniqueness examples via 'layered' ground states.

Result: Uniqueness of Gibbs measure with exponential decay of correlations in: (a) $d=2$, all $T$; (b) $d\\ge3$, all $T$, for $W$ with non-increasing $W''$ (e.g., $|x|^p$, $1\\le p\\le2$); (c) $d\\ge3$, low/high $T$ regimes. Also proves universal magnetization bound $|m|<1/2$ for integer-valued $[-S,S]$-heights, delocalization above a floor, uniqueness for $[-1,1]$-valued heights (any convex $W$), and $|m|<1$ for half-integer heights.

Conclusion: Bounded-height $\nabla\varphi$ models exhibit robust uniqueness under mild convexity assumptions on $W$, but non-uniqueness is possible for carefully chosen $W$ in high dimensions; entropic repulsion enforces strict magnetization constraints and delocalization universally.

Abstract: We consider $\nabla\varphi$ height functions with even and convex interaction energy $W$ on the lattice $\mathbb{Z}^d$, which are restricted to take values in the set $\{-S, \ldots, S\}$ for some integer $S \ge 1$. We study the effect of entropic repulsion, which tends to push the spin values to the middle layer.
  We prove that the model has a unique Gibbs measure, with exponential decay of correlations, in the cases: (a) Dimension $d=2$ at all temperatures. (b) Dimensions $d\ge3$ at all temperatures, for a wide class of $W$ with non-increasing second derivative, including the family $W(x)=|x|^p$ for $p\in[1,2]$. (c) Dimensions $d\ge 3$ at both low and high temperatures, $T\in (0,\frac{W(1)d}{4(\ln d+\ln 8)})\cup(d W(2S),\infty]$, with the normalization $W(0)=0$. At low temperatures, our proof provides an alternative to Pirogov--Sinai methods.
  Conversely, we exhibit a class of even and convex interaction energies $W$ which, in high dimensions and suitable temperature regimes, have multiple Gibbs measures.
  Though uniqueness may fail, we show that the magnetization of every Gibbs measure lies in $(-\frac{1}{2},\frac{1}{2})$. This implies the delocalization of the model restricted to take values in $\{0,1,\ldots\}$ (i.e., conditioned to lie above a floor) for all dimensions, any even and convex $W$, and all temperatures.
  Our methods extend to additional setups: We prove that height functions taking values in the real interval $[-1,1]$ always have a unique Gibbs measure, a result previously proved only for the quadratic interaction. For height functions taking values in $\{-S+\frac{1}{2}, \ldots, S-\frac{1}{2}\}$, $S \ge1 $ integer, we prove that the magnetization of every Gibbs measure lies in $(-1,1)$.
  The special case $W(x)=x^2$ of our results addresses questions left open in the work of Bricmont--El Mellouki--Fröhlich (1986).

</details>


### [55] [Classification of Conformally Covariant 2-Tensors from the Kulkarni--Nomizu Product in Dimension 4](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.07750)
*Yury N. Berdinsky*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: Classifies all natural, conformally covariant, symmetric (0,2)-tensors of weight -2 and order ≤4 in 4D; proves they form a 2D space spanned by the Bach and Eastwood-Singer tensors; formal verification in Lean 4 and exact computational validation of constraints.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To systematically classify all natural, conformally covariant, symmetric (0,2)-tensors of fixed conformal weight (-2) and bounded differential order (≤4) in dimension 4 — motivated by geometric invariant theory, conformal geometry, and the search for canonical curvature obstructions.

Method: Algebraic classification using representation-theoretic and invariant-theoretic constraints; explicit construction and linear independence proof of candidate tensors; formal verification of algebraic relations in Lean 4 with Mathlib; exact rational arithmetic computation of the 8×11 constraint matrix and divergence-free condition.

Result: The space of such tensors is precisely 2-dimensional, with basis given by the Bach tensor (order 2) and the Eastwood-Singer tensor (order 4); all other candidates are linearly dependent or fail covariance/divergence conditions.

Conclusion: The Bach and Eastwood-Singer tensors exhaust the natural, conformally covariant, symmetric (0,2)-tensors of weight -2 and order ≤4 in four dimensions; this classification is rigorously verified both formally and computationally.

Abstract: We classify all natural, conformally covariant, symmetric (0,2)-tensors of conformal weight -2 and differential order less than or equal to 4 in dimension 4, built from the metric, the Schouten tensor, covariant derivatives, and the Kulkarni-Nomizu product. We prove that the space of such tensors is exactly 2-dimensional, spanned by the Bach tensor (order 2) and the Eastwood-Singer tensor (order 4). The algebraic core is formally verified using Lean 4 with Mathlib. The 8x11 constraint matrix and divergence-free condition are verified computationally with exact rational arithmetic. Both English and Russian versions provided; Russian version available as ancillary.

</details>


### [56] [Equation of Motion for Open Thermodynamic Systems and Its Dynamical Equivalence with Mechanical and Electrical Systems](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.07810)
*E Aydiner*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper derives an explicit equation of motion for open thermodynamic systems directly from the first law via dimensional reduction, revealing its equivalence to a driven damped harmonic oscillator and a driven RLC circuit; it establishes universal dynamical structure across thermodynamic, mechanical, and electrical systems, and constructs a consistent Lagrangian formulation.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: The first law of thermodynamics lacks an explicit dynamical formulation (i.e., equation of motion), unlike mechanical and electrical systems; the authors aim to bridge this gap by deriving such a formulation directly from first principles.

Method: Dimensional reduction applied to the first law for open thermodynamic systems; dimensional analysis of derived coefficients; systematic cross-domain comparison (mechanical, electrical, thermodynamic); construction of a thermodynamic Lagrangian and verification of correspondence with known mechanical and electrical Lagrangians.

Result: An explicit second-order ordinary differential equation of motion is obtained, structurally identical to that of a driven damped harmonic oscillator and a driven RLC circuit; effective thermodynamic 'mass', 'damping', 'stiffness', and 'driving' terms are identified and shown dimensionally consistent; Lagrangian formulation confirms variational consistency and cross-domain unification.

Conclusion: The first law admits a direct, rigorous derivation of an equation of motion for open thermodynamic systems; thermodynamic dynamics are formally isomorphic to canonical mechanical and electrical dynamics, implying a universal underlying dynamical framework governed by shared Lagrangian and dissipative structures.

Abstract: Changes in the internal energy of a thermodynamic system are fundamentally described by the first law of thermodynamics. However, the first law has a highly compact form that does not explicitly reveal the dynamics of the system or provide a corresponding equation of motion. While equations of motion can be written explicitly for mechanical and electrical systems, an analogous dynamical formulation for thermodynamic systems has, to our knowledge, not been established directly from the first law. In this work, we show that, through dimensional reduction, the first law can be transformed from its compact thermodynamic form into an explicit equation of motion for an open thermodynamic system. Remarkably, the resulting equation reveals that the dynamics of the thermodynamic system is equivalent to that of a driven damped harmonic oscillator and a driven RLC electrical circuit. Detailed dimensional analyses of the derived equations and effective coefficients demonstrate the internal consistency of the resulting formulation. We further perform a systematic comparison of the mechanical, electrical, and thermodynamic systems and show that, despite their distinct physical origins, they share the same universal dynamical structure. Finally, we construct a Lagrangian formulation of the thermodynamic system and establish its correspondence with the Lagrangian descriptions of the mechanical and electrical systems. These results demonstrate that an explicit equation of motion can be generated directly from the first law of thermodynamics and reveal a common dynamical framework underlying thermodynamic, mechanical, and electrical systems.

</details>


### [57] [Energy diffusion in a pinned harmonic chain with free boundary condition and interactions of increasing range](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.08138)
*Yipeng Lu,Wei Wang*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper proves convergence of empirical energy measures to the heat equation's weak solution for a 2D pinned harmonic chain with diverging-range interaction and rotational noise under specific scaling.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand how macroscopic diffusive behavior (e.g., heat conduction) emerges from microscopic stochastic lattice dynamics with long-range interactions.

Method: Rigorous probabilistic analysis using hydrodynamic limits, scaling limits, and measure-valued weak solutions; employs joint scaling where interaction range diverges microscopically but vanishes macroscopically.

Result: The expected empirical energy measures converge to the unique measure-valued weak solution of the heat equation.

Conclusion: Diffusive energy transport is robust even in the presence of diverging interaction range and conservative noise, confirming universality of the heat equation as the macroscopic limit.

Abstract: We consider a finite two-dimensional pinned harmonic chain with an interaction range that diverges with the system size and with conservative rotational noise. Under a joint scaling in which the interaction range diverges microscopically but vanishes macroscopically, we prove that the expected em- pirical energy measures converge to the unique measure-valued weak solution of the heat equation.

</details>


### [58] [Asymptotics of Ground States for Helium-Like and Bosonic Atoms in a Two-Cluster Region](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.08257)
*Yukimi Goto*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper analyzes the asymptotic behavior of ground states for N-particle atomic Coulomb systems in a two-cluster configuration, deriving precise decay estimates and convergence rates for the many-body wavefunction relative to reduced subsystems.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To rigorously characterize how the N-particle ground state decouples asymptotically when one particle escapes to infinity — a fundamental question in quantum many-body theory and scattering theory for atomic systems.

Method: Rigorous analysis of the Schrödinger equation for the Coulomb Hamiltonian using weighted Sobolev spaces, exponential decay estimates, and careful separation of variables in the two-cluster regime; employs asymptotic expansion techniques and $L^2$-error estimation in scaled regions.

Result: Derives uniform asymptotic expansion: $ψ/\sqrt{ρ(x_N)} = φ + O(|x_N|^{-2+α} \ln |x_N|)$ for $|x_i| ≤ |x_N|^α$, improved rate $O(|x_N|^{-2}(\ln|x_N|)^2)$ in logarithmic region $|x_i| ≤ R_0 \ln|x_N|$, and $O(|x_N|^{-2})$ bound on squared $L^2$ error — all valid both below and at the essential spectrum threshold.

Conclusion: The N-particle ground state asymptotically factorizes into the product of the $(N−1)$-particle ground state and the square root of the one-particle density of the escaping particle, with quantified, near-optimal remainder estimates — confirming and sharpening physical expectations of cluster decomposition in atomic systems.

Abstract: We study the asymptotic behavior of positive ground states of $N$-particle atomic Coulomb Hamiltonians in a two-cluster region. For the $N$-particle ground state $ψ$, its one-particle density $ρ$, and $(N-1)$-particle ground state $φ$, we show that for $0<α<1$, as $|x_N|\to\infty$, $ψ(x_1,\dots,x_N)/\sqrt{ρ(x_N)}= φ(x_1,\dots,x_{N-1})+O(|x_N|^{-2+α} \ln |x_N|)$ uniformly in the region $|x_i|\le |x_N|^α$. In the region $|x_i|\le R_0\ln |x_N|$, the convergence rate is $O(|x_N|^{-2} (\ln |x_N|)^2)$. We also establish an $O(|x_N|^{-2})$ bound on the squared $L^2$ error. These results hold both below the essential spectrum and at threshold.

</details>


### [59] [Arithmetic triangular structures in the transfer-matrix of finite Kronig-Penney models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.08523)
*Lidia Aceto,Pietro Antonio Grassi,Helmuth Robert Malonek*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper derives closed-form expressions for transmission and reflection amplitudes in the finite Kronig-Penney model with Dirac delta potentials, using Chebyshev polynomials of the second kind to analyze the Nth power of the unit-cell transfer matrix.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To rigorously derive closed-form expressions for transfer-matrix entries and combinatorial coefficients previously identified but not analytically established in the finite Kronig-Penney model with Dirac delta potentials.

Method: Expressing the Nth power of the unit-cell transfer matrix via Chebyshev polynomials of the second kind to obtain explicit closed-form representations of global transmission and reflection amplitudes.

Result: Explicit closed-form expressions for transmission and reflection amplitudes; discovery of a structural correspondence among quantum scattering, discrete convolutional patterns, and hypercomplex combinatorial structures.

Conclusion: The analytical framework resolves an open problem in the Kronig-Penney model and uncovers deep algebraic–combinatorial–physical connections in one-dimensional quantum scattering.

Abstract: This work provides a complete analytical characterization of the transfer- matrix structure associated with the finite Kronig-Penney model consisting of one-dimensional arrays of Dirac delta potentials recently introduced by Figueroa et al. (2025). Although their study identified the emergence of specific transfer-matrix entries and related combinatorial coefficients, a rig- orous derivation of the corresponding closed-form expressions has not yet been established. By expressing the N th power of the unit-cell transfer ma- trix in terms of Chebyshev polynomials of the second kind, we obtain explicit closed-form representations for the global transmission and reflection ampli- tudes. The proposed formulation reveals a previously unrecognized structural correspondence between multiple quantum scattering processes, discrete con- volutional patterns, and hypercomplex combinatorial structures.

</details>


### [60] [The phase coordinates transformation in Moyal noncommutative framework 2-dimensional harmonic oscillator and Painlevé second equation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.08525)
*Irfan Mahmood*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper introduces new coordinate transformations linking canonical to purely noncommuting phase-space variables within the Moyal noncommutative framework, yielding a 16-component traceless antisymmetric tensor; it constructs noncommutative analogs of the 2D harmonic oscillator and Painlevé II equation, revealing extended symmetries and generating the first Yablonskii–Vorob’ev polynomial via noncommutative parameter transformations.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To extend the physical applicability of Moyal noncommutative geometry beyond standard Heisenberg-type (coordinate-only) noncommutativity by incorporating simultaneous space-space and space-momentum noncommutativity, and to uncover novel integrable structures and symmetries in deformed systems.

Method: Derivation of novel canonical-to-noncommutative phase-space transformations consistent with Moyal brackets; construction of a 16-component traceless antisymmetric tensor encoding the deformation parameters; formulation of deformed Hamiltonians for the 2D harmonic oscillator and Painlevé II system; exploitation of superintegrability and symmetry analysis in the noncommutative setting; application of noncommutative coordinate/parameter transformations to generate Yablonskii–Vorob’ev polynomials.

Result: Consistent Moyal-compatible transformations enabling full phase-space noncommutativity; emergence of extended symmetries in deformed 2D harmonic oscillator and Painlevé II systems; explicit construction of noncommutative analogs preserving superintegrability; derivation of the first Yablonskii–Vorob’ev polynomial via noncommutative Painlevé II parameter transformation.

Conclusion: The Moyal noncommutative formalism—when generalized to full phase-space noncommutativity via the proposed transformations—enriches integrable systems theory by unveiling hidden symmetries, enabling exact solvability through special polynomials, and providing deeper insight into the physical role of noncommutative structure beyond quantization.

Abstract: This work aims to explore the physical application of Moyal noncom- mutative formalism with the the presentation of new transformations which connect the old canonical coordinates to purly noncommuting phase coordi- nates through the parameters describe the Moyal noncommutative deforma- tion. These transformations are consistent with the Moyal noncommunica- tive brackets and build sixteen components traceless anti-symmetric tensor. This formalism incorporates space-space and space-momentum noncommu- taivity rather then noncommutativity (Heisenberg quantum commutation ) only for the canonical coordinates. The transformations imply to construct noncommutative analogs of 2-dimensional harmonic oscillator with
  its superintegrability and Painlevé second equation. The associated deformed Hamiltonians additionally involve some extra symmetries and reveal a deep physical understanding of about the additional symmetries with noncommunicative structure. The Painlevé second noncommutative coordinates transformation are also efficiently applied to generate the first Yablonskii Vorobev polynomial with Painlevé second parameter transformation.

</details>


### [61] [On spatial decoherence in many-body systems](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.08910)
*Stefano Marcantoni,Marco Merkli*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper analyzes quantum decoherence in a many-body system coupled to a bosonic reservoir at strong coupling; it identifies distinct temporal regimes, resolves the decoherence process exactly at the fine-grained scale τ = λt, constructs a controlled effective Markovian (but non-semigroup) evolution, and rigorously explains position-space localization of macroscopic quantum objects.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand how strong system-reservoir coupling induces rapid spatial decoherence in many-body quantum systems, and to rigorously characterize the timescales and effective dynamics governing this process—particularly for macroscopic quantum localization.

Method: Mathematical analysis of the unitary evolution in the limit of infinite coupling strength λ → ∞; multiscale asymptotics with time scaling t ∝ λ⁻ᵃ; exact resolution at the critical scale α = 1 (τ = λt); derivation of an approximate effective evolution map with rigorous error bounds using operator-theoretic and open quantum systems techniques.

Result: Instantaneous spatial decoherence emerges for α < 1; trivial dynamics for α > 1; exact solvability and nontrivial effective dynamics at α = 1; the effective map is Markovian but violates semigroup composition (i.e., it is not CP-divisible in general); provides first-principles justification for position localization of massive quantum objects.

Conclusion: Strong coupling to a bosonic field generically enforces rapid suppression of spatial coherence in many-body systems; the physically relevant decoherence dynamics unfolds on the rescaled time τ = λt, yielding a controllable, non-semigroup Markovian effective description that rigorously underpins macroscopic quantum localization.

Abstract: We study the dynamics of a many-body quantum system strongly interacting with a bosonic reservoir. The coupling is given by a potential operator for the system and it is linear in the field. We show that in the limit of infinite coupling strength the system undergoes instantaneous spatial decoherence. To resolve the decoherence process in time we consider large coupling strengths $λ$ and short times scaling as $t\propto λ^{-α}$, with $α\ge0$, in the limit $λ\rightarrow\infty$. On short time scales $α>1$ the dynamics is trivial while on longer ones $0\leα<1$ the decoherence is instantaneous. We show that the decoherence process is resolved exactly for $α=1$, defining the fine-grained time scale $τ=λt$. We construct an approximate effective evolution map of the many-body system with controlled error estimates. Generically, the effective dynamics is markovian, but not given by a dynamical semigroup. As a physical application we give a rigorous description of the phenomenon of localization of macroscopic quantum objects in position space.

</details>


### [62] [The inverse problem of time-dependent density functional theory on the torus](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.08984)
*Asbjørn Bækgaard Lauritsen,Mathieu Lewin,Jakob Oldenburg*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: Proves the existence and uniqueness of an external potential that reproduces a given sufficiently regular one-particle density in the many-body Schrödinger equation—establishing rigorous foundations for time-dependent density functional theory (TDDFT), including for Coulomb interactions and extended nuclei.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To provide a rigorous mathematical foundation for time-dependent density functional theory (TDDFT) by proving the existence and uniqueness of the external potential corresponding to a prescribed time-dependent density—a key assumption (the 'runge-gross theorem') requiring justification beyond static cases.

Method: Functional analytic techniques applied to the many-body Schrödinger equation; employs regularity assumptions on the target one-particle density and handles the full Coulomb interaction and extended nuclear charge distributions.

Result: For any sufficiently regular one-particle density, there exists a unique external potential such that the many-body Schrödinger evolution yields exactly that density; this holds for physical interactions including the Coulomb potential and extended nuclei.

Conclusion: The mapping from external potential to time-dependent particle density is invertible under appropriate regularity conditions—validating the central premise of TDDFT and extending its rigorous scope beyond prior restricted settings.

Abstract: We prove that, for any given sufficiently regular one-particle density, there exists a unique external potential for which the solution to the many-body Schrödinger equation has this prescribed density. This implies in particular that one can exactly reproduce the time-dependent density of an interacting system using non-interacting electrons, which is at the heart of time-dependent density functional theory. Our result covers the Coulomb interaction and densities arising from extended nuclei.

</details>

### [63] [Coherent multipath wave response on Reissner-Nordström analogue surface](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.05534)
*Peng-Yu Chen,Sen Guo,Qing-Quan Jiang,Yu Liang,Kai Lin*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper develops a theoretical framework linking the geometry of a charged Reissner-Nordström black hole to wave phenomena on an analogue curved surface, using geodesic path spectra and Huygens-Fresnel construction to unify interference, resonance, and echo phenomena under charge-controlled geometry.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To uncover how the intrinsic metric of a relativistic compact object governs macroscopic wave phenomena, and to bridge strong-field gravitational lensing with tabletop transformation optics via analogue gravity.

Method: Solving an exact spatial geodesic boundary value problem on an isometrically embedded equatorial slice of a Reissner-Nordström spacetime; extracting discrete multi-loop path-length spectrum; converting it into a physical wave field via finite-path surface Huygens-Fresnel construction; analytically computing arbitrary-order winding trajectories and their high-winding limits.

Result: The RN charge acts as a precise dial reconfiguring the event horizon throat and reorganizing the discrete path sequence; geometric deformation uniquely dictates macroscopic interference patterns—spatial fringes, spectral resonance combs, and temporal echo ladders—all emerge as unified projections of a single charge-controlled path spectrum.

Conclusion: A rigorous theoretical bridge is established between strong-field gravity and transformation optics, enabling highly tunable, multi-domain analogue gravity experiments grounded in geometric parameter-to-wave-response mapping.

Abstract: To uncover how the intrinsic metric of a relativistic compact object governs macroscopic wave phenomena, we establish a theoretical framework mapping the charge dependent spatial geometry of a Reissner-Nordström (RN) black hole onto the coherent response of an analogue curved surface. By solving an exact spatial geodesic boundary value problem on an isometrically embedded equatorial slice, we extract the discrete multi-loop path-length spectrum and convert this geometric backbone into a physical wave field via a finite-path surface Huygens-Fresnel construction. We analytically compute the arbitrary order winding trajectories alongside their high winding accumulation limits, demonstrating that the analogue charge acts as a precise physical dial that reconfigures the event horizon throat and fundamentally reorganizes the discrete path sequence. Furthermore, we find that this underlying geometric deformation uniquely dictates the macroscopic interference, revealing that steady state spatial fringes, spectral resonance combs, and transient temporal echo ladders are intrinsically unified physical projections of a single charge controlled path spectrum. This systematic parameter to response methodology establishes a rigorous theoretical bridge between strong field gravitational lensing and tabletop transformation optics, providing a highly tunable blueprint for future multi domain analogue gravity experiments.

</details>


### [64] [Quantum-gravity-inspired Alcubierre warp-drive geometries](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.05554)
*Kimet Jusufi,Francisco S. N. Lobo*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper proposes a T-duality-inspired modification of the Alcubierre warp drive geometry, replacing the sharp warp profile with a smooth, string-motivated smearing characterized by a minimal length scale $l_0 = 2\pi\sqrt{\alpha'}$. The resulting metric is $C^2$-smooth, yields finite, closed-form expressions for energy density and total energy, and avoids the divergent energy cost of the profile-contraction limit ($R \to 0$) by bounding the effective width $l$ below by $l_0$. However, it remains an effective ansatz—not a derivation from string-corrected gravity—and still requires exotic matter without proven semiclassical stability.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To construct a physically more viable Alcubierre-type warp geometry by incorporating string-theoretic short-distance physics—specifically, the zero-point length $l_0 = 2\pi\sqrt{\alpha'}$ from string T-duality—which regularizes gravitational singularities, as demonstrated in regular black-hole models with smeared sources.

Method: The authors identify the Alcubierre warp function $f(r)$ with the complementary cumulative mass fraction of a spherically symmetric, T-duality-motivated smeared source. They adopt a one-scale ansatz $f(r_s) = 1 - r_s^3/(r_s^2 + l^2)^{3/2}$ where $l^2 = R^2 + l_0^2$, ensuring $C^2$ continuity at the bubble center; then compute the induced energy density $\rho_E$, its volume integral (total energy $E$), and York expansion analytically.

Result: The constructed metric is globally smooth ($C^2$ at origin, smooth elsewhere); the energy density $\rho_E$ is bounded: $|\rho_E| \lesssim \text{const} \times v_s^2 / l^2$; total energy is finite and closed-form: $E = -(15\pi/1024) v_s^2 l$ (geometric units); crucially, the divergence as $R \to 0$ is eliminated because $l \geq l_0$, unlike the conventional thin-wall limit.

Conclusion: The T-duality-inspired smearing provides a finite-energy, smooth warp geometry that evades the profile-contraction singularity, offering a more robust effective framework than the original Alcubierre metric—yet it does not eliminate exotic matter requirements, nor does it constitute a rigorous solution to string-corrected gravity; stability and quantum energy conditions remain unverified without full stress-tensor renormalization.

Abstract: String T-duality, through its correspondence with path-integral duality, endows the low-energy propagator with a zero-point length $l_0=2π\sqrt{α'}$ that softens the short-distance behavior of gravitational fields. Motivated by the regular black-hole construction obtained from the corresponding smeared source, we formulate a T-duality-inspired Alcubierre geometry by identifying the warp profile with the complementary cumulative mass fraction of that source. For the effective one-scale choice $f(r_s)=1-r_s^3/(r_s^2+l^2)^{3/2}$, with $l^2=R^2+l_0^2$, the metric is $C^2$ at the bubble center and smooth elsewhere, while the energy density, its volume integral, and the York expansion are available in closed form. In particular, $E=-(15π/1024)v_s^2 l$ in geometric units and $|ρ_E|$ is bounded by a constant times $v_s^2/l^2$. Within this one-scale family, the model therefore removes the divergence associated with the profile-contraction limit $R\to 0$ at fixed velocity, because $l$ cannot fall below $l_0$. This should not be confused with a regularization of the conventional Alcubierre thin-wall limit at fixed macroscopic bubble radius, in which an independent wall thickness is taken to zero. We emphasize, however, that the construction is an effective ansatz rather than a derivation from string-corrected field equations: $R$ is a macroscopic profile scale, the choice $l^2=R^2+l_0^2$ is an interpolation, and the classical $l_0\to 0$ limit remains a smooth thick-walled profile rather than the distributional Alcubierre top hat. Exotic matter remains necessary, and neither semiclassical stability nor a modified quantum energy inequality is claimed without an explicit renormalized stress-tensor calculation.

</details>


### [65] [The Einstein Tensor from UV-regulated Field Correlations](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.05558)
*Matheus H. Zambianco,Achim Kempf*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper shows that spacetime curvature tensors—including the Einstein and Ricci tensors—can be directly expressed in terms of UV-regulated quantum field correlators, and that the renormalized stress-energy tensor fits naturally into this correlation-based framework, advancing the reconstruction of semiclassical gravity from quantum field data.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To extend the programme of recovering spacetime geometry from quantum-field correlators by directly encoding gravitational curvature tensors—and ultimately the semiclassical Einstein equations—in terms of field correlations, without presupposing a classical metric background.

Method: Analyzing coincidence limits and higher-order derivatives of UV-regulated two-point (and possibly higher) quantum field correlators on curved spacetime; employing Hadamard regularization and geometric expansion techniques to isolate local curvature tensors from correlation structure.

Result: The Einstein tensor, Ricci tensor, Levi-Civita connection, and Riemann tensor are all explicitly recoverable from specific algebraic and differential operations on regulated field correlators; the renormalized stress-energy tensor is formulated within the same framework, modulo standard finite ambiguities.

Conclusion: Spacetime geometry and semiclassical gravity can be organized entirely around quantum field correlations, supporting a deeper operational and background-independent understanding of gravity in quantum field theory.

Abstract: It is known that the spacetime metric can be recovered from quantum-field correlators. We extend this programme by showing that the Einstein tensor can be directly written in terms of UV-regulated field correlations. The same correlation structure also directly determines the Ricci tensor, while higher coincidence derivatives recover the connection and Riemann tensor. We further show that the renormalized stress-energy tensor can be formulated within this framework, up to the standard finite renormalization freedom. These results provide a correlation-based organization of the semiclassical Einstein equations and advance the programme of formulating spacetime geometry in terms of quantum field correlations.

</details>


### [66] [End state of the experimental black-hole bomb](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.05595)
*Patrik Švančara,Leonardo Solidoro,Pietro Smaniotto,Sam Patrick,Silvia Schiattarella,Maciej T. Jarema,Sean M. D. Gregory,Vitor S. Barroso,Maurício Richartz,Anastasios Avgoustidis,Carlo F. Barenghi,Ruth Gregory,Silke Weinfurtner*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper experimentally realizes the black-hole bomb instability in a superfluid helium gravity simulator, observing exponential growth of a superradiant mode followed by nonlinear saturation into a steady state via wave mixing.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand the poorly understood nonlinear evolution of the black-hole bomb instability—central to models of bosonic clouds around astrophysical black holes—by testing it in a controlled laboratory analog.

Method: Uses a draining vortex in superfluid helium as a gravity simulator, where surface waves propagate on an effective rotating spacetime; confines waves in a cylindrical cavity, tunes temperature and flow to trigger superradiance, and performs spatially and temporally resolved measurements to track linear growth and nonlinear dynamics (frequency shifts, harmonic generation, three- and four-wave mixing).

Result: Observation of exponential growth of a low-frequency resonant mode, followed by arrest of instability and formation of a long-lived non-equilibrium steady state mediated by coherent nonlinear wave interactions.

Conclusion: Nonlinear wave mixing—not present in linear theory—quenches runaway superradiant growth and governs late-time dynamics; this establishes a laboratory framework for probing nonlinear black-hole bomb physics relevant to ultralight boson astrophysics.

Abstract: Rotating black holes can amplify incident waves through superradiant scattering. When these waves are confined, repeated amplification gives rise to the black-hole bomb instability, whose nonlinear evolution remains poorly understood despite its central role in models of bosonic clouds around astrophysical black holes. Here, we reproduce the black-hole bomb mechanism in a laboratory setting using a gravity simulator based on a draining vortex in superfluid helium. Surface waves propagating on the superfluid interface experience an effective rotating spacetime and undergo repeated superradiant amplification within a cylindrical cavity. By tuning the temperature and flow parameters, we achieve exponential growth of a low-frequency resonant mode, followed by the arrest of the instability and the formation of a long-lived non-equilibrium steady state. Using spatially and temporally resolved measurements, we identify nonlinear frequency shifts, harmonic generation, and coherent three- and four-wave mixing that redistribute energy among interacting modes. This novel end state of the experimental black-hole bomb highlights the role of nonlinear wave interactions in quenching the runaway growth expected from linear theory and governing the system's late-time dynamics. Our results establish a laboratory framework for investigating the nonlinear evolution of black-hole bombs, with implications for analogous phenomena involving ultralight bosonic fields around rotating black holes.

</details>


### [67] [A compact time-frequency representation for gravitational-wave data analysis](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.05601)
*Noah Pearson,Mesut Çalışkan,Sophie Bini,Neil J. Cornish*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper proposes a new Gaussian-based window function for the Wilson-Daubechies-Meyer (WDM) wavelet transform, designed to be more time-frequency symmetric and compact than the standard WDM window; it analytically and numerically demonstrates near-optimal time-frequency localization—within ~2.4% of the Heisenberg-Gabor limit—and conjectures it minimizes time-frequency area among WD wavelet windows.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To improve time-frequency localization in gravitational-wave data analysis by addressing the asymmetry and temporal spread of the standard Wilson-Daubechies-Meyer (WDM) wavelet window, especially under non-stationary noise conditions.

Method: Constructs a novel window function from a sum of phase-shifted Gaussians; derives and computes its time-frequency variance product analytically; validates results numerically; compares symmetry and compactness against the standard WDM window and the Heisenberg-Gabor uncertainty limit.

Result: The Gaussian window achieves near-minimal time-frequency uncertainty—within ~2.4% of the Heisenberg-Gabor limit for the symmetric case—and exhibits superior time-frequency compactness and symmetry compared to the standard WDM window.

Conclusion: The proposed Gaussian window offers improved time-frequency localization for wavelet-based gravitational-wave analysis; its symmetry and compactness suggest it may minimize the time-frequency area among all WD wavelet windows—a property supported by analytical derivation and numerical verification.

Abstract: Time-frequency (wavelet) domain analyses are seeing greater use for gravitational-wave data analysis due to the advantages they have in handling non-stationary noise. A popular choice is the Wilson-Daubechies-Meyer (WDM) wavelet transform, which uses a window function that is very compact in frequency, but more spread out in time. In this work, we consider an alternative window function that is built from a sum of phase-shifted Gaussians. This "Gaussian" window is more symmetric in time-frequency, and consequently more compact. We examine the properties of this window function and the implications it has for gravitational-wave analyses. We calculate the window's time-frequency variance product analytically and verify it numerically. For the symmetric case, where the window has the same form in time and frequency, the construction comes within $\sim2.4\%$ of saturating the Heisenberg-Gabor uncertainty limit. We conjecture that this Gaussian window achieves the minimum time-frequency area of any WD wavelet window.

</details>


### [68] [Effective Field Theory of Gravity in Relativistic Media](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.05639)
*Beka Modrekiladze*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper develops an effective field theory for gravity in relativistic media, showing how environmental stress-energy correlators modify vacuum gravity's Feynman rules—yielding new physical effects like running Love numbers, graviton decay (h→hh), gravitational opacity, and a flow-direction-sensitive memory effect ('astrophysical weathervane').


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To systematically incorporate the influence of relativistic media (e.g., fluids, wave dark matter) on gravitational dynamics beyond standard vacuum GR, enabling consistent treatment of medium-induced corrections to gravitational potentials, radiation, and tidal responses.

Method: Constructs a Wilsonian effective field theory by integrating out the medium degrees of freedom; computes dressed graviton propagators and medium-induced vertices using stress-energy correlators; applies matching to derive 1PN potentials, memory, and dissipative effects; analyzes dispersion and decay kinematics in moving media.

Result: Derives full 1PN Einstein-Infeld-Hoffmann potential and Stokes drag; identifies generalized Christodoulou memory with flow-dependent tensor structure; shows symmetry-breaking by medium flow converts static Love numbers into resonant, running ones; discovers h→hh decay channel and gravitational birefringent opacity; provides closed-form decay rate and observational estimates for ET/LISA.

Conclusion: Gravity in relativistic media is not a new theory but vacuum GR augmented by environment-specific correlators; this framework unifies diverse astrophysical environments and predicts novel, testable phenomena—including directional memory, medium-driven graviton decay, and flow-dependent tidal response—that extend the phenomenology of gravitational-wave and precision gravity experiments.

Abstract: We develop an effective field theory of gravity in relativistic media. Integrating out the medium leaves vacuum gravity with updated Feynman rules: a graviton propagator dressed by the stress-energy two-point function of the environment, medium-induced bulk graviton vertices from higher correlators, and generalized worldline couplings encoded in matched Wilson coefficients. The diagram topologies are unchanged from vacuum, so different media (perfect fluids, collisionless matter, coherent scalar fields such as wave dark matter) are not different theories but different correlators inserted into the same diagrams. We derive the in-medium rules for a relativistic fluid and obtain the full 1PN Einstein-Infeld-Hoffmann potential, the 1PN Stokes drag, the gravitational self-energy and a generalized Christodoulou memory whose new tensor structure records the direction of the surrounding flow, turning the permanent strain into an $\textit{astrophysical weathervane}$. The same rules activate phenomena forbidden in vacuum: the sound pole converts the symmetry-protected, non-running black-hole Love number into a resonant, running one, and opens the channel $h\to hh$ in a moving medium, yielding a closed-form decay rate and a birefringent $\textit{gravitational opacity}$ set by the local flow geometry. We assess observational prospects, from dephasing and tidal resonances within reach of the Einstein Telescope and LISA to proof-of-principle memory and opacity signatures.

</details>


### [69] [Fermionic Casimir memory from an anisotropic gravitational pulse](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.05666)
*Celio R. Muniz,Herondy F. Santana Mota*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper studies how a weak, anisotropic gravitational pulse deposits energy in a massless Dirac field confined between two parallel plates with MIT bag boundary conditions; it finds a finite, boundary-dependent, late-time fermionic memory effect—distinct from bosonic vacuum-polarization memory—manifesting as a nonmonotonic pressure correction to the Casimir force.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand the late-time quantum response of confined fermionic fields to transient gravitational perturbations, specifically whether and how boundaries induce persistent, dissipative memory effects beyond known bosonic vacuum-polarization memory.

Method: Tetrad formalism with linear spin-connection analysis; Bogoliubov transformation technique to compute pair production; subtraction of unbounded-space contribution to isolate boundary-dependent energy deposition; asymptotic analysis in adiabatic and impulsive limits.

Result: A finite, negative (adiabatic) to vanishing-positive (impulsive) boundary-dependent energy deposition arises, leading to a nonmonotonic pressure correction with two sign changes relative to the Casimir force; this constitutes a dissipative fermionic memory effect carried by gravitationally created pairs.

Conclusion: Confinement modifies gravitational particle creation in Dirac fields, yielding a novel, dissipative fermionic memory distinct from bosonic vacuum-polarization memory; the effect is intrinsically tied to boundary-induced spectral discreteness and shear coupling, and alters Casimir pressure nontrivially.

Abstract: We investigate the late-time energy deposited by a weak, spatially homogeneous anisotropic gravitational pulse in a massless Dirac field confined between two static parallel plates. The field satisfies MIT bag boundary conditions, while the prescribed geometry approaches Minkowski spacetime in both asymptotic time regions. Using a tetrad formulation, we show that the linear spin-connection contribution cancels for the traceless anisotropy, leaving a coupling to the shear component of the Dirac stress tensor. The pulse produces real fermion--antifermion pairs, whose deposited energy is evaluated through the corresponding Bogoliubov coefficients. Subtracting the production in unbounded space isolates a finite boundary-dependent contribution governed by the pulse duration relative to the characteristic spectral timescale set by the MIT gap. This contribution is negative in the adiabatic regime, where confinement suppresses excitations relative to the continuous free spectrum, and approaches zero from above in the impulsive limit. The resulting pressure correction is nonmonotonic and exhibits two sign changes, alternately reinforcing and reducing the ordinary attractive Casimir force. Unlike the bosonic vacuum-polarization memory previously obtained for the same class of backgrounds, the present effect is a dissipative fermionic memory carried by gravitationally created pairs. Here, memory denotes the persistent late-time modification of the quantum state after the pulse has vanished.

</details>


### [70] [Self-supervised reconstruction of transients in data from space-borne gravitational-wave detectors](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.05890)
*Yuxiang Xu,Minghui Du,Bo Liang,Zihao Xiao,Tianyu Zhao,Peng Xu*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper introduces a self-supervised Noise2Noise-inspired framework for extracting unknown gravitational-wave transients from space-based data without clean labels or waveform templates, enabling robust recovery and conditional subtraction of instrumental glitches and astrophysical signals.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Existing DNN-based GW transient extraction methods require clean training targets and waveform-class-specific examples, limiting applicability to previously uncharacterized or unknown-morphology transients (e.g., novel astrophysical sources or instrumental glitches).

Method: A self-supervised framework inspired by Noise2Noise (N2N) is developed, trained solely on noisy observations of massive black-hole binaries (MBHBs) without clean targets or transient-specific templates; inference is template-free and generalizes to diverse transient morphologies including instrumental glitches and cosmic string bursts.

Result: The single trained model achieves high-overlap MBHB recovery, successfully extracts dominant morphologies of glitches and cosmic string bursts in source-confused test data, and enables conditional subtraction of identified instrumental transients—reducing injected-anomaly power in glitch-dominated bands within simulated continuous data streams.

Conclusion: Self-supervised extraction provides an effective initial waveform estimation tool for transients with unknown morphologies, facilitating downstream characterization and targeted mitigation of instrumental anomalies in space-based GW data.

Abstract: Space-based gravitational-wave (GW) data may contain transient signals whose waveform morphologies are not known in advance. Extracting these signals is important for characterizing new sources and mitigating instrumental anomalies. However, existing deep neural network (DNN)-based extraction approaches rely on clean training targets and waveform-class-specific examples, which may limit their applicability when the transient morphology is not specified in advance. This work develops a Noise2Noise (N2N)-inspired self-supervised framework that learns from noisy observations without clean training targets and requires no transient-specific waveform templates at inference. A single model trained on noisy massive black-hole binary (MBHB) observations provides high-overlap MBHB recovery and recovers the dominant morphologies of instrumental glitches and other GW transient signals such as cosmic string bursts in source-confused test data. Beyond waveform recovery, when independent information identifies a candidate transient as instrumental, its extracted waveform can be subtracted from the data without an anomaly-specific template. In a simulated continuous data stream, this procedure substantially suppresses the injected-anomaly power within the glitch-dominated frequency band. These results support the use of self-supervised extraction for initial waveform estimation of candidate transients with unknown morphologies, enabling subsequent characterization and, where appropriate, conditional subtraction of instrumental anomalies.

</details>


### [71] [Thin accretion disk and gravitational capture cross sections of a quantum Oppenheimer--Snyder black hole immersed in an external magnetic field](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.05906)
*Anuar Idrissov,Hernando Quevedo*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper investigates how quantum corrections (via an Oppenheimer–Snyder black hole model with a single quantum parameter) and an external uniform magnetic field affect accretion disk structure and particle capture in a static, spherically symmetric spacetime. It computes key observables—including ISCO, radiative flux, spectral luminosity, efficiency, and capture cross sections—for massless, massive, and charged particles—and finds that quantum effects are weak on disk emission but strongly suppress all capture cross sections, while the magnetic coupling boosts efficiency and spectral peak but leaves photon shadow unaffected.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To probe quantum-gravitational signatures in black hole astrophysics by studying how a quantum-corrected black hole geometry—interpolating between two-horizon, extremal, and horizonless configurations—affects standard accretion disk physics and particle dynamics in an external magnetic field, distinguishing quantum vs. magnetic contributions to observable quantities.

Method: Analytic and semi-analytic computation of geodesic and charged-particle orbits in a static, spherically symmetric quantum black hole spacetime with Wald-type magnetic field; derivation of Novikov–Thorne disk structure (flux, temperature, redshift, luminosity, efficiency); calculation of capture cross sections for null, timelike, and charged trajectories; consistency checks against Schwarzschild limit and bolometric luminosity–efficiency identity.

Result: Quantum parameter weakly modifies disk emission (e.g., flux, temperature) but monotonically shrinks all particle capture cross sections; magnetic coupling strongly enhances radiative efficiency, peak flux, and spectral peak frequency, shifts peak to higher frequencies, and enlarges massive-particle capture cross section while leaving photon cross section invariant; charged particles are magnetically confined beyond a shielding radius.

Conclusion: Shadow observables (e.g., photon capture cross section) are pure probes of the quantum parameter in this model, decoupled from magnetic effects; accretion disk spectra encode both quantum and magnetic physics, but with distinguishable, opposing signatures—enabling potential disentanglement in multi-messenger observations.

Abstract: We study a geometrically thin, optically thick Novikov--Thorne accretion disk and the gravitational capture cross sections of a quantum Oppenheimer--Snyder black hole immersed in an external, asymptotically uniform magnetic field. The exterior geometry carries a single quantum parameter, and we work over its entire admissible range, which contains a two-horizon black hole, an extremal configuration and a horizonless compact object, and which ends where the photon sphere disappears. Since the spacetime is static, the Wald potential is purely axial, so the field acts on the disk only through the Lorentz force on weakly charged accreting matter and all of its effects are controlled by a single magnetic coupling. We compute the charged circular orbits, the innermost stable circular orbit, the radiative flux, the effective temperature, the redshift factor, the differential and spectral luminosity and the radiative efficiency, together with the marginally bound orbit and the capture cross sections of massless, massive and charged particles. The construction is checked against the exact Schwarzschild limit and against the identity that relates the bolometric luminosity to the efficiency. The quantum parameter changes the disk observables only weakly, whereas the magnetic coupling raises the efficiency, the peak flux and the height of the spectral peak by large factors and shifts that peak to higher frequency. The two parameters act with opposite signs on absorption. The quantum parameter shrinks every capture cross section, while the magnetic coupling enlarges the one for massive particles and leaves the photon cross section unchanged, so that within this test-field model shadow observables respond to the quantum parameter alone. For charged particles the uniform field confines the motion, and no particle reaches the hole from beyond a magnetic shielding radius.

</details>


### [72] [Slow rotation of a localized Kalb-Ramond wormhole: Wald charges and fractional quadrupolar hair](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.05921)
*Sardor Murodov*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper constructs a slow-rotation expansion (up to second order) for a traversable wormhole supported by phantom scalar, localized string, and nonminimally coupled Kalb-Ramond field; finds vanishing ADM mass, finite angular momentum, oblate throat geometry under rotation, and—most notably—a long-range, fractional-decay Kalb-Ramond–induced metric tail that breaks smooth multipolar asymptotics, invalidating standard quadrupole definitions while preserving well-defined global charges.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To investigate how nonminimal couplings of the Kalb-Ramond field to curvature affect asymptotic structure and multipole moments in rotating wormhole spacetimes, particularly whether rotational quadrupoles retain standard smooth asymptotic behavior or exhibit novel 'fractional hair'.

Method: Covariant slow-rotation expansion (up to second order in angular velocity) combined with asymptotic analysis at spatial infinity; use of Iyer-Wald formalism for angular momentum; comparison of covariant vs. metric angular momentum definitions; analysis of conformal completion differentiability; evaluation of Geroch-Hansen and mass-centered Cartesian quadrupole constructions.

Result: Vanishing ADM mass; finite first-order axial angular momentum agreeing across covariant/metric definitions; no second-order mass correction; rotation increases throat area and induces oblateness; emergence of a long-range polar Kalb-Ramond–driven metric tail with noninteger (fractional) decay exponent, degrading conformal differentiability at spatial infinity and invalidating standard smooth quadrupole definitions.

Conclusion: The benchmark wormhole exhibits charge-level asymptotic flatness (well-defined global mass and angular momentum) but lacks smooth multipolar asymptotics due to fractional Kalb-Ramond hair, establishing it as the leading rotational quadrupolar signature—thus separating asymptotic flatness from multipolar regularity.

Abstract: We construct an action-consistent slow-rotation branch, through second order, for a traversable wormhole supported by a phantom scalar, a scalar-localized radial string sector, and a Kalb-Ramond two-form coupled nonminimally to both the Ricci scalar and Ricci tensor. For the regular benchmark branch, covariant asymptotic analysis shows a vanishing ADM mass, while the first-order axial mode carries finite Iyer-Wald angular momentum. The nonminimal contributions to the angular charge decay at infinity, so the covariant and metric definitions of angular momentum agree. At second order, no nonzero mass correction is resolved; at fixed asymptotic scalar charge, rotation increases the throat area and makes the throat oblate. The most distinctive result arises in the quadrupolar sector. A long-range polar Kalb-Ramond response couples to the localized background and generates a matter-supported metric tail that decays more slowly than the ordinary vacuum quadrupole. The same noninteger hierarchy appears in curvature and reduces the differentiability of the conformal completion at spatial infinity. Consequently, the Geroch-Hansen and asymptotically Cartesian mass-centered quadrupole constructions are not applicable in their standard smooth form to this branch, even though its global mass and angular-momentum charges remain well defined. This separates charge-level asymptotic flatness from smooth multipolar asymptotics and identifies fractional Kalb-Ramond hair as the leading rotational quadrupolar signature of the benchmark solution.

</details>


### [73] [Physics-based phenomenological modeling of binary black hole hierarchical formation 1: Synthetic universes from globular cluster simulations for GWTC](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.05996)
*R. O'Shaughnessy,R. Mechum,M. Qazalbash,Z. Rosenberg,M. Zeeshan*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper models the GWTC-5.0 binary black hole population using three astrophysical components—globular cluster (GC), field (isolated binary), and an intermediate-mass isotropic component—to jointly explain mass, spin, and merger rate observations; it yields physically normalized local rates and makes testable, mass-resolved predictions on spectral breaks, mass ratio features, and spin distribution.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To resolve residual tensions in the observed binary black hole mass and spin distributions—particularly at 15–30 M⊙ and in effective-spin dispersion—by jointly modeling contributions from globular clusters, isolated field binaries, and a hypothesized reprocessed intermediate-mass component.

Method: Hierarchical Bayesian population modeling of GWTC-5.0 data, incorporating: (1) physically normalized GC simulations (varying cluster mass, metallicity, redshift, compactness, natal spin); (2) phenomenological field channel with aligned, low-mass binaries; and (3) an isotropic intermediate-mass component to address 15–30 M⊙ tension. Physical normalization anchors high-mass event rates to astrophysical priors.

Result: The best-fit model yields local merger rates R_cl ≈ 9.1 and R_field ≈ 16.0 Gpc⁻³ yr⁻¹, GC contribution fraction f̂_GC ≈ 0.39%; predicts correlated features: mass-spectrum breaks near 35 and 70 M⊙, a q ≈ 0.5 excess from first+second-generation pairings, and a symmetric but sharply broadening effective-spin distribution above 45 M⊙.

Conclusion: A multi-channel astrophysical model—combining GC, field, and reprocessed components—provides a self-consistent explanation for current GWTC-5.0 census statistics; its mass-resolved predictions are falsifiable with future detections, enabling discrimination between formation scenarios.

Abstract: We iteratively model the GWTC-5.0 binary-black-hole census with three components. First, a physically normalized population from Rapster globular-cluster simulations spans cluster mass, metallicity, formation redshift, compactness, and natal black-hole spin. Hierarchical mergers reproduce higher-mass events and their larger effective-spin dispersion, provided black holes are born with zero natal spin. Second, a phenomenological field (isolated-binary) channel supplies the low-mass, preferentially aligned population. Its inclusion flattens the cluster compactness likelihood, permitting ordinary dense globular-cluster birth radii without requiring nuclear-cluster-like conditions. Finally, residual tension at $15$--$30 M_\odot$ motivates an intermediate-mass isotropic component representing field remnants reprocessed in clusters. This is a hypothesis, not a detection: current numerical support precludes a reliable evidence comparison. Physical normalization converts the high-mass rate into $\hat{f}_{\rm GC}\simeq0.39\%$, with local rates $R_{\rm cl}\simeq9.1$ and $R_{\rm field}\simeq16.0$ Gpc$^{-3}$ yr$^{-1}$. The model predicts linked mass-spectrum breaks near $35$ and $70 M_\odot$, a $q\simeq0.5$ feature from first-plus-second-generation pairings, and a symmetric effective-spin distribution that broadens sharply above $45 M_\odot$. These correlated, mass-resolved predictions can be tested as the gravitational-wave census grows.

</details>


### [74] [A principal-symbol obstruction in metric-affine Gauss--Bonnet gravity](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.06084)
*Hamed Bouzari Nezhad*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper compares two affine continuations of the 4D Gauss-Bonnet term—metric-contracted and double-epsilon—in metric-affine gravity with scalar coupling f(φ), analyzing their quadratic two-derivative principal forms for the connection near the Levi-Civita limit; the former yields a nonvanishing, indefinite principal symbol (rank 18, nullity 6) obstructing algebraic elimination of the connection, while the latter has identically vanishing principal symbol, removing that obstruction—but not guaranteeing eliminability.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To determine whether different affine continuations of the Riemannian Gauss-Bonnet term yield the same leading differential structure for the independent affine connection in metric-affine gravity, especially near the Levi-Civita limit.

Method: Analysis of quadratic two-derivative principal forms of the linearized connection field equations for two distinct metric-affine extensions (metric-contracted and double-epsilon) of the Gauss-Bonnet term, evaluated on torsion-free subspaces and at timelike principal covectors, within a scalar-coupled f(φ)Gauss–Bonnet model.

Result: The metric-contracted continuation yields a nonvanishing, indefinite principal form of rank 18 and nullity 6 on a hook-free torsion-free subspace, implying nonzero principal symbol and obstruction to algebraic local elimination of the connection where f(φ) ≠ 0; the double-epsilon continuation yields identically vanishing principal form—and thus vanishing principal symbol—removing that obstruction.

Conclusion: The leading differential structure (i.e., hyperbolicity, constraint character, and algebraic eliminability) of the independent connection is not unique but depends intrinsically on the choice of affine continuation of the Gauss-Bonnet term.

Abstract: The four-dimensional Riemannian Gauss--Bonnet combination admits more than one continuation to a geometry with an independent affine connection. Two natural candidates, the metric-contracted representative and the double-epsilon representative, agree in their Levi-Civita limit, and we ask whether they also give the independent connection the same leading differential structure. In a metric-affine model with a scalar coupling $f(φ)$ to the Gauss--Bonnet term, we compare their quadratic two-derivative connection principal forms about the Levi-Civita locus. For the metric-contracted continuation, the principal form, evaluated on a twenty-four-dimensional hook-free torsion-free witness subspace at a timelike principal covector, has rank $18$ and nullity $6$ and is indefinite; the full torsion-free form at the same covector is therefore indefinite with rank at least $18$. Locally where $f(φ)\neq0$, the two-derivative principal symbol of the linearized connection equations is nonzero, which obstructs purely algebraic local elimination of the connection in the activated directions. For the double-epsilon continuation, the corresponding quadratic two-derivative form vanishes identically for an arbitrary perturbation of the connection about that locus, so its principal symbol vanishes with it, and that particular obstruction is absent, though its absence does not by itself establish algebraic eliminability. Thus the leading differential structure of the independent connection depends on the affine continuation of the Riemannian Gauss--Bonnet combination.

</details>


### [75] [Regular black holes from dynamical-tension string hedgehogs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.06097)
*Sebastian Bahamonde,Eduardo I. Guendelman*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper proposes a modified-measure string theory framework to construct regular, asymptotically flat black holes from radial string clouds, bypassing conical singularities; it derives model-independent conditions on the radial tension profile 𝒯(r) for regularity, energy condition compliance, and finite mass, and constructs an explicit cubic-tension solution yielding geometrically regular black holes with r⁻⁴ Schwarzschild corrections.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
