Motivation: To resolve the conical singularity inherent in standard spherical clouds of radial strings with constant tension — which yield singular, non-black-hole geometries due to r⁻² energy density — by developing a consistent, geometrically regular black hole model within string-inspired gravity.

Method: Employs the modified-measure formulation of string theory, introducing a composite bulk tension scalar that generates variable worldsheet tensions; imposes an SO(3)-hedgehog constraint to reduce to Einstein gravity coupled to a nonlinear kinetic term K(Y) and an auxiliary three-form; derives necessary and sufficient conditions on the radial tension profile 𝒯(r) from curvature finiteness, null/dominant energy conditions, and ADM mass finiteness; constructs an exact analytic solution with cubic tension 𝒯(r) ∝ r²(1 − r²/R²).

Result: Derives universal conditions: finite central curvature ⇔ 𝒯(r) = 𝒪(r²); null energy condition ⇔ leading power cannot exceed r² ⇒ central geometry must be de Sitter; finite ADM mass ⇔ tension screening at infinity (𝒯 → 0 as r → ∞); dominant energy condition cannot hold globally if 𝒯 is screened faster than r⁻². An exact cubic-tension solution yields asymptotically flat, geometrically regular black holes with no curvature singularities and first-order deviation from Schwarzschild at O(r⁻⁴).

Conclusion: Geometrically regular, asymptotically flat black holes can emerge from radial string clouds only when tension is radially varying and satisfies tightly constrained power-law behavior near the centre and at infinity; the modified-measure framework provides a consistent, model-independent pathway to such solutions, with the cubic-tension model serving as a concrete, physically viable realization.

Abstract: A spherical cloud of radial strings with non-zero constant tension has an energy density proportional to $r^{-2}$ and therefore produces a singular conical geometry rather than an asymptotically flat regular black hole. We show that this obstruction can be removed within the modified-measure formulation of strings. A continuum of radial worldsheets whose tensions are generated by a composite bulk tension scalar reduces, on a constrained $\mathrm{SO}(3)$ hedgehog branch, to Einstein gravity coupled to a nonlinear function $K(Y)$ and an auxiliary three-form. This construction gives model-independent conditions directly in terms of the radial tension $\mathcal{T}(r)$. Finite curvature at the centre requires $\mathcal{T}=\mathcal{O}(r^2)$, while the null energy condition forbids a faster leading power. Thus a positive regular centre satisfying the null energy condition is necessarily de Sitter. Finite ADM mass requires the tension to be screened at infinity, whereas the dominant energy condition cannot hold globally for a non-trivial positive profile screened faster than $r^{-2}$. An exact cubic profile realises these properties and gives a continuous family of asymptotically flat geometrically regular black holes whose first correction to Schwarzschild appears at order $r^{-4}$.

</details>


### [76] [Heat distribution of quantum fields interacting with Unruh-DeWitt detectors](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.06185)
*Marcos L. W. Basso,Alberto Saa*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper develops a relativistically causal framework for heat-exchange statistics between an Unruh-DeWitt detector and a quantum field in Minkowski spacetime, using an interferometric protocol to define the heat characteristic function without projective measurements; it derives perturbative and exact (in gapless/delta-switching limits) expressions showing Poissonian heat statistics—unidirectional in vacuum, bidirectional for KMS states—and recovers fluctuation relations, Landauer bounds, and first/second laws with explicit switching work.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To establish a consistent, causally compliant operational definition of heat exchange in relativistic quantum field theory, where standard measurement-based approaches violate locality or fail for delocalized fields.

Method: Interferometric protocol to define the heat characteristic function without projective measurements; perturbative analysis (Dyson series) of detector-field interaction; exact treatment in gapless and delta-switching limits; application of algebraic entropy-balance theorem (Jakšić–Pillet); derivation of first/second laws including external switching work.

Result: Heat statistics are unidirectional Poissonian in vacuum and bidirectional Poissonian for KMS states (up to second order); exact fluctuation theorem emerges in gapless/delta-switching regimes governed by KMS condition; entropic Landauer inequality is verified perturbatively and compared with a complementary bound from full counting statistics; first/second laws are formulated with explicit switching work reconciling energy changes.

Conclusion: A fully relativistic, measurement-free framework for quantum heat statistics is established, unifying fluctuation relations, thermodynamic laws, and Landauer bounds within QFT—demonstrating their origin in field correlations, detector properties, and switching protocols.

Abstract: We introduce an operational framework for heat-exchange statistics between a quantum field and an Unruh-DeWitt detector in Minkowski spacetime. Using an interferometric protocol, we define the characteristic function of heat without projective measurements, consistently with relativistic causality. We derive perturbative expressions for this function and the heat distribution. In the vacuum, the statistics coincides with the low-intensity expansion of a unidirectional Poisson law, while for Kubo-Martin-Schwinger (KMS) states it has a bidirectional Poisson structure up to second order in perturbation theory. We study how fluctuation relations emerge from detector properties and field correlations, identifying regimes where they reduce to known heat-exchange fluctuation relations. We derive the entropic Landauer inequality as a specialization of the algebraic entropy-balance theorem of Jakšić and Pillet and verify it numerically within the perturbative detector-field interaction. We compare its tightness with a complementary Landauer bound derived from the full counting statistics of heat, analyzing their dependence on the detector's initial state and interaction time. We also formulate the first and second laws for the explicitly switched detector-field dynamics, identifying the external switching work that reconciles the field-energy gain with the detector-energy change. In the gapless and delta-switching regimes, where the dynamics admits a non-perturbative treatment, the characteristic function coincides with that of a bidirectional Poisson process, yielding an exact fluctuation theorem determined by the KMS condition. These results provide a consistent framework for heat statistics in relativistic quantum field theory and clarify the emergence of fluctuation relations.

</details>


### [77] [Alternative neural-network follow-up for all-sky FrequencyHough in continuous gravitational-wave searches](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.06211)
*Martina Di Cesare,Pia Astone,Rosario De Rosa,David Keitel,Cristiano Palomba,Marco Serra*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper introduces a neural network classifier integrated into an all-sky continuous gravitational wave search pipeline (FrequencyHough) to improve sensitivity and reduce false alarms without added computational cost, validated on LIGO/Virgo O3 data in the 129–229 Hz band.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: The vast majority of Galactic neutron stars (~10^8–10^9) remain undetected electromagnetically; continuous gravitational wave searches offer a unique probe for these silent sources, but require improved sensitivity and background rejection in all-sky, untargeted analyses.

Method: A neural network classifier is trained on statistically realistic simulated noise mimicking LIGO/Virgo O3 data and applied to FrequencyHough candidates; the method operates post-processing, is detector-parallelizable, and generalizes beyond the training spin-down range.

Result: The NN achieves robust signal-noise discrimination at low strain amplitudes and successfully identifies hardware injections even outside the trained spin-down interval (−2.5×10⁻⁹ to 1.0×10⁻⁹ Hz/s), demonstrating generalization and operational readiness.

Conclusion: Integrating a neural network into the FrequencyHough pipeline enhances detection sensitivity and reliability for all-sky continuous GW searches without increasing computational load—enabling more efficient exploration of the hidden neutron star population.

Abstract: Continuous gravitational waves are long-lived signals emitted by spinning neutron stars (NSs) or boson clouds around black holes. While the Milky Way is expected to host $\mathrm{O}(10^{8}-10^{9})$ NSs, only $\mathrm{O}(10^{3})$ are currently identified through electromagnetic observations. This large observational gap motivates searches based on alternative messengers. In particular, all-sky searches for continuous gravitational waves (GWs) offer a unique opportunity to detect NSs that are electromagnetically silent or otherwise undiscovered. By exploring a broad region of parameter space without any a priori source information, these searches can probe the vast hidden NS population of our Galaxy.
  In this work, we present a novel way to include a Neural Network (NN) classifier into an all-sky search strategy for isolated NSs. Starting from candidates produced by the FrequencyHough pipeline running on data from ground-based detectors such as LIGO and Virgo, the proposed method improves sensitivity without increasing computational cost and can be parallelized across multiple detectors to enhance detection probability and reduce false alarms.
  We analyze data from the third observing run (O3) --- April 1, 2019 to March 27, 2020 --- and focus on the frequency range [129, 229] Hz and spin-down interval [$-2.5\cdot10^{-9}, 1.0\cdot10^{-9}$] Hz/s. The model is trained on noise constructed to be statistically consistent with real O3 data and tested on real O3 data. Results show robust performance in distinguishing signal from noise, even at low strain, and successful identification of hardware injections outside the training spin-down range.

</details>


### [78] [Distinguishing lensing and precessional modulation in binary black-hole inspiral waveforms](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.06347)
*Tien N. Nguyen-Vo,Tamanjyot Singh,Benjamin McKallip,Michael Kesden,Lindsay King*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper investigates whether gravitational-wave (GW) modulation from spin-orbit precession in binary black holes (BBHs) can be confused with modulation from strong gravitational lensing, finding that precession can mimic two-image lensing when time delays are short (<3 interference fringes in detector band); thus, disentangling precession is critical for robust lensed-event identification.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Strong gravitational lensing of BBH GWs is rare but astrophysically valuable; however, spin-induced orbital angular momentum precession produces similar amplitude/phase modulations in GW signals, risking false lensing identification. Distinguishing them is essential for reliable lensing detection.

Method: Analytical and phenomenological modeling of GW waveform modulations from both strong lensing (two-image interference with fixed time delay) and spin-orbit precession (L-precession about J causing amplitude/phase modulations), assessed via single L-shaped detector response and fringe counting in the detector sensitivity band.

Result: Precessional modulation can degenerately mimic two-image lensing modulation when the lensing time delay yields fewer than three interference fringes within the GW signal’s detector-band duration; this degeneracy is most relevant for moderate-redshift sources where lensing is rare but misaligned spins are common.

Conclusion: Robust identification of lensed BBHs requires explicit modeling and exclusion of precessional effects; failure to do so risks false positives, especially in current and near-future GW observing runs where lensing candidates are sought.

Abstract: Binary black holes (BBHs) emit gravitational waves (GWs) as they inspiral towards merger. These GWs can be gravitationally lensed by large-scale structure along the line of sight, potentially creating multiple images of the same source with fixed time delays determined by the lensing geometry. As the BBHs inspiral, the GW frequency increases, leading to successive constructive and destructive interference between the multiple images. BBHs also have spins $\mathbf{S}_i$ that may be misaligned with their orbital angular momentum $\mathbf{L}$. As the BBHs inspiral, these misaligned spins cause $\mathbf{L}$ to precess about the total angular momentum $\mathbf{J}$, modulating the GW emission similar to pulsar emission resulting from a misaligned jet rotating in and out of the line of sight. We investigate the ability of a single L-shaped GW detector to distinguish between these two sources of modulation. We find that precessional modulation can mimic the lensing modulation between two images with comparable magnifications when the time delay between the images is short enough that fewer than three interference fringes occur during the time the GW signal spends in the sensitivity band of the detector. As strong lensing is rare for GW sources at moderate redshift while misaligned spins are common for BBHs produced in certain formation channels, ruling out precessional modulation is essential to identifying genuinely lensed systems.

</details>


### [79] [Cycles Without End: An Ekpyrotic Braneworld Bounce with a Kinetically Coupled Entropic Mechanism](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.06485)
*Rikpratik Sengupta*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper proposes a cyclic cosmological model that resolves three longstanding problems—singular bounces, blue spectral tilt in ekpyrotic contraction, and unbounded cycle growth due to entropy—using a Randall-Sundrum brane with timelike extra dimension, a single scalar field for smooth turnaround/contraction, and a kinetically coupled entropy field to achieve near-scale-invariant perturbations (ns ≈ 0.965), negligible non-Gaussianity (fNL ≈ −0.02), and ultra-suppressed tensor modes (r ∼ 10⁻²⁶); spatial flatness evades Tolman’s entropy bound, requiring ~75 e-folds of dark-energy-driven expansion per cycle to reset entropy.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To construct a viable, non-inflationary cyclic cosmology that simultaneously resolves (i) singularity at the bounce, (ii) the generic blue tilt (ns ≈ 3) of single-field ekpyrotic contraction, and (iii) Tolman’s entropy-driven divergence of cycle length and size.

Method: Uses a Randall-Sundrum brane model with timelike extra dimension to regularize the bounce without violating the null energy condition; employs a single scalar field with a tailored potential to drive thawing dark energy → sign-changing turnaround → BKL-safe ekpyrotic contraction → re-expansion; introduces a minimally coupled entropy field with kinetic coupling to generate scale-invariant curvature perturbations via the Ijjas–Lehners–Steinhardt mechanism; computes spectral index, fNL, r, and entropy dilution requirements analytically and numerically.

Result: Achieves ns = 0.9649 with <1% tuning of kinetic coupling strength; predicts fNL ≈ −0.02 and r ≈ 7.6×10⁻²⁶; fixes brane tension and conversion coupling to physically reasonable values (λ ≈ 5.7×10⁻³, ρc¹ᐟ⁴ = 10¹³ GeV); shows spatial flatness nullifies Tolman’s entropy bound; derives N_DE ≈ 70–80 e-folds of dark-energy expansion required per cycle to dilute entropy to negligible density before next contraction.

Conclusion: A fully explicit, singularity-free, observationally consistent cyclic cosmology is realized—resolving all three classical objections—and provides a compelling alternative to inflation, with falsifiable predictions for primordial non-Gaussianity and tensor modes.

Abstract: Cyclic cosmologies replace inflation's single creation event with an eternal sequence of smooth turnarounds, but three problems have historically stood in their way: singular bounces, the blue tilt generic to single-field ekpyrotic contraction, and Tolman's observation that entropy production should make successive cycles longer and larger without bound. We resolve all three within one explicit model. A Randall-Sundrum brane with a timelike extra dimension regularizes the bounce without violating the null energy condition, and a single scalar field's potential carries the universe from a thawing dark-energy plateau through a sign-changing turnaround into a BKL-safe ekpyrotic contraction and back. We show that no correction internal to the brane's Friedmann equation, and no straight two-field trajectory, can rescue the spectral index from the generic ekpyrotic result $n_s\approx3$. We then build the minimal completion that can: a kinetically coupled entropy field, following the general mechanism of Ijjas, Lehners, and Steinhardt, whose coupling steepness has a closed-form, exact-scale-invariance value for this model's ekpyrotic potential, with only a percent-level offset needed to match the measured tilt $n_s=0.9649$. This fixes the local non-Gaussianity ($f_{\rm NL}\approx-0.02$) and tensor-to-scalar ratio ($r\approx7.6\times10^{-26}$). Matching the observed amplitude fixes the conversion coupling and brane tension to unremarkable values ($λ\approx5.7\times10^{-3}$, $ρ_c^{1/4}=10^{13}\,$GeV). Finally, because the brane construction is spatially flat, Tolman's entropy argument does not bound the number of cycles here: we derive $N_{\rm DE}\sim70$-80 e-folds of accelerated expansion needed each cycle to dilute the entropy produced back to a negligible density before the next contraction.

</details>


### [80] [Semilinear wave equations on the Witten bubble spacetime](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.06609)
*Onyx Gautam*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper establishes small-data global existence and improved decay for nonlinear wave equations on the Witten bubble spacetime — a 5D vacuum solution modeling semiclassical instability of Kaluza–Klein spacetime — using novel linear wave estimates and a U(1)-adapted null condition.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To study nonlinear wave dynamics on the Witten bubble spacetime, introduced by Witten to model the semiclassical instability of the classically stable Kaluza–Klein vacuum R^{3+1} × S^1, thereby bridging physics-motivated geometry and mathematical PDE analysis.

Method: Develops novel energy and pointwise estimates for the linear wave equation on the Witten bubble background; combines them with vector field methods and a U(1)-equivariant adaptation of the null condition to handle semilinear nonlinearities without full symmetry assumptions.

Result: Proves small-data global existence for general (non-symmetric) solutions to a class of semilinear wave equations; obtains improved decay rates for U(1)-symmetric solutions satisfying a modified null condition.

Conclusion: The Witten bubble spacetime supports global well-posedness for nonlinear waves under small data, with symmetry enhancing decay — confirming its suitability as a geometric testing ground for wave dynamics in unstable higher-dimensional vacua.

Abstract: We initiate the study of nonlinear wave equations on the Witten bubble spacetime (also known as the "bubble of nothing"), which is an $\mathrm{SO}(3,1)\times \mathrm{U}(1)$-symmetric solution to the Einstein vacuum equations in $(4 + 1)$ dimensions. This spacetime was introduced by Witten to model the semiclassical instability of the Kaluza--Klein spacetime $\mathbf{R}^{3+1}\times S^1$ (which is classically stable by work of Huneau--Stingo--Wyatt (arXiv:2307.15267)). We prove a small-data global existence result without symmetry assumptions and an improved decay result for $\mathrm{U}(1)$-symmetric solutions to a class of semilinear equations satisfying a version of the null condition. Key to the proof are novel estimates for the linear wave equation, which has been studied in the physics literature by Bhawal and Viveshwara and in the mathematics literature by Bachelot (arXiv:1601.03682).

</details>


### [81] [Shadows and Thin-Disk Images of Kerr-Newman Black Holes in a Bertotti-Robinson Magnetic Field](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.06648)
*Xi Wan,Haiyu Wang,Zhenyu Zhang,Zelin Zhang,Bin Chen*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper studies optical properties of Kerr-Newman-Bertotti-Robinson (KN-BR) black holes using null geodesic separability, critical curve construction for finite-distance observers, and backward ray tracing of thin accretion disks; it reveals charge- and magnetic-field-dependent image scaling behaviors and inclination-dependent shadow morphology.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To theoretically characterize the optical signatures of exotic magnetized KN-BR black holes—non-asymptotically flat spacetimes—for potential identification in future observations.

Method: Exploits separability of null geodesics to compute unstable spherical photon orbits and photon shell extent; constructs critical curves on finite-distance zero-angular-momentum observer screens; performs backward ray tracing for geometrically thin, optically thin disks extending from outer region to event horizon; analyzes images, intensity profiles, critical-curve areas, and inner-shadow areas.

Result: Neutral Kerr-BR₀ and specially charged Kerr-BRₛ configurations exhibit nearly identical optical appearances; electric charge decreases image size in Kerr-Newman limit but increases it in magnetized KN-BR configurations; external magnetic field strongly enlarges apparent image scale; observer inclination affects inner-shadow area more than critical-curve area.

Conclusion: KN-BR black holes display distinct, charge- and magnetization-dependent optical features—particularly counterintuitive charge effects in magnetized regimes—that provide testable theoretical predictions for distinguishing such exotic black holes observationally.

Abstract: In this paper, we investigate the optical properties of Kerr-Newman-Bertotti-Robinson (KN-BR) black holes. We use the separability of null geodesics to analyze unstable spherical photon orbits and determine the radial extent of the photon shell. Because the spacetime is not asymptotically flat, we construct the critical curve on the screen of a finite-distance zero-angular-momentum observer. We then perform backward ray tracing for a geometrically thin and optically thin disk that extends from the outer region to the event horizon, and examine the resulting images, intensity profiles, critical-curve areas, and inner-shadow areas. We find that the genuine neutral Kerr-BR$_0$ and specially charged Kerr-BR$_s$ configurations have nearly identical optical appearances. It is remarkable that for the KN-BR black holes increasing the electric charge reduces the characteristic image size in the Kerr-Newman limit but enlarges it in the magnetized configurations considered here. We also find that the external magnetic field strongly increases the apparent image scale, while the observer inclination affects the inner-shadow area more significantly than the critical-curve area. These results may provide useful theoretical insight for future observations aimed at identifying such exotic magnetized black holes.

</details>


### [82] [Physics-based phenomenological modeling of binary black hole hierarchical formation 2: Autodifferentiable functional inference of hierarchical compact-binary populations](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.06728)
*R. O'Shaughnessy,M. Zeeshan,M. Qazalbash*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper introduces an autodifferentiable, physics-based phenomenological model—integrated into the gwkokab Poisson-likelihood framework—to jointly infer natal black hole population and dynamical interaction parameters from gravitational-wave (GW) merger data (e.g., GWTC-5.0), revealing limitations of simple pairwise coagulation models in explaining high-mass, comparable-mass mergers.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To connect observed mass-spin structure in the GW census to formation physics via interpretable, tractable population models that jointly constrain natal distributions and dense-environment interaction mechanisms.

Method: Develops an autodifferentiable phenomenological model where each dense stellar environment is characterized by a 'coagulation response'; aggregates contributions from multiple such environments into an observable merger-rate density; embeds the model within the gwkokab Poisson-likelihood framework for hierarchical Bayesian inference on GWTC-5.0.

Result: The framework demonstrates that simple pairwise coagulation models fail to reproduce the observed high-mass, comparable-mass merger population; it quantitatively tests alternative interaction topologies while preserving explicit modeling of the natal BH population.

Conclusion: Physics-informed, differentiable coagulation-based population models—when embedded in robust likelihood frameworks—enable joint, interpretable inference of both natal and dynamical formation channels from GW data, advancing beyond phenomenological or purely simulation-driven approaches.

Abstract: The gravitational-wave (GW) census contains mass and spin structure consistent with contributions from black holes assembled through repeated mergers in dense environments. Connecting that structure to formation physics requires models that are both physically interpretable and tractable within population inference. We construct an autodifferentiable, physics-based phenomenological model in which each dense environment is represented by a coagulation response and a population of such environments produces an observable merger-rate density. Embedded in the gwkokab Poisson-likelihood framework, this model enables joint inference of natal-population and interaction parameters from the GW census. Applied to GWTC-5.0, the framework shows why simple pairwise coagulation models struggle to reproduce the observed high-mass, comparable-mass population and tests alternative interaction structures against the data, while retaining an explicitly modeled natal component.

</details>


### [83] [Dissection of a merger-ringdown waveform in the small-mass-ratio limit](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.06861)
*Patrick Bourg,Lorenzo Küchler,Benjamin Leather,Kevin Cunningham,Jack Lewis,Adam Pound,Béatrice Bonga,Marc Casals,Rodrigo Panosso Macedo*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper develops a framework to analyze nonlinear features in black hole binary merger-ringdown waveforms in the small-mass-ratio limit, identifying distinct inspiral, merger, and ringdown phases; it shows quadratic quasinormal modes dominate over linear overtones in ringdown for comparable-to-intermediate mass ratios and reveals cubic memory effects analogous to horizon absorption.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To isolate and understand individual nonlinear effects—such as quasinormal modes, tails, transients, memory—in black hole binary mergers and ringdowns, especially where traditional perturbative or numerical methods obscure their contributions.

Method: Analytical framework in the small-mass-ratio limit, specializing to quasicircular, nonspinning black hole binaries; decomposition of the waveform into physical phases and systematic identification of nonlinear mode couplings (e.g., quadratic and cubic QNMs) and memory contributions.

Result: Waveform cleanly separates into extended inspiral, ~0.5-cycle merger, and QNM-dominated ringdown; quadratic QNMs dominate linear overtones in ringdown for comparable-to-intermediate mass ratios; gravitational-wave memory exhibits cubic wave-zone effects structurally analogous to horizon absorption.

Conclusion: Nonlinearities—particularly mode coupling and memory—are essential and quantitatively dominant in the merger-ringdown transition; the small-mass-ratio framework enables clean dissection of these effects, bridging extreme- and intermediate-mass-ratio regimes.

Abstract: Work over the past two decades has unveiled the rich phenomenology of black hole binary mergers and subsequent ringdowns, involving a tapestry of quasinormal modes (QNMs), nonlinearities, tails, transients, and secular effects including gravitational memory. Here we develop a framework for analyzing nonlinear merger-ringdown features in the small-mass-ratio limit, where individual effects can be cleanly isolated. Specializing to the case of a quasicircular, nonspinning black hole binary, we find the waveform sharply divides into a pre-merger extended inspiral phase, a merger phase lasting roughly half a cycle, and a post-merger ringdown dominated by QNMs. We show quadratic QNMs dominate over linear overtones in the ringdown phase for comparable-to-intermediate mass ratios, and we highlight nonlinear effects of gravitational-wave memory, including cubic wave-zone phenomena analogous to horizon absorption effects.

</details>


### [84] [The Dynamical Instability of Rotating Boson Stars](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.06954)
*Kai-Dong Zhou,Shou-Shan Bao,Jian Deng,Hong Zhang*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper studies dynamical instability in rotating boson stars using Gross–Pitaevskii–Poisson equations; identifies quasiperiodic ring/twin-star oscillations via 3D simulations; develops linear stability analysis and a three-mode Hamiltonian to explain the instability mechanism and quantify lifetime extension by repulsive self-interactions.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand the nonlinear dynamical instability of rotating boson stars—astrophysical candidates for dark matter halos or compact objects—and assess how contact self-interactions affect their structural stability and longevity.

Method: Three-dimensional numerical simulations of the Gross–Pitaevskii–Poisson system; systematic linear stability analysis to isolate unstable modes; derivation and application of a reduced three-mode Hamiltonian for early nonlinear dynamics.

Result: Rotating boson stars exhibit quasiperiodic transitions between ring-like and twin-star-like configurations; specific unstable modes are identified; repulsive self-interactions suppress instability growth, extending lifetimes; the three-mode Hamiltonian quantitatively reproduces simulation-observed quasiperiodicity.

Conclusion: The instability is mode-coupled and analytically tractable via a low-dimensional Hamiltonian framework; repulsive interactions enhance stability, suggesting observational signatures and implications for bosonic dark matter models.

Abstract: We investigate the dynamical instability of rotating boson stars described by the Gross--Pitaevskii--Poisson equations with contact self-interactions. Through three-dimensional simulations, we confirm that the rotating boson star undergoes a quasiperiodic conversion between ring-like and twin-star-like density configurations in the early nonlinear stage. We develop a systematic linear stability analysis to identify the modes driving this instability and show that repulsive self-interactions could significantly increase the lifetime of the rotating boson stars. We further construct a three-mode Hamiltonian to describe the early nonlinear stage, which explains the quasiperiodic conversion. This analytical framework agrees reasonably well with the simulation results and provides a clear picture to understand the dynamics of rotating boson stars.

</details>


### [85] [A New Window for Testing the PPN in the Strong-Field Regime: Stellar Rotation of S301](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.06977)
*Mohsen Khodadi,Salvatore Capozziello*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper proposes using the newly discovered S301 star—whose highly eccentric, close-pericenter orbit around Sgr A* enables strong-field gravity tests—to constrain PPN parameters γ and β via two independent relativistic effects: stellar spin precession (sensitive to γ) and orbital pericenter advance (sensitive to combination of β and γ). Joint MCMC analysis of S2 and S301 yields σ_β ≈ 2.3×10⁻⁴ and σ_γ ≈ 0.1, with spin precession probing gravity at φ/c² ∼ 10⁻⁴ and v/c ∼ 0.08—four orders stronger than Solar System tests.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To break the β–γ degeneracy in parametrized post-Newtonian (PPN) tests of gravity using complementary relativistic observables from stars orbiting Sgr A*, especially leveraging S301’s extreme orbit for stronger-field probes beyond current S-star limits.

Method: Analytical modeling of spin precession rate scaling as (2γ_PPN + 1)/3 relative to GR; orbital pericenter advance scaling as (2β_PPN + 2γ_PPN − 1)/3; joint Bayesian (MCMC) inference on simulated high-precision astrometric (S2, S301) and spectroscopic (S301 spin-induced velocity shifts) data.

Result: Projected constraints: σ_γ ≈ 0.1 from spin precession (via ~46.1 km/s velocity shifts), σ_β ≈ 2.3×10⁻⁴ from combined orbital + spin analysis; S301’s spin precession probes gravity at gravitational potential φ/c² ∼ 10⁻⁴ and velocity v/c ∼ 0.08—four orders stronger than Solar System tests and one order stronger than current S-star constraints.

Conclusion: Stellar spin precession in high-eccentricity S-stars like S301 provides a novel, independent, and complementary probe of PPN parameter γ in the strong-field regime; when combined with orbital precession, it breaks the β–γ degeneracy and significantly extends the domain of empirical PPN testing toward the strong-field limit near Sgr A*.

Abstract: The recently discovered S301 star, with an orbital period of 8.7 years and eccentricity $e = 0.982$, reaches a pericenter of just $140\,R_{\mathrm{s}}$ --- approximately nine times closer than S2's $\sim 1200R_{\mathrm{s}}$ --- offering an unprecedented laboratory for testing strong-field gravity. We demonstrate that S301's stellar spin precession provides a novel probe of the PPN parameter $γ$, scaling as $(2γ_{\mathrm{PPN}} + 1)/3$ relative to general relativity (GR). For maximum projected velocity shifts of $46.1~\mathrm{km\,s^{-1}}$, next-generation spectrographs could constrain $γ_{\mathrm{PPN}}$ to $\sim 10\%$ precision. When combined with S301's orbital precession --- a substantial $\sim 1.95^\circ$ pericenter advance per orbit (GR prediction), approximately an order of magnitude larger than S2's $\sim 0.2^\circ$, constraining $(2β_{\mathrm{PPN}} + 2γ_{\mathrm{PPN}} - 1)/3$ --- the spin measurement breaks the $β$-$γ$ degeneracy. A MCMC analysis of projected S2 and S301 observations yields $σ_β\approx 2.3 \times 10^{-4}$, with the $γ$ precision limited by the spin precession measurement to $σ_γ\approx 0.1$. While the spin precession provides an independent consistency check, its true significance lies in probing gravity at $φ/c^2 \sim 10^{-4}$ and $v/c \sim 0.08$ --- four orders of magnitude stronger than Solar System tests and an order of magnitude stronger than current S-star constraints. This complementary approach opens a new window for testing the PPN framework in the strong-field regime and demonstrates the power of combining multiple stellar probes at the Galactic Center.

</details>


### [86] [The (in)stability on total transmission modes with small bumps](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.07167)
*Xi-Ning Dong,Liang-Bi Wu,Yu-Sen Zhou,Zong-Kuan Guo*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper studies spectral stability of total transmission modes (TTMs) in Tangherlini black holes under localized Pöschl-Teller potential perturbations, using a modified Chebyshev-Lobatto grid for improved numerical accuracy; results show strong stability for purely imaginary TTMs but high sensitivity for complex TTMs.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: TTMs are reflectionless solutions closely related to QNMs and known to be highly sensitive to weak environmental perturbations; understanding their spectral stability is essential for assessing robustness in gravitational wave modeling and black hole spectroscopy.

Method: Introduces a localized Pöschl-Teller bump perturbation into the effective potential of Tangherlini black holes and employs a modified Chebyshev-Lobatto spectral grid to enhance numerical resolution and accuracy in computing TTM eigenvalues.

Result: For d=14, ℓ=2, s=2: the purely imaginary TTM remains stable under small perturbations, while genuine complex TTMs exhibit large eigenvalue migrations even for weak perturbations—consistent with prior pseudospectrum analysis.

Conclusion: TTM spectral stability is mode-dependent: purely imaginary TTMs are robust, whereas complex TTMs are inherently unstable under localized potential deformations, implying caution in interpreting them as physical observables in realistic (non-idealized) spacetimes.

Abstract: Total transmission modes (TTMs) are a class of reflectionless solutions to black hole perturbation equations, closely related to quasinormal modes (QNMs), and can exhibit significant sensitivity to weak environmental perturbations. In this work, we investigate the spectrum (in)stability of TTMs of Tangherlini black holes by introducing a localized Pöschl-Teller bump perturbation into the effective potential, and employ a modified Chebyshev-Lobatto grid to improve the numerical accuracy of the localized perturbation. For $d=14$, $\ell=2$, and $s=2$, the purely imaginary TTM exhibits relatively strong spectrum stability, whereas the genuine complex TTMs undergo significant migrations even for small perturbations, consistent with the spectrum stability revealed by previous pseudospectrum analyses.

</details>


### [87] [Thin accretion-disc transfer functions in the q-metric spacetime: quadrupolar lensing signatures](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.07259)
*Saken Toktarbay,Manas Khassanov,Aray Muratkhan,Zhantay Muratkhan,Aliya Taukenova,Hernando Quevedo*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper investigates thin equatorial accretion disc images in the q-metric spacetime (a static, axisymmetric deformation of Schwarzschild) using backward ray tracing; it computes transfer functions up to third photon crossing, analyzes how disc image morphology—especially brightness distribution width and centroid—varies with quadrupole deformation parameter q, and finds modest (<2.4%) integrated flux changes but significant (8.8–14.5%) structural shifts when scaled to physical monopole mass ℳ.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To quantify how deviations from Schwarzschild geometry—specifically, static axisymmetric quadrupole deformations encoded in the q-metric—affect observable features of thin accretion disc images, especially for nearly face-on observers, in anticipation of high-resolution black hole imaging tests of general relativity.

Method: Backward ray tracing in the q-metric spacetime; derivation and numerical evaluation of equatorial transfer functions r_m(b;q) for first three photon crossings; construction of direct disc images using prescribed power-law emissivity and relativistic Doppler/gravitational redshift weighting; analysis of brightness distribution statistics (median position, interquartile width) as functions of q.

Result: The third-crossing transfer function branch center b_{3,c} shifts significantly with q in coordinate units (from ~3.716M to 6.702M), but much of this reflects scaling by the physical monopole mass ℳ = (1+q)M—when normalized to ℳ, it decreases only mildly (from ~5.309ℳ to 5.156ℳ); at fixed ℳ, disc contours expand with q, especially near the inner edge; integrated flux varies by ≤2.4% across q ∈ [−0.3, 0.3], while median-flux position shifts +8.8% horizontally and interquartile width broadens +14.5%.

Conclusion: For geometrically thin, equatorially confined discs viewed nearly face-on, the q-metric’s quadrupole deformation primarily manifests as a rigid displacement and broadening of the observed brightness distribution—not substantial flux alteration—indicating that image morphology (rather than total flux) is the more sensitive probe of spacetime multipole structure in such configurations.

Abstract: We study geometrically thin equatorial disc images in the static, axisymmetric q-metric spacetime using backward ray tracing. The Schwarzschild solution is recovered at $q=0$; for the nonzero deformations considered here, $r=2M$ is a curvature singularity rather than an event horizon. For a distant nearly face-on observer, we construct the first three equatorial transfer functions $r_m(b;q)$. In the {Schwarzschild} limit, the resolved third-crossing interval contains the critical impact parameter $b=3\sqrt{3}M$. As $q$ increases from $-0.3$ to $0.3$, the branch center shifts from $b_{3,c}/M\simeq3.716$ to $6.702$. In units of the physical monopole mass $\mathcal M=(1+q)M$, it instead decreases from $b_{3,c}/\mathcal M\simeq5.309$ to $5.156$. Much of the displacement in metric-parameter units therefore reflects the changing monopole scale. At fixed $\mathcal M$, the inclined first-intersection maps show that the contour dimensions increase with $q$, with larger relative changes near the inner disc. A prescribed power-law emissivity and relativistic frequency-shift weighting yield direct images whose integrated flux differs from the Schwarzschild value by at most $2.4\%$. Between the endpoint models, the horizontal median-flux position increases by $8.8\%$ and the interquartile width by $14.5\%$. Within this emission prescription, the residual deformation dependence appears mainly as a displacement and broadening of the brightness distribution. Higher-order radiative contributions are not included.

</details>


### [88] [Darboux Isospectrality Constraints on Quasinormal Modes Deformed by Bumps](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.07323)
*Zhen-Xiao Zhang,Chen Lan,Yan-Gang Miao*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper identifies an inconsistency in using additive Gaussian or Pöschl-Teller bumps to probe Schwarzschild quasinormal modes, due to violation of the Darboux transformation linking axial and polar sectors; it classifies admissible bump pairs and proposes two consistent prescriptions—Darboux generator and Riccati completion—to eliminate spurious axial-polar mode splitting while preserving centroid shifts.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To resolve the inconsistency between commonly used additive localized bumps (e.g., Gaussian, Pöschl-Teller) and the fundamental Darboux symmetry linking Regge-Wheeler (axial) and Zerilli (polar) potentials in Schwarzschild perturbation theory.

Method: Analytical derivation of constraints imposed by the Darboux transformation on parity-blind perturbations; classification of admissible axial-polar bump pairs; formulation of two consistent prescriptions—(i) Darboux generator (where the Gaussian/PT profile serves as a Darboux seed, not the bump itself), and (ii) Riccati completion (where one bump is prescribed and its partner is solved via the Riccati equation); frequency-domain numerical validation using quasinormal mode spectra.

Result: Additive Gaussian/PT bumps generically violate Darboux consistency and induce finite, unphysical axial-polar quasinormal mode splitting; only bumps with $r^{-2}$ asymptotics are Darboux-admissible; the proposed prescriptions eliminate this splitting while leaving the fundamental mode’s centroid shift nearly unchanged.

Conclusion: The standard additive bump prescription is fundamentally incompatible with the underlying symmetry of the Schwarzschild perturbation problem; physical interpretations of mode sensitivity require Darboux-consistent constructions, and the two proposed prescriptions provide rigorous, symmetry-respecting alternatives.

Abstract: A localized Gaussian or Pöschl-Teller bump is widely used to probe the sensitivity of Schwarzschild quasinormal modes, where it is typically added to both the Regge-Wheeler and Zerilli potentials. We show that such an additive prescription is generically incompatible with the Darboux transformation connecting the two parity sectors. The reason is that such a transformation restricts a parity-blind bump to a fixed family of bumps with an unavoidable $r^{-2}$ tail, excluding any Gaussian or Pöschl-Teller profile with arbitrary amplitude, center, or width in the additive prescription. We give the complete classification of Darboux-admissible axial and polar pairs of bumps and propose two consistent scenarios: Darboux generator prescription in which a Gaussian or Pöschl-Teller profile is retained as a generator of the bump pair rather than as the bump itself, and Riccati completion prescription in which a Gaussian or Pöschl-Teller profile is regarded as one of the bump pair and its partner will be solved from the Riccati equation. Our frequency-domain calculations confirm that a finite and spurious axial-polar splitting of quasinormal modes will be produced if the Darboux-consistent scenarios are absent, while the centroid shift of the fundamental mode is nearly unchanged.

</details>


### [89] [Static spherically symmetric electroweak models with fermions and gravity](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.08667)
*Marko Sobak*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper systematically reduces the Einstein-Yang-Mills-Higgs-Dirac-Yukawa system with SU(2)×U(1) gauge group to static spherical symmetry, derives the most general ansatz, and provides an open-source C++ GUI solver for the resulting ODEs.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To enable rigorous study of gravitating non-Abelian gauge-Higgs-fermion systems analogous to the lepton sector of the Standard Model, requiring a controlled symmetry reduction to tractable ODEs.

Method: Systematic Lie-theoretic reduction to static spherical symmetry; derivation of the most general invariant ansatz for the SU(2)×U(1) gauge potential, Higgs doublet, and Dirac spinor coupled to gravity; formulation of the full field equations as a boundary-value problem for ODEs.

Result: Complete set of reduced, static spherically symmetric field equations (Einstein-Yang-Mills-Higgs-Dirac-Yukawa) in terms of invariant functions; implementation of an open-source, interactive C++ GUI solver for numerical exploration.

Conclusion: The reduction yields a well-defined, computationally accessible ODE system capturing essential features of electroweak-scale physics in strong gravitational fields; the provided solver enables systematic investigation of global solutions and their physical interpretation.

Abstract: In this article we provide a systematic reduction to static spherical symmetry for (classical) gauge theoretic models with structure group SU(2) x U(1), coupled to gravity. In particular, we derive the most general static spherically symmetric ansatz for the Einstein-Yang- Mills-Higgs-Dirac-Yukawa equation system. This model is reminiscent of the lepton sector of the Standard Model coupled to gravity. The resulting total system of ordinary differential equations is rather large, so that finding global solutions is non-trivial. For this reason, an open-source interactive solver GUI written in C++ is provided together with the article.

</details>


### [90] [Exact Cancellation of Horizon Modes in Kerr Ringdown](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.07326)
*Kei-ichiro Kubota,Yusuke Manita,Hayato Motohashi,Daiki Watarai*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper refutes the interpretation of oscillatory features in QNM-filtered ringdown waveforms as near-horizon 'direct waves', showing via exact analysis and numerical confirmation that source-induced horizon-mode poles are fully canceled by Matsubara zeros, eliminating such signals at late times.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To resolve conflicting interpretations of oscillatory features in quasinormal mode (QNM)-filtered gravitational waveforms—specifically, whether they represent a near-horizon 'direct wave'—by rigorously testing the validity of the saddle-point approximation near the black hole horizon.

Method: Exact analytic treatment of horizon-mode pole structure for a particle plunging into a Kerr black hole; identification of cancellation between source-induced horizon-mode poles and an infinite tower of Matsubara zeros; validation via time-domain numerical integration of the first four stages of sequential screening.

Result: Every source-induced horizon-mode pole is exactly canceled by an infinite tower of Matsubara zeros, independent of orbital or black-hole parameters; this cancellation is numerically confirmed for the first four stages; the saddle-point approximation fails near the horizon, invalidating the 'direct wave' interpretation.

Conclusion: Within the near-horizon perturbative framework, source-induced horizon-mode signals do not survive at late times; thus, oscillatory features in QNM-filtered ringdowns cannot be attributed to a near-horizon direct wave.

Abstract: Ringdown gravitational waves probe black hole spacetimes via quasinormal modes (QNMs). Recent studies have reported oscillatory features in QNM-filtered waveforms, and have led to conflicting interpretations as to whether they represent a near-horizon "direct wave". Reexamining a particle plunging into a Kerr black hole, we show that the saddle-point approximation underlying the proposed interpretation fails near the horizon. Our exact analysis reveals that every source-induced horizon-mode pole is canceled by an infinite tower of Matsubara zeros, independently of orbital and black-hole parameters. The first four stages of this sequential screening are also confirmed by time-domain numerical integration. Thus, within the near-horizon perturbative framework, source-induced horizon-mode signals do not survive at late times.

</details>


### [91] [Black Holes Thermodynamic Topology in Sharma-Mittal Statistics](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.07354)
*Issam Najma,Yahya Ladghami,Amine Bouali,Taoufik Ouali*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper investigates black hole thermodynamic topology using Sharma-Mittal entropy (with parameters δ and R), revealing dimension-independent topological classification for d > 4 via Duan’s Φ-mapping and winding number W, while highlighting deviations from Boltzmann-Gibbs statistics.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand how generalized non-extensive statistics—specifically Sharma-Mittal entropy—affect the thermodynamic topology and stability of black holes beyond standard Boltzmann-Gibbs assumptions.

Method: Applies Sharma-Mittal entropy with two deformation parameters (δ, R) to various black hole solutions (Schwarzschild, Reissner-Nordström, d-dimensional charged/uncharged); employs Duan's Φ-mapping topological theory to compute winding numbers W and classify thermodynamic phases.

Result: Thermodynamic topological classification via winding number W is independent of spacetime dimension for d > 4; however, d > 4 black holes exhibit qualitatively distinct thermodynamic topology compared to their 4D counterparts under Sharma-Mittal statistics.

Conclusion: Sharma-Mittal entropy induces robust topological categorization of black holes, where thermodynamic phase structure is governed by universal topological invariants (W), insensitive to dimensionality above four, yet sensitive to the underlying statistical framework.

Abstract: In this study, we explore the thermodynamic topology of black holes within the Sharma-Mittal entropy framework. Our study covers several black hole solutions, including charged and uncharged black holes in $d$-dimensional, as well as Schwarzschild and Reissner-Nordström black holes. By introducing the Sharma-Mittal entropy characterized by two parameters $δ$ and $R$, we explore how deviations from the standard Boltzmann-Gibbs statistics modify the thermodynamic structure and stability properties of these systems. Using Duan's $Φ$-mapping theory, we compute the corresponding topological numbers and classify black holes into distinct topological categories according to the winding number $W$. Moreover, our analysis shows that, within the Sharma-Mittal formalism, the topological classification is independent of spacetime dimension in the case $d>4$, although dimensions higher than four exhibit distinct features compared to the four-dimensional Schwarzschild and Reissner-Nordström black holes, reveal different behavior of thermodynamic topology under generalized statistics.

</details>


### [92] [Binary Black Holes in Einstein-Maxwell-Dilaton Theory: Second Post-Newtonian Dynamics from Effective Field Theory](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.07413)
*Pawan Kumar Gupta*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper derives the conservative two-body Lagrangian for charged black-hole binaries in Einstein-Maxwell-Dilaton theory up to second post-Newtonian order using effective field theory and Kaluza-Klein decomposition, extending prior 1PN results and enabling improved gravitational-wave waveform modeling and tests of alternative gravity.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To enable high-precision tests of Einstein-Maxwell-Dilaton (EMd) theory—where black holes carry electric and dilatonic charges—using gravitational-wave observations from compact binary coalescences, particularly in the strong-field regime.

Method: Effective field theory framework combined with temporal Kaluza-Klein decomposition of the metric into non-relativistic gravitational fields; systematic derivation of the conservative two-body Lagrangian for EMd black-hole binaries through second post-Newtonian (2PN) order.

Result: Complete conservative two-body Lagrangian for EMd black-hole binaries at 2PN order, including gravitational, electromagnetic, and dilaton interactions; verified against Einstein-Maxwell, scalar-tensor, and general relativity limits, and validated via test-body-limit check of the static 2PN sector.

Conclusion: The derived Lagrangian provides the essential conservative dynamics required to construct higher-accuracy gravitational-wave templates for testing EMd theory with current and future LIGO/Virgo/KAGRA and LISA observations.

Abstract: The detection of gravitational waves from compact binary coalescences provides a powerful opportunity to test general relativity in the strong-field regime and to search for signatures of alternative theories of gravity. In this work, we consider Einstein-Maxwell-Dilaton (EMd) theory, in which black holes can carry both electric and scalar (dilatonic) charges. We employ the effective field theory approach, together with a temporal Kaluza-Klein decomposition of the metric in terms of non-relativistic gravitational fields, to derive the conservative two-body Lagrangian for charged black-hole binaries in EMd theory through second post-Newtonian (PN) order. Our calculation extends the previously known conservative dynamics at 1PN order and includes the gravitational, electromagnetic, and dilaton interactions at 2PN order. We verify the result in the appropriate Einstein-Maxwell, scalar-tensor, and general relativistic limits, and perform an independent test-body-limit check of the static 2PN sector. These results provide the conservative dynamics needed for developing higher-accuracy waveform models and testing EMd theory with gravitational-wave observations.

</details>


### [93] [Thermodynamics and phase transitions of spherically symmetric AdS regular black holes](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.07491)
*Qi-Quan Li,Yu Zhang,Hoernisa Iminniyaz*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper derives thermodynamic properties of a class of spherically symmetric AdS regular black holes—termed 'mother' black holes—by coupling Einstein gravity with a cosmological constant and a nonlinear electromagnetic field. It emphasizes correct ordering (constraint before differentiation) in computing response functions like heat capacity, revealing novel 8-shaped and C-shaped Gibbs free energy–temperature diagrams signaling zeroth- and first-order phase transitions, richer than standard RN-AdS black holes.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To systematically derive and clarify the thermodynamics of regular black holes arising from Einstein gravity coupled to a nonlinear electromagnetic field and cosmological constant, resolving ambiguities in constraint handling for response functions and uncovering new phase structures beyond the RN-AdS paradigm.

Method: Solve the full coupled gravitational–electromagnetic field equations to obtain the singular 'mother' black hole solution; derive thermodynamic quantities in the unconstrained phase space (S, q, α, P); impose the regularity condition M = q³/α to obtain self-consistent thermodynamics for regular black holes; distinguish fundamental conjugate variables (from first law) from response functions (e.g., heat capacity, involving second derivatives); rigorously compare derivative-before-constraint vs. constraint-before-derivative procedures.

Result: Demonstrates that imposing the regularity condition before computing second-order response functions is mandatory; reveals non-swallowtail G–T phase diagrams: 8-shaped for P < P_z (first-order transition) and C-shaped for P_z < P < P_c (zeroth-order transition) between small and large black hole phases.

Conclusion: The regularity condition fundamentally alters the thermodynamic phase structure, introducing qualitatively new zeroth- and first-order phase transitions absent in RN-AdS black holes, thereby enriching the landscape of black hole thermodynamics in AdS space.

Abstract: We obtain the singular ''mother'' black hole solution by solving the coupling between Einstein gravity with a cosmological constant and a nonlinear electromagnetic field. The thermodynamic quantities of the ''mother'' black hole are first derived in the unconstrained phase space $(S, q, α, P)$, after which the regularity condition $M = q^3/α$ is imposed to obtain the thermodynamic quantities of a class of spherically symmetric AdS regular black holes self-consistently. We distinguish two categories of thermodynamic quantities: fundamental conjugate variables defined by the first law, and thermodynamic response functions constructed from them. For response functions involving second-order derivatives -- in particular the heat capacity -- we show that the order of constraint imposition and derivative calculation cannot be interchanged, and the correct procedure is to first impose the constraint and then construct the response function. Within this framework, we find that when $P<P_c$, the $G$--$T$ diagram exhibits a behavior qualitatively different from the swallowtail behavior of the standard RN-AdS black hole. Instead, it exhibits an 8-shaped structure for $P<P_z$ and a C-shaped structure for $P_z<P<P_c$, corresponding to first-order and zeroth-order phase transitions between small and large black hole phases, respectively. Imposing the regularity condition thus leads to a richer phase structure for this class of black holes than the standard RN-AdS case.

</details>


### [94] [Globally regular charged black holes in non-polynomial quasi-topological gravity with Born-Infeld electrodynamics](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.07576)
*Hong-Lin Liu,Zhong-Wen Feng,Qing-Quan Jiang,Xia Zhou,Xue-Ling Mu*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper constructs exact static, spherically symmetric charged black hole solutions in 4D non-polynomial quasi-topological gravity coupled to Born-Infeld electrodynamics; it shows that Born-Infeld nonlinearity can resolve a finite-radius curvature singularity present in the vacuum solution, yielding globally regular spacetimes—including regular black holes (RBHs) with novel horizon structure (e.g., triple-degenerate inner horizon and nonzero outer surface gravity).


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To resolve the finite-radius curvature singularity inherent in the vacuum branch of a specific non-polynomial quasi-topological gravity model (h(p) = p/(1 + ℓ²p)), and to investigate whether nonlinear electrodynamics—specifically Born-Infeld—can restore global regularity in charged extensions.

Method: Analytic construction of exact static, spherically symmetric solutions to the field equations of four-dimensional non-polynomial quasi-topological gravity minimally coupled to Born-Infeld electrodynamics; systematic analysis of curvature invariants, horizon structure, surface gravities (κ₊, κ₋), and parameter-space regions supporting regularity.

Result: Born-Infeld electrodynamics eliminates the vacuum’s finite-radius singularity over a finite region of parameter space, producing globally regular geometries: asymptotically flat exteriors matched to finite-curvature AdS-type cores; both horizonless stars and regular black holes (RBHs) exist, separated by a degenerate-horizon boundary; a continuous family of RBHs with triple-degenerate inner horizon (κ₋ = 0) and simple outer horizon (κ₊ ≠ 0) is identified.

Conclusion: Nonlinear electrodynamics can actively improve gravitational regularity—here, curing a vacuum singularity rather than inducing one—enabling new classes of physically viable RBHs with rich internal horizon structure, challenging the notion that charge generically disrupts regularity.

Abstract: We construct exact static, spherically symmetric charged solutions in four-dimensional non-polynomial quasi-topological gravity coupled to Born-Infeld electrodynamics. We focus on the model $h(p)=p/(1+\ell^{2}p)$, whose vacuum branch develops a curvature singularity at a finite radius. We show that Born-Infeld nonlinearities can remove this singularity within a finite region of parameter space, yielding globally regular geometries with an asymptotically flat exterior and a finite-curvature AdS-type core. The regular sector contains both horizonless configurations and RBHs, separated by a degenerate-horizon boundary. We further identify a continuous branch of regular black holes with a triple-degenerate inner horizon and a simple outer event horizon, satisfying $κ_-=0$ and $κ_+\neq0$. This provides a converse example to cases in which introducing charge spoils the regularity of a vacuum regular black hole. In the present model, Born-Infeld electrodynamics instead removes the finite-radius singularity of a vacuum-singular gravitational branch while supporting globally regular charged geometries, including RBHs with nontrivial inner-horizon structure.

</details>


### [95] [Global structure and stability of Kerr-Bertotti-Robinson spacetime](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.07602)
*Yu-Sen Zhou,Liang-Bi Wu,Ming-Fei Ji,Wen-Tao Fu,Li-Ming Cao,Rong-Gen Cai*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper analyzes the Kerr-Bertotti-Robinson (KBR) spacetime, showing it admits an analytic extension forming an infinite chain of universes connected by wormhole bridges; this exposes ring singularities without horizons—violating weak cosmic censorship. It further demonstrates linear instability of the KBR geometry under axisymmetric scalar perturbations via purely imaginary growing quasinormal modes, rooted in chronology violation, and identifies superradiant, black-hole-bomb–like instabilities and echo-capable quasi-normal mode families in the (2,2) sector.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To investigate the global structure and linear stability of the Kerr-Bertotti-Robinson (KBR) spacetime—a maximally symmetric solution to Einstein-Maxwell equations with both rotation and magnetic field—and assess implications for cosmic censorship and chronology protection.

Method: Analytic continuation of the KBR metric to construct a maximal extension; analysis of geodesic completeness and causal structure; separation and exact solution of the massless scalar wave equation in the two-universe scattering segment; identification and classification of quasinormal modes (QNMs), including marginal modes satisfying horizon-flux balance; asymptotic and numerical exploration of QNM spectra in the (ℓ,m)=(2,2) sector.

Result: 1) The KBR spacetime extends beyond r=±∞ into an infinite chain of causally disconnected universes linked by smooth wormhole bridges; 2) This extension exposes naked ring singularities, violating weak cosmic censorship; 3) Axisymmetric scalar perturbations exhibit purely imaginary growing QNMs for all ℓ, originating from chronology-violating regions; 4) In the (2,2) sector, unstable modes arise for large rotation and small magnetic field; 5) Marginal modes satisfy exact horizon-flux balance, confirming a black-hole-bomb mechanism driven by superradiance and double-barrier trapping; 6) The same potential supports weakly damped modes potentially yielding gravitational echoes.

Conclusion: The KBR spacetime is globally pathological (naked singularities, CTCs) and linearly unstable under scalar perturbations. Its instability mechanism combines superradiance, chronology violation, and cavity-induced mode trapping—highlighting tension between exact solutions, cosmic censorship, and classical predictability in strong-field gravity.

Abstract: The surface $r=\infty$ of the Kerr-Bertotti-Robinson spacetime is not a genuine boundary. We construct its natural analytic extension and show that $r=+\infty$ of one KBR region is smoothly connected to $r=-\infty$ of a neighboring one. Repeated continuation produces an infinite chain of regions connected by wormhole-like bridges and exposes the neighboring ring singularity without an intervening horizon, which violates the weak cosmic censorship conjecture. We then study a test massless scalar field on a two-universe scattering segment to probe the stability of the spacetime. For axisymmetric perturbations, we analytically establish purely imaginary growing quasinormal modes for every $\ell$ and trace their origin to the chronology-violating region. In the $(\ell,m)=(2,2)$ sector, unstable branches occur for sufficiently large rotation and sufficiently small magnetic field. Their marginal real modes obey an exact horizon-flux balance, supporting a black-hole-bomb interpretation in which superradiant extraction is amplified by trapping within the double-barrier potential. The same cavity also supports families of weakly damped modes and may produce echo-like responses.

</details>


### [96] [The generalized second law for Kerr-Taub-NUT black holes perturbed by test field](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.07658)
*Koray Düztaş,Ahmet Cem Erdoğan*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper analyzes how bosonic and fermionic test fields perturb Kerr-Taub-NUT black holes, showing that area and entropy variations differ significantly from standard Kerr black holes; it identifies conditions under which the Generalized Second Law (GSL) is violated—specifically for fermionic fields violating the Null Energy Condition—and confirms GSL preservation for NEC-satisfying bosonic perturbations.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To investigate whether the Generalized Second Law (GSL) of black hole thermodynamics holds for Kerr-Taub-NUT black holes under bosonic and fermionic test field perturbations, given their non-standard entropy–area relation and NUT charge-induced complications.

Method: Analytical computation of first-order variations in horizon area and Bekenstein–Hawking entropy induced by bosonic (e.g., scalar) and fermionic (e.g., Dirac) test fields; comparison of critical frequencies for vanishing area change versus superradiance thresholds; evaluation of entropy balance including Von Neumann entropy of the test field; enforcement and violation analysis of the Null Energy Condition (NEC).

Result: For Kerr-Taub-NUT black holes: (1) the critical frequency for zero area variation exceeds the superradiance limit, enabling area decrease for both bosonic and sub-superradiant fermionic fields; (2) entropy is not proportional to area, so only sub-superradiant fermionic fields reduce entropy; (3) GSL is generically violated for fermionic perturbations violating NEC; (4) for NEC-satisfying bosonic perturbations (e.g., near-extremal case with scalar field slightly above superradiance limit), minimum entropy increase of the black hole compensates environment’s entropy loss when test-field Von Neumann entropy is included.

Conclusion: The GSL holds for bosonic perturbations satisfying the Null Energy Condition but can be violated for fermionic perturbations violating NEC—highlighting a fundamental distinction between bosonic and fermionic quantum fields in black hole thermodynamics within the Kerr-Taub-NUT spacetime.

Abstract: We evaluate the variations in the area and the entropy of Kerr-Taub-NUT black holes perturbed by bosonic and fermionic test fields. We demonstrate that the critical frequency for a test field at which the variation of the area vanishes is strictly larger than the superradiance limit for Kerr-Taub-NUT black holes. Therefore both bosonic fields in the relevant range and fermionic fields which do not exhibit superradiant scattering can induce a decrease in the area of a Kerr-Taub-NUT black hole. However, the entropy is not directly proportional to the area for Kerr-Taub-NUT black holes. Only fermionic fields with frequencies below the superradiance limit can induce an entropy decrease. The simultaneous loss of entropy by the black hole and the environment identifies a generic violation of the Generalized Second Law (GSL) for fermionic perturbations that fail to satisfy the Null Energy Condition (NEC). For bosonic perturbations that satisfy the NEC, we evaluate the limiting case, where a nearly extremal black hole is perturbed by a scalar field with frequency slightly above the superradiance limit. Our analysis reveals that the minimum increase in the black hole entropy compensates for the entropy loss of the environment, when one assigns Von Neumann entropy to the test field. The GSL remains valid for perturbations satisfying the NEC.

</details>


### [97] [Coherent Selection of Critical Kasner Response](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.07848)
*Yi-kun Li*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper shows that a weak coherent electromagnetic wave can significantly alter the initial dynamics of a contracting spacetime in Einstein–Maxwell–scalar theory with two spacelike Killing symmetries; it identifies a critical family (motivated by scalarized black-hole interiors) where magnetic delay enables longitudinal redistribution, and demonstrates—via quadratic source propagation—that low-frequency enhancement can either boost or suppress scalar response depending on background geometry; high-fidelity prediction of this reversal is achieved with <0.4% coherent profile error when cross terms are included.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand how weak coherent electromagnetic fields influence early-time dynamics in strongly curved, contracting spacetimes—particularly near singularities—and whether such fields encode predictive information about departure from Kasner behavior.

Method: Analyzes Einstein–Maxwell–scalar theory with two commuting spacelike Killing fields; constructs a critical family inspired by scalarized black-hole interiors; computes magnetic delay and longitudinal field redistribution; propagates electromagnetic field and momentum, then their quadratic scalar and geometric sources (including cross terms); compares linearized predictions against nonlinear evolution.

Result: A low-frequency electromagnetic component at fixed initial energy can enhance or suppress scalar response depending on background geometry; inclusion of cross terms reduces coherent profile error from 46.9% to 0.38%; the critical geometry preserves incident coherence as predictive information for first departure from Kasner epoch; emergent structure is a magnetic pulse involving electric and scalar exchange.

Conclusion: Coherent wave components—even with negligible self-energy—can dominantly shape the first response of contracting spacetimes; their predictive power arises from retained phase coherence, which governs nonlinear sourcing and determines qualitative features (e.g., suppression vs. enhancement) before full nonlinear evolution sets in.

Abstract: A coherent wave component with extremely small initial self-energy can substantially change the first response of a contracting spacetime. We demonstrate this effect in Einstein--Maxwell--scalar theory with two commuting spacelike Killing fields. Along a critical family motivated by scalarized black-hole interiors, the estimated magnetic delay leaves time for longitudinal propagation to redistribute the incident field. Increasing a low-frequency component at fixed initial electromagnetic energy enhances the scalar response on one background and suppresses it on another. A calculation that propagates the field and its momentum, then their quadratic scalar and geometric sources, predicts this reversal before nonlinear evolution. At the first suppression comparison, the coherent profile error is $0.38\%$, against $46.9\%$ when cross terms are omitted. The selected response develops into a magnetic pulse with electric and scalar exchange. The critical geometry thus preserves incident coherence as information that predicts its first departure from a Kasner epoch.

</details>


### [98] [Scalar field with nonminimal couplings to metric-affine geometry](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.07856)
*Ilaria Andrei,Damianos Iosifidis,Laur Järv,Margus Saal*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper studies a scalar field nonminimally coupled to metric-affine gravity with general quadratic torsion/nonmetricity terms and derivative couplings; it eliminates the independent connection to obtain an effective metric scalar-tensor theory, classifies how different geometric couplings modify the Einstein-frame kinetic function and potential, and derives modified Friedmann equations under cosmological symmetry.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To systematically explore how non-Riemannian geometry—specifically torsion, nonmetricity, and their mixed/derivative couplings—affects scalar-tensor theories beyond standard GR and metric-affine minimal models, particularly regarding kinetic structure, inflationary potentials, and cosmological dynamics.

Method: The authors construct the most general parity-even/odd action linear in affine curvature and quadratic in torsion/nonmetricity (including mixed contractions) plus Nieh-Yan-like derivative couplings. They derive full field equations, solve the algebraic connection equation on the nondegenerate branch, eliminate the connection to obtain an equivalent metric theory, classify sectors by coupling structure, impose projective invariance, analyze canonical field redefinitions and Einstein-frame potentials for polynomial couplings, and reduce the theory under the cosmological principle to obtain modified FLRW equations.

Result: The pure quadratic nonmetricity or pure quadratic torsion sectors preserve the standard Einstein-frame kinetic term, while mixed torsion–nonmetricity, derivative couplings, and generic interactions alter it; projective consistency constrains derivative couplings; derivative and mixed couplings reshape Jordan-frame quadratic/quartic potentials into nontrivial Einstein-frame forms—including natural inflation from a simple quadratic potential; cosmological reduction yields explicit modified Friedmann equations dependent on hypermomentum and reduced quadratic coupling combinations.

Conclusion: Non-Riemannian geometric structures in metric-affine gravity significantly enrich scalar-tensor phenomenology: they induce nontrivial kinetic and potential modifications in the Einstein frame, enable novel inflationary mechanisms (e.g., natural inflation from quadratic potentials), and produce testable deviations in cosmological evolution—all controlled by the interplay of torsion, nonmetricity, and their derivative couplings.

Abstract: We study a scalar field nonminimally coupled to metric-affine gravity within actions linear in the affine curvature and containing all independent parity-even and parity-odd terms quadratic in torsion and nonmetricity, including mixed contractions. We also include Nieh-Yan-like derivative couplings between the scalar-field derivative and the four torsion and nonmetricity vectors. We derive the connection, metric, and scalar-field equations and, since the connection equation is algebraic, eliminate the independent connection on the generic nondegenerate branch to obtain an equivalent metric scalar-tensor theory in which the non-Riemannian interactions are encoded in an effective kinetic function. We classify several sectors and find that the pure quadratic nonmetricity and pure quadratic torsion sectors separately leave the Einstein-frame kinetic function unchanged relative to the simplest metric-affine scalar-tensor model, whereas their simultaneous presence, the derivative couplings, and generic mixed torsion-nonmetricity interactions can modify it. In the derivative-coupling sector, projective consistency imposes a relation among the couplings. We then consider polynomial coupling functions and study the resulting canonical field redefinition and Einstein-frame potentials. In particular, we illustrate how derivative and mixed torsion--nonmetricity couplings reshape quadratic and quartic potentials in canonical-field space, and show that a quadratic Jordan-frame potential can be mapped, for a suitable choice of derivative and nonminimal couplings, into a natural-inflation potential after canonical normalization. Finally, we separately impose the cosmological principle, determine the reduced combinations of quadratic couplings and the scalar field hypermomentum, and obtain the corresponding modified cosmological equations in the simplest sectors.

</details>


### [99] [NRSur7dq4v2: A multi-domain precessing surrogate model with improved accuracy](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.07873)
*Abhishek Ravishankar,Vijay Varma,Scott E. Field,Keefe Mitman,Nils Deppe,Leo C. Stein,Michael Boyle,Mark A. Scheel*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: NRSur7dq4v2 is a multi-domain extension of the NRSur7dq4 surrogate model for binary black hole waveforms, improving accuracy—especially in merger-ringdown—by enabling independent error control per temporal subdomain; it yields 3–10× better remnant mass/spin estimation, extends to ℓ=5 modes, refines subdominant mode modeling, adds runtime complexity control, and ships with a 4× faster gwsurrogate implementation.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: NRSur7dq4, widely used in GW parameter estimation, exhibits insufficient accuracy in the merger-ringdown regime—critical for high-mass BBH events—motivating a more accurate, flexible, and efficient surrogate.

Method: Construct NRSur7dq4v2 as a multi-domain surrogate with overlapping temporal subdomains (inspiral vs. merger-ringdown), enabling independent, tighter error control; incorporate higher modes (ℓ ≤ 5), improve subdominant mode physics, add user-controllable runtime complexity, and optimize gwsurrogate via algorithmic and implementation improvements.

Result: NRSur7dq4v2 achieves 3–10× improvement in remnant mass and spin estimation from ringdown QNM fits; supports ℓ=5 modes with enhanced subdominant mode fidelity; enables accuracy–cost tradeoff tuning; and gwsurrogate now delivers 4× faster evaluation—making precessing surrogates marginally faster than LALSimulation equivalents.
