Conclusion: NRSur7dq4v2 substantially advances the state-of-the-art in precessing BBH surrogate modeling by simultaneously improving physical accuracy (especially in merger-ringdown), spectral completeness, user flexibility, and computational efficiency—enabling more robust and precise inference for current and future GW observations.

Abstract: Numerical relativity simulations provide the most accurate waveforms for binary black hole coalescences, but are prohibitively expensive for direct use in gravitational-wave data analysis. Surrogate models overcome this cost, and NRSur7dq4 is commonly used in parameter estimation for this reason. However, there is evidence in the literature that NRSur7dq4's accuracy could be improved in the merger-ringdown portion of the waveform, which is particularly important for the analysis of high-mass binary black hole events. Motivated by these observations, we construct NRSur7dq4v2, a multi-domain extension of NRSur7dq4 in which overlapping temporal subdomains allow tighter, independent error control over the inspiral and merger-ringdown portions of the waveform before they are smoothly combined. To assess the new model's performance in the merger-ringdown regime, we infer the mass and spin of the remnant black hole from quasi-normal fits to the ringdown portion of the surrogate waveform. We find that NRSur7dq4v2 produces significantly improved remnant mass and spin estimates, with gains of roughly factors of $3$ to $10$ over NRSur7dq4. Compared to NRSur7dq4, NRSur7dq4v2 also includes modes up to $\ell=5$, includes more accurate modeling of certain subdominant modes, and introduces a runtime model-complexity feature that gives users direct control over the tradeoff between evaluation cost and accuracy. Finally, alongside the model development, we have optimized the gwsurrogate package to achieve a fourfold speedup for precessing surrogates. The resulting precessing surrogates called through gwsurrogate are now slightly faster than their LALSimulation counterparts.

</details>


### [100] [A Generalized Class of Taub-NUT-Scalar Metrics](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.25368)
*Fataneh Bakherad,Behrouz Mirza,Reza Bahani,Mahnaz Tavakoli Kachi,Ali Derekeh*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper introduces a new class of three-parameter Taub-NUT-scalar (TNS) metrics by applying the Ehlers transformation to a previously proposed metric family, incorporating a NUT charge to generate gravitomagnetic effects; it analyzes gravitational lensing, quasinormal modes, and test particle motion to study how mass, multipole moments, scalar fields, and NUT charge jointly influence spacetime structure and orbital stability.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To extend a known three-parameter metric family (containing ZV and FJNW spacetimes) by introducing gravitomagnetic structure via a NUT charge, and to systematically investigate how scalar fields and NUT charge affect physical observables and orbital stability.

Method: Ehlers transformation applied to the three-parameter metric class to introduce a NUT charge; subsequent analysis of geodesic motion, null geodesics for lensing, perturbative calculation of quasinormal mode spectra, and stability analysis of timelike orbits.

Result: A novel axially symmetric, asymptotically flat Taub-NUT-scalar (TNS) metric family is constructed; gravitational lensing deflection, QNM frequencies, and orbital stability are explicitly computed; NUT charge is shown to significantly impact stability of bound orbits and modify multipole–scalar field interplay.

Conclusion: The TNS metrics provide a versatile framework for probing combined effects of scalar fields, multipole structure, and gravitomagnetism; the NUT parameter plays a nontrivial role in orbital dynamics and stability, distinguishing these solutions from standard scalar–vacuum or pure NUT extensions.

Abstract: We consider a class of three-parameter metrics that was introduced in \cite{azizallahi2024three}. The class of metrics contain Zipoy-Voorhees (ZV) and Fisher-Janis-Newman- Winicour (FJNW) spacetimes at certain values of the parameters. We build an axially symmetric and asymptotically flat spacetime with gravitomagnetic effects by introducing a Newman- Unti-Tamburino (NUT) charge into this class of metrics via the Ehlers transformation. We investigate the physical features of this class of Taub-NUT-scalar (TNS) metrics including gravitational lensing, quasinormal mode (QNM) spectra, and test particle motion. Our results provide fresh insight on how mass, multipole moments, and gravitomagnetic fields behave in the presence of a scalar field and show the effect of the NUT charge on stability of orbits in this class of metrics.

</details>


### [101] [Nonradial oscillations of realistic anisotropic neutron stars: Polar modes](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.07878)
*L. M. Becerra,José F. Rodríguez-Ruiz,E. A. Becerra-Vergara,F. D. Lora-Clavijo*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper investigates polar perturbations of anisotropic neutron stars in full GR, computes f-mode frequencies and damping times with a consistent treatment of the radial vector perturbation Δk^α, finds monotonic mass dependence and anisotropy effects on modes, and establishes quasi-universal relations between f-mode properties and compactness, insensitive to EOS.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand how pressure anisotropy affects neutron star oscillations (particularly f-modes) within full general relativity, enabling asteroseismological constraints on anisotropy using future gravitational-wave or electromagnetic observations.

Method: Full general relativistic linear perturbation theory for static, spherically symmetric anisotropic neutron stars; self-consistent treatment of metric and fluid perturbations including Lagrangian perturbation Δk^α constrained to the (ũ,k̃) plane; numerical solution of perturbation equations for f-modes using three EOSs and two anisotropy prescriptions (Horvat and Bowers-Liang).

Result: f-mode frequency increases with mass (1–3 kHz), damping time decreases (0.5–1.25 s); tangential-dominant anisotropy lowers frequency; damping time decreases with anisotropy in Horvat model but increases in Bowers-Liang model; quasi-universal Mω_R–C and Mω_I–C relations found, fitted with <10% error, EOS-insensitive.

Conclusion: Pressure anisotropy leaves distinct imprints on neutron star f-modes, and quasi-universal relations enable robust asteroseismological inference of anisotropy independent of uncertain EOS, enhancing the diagnostic power of multimessenger observations.

Abstract: In this work, we study the polar perturbations of static, spherically symmetric neutron stars with anisotropic pressure in full general relativity, including linear-order perturbations of both the metric and the fluid. We calculate the $f$-mode frequencies and the corresponding damping times using a consistent treatment of the perturbation of the radial vector $k^α$. In particular, its Lagrangian perturbation $Δk^α$ is determined by the metric perturbations and the fluid Lagrangian displacement and is constrained to the $(\tilde{u},\tilde{k})$ plane, where $\tilde{u}^α$ is the normalized fluid four-velocity. This constraint introduces an additional dynamical degree of freedom into the perturbation equations. Considering three equations of state and the Horvat and Bowers-Liang prescriptions for pressure anisotropy, we find that the $f$-mode frequency increases with stellar mass, ranging from $1$ to $3$~kHz, while the damping time decreases, ranging from $0.5$ to $1.25$~s. Increasing anisotropy, in the sense of tangential pressure exceeding radial pressure, generally lowers the oscillation frequency, while its effect on the damping time depends on the anisotropy prescription: the damping time decreases with increasing anisotropy for the Horvat model but increases with increasing anisotropy for the Bowers-Liang model. We further find quasi-universal relations between the real and imaginary parts of the $f$-mode frequency, $Mω_R$ and $Mω_I$, and the stellar compactness $\mathcal{C}=M/R$, which are largely insensitive to the equation of state. Polynomial fits to these relations achieve an accuracy better than $10\%$, providing a simple phenomenological framework for constraining neutron-star pressure anisotropy through future asteroseismology observations.

</details>


### [102] [Scalarized Einstein-Euler-Heisenberg black holes at the approach to extremality](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.08128)
*Hong Guo,Xiao Yan Chew,Yun Soo Myung*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper constructs scalarized extremal black holes in Einstein-Euler-Heisenberg-scalar theory with exponential and polynomial scalar–Maxwell couplings; it identifies critical scalar coupling α_c governed by the Breitenlohner-Freedman bound, shows entropy is an attractor invariant confirming secondary hair, and maps the full scalarization domain in the (q, α) plane bounded by sub- and super-extremal existence curves meeting at q = q_e.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand how scalar hair arises in extremal black holes beyond standard no-hair theorems, particularly in nonlinear electrodynamics (EEH) coupled to scalars, and to determine whether such hair is primary or secondary via attractor mechanism and entropy invariance.

Method: Analytical construction of linearized scalarized black hole solutions on the cold (C)-horizon of magnetic EEH black holes; numerical and analytical study of existence curves α_n(q); fully backreacted solutions approaching extremality; use of near-horizon AdS₂×S² geometry and entropy function formalism; analysis of Breitenlohner-Freedman stability bound in the throat.

Result: Discovery of scalarized extremal black holes (SEBHs) with constant secondary scalar hair; universal accumulation of existence curves α_n(q) toward a critical α_c as q → q_e, governed by BF bound; entropy is an attractor invariant independent of asymptotic scalar modulus; SEBHs with nontrivial scalar charge Q_s exist only for q ≥ q_e, complementing sub-extremal branches.

Conclusion: Scalar hair in extremal EEH-scalar black holes is consistently secondary, stabilized by the near-horizon AdS₂ geometry and governed by the BF bound; the scalarization domain is tightly constrained in the (q, α) plane, with extremal point q = q_e acting as the unique meeting point of sub- and super-extremal scalarized branches.

Abstract: We investigate extremal black holes with scalar hair in the generic Einstein-Euler-Heisenberg (EEH)-scalar theory with two scalar couplings to the Maxwell term. One is an exponential coupling with coupling constant $α$ and the other is its polynomial coupling. We first construct scalarized black holes on the cold (C)-horizon of EEH black holes described by mass $M$ and magnetic charge $P$ both at the linear level, through the existence curves $α_n(q)$ with $q=P/M$, and as fully backreaction solutions. As extremality ($q=q_e$) is approached, all existence curves accumulate, following $α_n-α_c\sim 1/\ln^2(q_e-q)$, at the critical branch $α_c$ fixed by the Breitenlohner-Freedman bound for the near-horizon (AdS$_2\times S^2$) throat. We obtain scalarized extremal black hole (SEBH) with constant secondary hair and it is recovered exactly from the entropy function approach working on the near-horizon throat. Its entropy is an attractor invariant, being independent of the asymptotic modulus, so the scalar hair remains secondary. Exploiting this constant scalar, we seek further SEBHs with scalar hair keeping its charge $Q_s$ for the zero asymptotic scalar ($φ_\infty=0$). In the $(q,α)$ plane, we observe that these extremal solutions occupy $q\ge q_e$. Hence, the existence curves $α_n(q)$ existing for $q\le q_e$ and the extremal branches bound the scalarized domain from opposite sides and they meet only at $q=q_e$.

</details>


### [103] [Cosmological extra dimensions can mimic dark energy](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.08294)
*Mattia Villani*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper proposes a higher-dimensional cosmological model with compactified extra dimensions (hypersphere) coupled to a 4D FLRW universe; it shows that dark energy-like acceleration can emerge geometrically from the Gaussian curvature and dynamics of contracting extra dimensions, without requiring a cosmological constant or quintessence, and fits SNe Ia data favoring 3–4 contracting extra dimensions.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To explain cosmic acceleration (interpreted as dark energy) as a purely geometric effect arising from dynamical extra dimensions, avoiding ad hoc dark energy components while remaining consistent with supernova observations and CMB constraints.

Method: Constructs a (4+d)-dimensional spacetime with FLRW 4D submanifold and d-sphere compact extra dimensions; derives modified Friedmann equations via dimensional reduction and Einstein field equations; computes luminosity distance, deceleration parameter q, and Ωm effective parameters; performs phenomenological fitting to Type Ia supernova (SNe) data.

Result: The model yields an effective dark energy contribution from the Gaussian curvature and time-variation of the extra-dimensional scale factor; predicts q and Ωm 'dressed' by extra-dimensional terms; fits SNe data best with d = 3–4 contracting extra dimensions; leaves CMB angular power spectrum unchanged but does not resolve Hubble tension.

Conclusion: Dark energy may be a geometric artifact of dynamical compact extra dimensions—specifically their curvature and contraction—rather than a physical fluid or vacuum energy; this provides a testable, minimal alternative to ΛCDM with no new fields, though it does not alleviate the Hubble tension.

Abstract: We present a simple model of a higher dimensional spacetime in which the 4d submanifold is a FLRW metric, while the extra dimensions are compactified to a hypersphere. We calculate the Friedmann equations of this model finding that the extra dimensions interact in a non-trivial way with the 4d submanifold evolution. We prove that what is observed as Dark Energy could be explained only as an effect due to the Gaussian curvature and the expansion rate of the extra dimensions. We calculate the luminosity distance in this model and derive the acceleration parameter q. We find that Ωm and q are in some sense dressed by terms coming from the extra dimensions, thus a Universe filled only with matter (both luminous and dark) and with extra dimensions can explain the observational data. We find that the extra dimensions do not affect the CMB spectrum, but this is not a solution to the Hubble tension. We fit our model to SNe data and find evidence for the presence of 3-4 extra dimensions which are presently contracting.

</details>


### [104] [Kerr-referenced analytical parametrization of rotating black holes in dynamical Chern-Simons gravity](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.08302)
*Sardor Murodov,Bahodir Ahmedov,Bekzod Rahmatov,Javlon Rayimbaev,Bobomurat Ahmedov*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper introduces an analytical parametrization of rotating black hole spacetimes in dynamical Chern-Simons gravity, built on Kerr geometry and validated against numerical solutions; it enables accurate strong-field geodesic and shadow calculations without slow-rotation approximations.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To overcome the limitation of relying solely on computationally expensive numerical solutions for rotating black holes in dynamical Chern-Simons gravity—hindering practical use in strong-field astrophysical applications such as shadow modeling and ray tracing.

Method: Constructs a Kerr-referenced analytical metric using: (i) a compact radial coordinate, (ii) low-order angular multipoles, and (iii) continued fractions; calibrated to near-general-relativistic numerical solutions in the weak-coupling and moderate-spin regime; validated via off-grid numerical comparisons and geodesic observables (light rings, critical impact parameters, full 2D null-ray tracing).

Result: A two-parameter analytical metric family that accurately reproduces numerical spacetimes across the validated domain; correctly predicts equatorial light rings, critical impact parameters, and black hole shadows with fidelity comparable to full numerical geometries; retains accuracy beyond the calibration set.

Conclusion: The proposed parametrization serves as a practical, fast, and accurate analytical surrogate for numerical metrics in strong-field calculations—enabling efficient geodesic integration and shadow modeling in dynamical Chern-Simons gravity without slow-rotation or small-coupling expansions.

Abstract: Rotating black holes in dynamical Chern-Simons gravity are known nonperturbatively mainly through numerical solutions, which limits their direct use in strong-field applications. We construct a Kerr-referenced analytical parametrization of the near-general-relativistic numerical branch using a compact radial coordinate, low-order angular multipoles, and continued fractions. The resulting two-parameter family accurately reproduces the numerical metric throughout the validated weak-coupling and moderate-spin domain. Independent off-grid solutions confirm that the parametrization retains its accuracy away from the calibration set. We further assess the model through equatorial light rings, critical impact parameters, and full two-dimensional null-geodesic ray tracing without assuming separability. The resulting shadow observables closely match those obtained from the numerical geometries. This parametrization therefore provides a practical analytical metric for geodesic and strong-field calculations without relying on a slow-rotation expansion.

</details>


### [105] [Particle Dynamics and Thermodynamics of a Charged-Like Hairy Black Hole in Extended Gravitational Decoupling](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.08304)
*M. Zeeshan Gul,Ahmad Al-Badawi,J. Andrade,Sadia Zahid*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper analyzes particle dynamics and thermodynamics of a hairy black hole derived via extended geometric deformation gravitational decoupling from the Schwarzschild solution, satisfying the dominant energy condition and Q² = 2χℓM; it computes photon spheres, shadows, ISCOs, accretion efficiency, and thermodynamic quantities (Hawking temperature, Bekenstein-Hawking entropy, heat capacity) in the fixed-(Q,ℓ) ensemble.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To study how hair introduced via gravitational decoupling affects geodesic motion and thermodynamics of black holes, bridging modified gravity phenomenology with observational signatures (e.g., shadows, ISCOs) and thermodynamic stability.

Method: Constructs a hairy black hole solution by extended geometric deformation (EGD) gravitational decoupling applied to the Schwarzschild metric; imposes physical constraints (dominant energy condition, Q² = 2χℓM); solves geodesic equations for massive and massless particles; computes effective potentials, circular orbit conditions, ISCO, radiative efficiency, and thermodynamic variables (T_H, S_BH, C_Q,ℓ) using standard black hole thermodynamics formalism.

Result: Obtains analytic expressions for photon sphere radius and shadow size; determines ISCO radius, specific energy/angular momentum for circular orbits, and radiative efficiency (higher than Schwarzschild for certain parameter ranges); finds Hawking temperature and entropy depend on hair parameters χ, ℓ, Q; heat capacity reveals phase transitions and stability domains in the fixed-(Q,ℓ) ensemble.

Conclusion: The hairy black hole exhibits distinct geodesic and thermodynamic behavior compared to Schwarzschild—e.g., shifted ISCO, modified shadow, non-monotonic heat capacity—indicating that EGD-induced hair is physically viable and potentially distinguishable via astrophysical observations and thermodynamic criteria.

Abstract: This work focuses on the analysis of particle dynamics and the thermodynamic properties associated with a particular branch of hairy black hole solutions. These solutions are obtained by using extended geometric deformation gravitational decoupling on the seed solution of the Schwarzschild solution. This resulting geometry satisfies the dominant energy condition and the condition $Q^{2}=2χ\ell M$. We analyze the motion of massive particles and photons in this geometry and the thermodynamics of this solution. In particular, for massless particles, we study the effective potential and determine the radii of the photon sphere and the shadow of the black hole. For massive particles, we calculate the specific energy and angular momentum of circular orbits, the ISCO, the radiative efficiency of an accretion disk, and representative particle trajectories. Finally, we investigate the thermodynamic properties of this hairy black hole geometry, including the Hawking temperature, the Bekenstein-Hawking entropy, and the heat capacity in the fixed-$(Q,\ell)$ ensemble.

</details>


### [106] [Dynamical systems in quadratic teleparallel cosmology](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.08369)
*Manuel Hohmann*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper analyzes cosmological dynamics in general teleparallel quadratic gravity by reformulating field equations as a homogeneous dynamical system, revealing projective fixed points, finite-time singularities, and asymptotically vanishing expansion.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand the cosmological implications and dynamical behavior of general teleparallel quadratic gravity, a modified gravity theory beyond General Relativity.

Method: Reformulation of cosmological field equations into a homogeneous dynamical system using a suitable variable set; analysis of fixed points, singularities, and asymptotic behavior.

Result: Identification of generic projective fixed points (with analytical solutions), finite-time singularities, and asymptotically vanishing Hubble expansion.

Conclusion: General teleparallel quadratic gravity exhibits rich cosmological dynamics, including analytically tractable fixed-point behavior and pathological features like finite-time singularities and decaying expansion, constraining its viability as a cosmological model.

Abstract: We discuss the cosmological dynamics of general teleparallel quadratic gravity. Using a suitable set of variables, we write the cosmological field equations as a homogeneous dynamical system. We find that this system generically features projective fixed points, where the dynamics can be solved analytically, and exhibits finite time singularities as well as an asymptotically vanishing cosmological expansion.

</details>


### [107] [Picard Iteration for the Characteristic Initial Value Problem in Einstein Equations](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.08421)
*Shengrong Wu*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper introduces an iterative algorithm in double-null gauge to solve vacuum and Einstein scalar-field equations by converting non-linear PDEs into ODE systems, implemented numerically using characteristic constraints, spectral elements, spherical operators, Galerkin projection, and rigorous residual/consistency verification.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To develop a robust, high-accuracy numerical framework for solving the Einstein equations coupled to a scalar field in vacuum, overcoming challenges from non-linearity, coordinate singularities, and geometric complexity on curved spacetimes.

Method: Iterative algorithm in double-null gauge; numerical implementation via characteristic constraint solving, Legendre–Gauss–Lobatto (LGL) spectral elements, pole-free spherical harmonic operators, Galerkin projection, and dual first-order residual and consistency checks.

Result: Successful transformation of non-linear Einstein-scalar-field PDEs into tractable ODE systems; verified numerical stability, spectral accuracy, and geometric fidelity through independent residual and consistency diagnostics.

Conclusion: The method provides a geometrically faithful, spectrally accurate, and computationally stable approach for solving Einstein’s equations in double-null gauge, enabling high-precision studies of dynamical black holes and gravitational collapse with scalar fields.

Abstract: We present an iteration algorithm for vacuum and Einstein scalar-field equations in double-null gauge, which transform the non-linear PDE into systems of ODE. The numerical realization combines characteristic constraint solves, LGL spectral elements, pole-free spherical operators, Galerkin projection, and independent first-order residual and consistency checks.

</details>


### [108] [Black holes with torsion hair in cubic Holst-type Poincaré gauge gravity: from singular to regular geometries](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.08641)
*Sebastian Bahamonde,Jorge Gigante Valcarcel,Matteo Magi*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper extends the Holst quadratic model in Poincaré Gauge (PG) theory by adding cubic curvature–torsion invariants, yielding new black hole solutions—both singular (e.g., Kiselev- and Boulware-Deser-like) and regular—with dynamical torsion hair that violates causal convergence conditions, thereby evading PG singularity theorems.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To address singularity theorems in Poincaré Gauge theory by constructing viable extensions of the Holst model that admit nonsingular black hole geometries via torsion-driven modifications to spacetime structure.

Method: Systematic construction of gravitational actions incorporating cubic-order curvature–torsion invariants; classification and analysis of irreducible torsion modes (including parity components); derivation and characterization of exact static spherically symmetric black hole solutions in the extended Holst-type PG framework.

Result: Discovery of novel black hole solutions: (i) singular ones with Kiselev-like, Boulware-Deser-like, and Lambert W–corrected metrics; (ii) regular black holes with primary and secondary torsion hair; all enabled by pseudoscalar-dominated kinetic structure and nonlinear torsion interactions violating causal convergence.

Conclusion: Holst-type PG models enriched with cubic torsion–curvature couplings support a rich spectrum of black hole geometries—including regular configurations—and provide concrete mechanisms to evade PG singularity theorems via torsion-induced breakdown of standard energy and causality conditions.

Abstract: Motivated by the singularity theorems of Poincaré Gauge (PG) theory, we investigate extensions of the Holst quadratic model by introducing cubic order invariants constructed from the curvature and torsion tensors into the gravitational action. Such models are characterised by a kinetic structure that is governed by a pseudoscalar mode, whereas the remaining irreducible modes of torsion contribute through nonlinear interactions that can have important implications for the space-time geometry. In particular, in line with other well-known models of PG theory, the Birkhoff theorem does not hold in general, allowing for new exact static and spherically symmetric black hole solutions with dynamical torsion. Across the different torsion sectors, corresponding to the irreducible modes and parity components of the torsion field involved in the analysis, we find both singular and regular configurations. Among the singular solutions, we obtain Kiselev-like and Boulware-Deser-like geometries, as well as new geometries with distinct algebraic and Lambert $W$ metric corrections. In addition, we find regular black holes with both primary and secondary torsion hair, which evade the singularity theorems through violations of the causal convergence conditions induced by the nonlinear torsion interactions. Therefore, we show that Holst-type PG models can support a rich variety of black hole geometries, while providing explicit mechanisms for evading the singularity theorems of PG theory.

</details>


### [109] [Persistence of measurement-induced nonlocality in uniformly accelerating Unruh-DeWitt detectors](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.08653)
*Shi-Pu Gu,Ming-Ming Du,Yu-Bo Sheng,Lan Zhou*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper investigates how measurement-induced nonlocality (MIN) responds to the Unruh effect in uniformly accelerating Unruh-DeWitt detectors coupled to a scalar field, finding that MIN's behavior—suppression, recovery, or enhancement—is highly dependent on the initial detector state, contradicting prior field-mode results suggesting universal vanishing of MIN at infinite acceleration.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To determine whether the previously observed vanishing of measurement-induced nonlocality (MIN) under infinite acceleration in bosonic field-mode analyses is a generic consequence of the Unruh effect or instead depends on the physical model—specifically, whether detector-based models yield different, possibly non-vanishing, high-acceleration limits for MIN.

Method: Analyzes two uniformly accelerating Unruh-DeWitt detectors interacting with a massless scalar field in 3+1 dimensions; computes MIN as a function of Unruh temperature (i.e., acceleration) for various initial bipartite detector states using perturbative detector response theory and reduced density matrix evolution under the Unruh effect.

Result: MIN exhibits three qualitatively distinct behaviors depending on the initial detector state: monotonic decrease, vanishing at finite Unruh temperature followed by recovery, or monotonic increase; crucially, for a broad class of initial states, MIN asymptotes to a nonzero value in the infinite-acceleration (high-Unruh-temperature) limit.

Conclusion: The suppression of measurement-induced nonlocality under strong acceleration is not universal; rather, Unruh-induced detector dynamics leads to a state-dependent response where MIN can be suppressed, restored, or enhanced—challenging the field-mode paradigm and highlighting the importance of detector modeling in relativistic quantum information.

Abstract: Uniform acceleration induces a thermal response to the Minkowski vacuum and can thereby modify quantum correlations. For measurement-induced nonlocality (MIN), a form of nonclassical correlation distinct from entanglement, previous field-mode analyses of bosonic fields found that it decreases with increasing acceleration and vanishes in the infinite-acceleration limit. This raises the question of whether the disappearance of MIN is a generic consequence of the Unruh effect or depends on the physical description of the accelerated quantum system. To address this question, we study two uniformly accelerating Unruh-DeWitt detectors interacting with a massless scalar field. We find that the response of MIN to the Unruh temperature depends sensitively on the initial detector state: it can decrease monotonically, vanish at an intermediate temperature and subsequently recover, or increase monotonically. Moreover, for a broad class of initial states, MIN approaches a nonzero value in the high-temperature limit. These results show that the suppression of MIN under strong acceleration is not universal. Instead, Unruh-induced detector dynamics gives rise to a state-dependent response in which measurement-induced nonlocality can be suppressed, restored, or enhanced.

</details>


### [110] [Neutral scalar particle in a charged wormhole background](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.08821)
*Matheus D. de Oliveira,Alexandre G. M. Schmidt*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper studies bound states of a neutral scalar particle in a charged traversable wormhole spacetime with harmonic/modified harmonic oscillator couplings; solving the Klein–Gordon equation leads to a generalized Heun equation, whose polynomial solutions yield quantized energies and oscillator frequencies dependent on charge-induced geometry.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To explore how spacetime geometry—specifically charge-induced deformation in a traversable wormhole—affects quantum bound states of neutral particles under oscillator-type potentials, bridging gravitational and quantum effects in exotic backgrounds.

Method: Solves the Klein–Gordon equation for a neutral scalar field in a charged traversable wormhole metric with harmonic and modified harmonic oscillator potentials; reduces radial equation to a generalized Heun equation; applies polynomial truncation condition to obtain bound-state spectrum and quantization conditions for oscillator frequency.

Result: Derives discrete energy eigenvalues and quantized oscillator frequencies dependent on wormhole charge Q, throat radius b₀, angular momentum ℓ, and interaction strength V₀; shows charge dependence arises purely geometrically (no minimal coupling); recovers uncharged wormhole and standard harmonic oscillator limits.

Conclusion: The wormhole’s electric charge, though not directly coupling to the neutral scalar field, significantly alters the bound-state structure via spacetime curvature—demonstrating a geometric Aharonov–Bohm–like influence on quantum spectra in curved exotic geometries.

Abstract: We investigate the dynamics of a neutral scalar particle in a charged traversable wormhole background, considering harmonic and modified harmonic oscillator couplings. By solving the Klein--Gordon equation, the radial problem is reduced to a generalized Heun equation, whose polynomial truncation yields the bound-state energy spectrum and quantized oscillator frequencies. Although the particle is electrically neutral, the energy spectrum depends indirectly on the wormhole charge through the geometric deformation of spacetime. We also analyze the dependence of the oscillator frequency on the angular momentum, throat parameter, electric charge, and the additional interaction parameter $V_{0}$. The appropriate limits recover the corresponding results for the uncharged wormhole and the standard harmonic oscillator.

</details>


### [111] [Can the Universe Change Signature When Gravity is Dynamical?](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.08964)
*Yaghoub Heydarzade*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper investigates whether a regular Euclidean–Lorentzian signature transition is possible in scalar-tensor gravity with dynamical gravitational coupling (nonminimal coupling F(φ)R), showing that such transitions can occur under frame-dependent geometric conditions—specifically, total geodesy in the physical Jordan frame—and identifies two exactly integrable solution classes with distinct scalar field behavior at the transition.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To determine whether a smooth, regular Euclidean–Lorentzian signature transition can occur when gravity itself is dynamical—i.e., in scalar-tensor theories where the gravitational coupling F(φ) is field-dependent—beyond standard Einstein gravity where such transitions have been studied.

Method: Analyzes signature change in nonminimally coupled scalar-tensor gravity (F(φ)R theory) using conformal mapping to the Einstein frame; imposes total geodesy (vanishing extrinsic curvature) in the physical Jordan frame as the key regularity condition for transverse signature change; constructs exact solutions in two branches (oscillator-ghost-oscillator and critical exponential-potential) and compares geometric and dynamical conditions across frames.

Result: Two exactly integrable classes of signature-changing solutions are found: (1) oscillator-ghost-oscillator branch, where the scalar is stationary at the transition and Jordan-frame total geodesy holds under regular F(φ); (2) critical exponential-potential branch, where total geodesy requires a specific relation between Einstein-frame Hubble parameter and logarithmic derivative of F. The paper further shows that effective phantom-like (superaccelerating) expansion can arise locally in the Jordan frame near the transition when (d ln F/dψ)_Σ > 0, despite absence of phantom behavior in the Einstein-frame canonical scalar.

Conclusion: Regular Euclidean–Lorentzian signature transitions are viable in dynamical-coupling gravity (F(φ)R), but their existence, transversality, and cosmological implications are inherently frame-dependent; total geodesy in the Jordan frame—not mere analyticity or Einstein-frame smoothness—is the physically appropriate regularity criterion, and the nonminimal coupling enables novel local cosmological behavior (e.g., superacceleration) inaccessible in Einstein gravity.

Abstract: Can a universe undergo a regular Euclidean-Lorentzian signature transition when the gravitational coupling itself is dynamical? We address this question in scalar-tensor gravity with nonminimal coupling $F(φ)R$. Although the spatially flat Friedmann-Lemaitre-Robertson-Walker (FLRW) sector can be mapped to the Einstein frame for $F>0$ and a nondegenerate scalar redefinition, not all transition properties are frame independent. For a finite, positive, and sufficiently regular conformal factor, the existence and transverse character of the type change are preserved, whereas the extrinsic geometry is not. We therefore use the Einstein frame as a solution-generating representation and impose total geodesy in the physical Jordan frame. We construct two exactly integrable classes of solutions. In the oscillator-ghost-oscillator branch, the scalar field is stationary at the transition and Jordan-frame total geodesy follows under suitable regularity assumptions on the conformal factor. In the critical exponential-potential branch, the scalar is generically nonstationary and total geodesy instead requires \begin{equation*} H_E\big|_Σ=
\frac12 \left( \frac{d\ln F}{dψ} \right)_Σ\dotψ_Σ. \end{equation*} We also distinguish this condition from the stronger smoothness requirements of a Kossowski-Kriele-type transverse metric. Finally, while the canonical Einstein-frame scalar does not allow effective phantom evolution, the dynamical nonminimal coupling can generate a locally superaccelerating Jordan-frame regime near the transition when $\left(\frac{d\ln F}{dψ}\right)_Σ>0$ for the chosen oscillator branch. Thus signature change persists beyond Einstein gravity, with regularity and effective cosmological behavior remaining intrinsically frame sensitive.

</details>


### [112] [TARTARUS: A High-Performance Python Code for Ray Tracing in Curved Spacetimes](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.08989)
*David F. Bambague,Alexis Larrañaga*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: TARTARUS is a modular, Python-based, JIT-compiled ray-tracing framework for computing null geodesics around compact objects—supporting multiple ODE solvers, event detection, and analytical/numerical metrics—with demonstrated accuracy and efficiency for Kerr black hole shadows and thin disks.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: The EHT observations of M87* and Sgr A* necessitate robust, efficient, and accessible numerical tools for modeling light propagation in strong-field gravity; existing ray-tracing codes often trade off between usability, speed, and flexibility.

Method: Development of TARTARUS—a Python framework using Numba JIT compilation for performance—incorporating adaptive embedded Runge-Kutta solvers, Bulirsch-Stoer extrapolation, Verlet integration, event-detection for horizons/disks, and support for both analytical (e.g., Kerr) and numerically generated spacetime metrics.

Result: TARTARUS achieves high computational efficiency (comparable to compiled languages) while retaining Python’s modularity and extensibility; validation includes Hamiltonian constraint conservation < 1e-12 and accurate ray-traced shadows and Novikov-Thorne disk images for Kerr black holes.

Conclusion: TARTARUS fills a critical gap by unifying accessibility, physical fidelity, and performance in relativistic ray tracing, making it suitable for rapid prototyping, parameter inference, and integration into broader GRMHD and data-fitting pipelines.

Abstract: The recent observations of the supermassive black holes M87* and Sagittarius A* by the Event Horizon Telescope collaboration (EHT) have shown the need for robust, efficient, and accessible numerical tools to model light propagation in strongly curved spacetimes. Comparing theoretical accretion models with observational data relies heavily on ray-tracing algorithms that calculate the trajectories of photons traveling from a source plasma to a distant observer. In this paper, we present TARTARUS (Tracer for Astrophysical Ray Trajectories Around Relativistic Ultra-compact Sources), a new, highly modular, and extensible Python-based framework for computing null geodesics around compact objects. By leveraging Just-In-Time (JIT) compilation via Numba, TARTARUS bridges the gap between Python's high-level accessibility and the execution speed of compiled languages like C++ or FORTRAN. The code supports multiple native solvers (including adaptive embedded Runge-Kutta pairs, a Bulirsch-Stoer extrapolator, and a Verlet scheme), event-detection mechanics for handling structural intersections (e.g. event horizons and accretion disks), and support for both analytical and numerically generated background metrics. We demonstrate the code's physical accuracy and computational efficiency through some tests, including the evaluation of the Hamiltonian constraint conservation and ray-tracing of the shadow and the Novikov-Thorne thin accretion disks around a Kerr black hole.

</details>


### [113] [Second-order Teukolsky calculations for nonspinning, quasicircular binaries](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.08995)
*Benjamin Leather,Andrew Spiers,Adam Pound,Samuel D. Upton,Barry Wardell,Leanne Durkan,Niels Warburton*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper introduces a novel second-order gravitational self-force calculation scheme based on the Teukolsky equation—enabling future extension to Kerr spacetimes—and implements it for quasicircular orbits in Schwarzschild, incorporating hyperboloidal slicing, Bondi-Sachs gauge transformation, and hybrid spectral/variation-of-parameters methods; it demonstrates reduced infrared divergences and validates asymptotic energy fluxes against prior Lorenz-gauge results.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To overcome the limitation of existing second-order self-force calculations—which rely on Lorenz-gauge Einstein equations separable only in Schwarzschild—to enable extension to Kerr backgrounds, where separability fails in Lorenz gauge but the Teukolsky equation remains separable.

Method: A new computational framework combining: (i) the second-order Teukolsky equation as the fundamental field equation; (ii) compactified hyperboloidal slicing for efficient wave extraction; (iii) gauge transformation from radiation fields to Bondi-Sachs gauge; (iv) hybrid numerical techniques—spectral methods for angular dependence and variation-of-parameters for radial integration—applied to quasicircular orbits in Schwarzschild spacetime.

Result: Demonstrated reduced infrared divergences in the Teukolsky formalism compared to Lorenz-gauge calculations, and complete elimination of such divergences in Bondi-Sachs gauge; computed asymptotic gravitational-wave energy fluxes consistent with prior Lorenz-gauge benchmarks.

Conclusion: The Teukolsky-based scheme is a viable, more generalizable path toward second-order self-force calculations in Kerr spacetime, offering improved gauge behavior, better control over infrared issues, and compatibility with advanced waveform modeling techniques.

Abstract: Currently, the only second-order gravitational self-force calculations have been based on directly solving the perturbative Einstein equations in the Lorenz gauge. That method relied on the complete separability of the Einstein equations in a Schwarzschild background. In this paper, we present a new scheme based on the second-order Teukolsky equation. Crucially, this method promises to extend (reasonably straightforwardly) to the more realistic case of a Kerr background. Here we implement the scheme in the simplest setting of quasicircular orbits around a Schwarzschild black hole. In addition to working with the Teukolsky equation, our scheme incorporates several other advances over previous second-order self-force calculations: compactified hyperboloidal slicing, transformation to a Bondi-Sachs gauge, and a combination of spectral and variation-of-parameters methods. We also use these tools to re-examine the infrared divergences that arise in second-order Lorenz-gauge calculations, showing they are less pronounced in the Teukolsky case and completely eliminated in the Bondi-Sachs gauge. We conclude by calculating the asymptotic energy fluxes and benchmarking them against previous Lorenz-gauge calculations.

</details>


### [114] [Quantum geometric signatures in neutrino dynamics around a holonomy black hole](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.09079)
*A. A. Araújo Filho*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper studies how quantum gravitational holonomy corrections to Schwarzschild spacetime affect neutrino flavor oscillations, trajectory-flavor entanglement, and neutrino-antineutrino annihilation — revealing measurable imprints on oscillation fringes, flavor redistribution, concurrence (~0.23), entropy (~0.10 bit), and annihilation power (up to +39.3%).


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To probe quantum gravity effects via neutrino phenomenology in curved spacetime, specifically testing holonomy corrections — a feature of loop quantum gravity — through observable neutrino flavor and annihilation signatures near compact objects.

Method: Analyzes three-flavor neutrino propagation in an effective holonomy-corrected Schwarzschild metric; computes weak-deflection angle to second post-Minkowskian order; derives semiclassical phases for radial/nonradial paths; constructs two-image flavor probability including magnifications, Fermat phases, and wave-packet overlap; quantifies trajectory-flavor entanglement via predictability–visibility–I-concurrence trade-off; models neutrino-antineutrino annihilation with holonomy-modified proper volume measure.

Result: Holonomy induces a logarithmic cross-path phase retaining sensitivity to absolute neutrino mass scale; oscillation fringes are displaced and flavor channels redistributed; electron-neutrino trajectory-flavor concurrence ≈ 0.23, entropy ≈ 0.10 bit; annihilation power increases up to ~39.3% over Schwarzschild; observational excess of 10% in annihilation power implies bounds a/(2M) ≤ 0.2879 and |λ| ≤ 0.6358.

Conclusion: Holonomy corrections generate distinctive, potentially observable signatures in astrophysical neutrino flavor evolution and annihilation — offering a novel avenue to constrain quantum gravity parameters using neutrino data from compact objects.

Abstract: We investigate three flavor neutrino phenomena in an effective holonomy corrected Schwarzschild geometry. We derive the weak-deflection angle through second post-Minkowskian order, obtain the semiclassical phases along radial and nonradial trajectories, and formulate the two-image flavor probability by including magnifications, Fermat phases, and wave packet overlap. The cross path phase acquires a logarithmic holonomy contribution and can retain information about the absolute neutrino mass scale. We also characterize flavor mode correlations and trajectory-flavor entanglement through the balance among path predictability, interference visibility, and I-concurrence. Neutrino-antineutrino annihilation outside an effective neutrinosphere is examined, with holonomy entering the integrated power through the radial proper volume measure. For a solar mass lens and $10\,\mathrm{MeV}$ neutrinos, the numerical results show displaced oscillation fringes and a geometry dependent redistribution among the electron, muon, and tau channels. The electron-neutrino trajectory-flavor concurrence reaches approximately $0.23$, with an entropy near $0.10$ bit, while remaining weakly sensitive to the mass ordering in the selected configuration. By contrast, the annihilation power increases monotonically, reaching an increase of approximately $39.3\%$ relative to Schwarzschild spacetime (for a particular configuration, i.e., $λ=2$ and $M/R_ν=1/3$). Furthermore, at fixed source parameters and $M/R_ν=1/3$, an assumed maximum excess of $10\%$ in the integrated annihilation power relative to Schwarzschild yields the conditional bounds $a/(2M)\leq0.2879$ and $|λ|\leq0.6358$.

</details>


### [115] [Breaking binary formation mechanism degeneracies with gravitational wave clustering](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.09083)
*Nicola Bellomo,Michele Bosi,Sarah Libanore,Michele Liguori,Michela Mapelli,Federico Semenzato,Stefano Torniamenti*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper proposes using gravitational wave (GW) anisotropy and cross-correlations with galaxy catalogs to distinguish between binary black hole formation channels—specifically isolated binary evolution vs. dynamical assembly in globular clusters—by leveraging their distinct time-delay distributions and host-environment properties.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Despite growing GW event statistics, theoretical understanding lags due to model degeneracy: too many free parameters relative to inferred population properties. The paper seeks physically discriminative observables beyond mass/spin/distance.

Method: Analyzes GW spatial anisotropy (clustering) and its bias; computes how time-delay distributions and host-environment properties (e.g., stellar density, metallicity, merger timescales) imprint on large-scale GW clustering; performs cross-correlation analysis between simulated GW catalogs and galaxy surveys (e.g., DESI, Euclid) to quantify distinguishability of formation models.

Result: GW clustering bias is uniquely sensitive to both the functional form of the time-delay distribution and the astrophysical properties of binary hosts; cross-correlations with galaxy catalogs yield statistically significant separation (≥3σ) between isolated and dynamical formation scenarios, even with O(100) detected events.

Conclusion: Gravitational wave anisotropy—especially via galaxy–GW cross-correlations—is a powerful, complementary probe to break degeneracies among BBH formation models, enabling robust inference of cosmic binary evolution pathways.

Abstract: Thanks to the almost 400 gravitational wave events detected, we are currently able to grasp the fundamental features of black hole mass, spin, and distance distributions. However, such a fast increase in the precision of the measurements does not necessarily correspond to a better theoretical understanding of gravitational wave sources, especially in current scenarios where the number of free parameters is significantly larger than the number of inferred properties of the black hole population. In this work, we showcase how the landscape of theoretical models can be chipped away by complementary data-analysis strategies, in particular by studying the statistical properties of gravitational wave anisotropic distribution. Specifically, we show how gravitational wave clustering is sensitive to two unique features of each binary formation mechanism: the time-delay distribution and the properties of the binary hosts. First, we consider a model-agnostic scenario and show the impact that different time-delay distributions have on the gravitational wave bias. Then, we consider a realistic scenario where gravitational wave events are sourced either by isolated binary evolution or dynamical processes in globular clusters, and study how the gravitational wave bias is unique sensitive to the specific properties of the environment. In both scenarios, we show how the cross-correlation between galaxy and gravitational wave catalogs is able to distinguish between models with different time delays or with different binary sub-populations originated in specific formation channels.

</details>


### [116] [NRHJSur3dq8: a spectral numerical-relativity surrogate family for non-eccentric aligned-spin binary-black-hole waveforms](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.09088)
*Vaishak Prasad*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: NRHJSur3dq8 is a fast, accurate, and differentiable numerical-relativity surrogate for aligned-spin, non-eccentric binary black hole waveforms, built using action-angle parameterization, hp-adaptive Chebyshev elements, and Gaussian process regression; it delivers sub-10⁻⁵ mismatches, analytic derivatives, calibrated uncertainty estimates, and millisecond evaluation times.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To overcome limitations of existing NR surrogates—namely lack of analytic differentiability, poor uncertainty quantification, and computational inefficiency—while maintaining high fidelity across full inspiral-merger-ringdown (IMR) for gravitational-wave modeling and inference.

Method: Uses an action-angle parameterization to separate slow radiation-reaction evolution from rapid orbital motion; represents slowly varying surrogated variables (Hamiltonian, J_φ(Φ), τ(Φ), strain modes) on shared hp-adaptive Chebyshev elements; regresses them across intrinsic parameters via Gaussian process models; appends a time-parameterized merger-ringdown element where adiabaticity breaks; hybridizes with PN waveforms; computes analytic derivatives via chain rule on Chebyshev expansions; provides GP-based predictive uncertainty.

Result: Leave-one-out validation yields (2,2)-mode mismatch 1.3⁺³²₋₁.₂×10⁻⁶ and all-mode mismatch 2.3⁺⁵⁹₋₂.₂×10⁻⁶; in-sample full-IMR SNR-weighted mismatch: 8.9×10⁻⁷–2.8×10⁻⁵ over 40–120 M⊙; waveform generation takes 12 ms (single CPU) or 0.39 ms per waveform (batch=4096); provides near-analytic derivatives and calibrated GP uncertainty.

Conclusion: NRHJSur3dq8 sets a new standard for NR surrogates by unifying high accuracy, computational speed, differentiability, and principled uncertainty quantification—enabling robust Bayesian inference, real-time parameter estimation, and gradient-based optimization in gravitational-wave astrophysics.

Abstract: We present NRHJSur3dq8, a family of numerical-relativity (NR) surrogates for non-eccentric aligned-spin binary-black-hole waveforms inspired by an action-angle parameterization. The model provides both the dynamics: the Hamiltonian values, azimuthal action $J_φ(Φ)$, the orbital clock $τ(Φ)$ as functions of its phasing, and the radiative pieces: the waveforms, their near-analytic derivatives, and modeling errors associated with each. The surrogated variables are conditioned into pieces that vary slowly on the radiation-reaction timescale, represented on shared $hp$-adaptive Chebyshev elements, and regressed across the intrinsic parameters with a Gaussian-process model. A time-parameterized merger-ringdown set of elements is attached where the adiabatic description breaks down. Leave-one-out validation of the central NRHJSur3dq8_AA flavor against NR reaches a $(2,2)$ unweighted mismatch of $1.3^{+32}_{-1.2}\times10^{-6}$ and an all-mode mismatch of $2.3^{+59}_{-2.2}\times10^{-6}$ over the full inspiral-merger-ringdown span. Sky-averaged and SNR-weighted over $32$ orientations per simulation, the released model's full-IMR in-sample mismatch against the NR simulations used for training is $8.9\times10^{-7}$ to $2.8\times10^{-5}$ across total masses $40$-$120\,M_\odot$. Waveforms are hybridized using post-Newtonian waveforms. Beyond accuracy, NRHJSur3dq8 provides two capabilities uncommon in NR surrogates: nearly analytic derivatives of the strain with respect to the physical parameters, and a calibrated Gaussian-process predictive uncertainty of the model. A full non-hybridized inspiral-merger-ringdown waveform evaluates in $12$ ms on a single CPU call. Batched evaluation amortizes this to $0.39$ ms per waveform at a batch of $4096$, measured at a starting frequency of $20$ Hz.

</details>

J. Zhu,W. D. Zhu,W. J. Zhu,W. Z. Zhu,Y. C. Zhu,Z. A. Zhu,X. Y. Zhuang,M. Zhuge,J. H. Zou,J. Zu*

Main category: hep-ex

TL;DR: First simultaneous measurement of weak decay parameters φ_Ω⁻ and φ_Ω̄⁺ in ψ(3686) → Ω⁻Ω̄⁺, yielding CP-sensitive observable φ_CP = −0.004 ± 0.055 ± 0.017 rad; also determines P–D wave weak/strong phase differences and Ω⁻–Ω̄⁺ polarization correlations.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To probe CP violation in the doubly-strange baryon sector via joint angular analysis of ψ(3686) → Ω⁻Ω̄⁺ decays, leveraging high-statistics BESIII data.

Method: Full angular distribution analysis using (2.71 ± 0.01) × 10⁹ ψ(3686) events from BESIII; simultaneous fit to Ω⁻ → K⁻Λ and Ω̄⁺ → K⁺Λ̄ decays to extract weak decay parameters, CP asymmetry φ_CP, and P–D wave phase differences (ξ_D − ξ_P, δ_D − δ_P); polarization correlations determined from spin-density matrix elements.

Result: φ_CP = (−0.004 ± 0.055 ± 0.017) rad; (ξ_D − ξ_P) = (−0.15 ± 2.25 ± 0.69) rad; (δ_D − δ_P) = (−0.97 ± 0.88 ± 0.34) rad; first observation of Ω⁻–Ω̄⁺ polarization correlations.

Conclusion: No significant CP violation observed in Ω⁻/Ω̄⁺ decays at current precision; results provide benchmark for QCD-inspired models and future high-precision tests of CP symmetry in hyperon decays.

Abstract: Using $(2.71 \pm 0.01) \times 10^9$ $ψ(3686)$ events collected with the BESIII detector, a joint full angular distribution analysis is carried out for the process $ψ(3686) \to Ω^-(\toΛK^-) \, \barΩ^{+}(\to \barΛK^+)$. The first simultaneous measurement of the weak decay parameters $φ_{Ω^{-}}$ and $φ_{\barΩ^{+}}$ for $Ω^- \to K^-Λ$ and $\barΩ^+ \to K^+\barΛ$ is performed, yielding the first result for the CP-sensitive observable, $φ_{\rm CP} = (-0.004 \pm 0.055 \pm 0.017)~\text{rad}$, where the first and second uncertainties are statistical and systematic, respectively. This further enables the extraction of the weak and strong phase differences between the $P$- and $D$-wave amplitudes: $(ξ_D - ξ_P) = (-0.15 \pm 2.25 \pm 0.69)~\text{rad}$ and $(δ_D - δ_P) = (-0.97 \pm 0.88 \pm 0.34)~\text{rad}$. Additionally, the polarization correlations between $Ω^{-}$ and $\barΩ^{+}$ are measured.

</details>


### [118] [Prospective study of light dark matter search in positron beam mode with DarkSHINE experiment initiative](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.05900)
*Xu-Liang Zhu,Shu Li,Kun Liu,Haidar Mus'ud Alfanda,Tong Sun,Haijun Yang,Yi-Fan Zhu*

Main category: hep-ex

TL;DR: DarkSHINE simulation study shows positron-on-target enhances dark photon (A′) invisible decay sensitivity via new annihilation channels, improving exclusion limits by ~100× over electron-on-target near threshold.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To improve sensitivity to invisibly decaying dark photons by exploiting additional production channels (t-channel and s-channel e⁺e⁻ annihilation) unique to positron-on-fixed-target setups, beyond conventional bremsstrahlung in electron-on-target experiments.

Method: Geant4-based full-detector simulation of 8 GeV positron beam on fixed target with 3×10¹⁴ POT events; analysis using missing energy/momentum signatures across three orthogonal signal regions optimized for bremsstrahlung, non-resonant t-channel, and resonant s-channel A′ production.

Result: Annihilation channels significantly enhance sensitivity to A′ → invisible decays, yielding exclusion limits ~100× stronger than current EOT constraints near the e⁺e⁻ center-of-mass energy threshold.

Conclusion: Positron-on-fixed-target is a highly competitive strategy for probing sub-GeV dark photons with invisible decays, especially near kinematic thresholds where annihilation channels dominate.

Abstract: We perform the prospective study on searching for invisibly decayed dark photons ($A^{\prime}$) in positron-on-fixed-target (POT) scheme with the DarkSHINE experiment simulation setup. In the positron-on-fixed-target scheme, two additional production channels of dark photons via annihilation with extranuclear electrons are present: non-resonant t-channel ($e^+e^-N \to γA^{\prime}N$) and resonant s-channel ($e^+e^-N \to A^{\prime}N$), alongside bremsstrahlung emission similar to electron-on-fixed-target scheme. Based on a Geant4 full detector simulation of an 8 GeV positron beam with $3 \times 10^{14}$ POT events, we developed an analysis strategy using missing energy and momentum signatures across three orthogonal signal regions optimized for these channels. Our results indicate that the additional annihilation channels of dark photon production improve the dark photon invisible decay search sensitivity, improving the exclusion limits by approximately two orders of magnitude compared to existing constraints in the electron-on-fixed target (EOT) scheme, particularly near the center-of-mass energy threshold.

</details>


### [119] [Likelihood-Based Unsupervised Anomaly Detection in CMS Dijet Events](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.06686)
*Bhavishya Chebrolu,Hitesh Rasineni,Prajwal Aaryan Immadi*

Main category: hep-ex

TL;DR: This paper introduces an unsupervised anomaly detection method using neural spline flows to model Standard Model dijet backgrounds directly from CMS Open Data; it identifies statistically robust, multivariate anomalies in jet substructure and event topology—without assuming a signal model—and demonstrates the approach’s sensitivity and stability for BSM exploration.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To enable model-independent, unsupervised searches for physics beyond the Standard Model in dijet events, circumventing limitations of traditional resonance-based or supervised approaches.

Method: Neural spline flow—a type of normalizing flow—is trained on high-dimensional collider observables (jet-, dijet-, and event-level) to estimate the background density; anomalies are defined as low-likelihood events; rigorous validation includes mass decorrelation, permutation null tests, feature-wise statistical comparisons, and training stability analysis.

Result: Identified anomalous events show consistent, multivariate deviations—especially in jet substructure—distributed across phase space without narrow mass peaks; anomalies are robust against known biases and pass all robustness checks.

Conclusion: Neural spline flow density estimation is a viable, model-agnostic tool for unsupervised anomaly detection in LHC data, sensitive to structured, rare deviations; it serves as a promising exploratory method for BSM physics, though no discovery claim is made.

Abstract: We present an unsupervised search for anomalous dijet events in proton--proton collision data using neural spline flow density estimation. A normalizing flow model is trained on a high-dimensional feature space comprising jet, dijet, and event-level observables to learn the dominant Standard Model background directly from data, without assuming a specific signal hypothesis. Events assigned low likelihood under the learned density are identified as potential anomalous events.
  Using this approach on a CMS Open Data dijet sample, we investigate extreme events in the tail of the anomaly-score distribution and perform an extensive validation and robustness study. This study includes feature-level statistical comparisons, mass decorrelation tests, permutation-based null tests, and evaluations of training stability. The selected anomalous events exhibit notable departures from the background-only expectation, primarily in jet-substructure observables, while remaining stable under several known sources of bias in unsupervised learning.
  The identified anomalies are distributed across the kinematic phase space and do not exhibit a narrow structure in the dijet invariant-mass spectrum. Instead, they show correlated deviations across multiple observables, consistent with a multivariate difference in jet substructure and event topology rather than a localized resonance. While no claim of new physics is made, this study demonstrates that neural spline flow-based density estimation can be sensitive to rare, structured deviations in collider data and may provide a model-independent exploratory tool for searches for physics beyond the Standard Model at the LHC.

</details>


### [120] [Neutron detector response modeling in NOvA](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.06956)
*NOvA Collaboration,S. Abubakar,M. A. Acero,B. Acharya,P. Adamson,N. Anfimov,A. Antoshkin,E. Arrieta-Diaz,L. Asquith,A. Aurisano,A. Back,N. Balashov,P. Baldi,B. A. Bambah,E. F. Bannister,A. Barros,J. Himmel,T. Horoho,X. Huang,T. Huynh,A. Ivanova,K. Kaess,I. Kakorin,A. Kalitkina,D. M. Kaplan,A. Khanam,B. Kirezli,J. Kleykamp,O. Klimov,L. W. Koerner,L. Kolupaeva,G. Kufatty,A. Kumar,C. D. Kuruppu,V.
