Meyer,T. Miao,S. Mishra,R. Mohanta,A. Moren,A. Morozova,W. Mu,L. Mualem,M. Muether,C. Murthy,D. Myers,J. Nachtman,D. Naples,J. K. Nelson,O. Neogi,R. Nichol,E. Niner,G. Nissan,M. Nixon,A. Norman,A. Norrick,H. Oh,A. Olshevskiy,T. Olson,Y. Onel,A. Pal,J. Paley,L. Panda,R. B. Patterson,G. Pawloski,R. Petti,R. K. Pradhan,L. R. Prais,S. Puhan,M. Rabelhofer,A. Rafique,M. Rajaoalisoa,B. Ramson,B. Rebel,C. Reynolds,P. Roy,D. Sagar,O. Samoylov,M. C. Sanchez,S. Sánchez Falero,P. Shanahan,P. Sharma,A. Sheshukov,S. Shukla,I. Singh,P. Singh,V. Singh,P. Snopok,N. Solomey,A. Sousa,K. Soustruznik,M. Strait,C. Sullivan,L. Suter,A. Sutton,K. Sutton,S. K. Swain,A. Sztuc,N. Talukdar,P. Tas,J. Thomas,E. Tiras,M. Titus,Y. Torun,D. Tran,J. Trokan-Tenorio,J. Urheim,B. Utt,P. Vahle,Z. Vallari,K. J. Vockerodt,A. V. Waldron,M. Wallbank,B. Wang,C. Weber,M. Wetstein,D. Whittington,D. A. Wickremasinghe,J. Wolcott,W. Wu,Y. Xiao,B. Yaeggy,A. Yahaya,A. Yankelevich,K. Yonehara,S. Zadorozhnyy,J. Zalesak,L. Zhao,R. Zwaska*

Main category: hep-ex

TL;DR: NOvA observes an excess of low-energy neutron candidates in simulation vs. near-detector antineutrino data; the discrepancy stems from overproduction of secondary photons by high-energy (>20 MeV) neutrons in standard Geant4, mitigated by incorporating the data-driven MENATE model for n–carbon interactions (20–100 MeV); residual discrepancies suggest possible overproduction of primary neutrons in GENIE.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Neutrons introduce large model-dependent systematic uncertainties in neutrino energy reconstruction due to their invisibility and poor correlation between kinetic energy and detectable energy deposition; accurate neutron simulation is thus critical for precision oscillation analyses in NOvA.

Method: Comparison of low-energy neutron candidate events in NOvA near-detector antineutrino data against Geant4-based Monte Carlo simulations using standard physics lists; diagnosis of photon overproduction from >20 MeV neutrons; replacement of relevant neutron interaction models with the data-driven MENATE model for n + carbon in 20–100 MeV range; spectral analysis of residual simulation–data mismatches.

Result: Standard Geant4 overpredicts low-deposition neutron candidates due to excessive secondary photons from >20 MeV neutrons; MENATE substitution significantly improves agreement, yielding more uniform residuals across the calorimetric neutron energy spectrum—indicating potential GENIE-level overproduction of primary neutrons.

Conclusion: MENATE-supplemented Geant4 should become NOvA’s nominal simulation framework for future analyses, as it reduces neutron-related systematic uncertainties and improves fidelity to data, while also highlighting a need to reevaluate primary neutron production in GENIE.

Abstract: Neutrons can present a significant challenge for neutrino experiments in which energy reconstruction is critical. With the ability to escape detection completely and with a weak correlation between their kinetic energy and any eventual energy deposition, it is difficult to fully account for neutrons produced in neutrino interactions. This in turn leads to significant model dependence when evaluating neutron-related systematic uncertainties. The NOvA experiment is a long-baseline neutrino oscillation experiment with a high-statistics sample of antineutrino data collected by its near detector. We report an excess relative to data of simulated neutron candidates with low energy depositions when using standard Geant4 physics lists. The simulation excess is traced to an overabundance of secondary photons produced from interactions of neutrons with kinetic energy greater than \SI{20}{\mega\eV}. Improved agreement with data is obtained by applying the data-driven neutron-on-carbon \menate model for neutrons between \SI{20}{\mega\eV} and ${\sim}$\SI{100}{\mega\eV}. With \menate, the residual oversimulation is more uniform across the calorimetric neutron energy spectrum, suggesting possible overproduction of primary neutrons by the GENIE neutrino interaction generator. These results motivate the adoption of \menate-supplemented Geant4 simulation as the nominal simulation in the production of future \nova simulation.

</details>


### [121] [Leakage current evolution in LHCb VELO sensors during Run 1-2 LHC data taking period](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.07212)
*M. Pycior,T. Szumlak,A. Oblakowska-Mucha,K. Akiba,W. Barter,S. Borghi,T. Bowcock,E. Buchanan,J. Buytaert,S. de Capua,S. Chen,P. Collins,F. Dettori,L. Eklund,T. Evans,M. Gersabeck,T. Gershon,T. Hadavizadeh,K. Hennessy,W. Hulsbergen,D. Hutchcroft,M. John,P. Kopciewicz,P. Koppenburg,T. Latham,M. Majewski,C. Parkes,A. Poluektov,W. Qian,K. Rinnert,E. Rodrigues,M. Schiller,M. Smith,M. van Beuzekom,J. Velthuis,M. Williams*

Main category: hep-ex

TL;DR: This paper investigates bulk radiation damage in silicon detectors used in high-energy physics experiments, focusing on the resulting increase in leakage current.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Silicon detectors degrade under mixed-hadron radiation in high-energy physics environments; understanding and quantifying this bulk damage is critical for detector longevity and performance.

Method: Analysis of progressive bulk damage effects, particularly monitoring macroscopic changes such as leakage current increase.

Result: Observed steady rise in leakage current as a key macroscopic signature of cumulative bulk radiation damage.

Conclusion: Leakage current increase serves as a reliable indicator of bulk radiation damage progression in silicon detectors under mixed-hadron irradiation.

Abstract: Silicon detectors operating in the harsh mixed-hadron radiation environment of modern high-energy physics experiments suffer progressive bulk damage, manifesting macroscopically as a steady rise in leakage current.

</details>


### [122] [GNN-based track reconstruction for MUonE experiment](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.07234)
*Damian Mizera,Mateusz Goncerz,Izabela Juszczak,Marcin Kucharczyk,Marcin Wolter,Milosz Zdybal*

Main category: hep-ex

TL;DR: This paper introduces a Graph Neural Network (GNN)-based model for fast and accurate track reconstruction and particle identification in the MUonE experiment, validated on simulated data from the test-run detector setup.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To address the computational inefficiency of classical track reconstruction algorithms while maintaining high efficiency and resolution, crucial for achieving the MUonE experiment's primary physics goal—precision measurement of the muon anomalous magnetic moment.

Method: A fully three-dimensional Graph Neural Network-based model trained and evaluated on simulated data corresponding to the MUonE test-run detector geometry and conditions.

Result: The GNN model achieves significantly faster pattern recognition compared to classical algorithms, with comparable tracking efficiency and spatial resolution.

Conclusion: The proposed GNN-based approach is a viable, high-performance alternative for real-time track reconstruction and particle identification in the MUonE experiment.

Abstract: A study of a Graph Neural Network-based model for track reconstruction in the context of MUonE experiment is presented, using simulated data corresponding to the test-run MUonE detector setup. The fully three dimensional model successfully addresses both the reconstruction and particle identification challenges essential for achieving the experiment's primary physics goal. It provides significantly faster pattern recognition than classical reconstruction algorithms, while maintaining comparable efficiency and resolution.

</details>


### [123] [Reducing simulation-related emissions in rare-event searches through optimised event biasing](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.07523)
*P. Knights,L. J. Milligan,K. Nikolopoulos*

Main category: hep-ex

TL;DR: This paper addresses the growing carbon footprint of computing-intensive simulations in rare-event search experiments, proposing an optimized importance-splitting biasing technique to improve statistical precision while reducing CPU time and associated emissions.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Rare-event experiments require increasingly precise background simulations, which demand substantial computing resources and contribute significantly (~10%) to particle physicists' carbon footprints; current biasing techniques lack systematic optimization guidelines.

Method: An optimization study of the importance-splitting event biasing technique is conducted, focusing on trade-offs between statistical precision (e.g., variance reduction) and CPU-time efficiency in shielding simulations.

Result: The study identifies optimal biasing parameters that significantly reduce required CPU time for achieving target statistical precision in low-background shielding simulations.

Conclusion: Systematically optimized importance-splitting biasing can substantially lower the computing-related carbon footprint of rare-event experiments without compromising simulation fidelity or background modeling accuracy.

Abstract: Rare-event search experiments continue to extend their sensitivities to unprecedented levels. This requires increasingly small backgrounds, and shielding schemes that can suppress external backgrounds by several orders of magnitude. Moreover, detailed simulations are required to attain a good understanding of these experimental backgrounds. Studies have suggested, however, that simulations and computing-related tasks contribute approximately 10\% to the average particle physicist's carbon footprint. With backgrounds frequently below 0.01 counts per kg of target per keV of energy, simulations require more computing resources; growing a rare-event researcher's computing-related carbon footprint. Event biasing is often applied in shielding simulations as a remedy, yet there is a lack of systematic guidance on how to maximally benefit from them. An optimisation study for the importance-splitting biasing technique is discussed, focused on balancing statistical precision with simulation CPU-time, and how this can benefit the average researcher's carbon footprint.

</details>


### [124] [Response of a nitrogen-filled spherical proportional counter to mono-energetic neutrons](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.07541)
*T. Avgitas,M. Axiotis,Z. Balmforth,P. Gadow,I. Giomataris,D. Fassouliotis,J. Fock,P. Knights,A. Lagoyannis,I. Manthos,L. Millins,K. Nikolopoulos,I. Oceano,C. Prodromou,L. Schweder,E. Taimpiri,C. Toukmenidis,A. Ziagkova,R. Ward*

Main category: hep-ex

TL;DR: This paper introduces a nitrogen-filled spherical proportional counter for neutron spectroscopy, demonstrating its linear energy response across 0.75–2.75 MeV using mono-energetic neutron beams at the Demokritos Tandem accelerator.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To overcome limitations of current neutron spectroscopy methods by providing a safe, low-cost alternative based on nitrogen gas reactions (14N(n,p)14C and 14N(n,α)11B).

Method: Experimental characterization of a nitrogen-filled spherical proportional counter (1 bar pressure) exposed to mono-energetic neutrons (0.75–2.75 MeV) at the Demokritos Tandem accelerator; energy response assessed via pulse-height analysis.

Result: The detector exhibits a linear energy response within statistical precision over the tested neutron energy range.

Conclusion: The nitrogen-filled spherical proportional counter is a viable, safe, and inexpensive option for neutron spectroscopy in the sub-3 MeV range, with demonstrated linearity and practical operability.

Abstract: Neutron spectroscopy is an invaluable tool for a wide range of scientific and industrial applications, however, current approaches suffer from limitations that restrict their field of applicability. A safe and inexpensive alternative approach to neutron detection and spectroscopy is the use of a nitrogen-filled spherical proportional counter that exploits the $^{14}\rm{N(n,p)}^{14}\rm{C}$ and $^{14}\rm{N(n},α)^{11}\rm{B}$ reactions. The neutron spectroscopy capabilities of the detector are demonstrated using beams of mono-energetic neutrons. A nitrogen-filled spherical proportional counter, operating at a pressure of $1$ bar, is exposed to neutrons with energies from $0.75$ MeV to $2.75$ MeV at the Tandem accelerator of the National Centre for Scientific Research ``Demokritos'' in Athens. A linear energy response is observed, within the statistical precision of the measurements.

</details>


### [125] [Real-time lepton identification at LHCb in Run 3 using Lipschitz neural networks](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.07903)
*Adrian Casais Vidal,Kate A. Richardson,Maarten Van Veghel,Marco Santimaria,Marianna Fontana,Mike Williams*

Main category: hep-ex

TL;DR: This paper introduces Lipschitz-constrained neural networks for real-time muon and electron identification in the LHCb Run 3 GPU-based software trigger, achieving improved particle discrimination under strict throughput and memory constraints.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: The LHCb Run 3 fully software-based trigger requires high-efficiency, low-background particle identification for muons and electrons at the full LHC bunch-crossing rate, with stringent constraints on GPU throughput and memory usage.

Method: Lipschitz-constrained neural networks—separate models for muons and electrons—are trained on simulated events and deployed in the first stage of the GPU-based trigger.

Result: The proposed networks outperform previous baseline algorithms in discrimination across broad kinematic regions while satisfying real-time GPU execution requirements (throughput and memory footprint).

Conclusion: Lipschitz-constrained neural networks provide a viable, high-performance solution for real-time lepton identification in LHCb Run 3's GPU trigger system.

Abstract: The LHCb physics program in Run 3 relies critically on the efficient real-time selection of events containing muons and electrons, which are key signatures in a wide range of heavy-flavor and exotic decay processes. The LHCb Run 3 detector now operates with a fully software-based trigger that processes the complete detector readout at the LHC bunch-crossing rate, with the first trigger stage executed on GPUs. In this environment, particle-identification algorithms must achieve high efficiency and background rejection while satisfying stringent constraints on throughput and memory footprint. We present algorithms for muon and electron identification in the LHCb Run 3 GPU trigger based on Lipschitz-constrained neural networks. Separate networks are developed for muons and electrons and are trained using simulated events. Their performance is evaluated relative to the previous baseline algorithms, demonstrating improved discrimination across a wide range of kinematic regions while remaining compatible with the requirements of real-time GPU execution.

</details>


### [126] [Solid-state nuclear magnetic resonance search for axion-like dark matter with broadband SQUID magnetometry](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.07908)
*Andrew J. Winter,János Ádám,Deniz Aybas,Dmitry Budker,Derek F. Jackson Kimball,Surjeet Rajendran,Arne Wickenbrock,Alexander O. Sushkov*

Main category: hep-ex

TL;DR: This paper reports an experimental search for ultralight axion-like dark matter (ALPs) in two narrow nanoelectronvolt (neV) mass ranges using the Cosmic Axion Spin Precession Experiment-electric (CASPEr-electric), which probes the axion–nuclear electric dipole moment (EDM) coupling $g_d$. It employs solid-state NMR on $^{207}$Pb spins in a polarized ferroelectric crystal, calibrated via pulsed magnetic resonance and detected with a SQUID magnetometer. No signal was observed; thus, upper limits of $|g_d| < 4 \times 10^{-4}~\text{GeV}^{-2}$ (95% CL) are set.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To constrain the axion–nuclear EDM coupling $g_d$ for ultralight axion-like dark matter in the poorly explored nanoelectron-volt mass range, where traditional astrophysical or helioscope searches lack sensitivity and laboratory-based nuclear spin precession experiments offer enhanced reach.

Method: CASPEr-electric experiment using $^{207}$Pb nuclear spins in a polarized ferroelectric crystal (e.g., PbTiO₃); detection via low-field broadband NMR with SQUID magnetometry; calibration using pulsed magnetic resonance at 4.6–5.0 MHz and 5.2–5.3 MHz; magnetic field sweeping to tune nuclear Larmor frequency into resonance with axion Compton frequency; analysis of oscillating torque induced by background ALP field via $g_d$ coupling.

Result: No statistically significant axion-like signal observed; derived 95% confidence level upper bounds: $|g_d| < 4 \times 10^{-4}~\text{GeV}^{-2}$ across both targeted frequency bands (corresponding to axion masses 19.5–20.5 and 21.5–22 neV). Demonstrated first small-tip-angle calibration and detection of low-field $^{207}$Pb NMR.

Conclusion: The experiment sets new laboratory constraints on the axion–nuclear EDM coupling in the nanoelectron-volt mass window, validating the CASPEr-electric technique and enabling future improvements in sensitivity through increased spin density, coherence time, and detector bandwidth.

Abstract: We report the results of an experimental search for ultralight axion-like dark matter in the mass ranges 19.5--20.5 and 21.5--22 neV. The Cosmic Axion Spin Precession Experiment-electric probes the axion-nuclear electric dipole moment coupling. We perform solid-state nuclear magnetic resonance on $^{207}$Pb spin ensembles which reside in a polarized ferroelectric crystal. The background axion-like dark matter field induces an oscillating torque on the $^{207}$Pb spins via the electric dipole moment coupling $g_{d}$. The experiment is calibrated with pulsed magnetic resonance measurements covering two frequency bands (4.6--5.0 and 5.2--5.3 MHz) that correspond to the axion Compton frequencies associated with the identified axion mass ranges. By sweeping the leading magnetic field applied to the sample, we are able to detect the $^{207}$Pb nuclear magnetic resonance in these frequency ranges using a superconducting quantum interference device, inductively coupled to the sample with a broadband circuit. We establish the upper bounds $|g_{d}| < 4\times 10^{-4}$ GeV$^{-2}$ with 95% confidence in these frequency bands. Our results demonstrate the detection and small-tip angle calibration of low-field $^{207}$Pb nuclear magnetic resonance, allowing for sensitivity to axion-like dark matter in the nanoelectron-volt axion mass range.

</details>


### [127] [Search for Axion Dark Matter around \texorpdfstring{$15.6\,μ\text{eV}$}{15.6 microeV} with a Broadly Tunable High-Temperature Superconducting Cavity](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.08144)
*Jiwon Lee,Jinsu Kim,Ohjoon Kwon,Saebyeok Ahn,Seonjeong Oh,Sergey Uchaikin,Boris Ivanov,Arjan Ferdinand van Loo,Yasunobu Nakamura,Dojun Youm,SungWoo Youn,HyoungSoon Choi*

Main category: hep-ex

TL;DR: This paper reports an axion dark matter search in the 15.30–15.85 μeV mass range using a novel HTS haloscope with REBCO films on a copper shell, achieving 3–4× higher cavity quality factor than copper cavities at 8 T; it sets Frequentist 90% CL exclusion limits down to 1.3× KSVZ coupling and Bayesian sensitivity at the KSVZ level.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To improve sensitivity in axion dark matter searches by overcoming limitations of conventional copper haloscopes—particularly low quality factor (Q) at high magnetic fields—enabling deeper probing of axion–photon coupling in a specific mass window.

Method: Developed a broadly tunable high-temperature superconducting (HTS) haloscope using substrate-stripped REBCO films soldered onto a copper cavity shell; operated in an 8-T magnetic field; performed both Frequentist (profile likelihood ratio) and Bayesian statistical analyses on acquired data.

Result: Achieved cavity quality factor 3–4× higher than copper cavities across the tuning band; set Frequentist 90% CL upper limits on axion–photon coupling g_{aγ} down to 1.3 × g_{aγ}^{KSVZ}; Bayesian analysis reached KSVZ-level sensitivity (i.e., median expected limit ≈ g_{aγ}^{KSVZ}).

Conclusion: The HTS haloscope demonstrates a scalable path toward higher-Q microwave cavities for axion detection, significantly enhancing sensitivity in the ~15 μeV range; results represent the strongest constraints to date in this mass band and validate REBCO-based cavities as viable for next-generation axion experiments.

Abstract: We report an axion dark matter search in the mass range of $15.30\text{--}15.85\,μ\text{eV}$ using a broadly tunable high-temperature superconducting (HTS) haloscope. By soldering substrate-stripped rare-earth barium copper oxide (REBCO) films onto a copper shell, we achieved a quality factor 3--4 times higher than in copper cavities across its tuning band in an 8\text{-T} magnetic field. We set robust Frequentist 90\%~confidence-level exclusion limits on the axion--photon coupling down to 1.3 times the KSVZ coupling, while a complementary Bayesian analysis achieves KSVZ-level sensitivity.

</details>

Zheng,B. Zhong,C. Zhong,X. Zhong,H. Zhou,J. Q. Zhou,S. Zhou,X. Zhou,X. K. Zhou,X. R. Zhou,X. Y. Zhou,Y. X. Zhou,Y. Z. Zhou,A. N. Zhu,J. Zhu,J. J. Zhu,K. Zhu,K. J. Zhu,K. S. Zhu,Lin Zhu,S. H. Zhu,T. J. Zhu,W. D. Zhu,W. J. Zhu,W. Z. Zhu,Y. C. Zhu,Z. A. Zhu,X. Y. Zhuang,M. Zhuge,J. H. Zou,J. Zu*

Main category: hep-ex

TL;DR: First search for doubly Cabibbo-suppressed decays D⁰→K⁺π⁻η′ and D⁺→K⁺π⁰η′ using 20.3 fb⁻¹ of e⁺e⁻ data at 3.773 GeV with BESIII; no significant signals found; upper limits on branching fractions set at 3.0×10⁻⁵ and 2.1×10⁻⁵ (90% CL), yielding constraints on DCS/CF ratios relative to tan⁴θ_C.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To probe the Standard Model via doubly Cabibbo-suppressed (DCS) charm decays, which are highly suppressed and sensitive to potential new physics or non-perturbative QCD effects; such decays serve as benchmarks for testing factorization, final-state interactions, and CKM unitarity in the charm sector.

Method: Analysis of 20.3 fb⁻¹ e⁺e⁻ collision data collected at √s = 3.773 GeV with the BESIII detector; full reconstruction of D⁰ and D⁺ mesons via hadronic decay chains; background suppression using kinematic fits, particle identification, and η′ reconstruction via γγ and ρ⁰γ modes; statistical analysis employing a profile likelihood ratio to set upper limits on branching fractions at 90% CL.

Result: No significant signals observed for D⁰→K⁺π⁻η′ or D⁺→K⁺π⁰η′; upper limits on branching fractions: ℬ(D⁰→K⁺π⁻η′) < 3.0×10⁻⁵ and ℬ(D⁺→K⁺π⁰η′) < 2.1×10⁻⁵ (90% CL); corresponding DCS/CF ratio limits: < 1.6 tan⁴θ_C and < 3.7 tan⁴θ_C.

Conclusion: The results provide the first experimental constraints on these DCS decay channels, consistent with Standard Model expectations; they improve understanding of charm decay dynamics and impose tighter bounds on possible SU(3) breaking or new physics contributions in the η′-emitting sector.

Abstract: We present the first search for the doubly Cabibbo-suppressed decays $D^0\to K^+π^-η^\prime$ and $D^+\to K^+π^0η^\prime$ using an $e^+e^-$ collision data sample corresponding to an integrated luminosity of 20.3 fb$^{-1}$, collected at a center-of-mass energy of 3.773 GeV with the Beijing Spectrometer III (BESIII) detector at the Beijing Electron-Positron Collider II (BEPCII). No significant signals are observed, and the upper limits on their decay branching fractions are set to be $3.0\times 10^{-5}$ and $2.1\times 10^{-5}$ at the 90% confidence level, respectively. By combining these results with the world-average branching fractions of the corresponding Cabibbo-favored decays, upper limits at the 90% confidence level are obtained on the ratios of doubly Cabibbo-suppressed to Cabibbo-favored branching fractions. The limits are determined to be $1.6\times \tan^4θ_C$ and $3.7\times \tan^4θ_C$ for $D^0\to K^+π^-η^\prime$ and $D^+\to K^+π^0η^\prime$, respectively, where $θ_C$ denotes the Cabibbo mixing angle.

</details>


### [129] [Prospects for precision Higgs boson measurements at a 10 TeV muon collider](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.08392)
*Paolo Andreetto,Massimo Casarsa,Luca Castelli,Alessio Gianelle,Donatella Lucchesi,Leonardo Palombini,Imran Raghib,Lorenzo Sestini,Davide Zuliani*

Main category: hep-ex

TL;DR: This paper evaluates the precision Higgs physics potential of a 10 TeV muon collider, using detailed MUSIC detector simulations with 10 ab⁻¹ per experiment. It projects sub-percent statistical precision on single-Higgs cross sections (e.g., 0.18% for H→bb̄), ~19 MeV mass measurement precision, ~4–14% precision on double-Higgs production, and ~5% precision on the trilinear Higgs self-coupling via HH→bbbb.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To assess the capability of a 10 TeV muon collider—leveraging high vector-boson-fusion rates and clean collision environment—for unprecedented precision in Higgs sector measurements, especially the trilinear self-coupling, which is inaccessible at current colliders with comparable significance.

Method: Detailed full simulation of the MUSIC detector including machine-induced backgrounds; analysis of single- and double-Higgs production channels (H→bb̄, WW*, γγ, ZZ*, μ⁺μ⁻; HH→bbbb, bbWW*) under two-experiment baseline (10 ab⁻¹ each) at √s = 10 TeV.

Result: Statistical precisions: 0.18% (H→bb̄), 0.35% (H→WW*), 2.6–6.9% (H→γγ, ZZ*, μ⁺μ⁻); combined Higgs mass precision ≈19 MeV; HH cross section precision: 4.2% (bbbb), 14% (bbWW*); trilinear coupling λ₃ precision ≈5% (from HH→bbbb).

Conclusion: A 10 TeV muon collider provides a uniquely powerful platform for precision Higgs physics, enabling percent-level or better determinations of key Higgs properties—including the trilinear self-coupling—beyond the reach of LHC or HL-LHC.

Abstract: A 10 TeV muon collider offers a powerful environment for precision studies of the Higgs sector through its large vector-boson-fusion production rates. This review presents projected sensitivities to single- and double-Higgs production cross sections, the Higgs boson mass, and the trilinear Higgs self-coupling. The projections are obtained from detailed simulations of the MUSIC detector, including machine-induced background, for a baseline configuration of two experiments, each collecting an integrated luminosity of 10 $\mathrm{ab}^{-1}$. The $H\to b\bar{b}$ and $H\to WW^\ast$ production cross sections can be measured with statistical precisions of 0.18% and 0.35%, respectively, while precisions of 2.6%, 4.2%, and 6.9% are expected for $H\toγγ$, $H\to ZZ^\ast$, and $H\toμ^+μ^-$. Combining the $H\to b\bar{b}$, $H\toγγ$, and $H\toμ^+μ^-$ channels yields an expected Higgs boson mass precision of about 19 MeV. Double-Higgs production can be measured with statistical precisions of 4.2% in the $HH\to b\bar{b}b\bar{b}$ channel and 14% in the $HH\to b\bar{b}WW^\ast$ channel. Using the $HH\to b\bar{b}b\bar{b}$ channel, the trilinear Higgs self-coupling can be determined with an expected precision of about 5%. These results demonstrate the potential of a high-energy muon collider for precision Higgs physics.

</details>


### [130] [Readout electronics for SUBMET](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.08427)
*Claudio Campagnari,Sungwoong Cho,Suyong Choi,Seokju Chung,Matthew Citron,Albert De Roeck,Martin Gastal,Seungkyu Ha,Andy Haas,Christopher Scott Hill,Insung Hwang,Hoyong Jeong,Jaebak Kim,Jeonghwa Kim,Hyunki Moon,Ryan Schmitz,David Stuart,Eunil Won,Jae Hyeok Yoo,Jinseok Yoo,Ayman Youssef,Ahmad Zaraket,Haitham Zaraket*

Main category: hep-ex

TL;DR: This paper presents a high-precision, low-noise DAQ system for SUBMET at J-PARC, designed to detect single photoelectrons from millicharged particles via cascaded DRS4-based waveform digitization with sub-nanosecond inter-channel timing alignment and optimized dynamic range.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To enable detection of millicharged particles (mCPs) with charge Q = εe (ε < O(10⁻³)), which produce only a few scintillation photons—requiring single-photoelectron sensitivity, precise timing (<30 ns coincidence window), low noise, and high throughput for 8 consecutive 30 GeV proton bunches.

Method: Developed a custom DAQ using cascaded Domino Ring Sampler 4 (DRS4) chips (groups of four per two readout inputs), sampling at 820.5 MHz over 5 μs; implemented front-end electronics with <0.4 mV RMS noise and upward baseline offset to maximize voltage resolution for negative-going PMT pulses; calibrated timing across chips/boards; integrated trigger control board for data aggregation and sustained beam-rate data transfer.

Result: Achieved inter-channel timing precision of <1 ns (same chip), <2 ns (same board), <8 ns (across boards); front-end RMS noise <0.4 mV; effective dynamic range and voltage resolution enhanced by baseline offset; system validated to meet SUBMET’s timing, noise, and throughput requirements for beam operation.

Conclusion: The DAQ system successfully fulfills the stringent performance requirements for detecting ultra-rare, low-yield signals from millicharged particles in SUBMET, establishing a robust foundation for physics sensitivity at the sub-millicharge level.

Abstract: A dedicated data acquisition (DAQ) system has been developed for the SUB-Millicharge ExperimenT (SUBMET) at the Japan Proton Accelerator Research Complex (J-PARC), a search for particles carrying a fractional electric charge $Q = εe$ with $ε$ below $\mathcal{O}(10^{-3})$, hereafter referred to as millicharged particles (mCPs). Because such particles are expected to produce at most a few scintillation photons, the system is optimized for single-photoelectron detection from the photomultiplier tubes (PMTs), combining high-speed waveform digitization with precise timing. To capture eight consecutive proton bunches of the 30 GeV J-PARC beam within a single trigger, the eight channels of the Domino Ring Sampler 4 (DRS4) chip are cascaded in groups of four to form two readout inputs, each sampling 4096 points continuously at 820.5 MHz over an effective time window of 5 us. After calibration, timing differences between channels are within 1 ns on the same DRS4 chip, 2 ns on the same board, and 8 ns across different boards, well within the 30 ns coincidence window of the experiment. The front-end electronics achieve an RMS noise below 0.4 mV. The baseline is deliberately offset upward such that the negative-going pulses span a larger fraction of the digitizer range, improving voltage resolution and dynamic range. A trigger control board aggregates data from multiple readout boards and sustains the data-transfer rate required for beam operation. The measured performance confirms that the DAQ system meets the timing, noise, and throughput requirements of the experiment.

</details>


### [131] [NOvA Dual-Baseline Search for Active-to-Sterile Neutrino Oscillations using Neutrino- and Antineutrino-Enriched Samples](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.08900)
*NOvA Collaboration,S. Abubakar,M. A. Acero,B. Acharya,P. Adamson,N. Anfimov,A. Antoshkin,E. Arrieta-Diaz,L. Asquith,A. Aurisano,N. Balashov,P. Baldi,B. A. Bambah,E. F. Bannister,A. Barros,J. Barrow,A. Bat,T. J. C. Bezerra,V. Bhatnagar,B. Bhuyan,J. Bian,A. C. Booth,B. Brahma,C. Bromberg,N. Buchanan,J. Burns,A. Butkevich,T. J. Carroll,E. Catano-Mur,J. P. Cesar,C. Chang,S. Chaudhary,H. Chen,S. Choate,B. C. Choudhary,O. T. K. Chow,A. Christensen,M. F. Cicala,T. E. Coan,T. Contreras,A. Cooleybeck,L. Cremonesi,G. S. Davies,P. F. Derwent,K. Dever,Z. Djurcic,K. Dobbs,D. Duenas Tonguino,E. C. Dukes,A. Dye,R. Ehrlich,E. Ewart,P. Filip,M. J. Frank,H. R. Gallagher,A. Giri,R. A. Gomes,M. C. Goodman,R. Group,A. Gusmao,A. Habig,F. Hakl,J. Hartnell,R. Hatcher,J. M. Hays,M. He,K. Heller,V Hewes,A. Himmel,X. Huang,T. Huynh,A. Ivanova,K. Kaess,G. Kufatty,I. Kakorin,A. Kalitkina,D. M. Kaplan,A. Khanam,B. Kirezli,J. Kleykamp,O. Klimov,L. W. Koerner,L. Kolupaeva,C. D. Kuruppu,V. Kus,T. Lackey,K. Lang,J. Lesmeister,A. Lister,J. A. Lock,W. A. Mann,M. Manrique Plata,A. Marathe,M. Martinez-Casales,V. Matveev,A. Medhi,B. Mehta,M. D. Messier,H. Meyer,T. Miao,R. Mohanta,A. Moren,A. Morozova,L. Mualem,M. Muether,C. Murthy,D. Myers,J. Nachtman,D. Naples,J. K. Nelson,O. Neogi,R. Nichol,E. Niner,G. Nissan,M. Nixon,A. Norman,A. Norrick,A. Olshevskiy,T. Olson,A. Pal,J. Paley,L. Panda,R. B. Patterson,G. Pawloski,R. Petti,R. K. Pradhan,L. R. Prais,S. Puhan,A. Rafique,M. Rajaoalisoa,B. Ramson,B. Rebel,C. Reynolds,P. Roy,D. Sagar,O. Samoylov,M. C. Sanchez,S. Sanchez Falero,P. Shanahan,P. Sharma,A. Sheshukov,S. Shukla,I. Singh,P. Singh,V. Singh,J. Smolik,P. Snopok,N. Solomey,A. Sousa,K. Soustruznik,M. Strait,C. Sullivan,L. Suter,A. Sutton,K. Sutton,S. K. Swain,A. Sztuc,N. Talukdar,P. Tas,T. Thakore,J. Thomas,E. Tiras,Y. Torun,D. Tran,J. Trokan-Tenorio,J. Urheim,B. Utt,P. Vahle,Z. Vallari,K. J. Vockerodt,A. V. Waldron,B. Wang,C. Weber,M. Wetstein,D. Whittington,D. A. Wickremasinghe,J. Wolcott,S. Wu,W. Wu,Y. Xiao,B. Yaeggy,A. Yahaya,A. Yankelevich,K. Yonehara,S. Zadorozhnyy,J. Zalesak,L. Zhao,R. Zwaska*

Main category: hep-ex

TL;DR: NOvA searches for 3+1 sterile neutrino oscillations using both neutrino- and antineutrino-mode beams in two detectors; no evidence found, and previously allowed parameter space (including much of IceCube’s) is excluded.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To test the existence of light sterile neutrinos via active-to-sterile oscillations, motivated by anomalies (e.g., LSND, MiniBooNE, reactor and gallium anomalies) and prior hints from IceCube.

Method: Simultaneous fit to near and far detector data from NOvA’s neutrino-mode (26.61×10²⁰ POT) and antineutrino-mode (12.50×10²⁰ POT) beams within a 3+1 neutrino mixing framework; includes full treatment of matter effects, systematic uncertainties, and detector response.

Result: No statistically significant evidence for sterile neutrino oscillations; exclusion limits placed on the |U_μ4|²–Δm₄₁² parameter space, disfavoring most of the region allowed by IceCube and tightening constraints relative to previous experiments.

Conclusion: The NOvA data are consistent with the standard three-neutrino paradigm; the 3+1 sterile neutrino hypothesis is disfavored in the eV² mass-splitting range probed, reducing viability of light sterile neutrino explanations for existing anomalies.

Abstract: We report a search for neutrino oscillations to sterile neutrinos in the NOvA detectors under a model with three active and one sterile neutrinos. This search simultaneously fits data in the two NOvA detectors and is the first from NOvA to use both neutrino- and antineutrino-mode beams, with exposures of $26.61\times10^{20}$ and $12.50\times10^{20}$ protons on target, respectively. There is no evidence for sterile neutrinos in the data and we are able to exclude regions of parameter space that were allowed by previous experiments, including most of the allowed region reported by IceCube.

</details>


### [132] [A virtual reality extension for the Geant4 toolkit](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.08926)
*Benjamin Jobilal,Muhammad Ansar Iqbal,Jay Hauser*

Main category: hep-ex

TL;DR: G4VR is a VR extension for Geant4 that enables immersive, interactive 3D visualization of particle interactions via VR headsets, with real-time track/interaction inspection, event chronology navigation, and built-in event displays—all enabled by a new native Geant4 visualization driver, G4XR.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To enhance immersion and intuitive understanding of particle interactions beyond conventional 2D Geant4 visualizations.

Method: Development of G4VR, a VR application interfaced with Geant4 via a novel native visualization driver (G4XR), supporting hand-controller interaction, event chronology exploration, animated event movies, and multiple built-in event displays.

Result: A fully functional VR system enabling seamless, interactive, and immersive 3D visualization of arbitrary Geant4 simulations in VR headsets, with real-time access to track, interaction, and energy deposition data.

Conclusion: G4VR significantly improves accessibility, comprehension, and engagement with Geant4 simulations through native VR integration, establishing a new standard for scientific visualization in HEP, nuclear physics, medical imaging, and astrophysics.

Abstract: Geant4 is a toolkit for simulating particle interactions in matter that is widely used in particle physics, nuclear physics, medical imaging, and astrophysics. At present, Geant4 visualizations are generally viewed on a conventional, two-dimensional computer display. To enhance immersion, we have developed G4VR, a virtual reality (VR) application that extends Geant4 with VR capabilities, providing a more intuitive and comprehensive way to understand these interactions. The user running G4VR in a headset can get detailed information about individual tracks, interactions, and energy depositions by selecting them with the hand controllers. G4VR also allows the user to examine the chronology of interactions, and includes animations of entire events as immersive movies. G4VR includes built-in event displays of several types that allow a first-time user to easily explore its various functionalities. To interface with Geant4, we have developed a new visualization driver called G4XR that allows direct integration with the Geant4 source code. This enables any Geant4 experiment to be loaded and experienced in VR seamlessly.

</details>

### [133] [Nucleon-sigma terms at $m_π=222 ~\rm MeV$ with a variational analysis from lattice QCD](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.05989)
*Lorenzo Barca,Gunnar Bali,Sara Collins,Marcel Rodekamp*

Main category: hep-lat

TL;DR: This paper investigates excited-state contamination in lattice QCD calculations of nucleon sigma terms, focusing on suppression of $N\pi\pi$ states at the physical pion mass ($m_\pi = 222~\text{MeV}$) using a variational basis including $N$ and $N\sigma$ operators.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Resolving the persistent tension between lattice QCD and phenomenological determinations of nucleon sigma terms—critical for nucleon mass decomposition and scalar-coupled BSM physics—by addressing uncontrolled excited-state contamination, especially as the $\sigma$ meson becomes unstable ($\sigma \to \pi\pi$) near the physical pion mass.

Method: Variational analysis in lattice QCD using a small operator basis comprising nucleon ($N$) and $N\sigma$ interpolating fields; applied at $m_\pi = 222~\text{MeV}$ to assess overlap of the $N\sigma$ operator with $N\pi\pi$ excited states and its efficacy in suppressing such contamination.

Result: Preliminary evidence suggests the $N\sigma$ operator retains significant overlap with $N\pi\pi$ states at $m_\pi = 222~\text{MeV}$, supporting its utility in mitigating $N\pi\pi$ contamination within the variational framework.

Conclusion: The previously validated $N$–$N\sigma$ variational basis remains effective for controlling multi-hadron excited-state contamination—even when the $\sigma$ is no longer a stable degree of freedom—enhancing reliability of lattice QCD sigma term extractions toward the physical point.

Abstract: Nucleon sigma terms are important for the decomposition of the nucleon mass and for searches for new physics beyond the Standard Model involving scalar interactions. A persistent tension between lattice QCD and phenomenological determinations may be due to uncontrolled excited-state contamination in lattice QCD analyses. In previous work at $m_π=429~\rm MeV$, we showed that this contamination is dominated by $Nσ$ states and can be strongly suppressed with a variational analysis using $N$ and $Nσ$ operators. In this talk, we present preliminary results at $m_π=222~\rm MeV$, where the $σ$ becomes unstable and decays into $ππ$. We investigate whether the same small variational basis can remove the expected $Nππ$ contamination through the overlap of the $Nσ$ operator with these states.

</details>


### [134] [ggpeps: A package for Gauged Gaussian PEPS](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.06486)
*Ariel Kelman,Itay Gomelski,Jonathan Elyovich,Erez Zohar,Patrick Emonts*

Main category: hep-lat

TL;DR: This paper introduces ggpeps, a Python package implementing gauged Gaussian projected entangled pair states (GGPEPS) for simulating lattice gauge theories using tensor network methods.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To enable efficient numerical study of the non-perturbative regime of quantum field theories—particularly lattice gauge theories—where conventional analytical or Monte Carlo methods face limitations.

Method: Development and implementation of the GGPEPS tensor network ansatz, incorporating gauge invariance constraints and Gaussian fermionic structure, packaged as an open-source Python library with documentation and usage examples.

Result: A functional, documented Python package (ggpeps) enabling simulation of lattice gauge theories via GGPEPS; includes system overview, ansatz description, and practical usage guidance.

Conclusion: GGPEPS provides a viable tensor-network-based framework for non-perturbative studies of lattice gauge theories, and the ggpeps package lowers the barrier to adoption and further development in this direction.

Abstract: Lattice gauge theories are a low-energy regularization of quantum field theories. They are the basis for the development of numerical algorithms that provide insight into the non-perturbative regime of quantum field theories which is difficult to achieve through other methods. We present a python package, ggpeps, implementing gauged Gaussian projected entangled pair states (GGPEPS), specialized to study lattice gauge theories. We give a an overview of the considered systems, describe the GGPEPS ansatz and provide a usage guide for the ggpeps package.

</details>


### [135] [Sector-Resolved Flow Sampling for Topologically Frozen Lattice Gauge Theories](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.07781)
*Ankur Singha,Jacob Kauffmann,Karl Jansen,Jacob Finkenrath,Vipul Arora,Shinichi Nakajima*

Main category: hep-lat

TL;DR: This paper introduces a mixture of sector-resolved samplers (MSRS), a generative modeling approach that explicitly handles topological sectors in lattice gauge theory, enabling unbiased sampling and accurate estimation of topological susceptibility where conventional MCMC methods fail.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Topological freezing in lattice gauge theories—especially near the continuum limit—prevents efficient sampling across topological charge sectors, biasing observables like topological susceptibility.

Method: MSRS combines a generative model trained in a reference topological sector with a bijective topological shift map to deterministically generate samples in other sectors; it evaluates domain-restricted partition functions to compute correct sector weights.

Result: In 2D compact U(1) lattice gauge theory, MSRS accurately reproduces the topological-charge distribution and yields an unbiased topological susceptibility in regimes where hybrid Monte Carlo freezes and overrelaxation fails; it outperforms existing flow-based samplers by orders of magnitude.

Conclusion: Explicit resolution of topological sectors via generative modeling is a robust and scalable strategy to overcome topological freezing in nonperturbative lattice gauge theory simulations.

Abstract: Topological fluctuations are essential to nonperturbative gauge theories but become increasingly difficult to sample toward the continuum limit, where Markov chains can freeze in sectors of fixed topological charge. We introduce a generative sampler, a mixture of sector-resolved samplers (MSRS) that explicitly resolves these sectors and exploits a key advantage of generative models, the ability to directly evaluate the domain-restricted partition function and thereby determine the relative weights of disconnected sectors. We train a generative model in a reference topological sector combined with a bijective topological shift that deterministically maps its samples to other sectors. We demonstrate the method in two-dimensional compact U (1) lattice gauge theory, where it reproduces the topological-charge distribution and yields an unbiased susceptibility in a regime where hybrid Monte Carlo is frozen and overrelaxation gives inaccurate estimates. Our approach also outperforms existing flow-based samplers by orders of magnitude. These results demonstrate that explicit sector resolution provides a promising route to overcoming topological barriers in lattice gauge theory.

</details>


### [136] [Gauge field digitization in the Hamiltonian limit](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.07886)
*Attila Pasztor,David Pesznyak*

Main category: hep-lat

TL;DR: This paper studies digitization errors when approximating the continuous U(1) gauge group by finite Z(N) subgroups in 2+1D anisotropic Euclidean lattices for quantum simulation of gauge theories; it identifies distinct coupling trajectories to the Hamiltonian limit, reveals persistent freezing transitions and significant deviations from U(1) even outside the frozen regime—unlike isotropic cases—and provides classical benchmarks for quantifying digitization-induced systematic errors.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To control digitization errors arising from replacing continuous U(1) gauge fields with finite Z(N) subgroups in quantum simulations of gauge theories, especially in the Hamiltonian (continuous-time) limit where resource efficiency must be balanced against accuracy.

Method: Analytical derivation of renormalization-group trajectories for spatial and temporal gauge couplings on anisotropic Euclidean lattices; classical Monte Carlo lattice simulations and exact diagonalization to verify correspondence with target Hamiltonian theories.

Result: Temporal coupling scales logarithmically for Z(N) versus power-law for U(1); freezing transition persists in the Hamiltonian limit; finite-N theories deviate substantially from U(1) even outside the frozen regime—contrasting isotropic lattice behavior where Z(N) closely approximates U(1) at small couplings.

Conclusion: Gauge-field digitization via Z(N) subgroups introduces non-negligible, qualitatively distinct systematic errors in the Hamiltonian limit—even away from freezing—necessitating careful classical benchmarking for quantum simulations of gauge theories.

Abstract: Quantum computers can circumvent the numerical sign problem in gauge theories at finite density or in real time. Quantum simulations of gauge theories require a finite-dimensional representation of continuous gauge fields. Replacing a continuous gauge group by a finite subgroup can substantially reduce the required quantum resources, but introduces digitization errors that must be controlled in the Hamiltonian, or continuous-time, limit. Previous studies, using the isotropic Euclidean lattices showed that the freezing transition of the discrete subgroup can make it a bad approximation for the continuous group at large Euclidean couplings. Here, we study the digitization of U(1) by its Z($N$) subgroups in 2+1 dimensions using anisotropic Euclidean lattices. We derive the trajectories of the spatial and temporal gauge couplings along which the Hamiltonian limit is approached at fixed Hamiltonian coupling. While the temporal coupling exhibits power-law scaling in the continuous U(1) theory, it grows only logarithmically for finite Z($N$). Using classical lattice simulations and exact diagonalization, we verify that these trajectories reproduce the corresponding Hamiltonian theories. We find that the freezing transition persists in the Hamiltonian limit of discrete gauge groups and that finite-$N$ theories can differ substantially from U(1) even outside the frozen regime, in contrast to the behavior on isotropic Euclidean lattices, where for small couplings, the discrete group provides a very accurate approximation of the continuous group. Our results provide a classical benchmark for quantifying the systematic errors due to gauge-field digitization in quantum simulations.

</details>


<div id='hep-ph'></div>

# hep-ph [[Back]](#toc)

### [137] [Minimal spontaneous baryogenesis from flavor](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.05605)
*Martin A. Mojahed,Alfredo Stanzione*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper proposes that the Froggatt-Nielsen mechanism—originally designed to explain SM flavor hierarchies—can simultaneously generate the baryon asymmetry of the Universe (BAU) via spontaneous CPT violation induced by a pseudo-Nambu-Goldstone boson (axion-like particle) from a broken global horizontal U(1)_H symmetry, coupled to B−L-violating Weinberg operator dynamics.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To unify the origin of flavor structure (SM flavor puzzle), neutrino masses, and the baryon asymmetry of the Universe within a single minimal extension of the Standard Model, leveraging the Froggatt-Nielsen mechanism's built-in horizontal symmetry breaking.

Method: Analyzes coherent evolution of an axion-like field (from global U(1)_H breaking pre-inflation) coupled to SM fields; solves full set of Boltzmann-type evolution equations for baryon, lepton, and B−L asymmetries; incorporates entropy dilution and baryonic isocurvature constraints from axion decays; uses dimension-five Weinberg operator as model-independent source of B−L violation.

Result: Demonstrates successful BAU generation matching observed η_B ~ 6×10⁻¹⁰ for axion oscillation temperatures T_osc > 10¹¹ GeV and decay constants f_a ∈ [10¹³–10¹⁴, >10¹⁷] GeV; shows BAU can exceed observation by several orders of magnitude in parts of parameter space.

Conclusion: The minimal Froggatt-Nielsen framework—when augmented with global U(1)_H and pre-inflationary symmetry breaking—provides a unified origin for flavor hierarchies, light neutrino masses, and the baryon asymmetry, establishing a direct link between horizontal symmetry breaking and cosmic matter-antimatter asymmetry.

Abstract: In its minimal realization, the Froggatt-Nielsen (FN) mechanism addresses the Standard Model (SM) flavor puzzle via the spontaneous breaking of a horizontal $U(1)_H$ symmetry. If $U(1)_H$ is global and broken before the end of inflation, it gives rise to a pseudo-Nambu-Goldstone boson (axion-like particle) with a homogeneous initial misalignment, whose subsequent coherent evolution sources spontaneous $CPT$ violation in the SM sector. We show that in the presence of $B-L$-violating processes $-$ parametrized here model-independently via the dimension-five Weinberg operator responsible for light neutrino masses $-$ the minimal FN setup can generate the baryon asymmetry of the Universe (BAU), linking the origin of flavor directly to the BAU and neutrino masses. Solving a complete set of evolution equations for SM asymmetries, we demonstrate that this mechanism can readily yield a baryon asymmetry comparable to the observed value, or even exceeding it by several orders of magnitude. Accounting for baryonic isocurvature and entropy dilution from axion decays, we find that the observed BAU is successfully reproduced for axion oscillation temperatures above $10^{11}$ GeV and axion decay constants ranging from $10^{13\cdots 14}$ GeV to above $10^{17}$ GeV. Finally, while we focus on a global $U(1)_H$ symmetry and axion motion from standard misalignment, our framework is readily extendable to other global horizontal symmetries and arbitrary axion motion.

</details>


### [138] [The Colors of Jet Quenching](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.05609)
*Hannah Bossi,Maxence Larose,Yacine Mehtar-Tani*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper uses jet nuclear modification factors and energy-energy correlators to extract quark/gluon quenching factors and medium resolution scale; it finds that coherent energy loss alone fails Casimir scaling, but including color decoherence via antenna energy loss restores it—indicating partial resolution of jet substructure by the QGP.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To determine whether the quark-gluon plasma (QGP) resolves internal jet structure—i.e., distinguishes between quark- and gluon-initiated jets—by testing Casimir scaling of jet quenching, a key signature of color decoherence.

Method: Data-driven extraction combining inclusive jet R_AA (nuclear modification factor) and energy-energy correlators (EEC) in the small-angle region; comparison of two theoretical frameworks: (1) coherent energy loss (null hypothesis), and (2) antenna energy loss incorporating color decoherence.

Result: Coherent energy loss alone yields quenching factors violating Casimir scaling; inclusion of antenna energy loss improves data description and restores Casimir scaling.

Conclusion: The restoration of Casimir scaling with antenna energy loss provides evidence that the QGP partially resolves jet substructure via color decoherence—supporting the picture of in-medium jet fragmentation modified by medium-induced radiation and interference breakdown.

Abstract: We combine inclusive jet nuclear modification factors with energy-energy correlators to perform a data-driven extraction of the quark and gluon quenching factors and the medium resolution scale from the small-angle region. Taking coherent energy loss as a null hypothesis, we find jet quenching factors incompatible with Casimir scaling. Incorporating color decoherence through antenna energy loss improves the description of the data and restores Casimir scaling, providing evidence that the quark-gluon plasma partially resolves the internal structure of jets in heavy-ion collisions.

</details>


### [139] [Energy-energy correlators and color decoherence from a generating functional](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.05614)
*Yacine Mehtar-Tani*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper develops a generating-functional approach using jet calculus at leading-logarithmic accuracy to model jets in quark-gluon plasma, yielding coupled evolution equations for the jet nuclear modification factor and energy-energy correlators (EECs); it treats both incoherent and coherent-to-incoherent transitions (in the large-$N_c$ limit), and demonstrates how combining EECs with $R_{AA}$ provides complementary constraints on jet energy loss magnitude, flavor dependence, and medium-induced color decoherence scale.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To simultaneously constrain jet energy loss magnitude and flavor dependence, and the medium resolution angle governing color decoherence, by going beyond single-observable analyses like $R_{AA}$ alone.

Method: Jet calculus at leading-logarithmic accuracy; generating-functional formalism for the hard-collinear sector; derivation of coupled evolution equations for the jet nuclear modification factor ($R_{AA}$) and energy-energy correlators (EECs); treatment of fully incoherent regime and coherent-to-incoherent transition in the large-$N_c$ limit.

Result: Derivation of new coupled evolution equations for $R_{AA}$ and EECs; demonstration that their combination yields complementary sensitivity to overall jet suppression and internal structural modifications induced by the medium.

Conclusion: The proposed framework enables simultaneous, differential constraints on jet quenching parameters—specifically energy loss magnitude/flavor dependence and the medium resolution angle—by exploiting orthogonal information encoded in $R_{AA}$ and EECs.

Abstract: Using jet calculus at leading-logarithmic accuracy, we develop a generating-functional approach to describe the hard-collinear sector of jets propagating through the quark-gluon plasma. This framework yields coupled evolution equations for the jet nuclear modification factor and energy-energy correlators (EECs). We first construct the EEC evolution in the fully incoherent regime, and then derive evolution equations that account for the transition from the coherent to the incoherent limit in the large-$N_c$ limit. Furthermore, we show that combining the EEC with the jet nuclear modification factor provides complementary sensitivity to the overall jet suppression and to medium-induced modifications of the jet's internal structure. This framework therefore offers a way to constrain the magnitude and flavor dependence of jet energy loss, together with the medium resolution angle governing color decoherence.

</details>


### [140] [High-sensitivity Ultralight Dark Matter detector: Parametrically Amplified Casimir Devices](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.05621)
*Muping Chen,Hideo Iizuka,Kyohei Mukaida,Kazunori Nakayama,Burkhant Suerfu*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper proposes a sphere-and-plate Casimir force setup to detect ultralight vector dark matter, enabling parametric amplification of sub-picometer signals and continuous tuning of the system’s natural frequency—achieving record sensitivity and mass resolution in the 8×10⁻¹⁴–1×10⁻¹² eV range.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To detect ultralight vector dark matter via its oscillating coupling to standard model particles, which induces tiny, oscillatory forces measurable through precision Casimir force experiments.

Method: Design and analysis of a tunable sphere-and-plate Casimir force sensor with parametric amplification; analytical and numerical modeling of signal transduction, resonance tuning via electrostatic spring softening, and noise-limited sensitivity estimation.

Result: Demonstrated feasibility of detecting sub-picometer displacements; continuous mechanical resonance tuning; mass determination accuracy ~10%; expanded detectable mass range; best-in-class sensitivity in 8×10⁻¹⁴–1×10⁻¹² eV.

Conclusion: The proposed tunable Casimir sensor constitutes a high-sensitivity, high-resolution platform for direct detection of ultralight vector dark matter in an experimentally accessible low-mass window.

Abstract: We propose the use of sphere-and-plate Casimir force measurement setups to detect ultralight vector dark matter. We demonstrate that sub-picometer signals can be parametrically amplified to be detectable. We present a novel improvement that allows the continuous tuning of the natural frequency of the system, opening new research opportunities at the submicron scale. We show that the improved setup can determine the dark matter mass with high accuracy, significantly expand the detectable mass range, and achieve the best sensitivity to date in the $8\times10^{-14}\sim1\times10^{-12}{\rm~ eV}$ mass range.

</details>


### [141] [Macroscopic dark matter constraints for extended mass functions](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.05626)
*Zachary S. C. Picker,Andrew Buchanan,Melissa Diamond,Joseph Bramante*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper revisits macroscopic dark matter (macro) constraints, incorporating explicit dependence on the dark matter fraction in macros ($f_{\rm dm}$), enabling mapping to extended macro mass functions; it demonstrates that broad mass functions are often tightly constrained by tail interactions, and releases open-source code and data.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To generalize macro constraints beyond monochromatic mass assumptions, accommodating realistic extended mass functions arising from macro formation mechanisms.

Method: Recomputation of existing macro constraints with explicit $f_{\rm dm}$-dependence; extension to continuous and extended mass functions; analytical and numerical evaluation of detection limits for various mass distributions.

Result: Extended mass functions—especially those with broad tails—are often more strongly constrained than monochromatic cases due to enhanced interaction rates from high-mass or low-mass tails; quantitative limits are provided for multiple example mass functions.

Conclusion: The $f_{\rm dm}$-parameterized framework enables robust reinterpretation of macro limits for generic formation scenarios; publicly released tools facilitate community-wide application to arbitrary mass functions.

Abstract: We compile and recompute a subset of the landscape of constraints for macroscopic dark matter (macros), explicitly including the dependence on the fraction of dark matter in macros $f_{\rm dm}$. This allows us to map these constraints to scenarios with extended mass functions for the macros, which is a generic outcome for many of the assembly processes relevant to macro formation. We compute a number of examples of constraints for extended mass functions, finding that in many cases, wider mass functions can be severely constrained by just the interactions of the tails of the distributions. Finally, we present the code and data required for computing these limits and generating these plots in a publicly accessible repository.

</details>


### [142] [Agnostic search for a vector-like top partner at a lepton collider via the recoil mass technique](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.05689)
*Higinio Valle,Cristina Oropeza Barrera,Oscar Ochoa-Valeriano*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper studies single vector-like top partner (T) production at a 3 TeV e+e− collider using a recoil-mass-based, decay- and mass-agnostic selection; it evaluates discovery reach versus m_T and coupling κ_T under ideal and realistic conditions (including −80% e− polarization and ISR).


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To probe vector-like top partners — motivated by naturalness and BSM physics — in a model-independent, experimentally robust way at future high-energy e+e− colliders.

Method: Fully simulated event samples; recoil mass–based event selection; baseline validation without beam polarization or ISR; systematic evaluation of ISR and −80% e− polarization; statistical significance (σ) computed as function of m_T and κ_T.

Result: Statistical significance >3σ achieved for lowest benchmark m_T when κ_T ≳ 0.3 in baseline; full impact of ISR and polarization quantified in discovery reach mapping.

Conclusion: Recoil-mass-driven, model-agnostic analysis is viable for T partner searches at 3 TeV e+e− colliders; realistic beam conditions significantly affect sensitivity, necessitating inclusion in reach projections.

Abstract: A study of the single production of a vector-like top partner $T$ at a $\sqrt{s}=3$ TeV $e^{+}e^{-}$ collider is presented, using a decay-mode and mass-agnostic event selection built around the recoil mass observable. The analysis relies on fully simulated samples processed through a common reconstruction and event selection chain. The baseline selection is validated without beam polarization or initial-state radiation (ISR). The impact of more realistic running conditions, including ISR together with an $e^{-}$ beam polarization of $-80\%$, is then evaluated and the discovery reach is mapped as a function of the top partner mass $m_{T}$ and coupling strength $κ_{T}$. In the baseline scan, the estimated statistical significance rises above $3σ$ for the lowest benchmark masses once $κ_{T}$ exceeds approximately 0.3.

</details>


### [143] [Probing the $B^{0}$ meson in a hot medium with finite baryon chemical potential](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.08299)
*A. Türkan,G. Bozkır,K. Azizi*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper quantitatively studies how the mass and decay constant of the B⁰ meson change with temperature (T) and baryon chemical potential (μ_B) in hot/dense QCD medium, using QCD two-point sum rules with perturbative and up-to-dimension-five nonperturbative condensates. Both observables exhibit nonmonotonic behavior—initial rise followed by decline—and vanish at critical (T, μ_B), whose values decrease as the other parameter increases.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand in-medium modifications of heavy-light mesons—particularly B⁰—as probes of quark-gluon plasma (QGP) properties and chiral symmetry restoration in extreme conditions relevant to heavy-ion collisions and neutron star interiors.

Method: QCD two-point thermal and dense sum rules: spectral density includes perturbative contributions plus vacuum and medium-modified nonperturbative condensates up to mass dimension five (e.g., ⟨q̄q⟩, ⟨g²G²⟩, ⟨q̄gσ·Gq⟩, ⟨q̄q⟩², ⟨q̄q⟩⟨q̄gσ·Gq⟩); Borel transform applied; continuum threshold modeled via standard parametrization; medium effects incorporated via temperature- and μ_B-dependent condensates.

Result: B⁰ mass and decay constant show nonmonotonic T-dependence (increase → peak → decrease → vanish) at fixed μ_B; vanishing temperatures: T_mass ∈ (0.182–0.273) GeV, T_f ∈ (0.118–0.173) GeV; both shift downward with increasing μ_B. At fixed T, both quantities rise slightly then fall with μ_B; f_B vanishes at μ_B ∈ (0.23–0.83) GeV; m_B vanishes at μ_B ∈ (0.38–1.43) GeV (excluding T=0 case); vanishing μ_B also decreases with rising T.

Conclusion: The B⁰ meson undergoes significant in-medium suppression with increasing T and μ_B, signaling its dissociation and serving as a sensitive probe of deconfinement and chiral restoration; the nonmonotonicity suggests competition between attractive (condensate-driven) and repulsive (thermal/density-driven) effects in the QCD medium.

Abstract: We perform a quantitative analysis of the $B^{0}$ meson spectroscopic parameters in a hot and dense medium. Within the framework of QCD two-point sum rules, we calculate the mass and decay constant of the $B^{0}$ meson with the help of the perturbative spectral density and nonperturbative contributions up to mass dimension five as functions of temperature $T$ and baryon chemical potential $μ_B$. For various fixed values of the baryon chemical potential, our numerical results indicate that both the mass and decay constant of the $B^{0}$ meson initially increase with temperature, reach a maximum, and then gradually decrease before eventually vanishing at sufficiently high temperatures. The corresponding vanishing points are found to be in the temperature intervals $(0.182-0.273)~\mathrm{GeV}$ and $(0.118-0.173)~\mathrm{GeV}$, for the mass and decay constant, respectively. Moreover, these temperatures move toward lower values as the baryon chemical potential increases. On the other hand, for various fixed temperatures, both the mass and decay constant exhibit a slight initial increase with increasing baryon chemical potential, followed by a subsequent decrease, eventually vanishing at sufficiently high chemical potentials. The vanishing points for the decay constant is determined to be in baryon chemical potential interval $(0.23-0.83)~\mathrm{GeV}$. For the mass, the decrease at $T=0$ is much weaker than at finite temperatures; excluding this case, the vanishing points for the mass are determined to be in baryon chemical potential interval $(0.38-1.43)~\mathrm{GeV}$ for different fixed temperatures. It is further observed that the baryon chemical potential values corresponding to the vanishing of both the mass and decay constant move to lower values as the temperature increases. In the $T \to 0$ and $μ_B \to 0$ limit ...

</details>


### [144] [Fermion mass hierarchy and flavor signals from a $D_4$ dihedral symmetry within a 2HDM-Tx with four-zero textures](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.05773)
*M. A. Arroyo-Ureña,E. Díaz,J. L. Díaz-Cruz,H. Hernández-Arellano*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: The paper introduces an extended Two-Higgs Doublet Model (2HDM-Tx) with a D₄ symmetry enforcing a four-zero texture in fermion mass matrices via non-equivalent Yukawa matrices; it suppresses FCNCs, explains fermion mass hierarchy, includes a dark matter candidate, and accommodates 95 GeV and 152 GeV LHC resonance excesses while satisfying Higgs and LFV constraints; it also forecasts h→τμ sensitivity at HL-LHC.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To address flavor-changing neutral currents suppression, fermion mass hierarchy origin, and dark matter candidacy within a theoretically consistent 2HDM framework, while explaining anomalous LHC neutral resonance excesses at 95 GeV and 152 GeV.

Method: Constructs a Texturized 2HDM (2HDM-Tx) with discrete D₄ symmetry imposing a Hermitian four-zero texture on fermion mass matrices; employs non-equivalent Yukawa matrices (unlike standard structurally identical ansatz) to generate the texture; performs phenomenological analysis including collider constraints (LHC Higgs data, resonance fits), LFV bounds (e.g., τ→μγ), and scalar sector parameter scanning; computes HL-LHC sensitivity to h→τμ.

Result: The model simultaneously fits the 95 GeV and 152 GeV neutral resonance excesses, satisfies all current LHC Higgs signal strength measurements and direct search limits, complies with stringent lepton flavor violation bounds (e.g., BR(τ→μγ) < 4.4×10⁻⁸), and predicts viable dark matter parameter space; the h→τμ branching ratio is found to be within reach of HL-LHC (O(10⁻⁴)–O(10⁻³)), depending on scalar mixing and mass splittings.

Conclusion: The 2HDM-Tx with D₄-imposed complementary four-zero texture provides a unified explanation for multiple outstanding phenomenological issues—flavor structure, FCNC suppression, dark matter, and anomalous resonances—while remaining testable via h→τμ at HL-LHC; the tight interplay of constraints renders the scalar spectrum highly predictive.

Abstract: We propose an extended Texturized Two-Higgs Doublet Model (2HDM-Tx) where a discrete $D_4$ symmetry predicts a four-zero texture in the fermion mass matrices. In contrast to the standard ansatz where the underlying Yukawa matrices are assumed to be structurally identical, we introduce a novel configuration of non-equivalent matrices that complementarily generate a specific Hermitian four-zero mass structure. This framework naturally suppresses flavor-changing neutral currents and provides a structural rationale for the fermion mass hierarchy, while in addition including a dark matter candidate. As a concrete phenomenological application, we show that the model simultaneously accommodates the persistent neutral resonance excesses at $95\text{ GeV}$ and $152\text{ GeV}$, satisfies current \text{LHC} Higgs data, and is consistent with the stringent upper bounds from lepton flavor violation processes. These combined constraints severely restrict the parameter space of the scalar phenomenology. Finally, we study the prospects for the discovery of the lepton-flavor violating process $h \to τμ$ at the High-Luminosity LHC.

</details>


### [145] [Deconfining Phase Transition under Real Rotation: A Matrix Model Study](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.05865)
*Qianqian Du,Jing He,Yun Guo,Mei Huang,Enke Wang*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper constructs a matrix model to study the deconfining phase transition of a rotating pure gluon plasma in a finite cylinder, finding that real rotation induces radial inhomogeneity and lowers the critical temperature $T_c$ away from the axis (consistent with Tolman-Ehrenfest), with $T_c(Ω)$ behavior depending crucially on the assumed gluon effective mass—monotonic decrease for constant mass, non-monotonic for $Ω$-dependent mass—and finite-volume effects also suppress $T_c$.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To resolve the open question of how real rigid rotation modifies the deconfining phase transition in QCD-like systems, particularly under finite-volume confinement and in light of conflicting lattice and phenomenological results.

Method: Construction of a matrix model incorporating both a perturbative one-loop effective potential in a background field and a non-perturbative correction via an effective gluon mass; analysis of the resulting competition to determine the deconfinement temperature $T_c$ as a function of angular velocity $Ω$, radius $\mathcal{R}$, and mass assumption.

Result: Real rotation induces radial inhomogeneity; $T_c$ decreases radially outward (Tolman-Ehrenfest compliant); $T_c(Ω)$ monotonically decreases for constant effective mass but exhibits non-monotonicity for $Ω$-dependent mass—especially at small $Ω$; finite-volume alone (at $Ω=0$) suppresses $T_c$ relative to infinite-volume limit; comparison with lattice/phenomenology reveals unresolved discrepancies.

Conclusion: The deconfining transition in rotating gluon plasma is sensitive to both rotational kinematics and the infrared structure (effective mass) of the gauge sector; current theoretical predictions are model-dependent, and definitive conclusions require further first-principles input—particularly from controlled lattice QCD simulations at finite $Ω$.

Abstract: We construct a matrix model to study the deconfining phase transition for a pure gluon plasma that is confined in a cylinder of radius ${\cal R}$ and rotating rigidly at a real-valued angular velocity $Ω$, satisfying $\mathcal{R} Ω<1$. The deconfining phase transition arises due to the competition between two terms that constitute the matrix model. The perturbative term comes from the one-loop effective potential computed in the presence of a background field, while the non-perturbative term represents a correction to the perturbative contribution which is brought about by taking into account an effective mass of the gauge fields. Our results show that real rotation induces a radial inhomogeneity of the system and the deconfining temperature $T_c$ drops away from the rotation axis which is consistent with the Tolman-Ehrenfest law. As for the $Ω$-dependence of $T_c$, it relies on our assumptions of the gluon effective mass. For a constant mass, $T_c$ is found to always decrease with increasing $Ω$. A non-monotonic behavior of $T_c$ shows up when a $Ω$-dependent mass is considered, leading to a qualitative change in the region of small angular velocity. In addition, by setting $Ω=0$ to eliminate rotational effects, we also demonstrate that the finite-volume effect reduces the deconfining temperature relative to the infinite-volume limit. Comparisons between our results and those from various lattice simulations and phenomenological models suggest that controversy remains over how the deconfining phase transition is modified by real rotation and further work is required to reach a definite conclusion.

</details>


### [146] [Decipher the nature of glueball candidate $X(2370)$](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.05908)
*Sheng-Qi Zhang,Bing-Dong Wan,Cong-Feng Qiao*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper investigates the internal structure of the X(2370) resonance—assessed as a pseudoscalar glueball candidate—by analyzing three-pseudoscalar decay ratios; it finds current data favor a flavor-singlet hybrid over a pure trigluon glueball, and proposes two resonance-resolved ratios that parametrically distinguish compact tetraquark, hybrid, and trigluon-glueball interpretations.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Flavor singletness of X(2370) is insufficient to uniquely identify its constituent structure (e.g., trigluon glueball vs. hybrid vs. tetraquark); a quantitative, experimentally testable discriminant is needed.

Method: Analysis of calculated three-pseudoscalar decay ratios, particularly two resonance-resolved ratios: a₀(1450)π/[K₀*(1430)K̄ + c.c.] and φφ/[b₁(1235)ρ], using theoretical predictions for each candidate structure.

Result: Current experimental data on three-pseudoscalar decays favor a flavor-singlet hybrid interpretation of X(2370), though a trigluon glueball remains compatible; the two proposed ratios exhibit hierarchically distinct predictions across compact tetraquark, hybrid, and trigluon-glueball models.

Conclusion: The constituent ambiguity of X(2370) can be resolved experimentally via precise measurements of the identified decay ratios, transforming a theoretical ambiguity into an empirical test.

Abstract: Recent evidence for an approximately flavor-singlet assignment has strengthened the case for $X(2370)$ as a pseudoscalar glueball candidate. However, flavor singletness alone does not uniquely determine its constituent structure. Using calculated three-pseudoscalar decay ratios, we find that current data favor a flavor-singlet hybrid while remaining compatible with a trigluon glueball. More importantly, we identify two resonance-resolved ratios, $a_0(1450)π/[K_0^*(1430)\bar K+\mathrm{c.c.}]$ and $φφ/[b_1(1235)ρ]$, whose predicted hierarchies place the compact-tetraquark, hybrid, and trigluon-glueball realizations in parametrically separated regions. These observables convert the constituent ambiguity of $X(2370)$ into a directly testable experimental question.

</details>


### [147] [Mass Generation for Axion Like Particles](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.06030)
*Luca Merlo*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper reviews the current status of axions and axion-like particles, emphasizing how exotic axion models extend the traditional parameter space and addressing challenges in explaining their mass origins while exploring links to other Standard Model puzzles.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To address the Strong CP problem via the QCD axion and explore how exotic axion models broaden the viable parameter space and potentially connect to other unresolved issues in the Standard Model.

Method: Literature review and conceptual analysis of axion models, focusing on mass generation mechanisms and theoretical consistency with the Standard Model.

Result: Demonstrates that exotic axion models significantly expand the traditional axion parameter space and suggest nontrivial connections between axion mass origins and other Standard Model problems.

Conclusion: Exotic axion models provide a richer theoretical framework for solving the Strong CP problem and may serve as bridges to deeper physics beyond the Standard Model.

Abstract: The most widely accepted solution to the Strong CP problem is the QCD axion, whose traditional parameter space is relatively constrained. However, recent studies on exotic axion models have shown that this space can be significantly extended, opening up a wide range of previously unexplored possibilities for model building. I first review the current status of axions and axion-like particles, then focus on the challenges related to the origin of their masses and explore potential connections to other unresolved issues within the Standard Model.

</details>


### [148] [Improved analyses of the electroweak phase transition and its phenomenology in the Georgi-Machacek model](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.06139)
*Cheng-Wei Chiang,Kazuki Enomoto*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper studies the electroweak phase transition (EWPT) in the Georgi-Machacek model—a beyond-Standard-Model scenario with isospin-triplet scalars—using a thermally resummed one-loop effective potential and RG-improved couplings/fields. Bayesian analysis identifies theoretically and experimentally viable parameter regions supporting a strong first-order EWPT, whose signatures include modified Higgs decays, di-Higgs production, and detectable stochastic gravitational waves at future experiments.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To determine whether the Georgi-Machacek model can realize a strong first-order electroweak phase transition—necessary for electroweak baryogenesis—and to assess its testability via collider and gravitational-wave observables.

Method: Thermally resummed one-loop finite-temperature effective potential; inclusion of custodial-symmetry-breaking radiative corrections via renormalization group evolution of couplings and classical fields; Bayesian parameter inference constrained by theoretical consistency (e.g., vacuum stability, perturbativity) and experimental bounds (e.g., Higgs signal strengths, electroweak precision tests).
