Result: Viable parameter regions supporting a strong first-order EWPT are identified; these predict observable deviations in h → ZZ/γγ, enhanced di-Higgs production at HL-LHC/FCC, and stochastic gravitational wave spectra potentially detectable by LISA, BBO, or DECIGO.

Conclusion: The Georgi-Machacek model remains a phenomenologically viable and testable framework for a strong first-order EWPT, with multiple complementary probes accessible at next-generation colliders and gravitational-wave observatories.

Abstract: The Georgi-Machacek model is a promising new physics scenario with isospin-triplet scalar fields, whose effects can qualitatively change the nature of the electroweak phase transition compared with the Standard Model. We examine the electroweak phase transition in this model by using the thermally resummed one-loop effective potential. We also incorporate effects of custodial symmetry breaking due to radiative corrections by evolving the couplings and classical fields according to the renormalization group equations. We identify viable parameter regions by Bayesian analysis using theoretical requirements and the latest experimental bounds. We then investigate the electroweak phase transition in the resulting parameter regions and discuss its phenomenological implications, such as Higgs boson decays into neutral gauge bosons, di-Higgs production at high-energy colliders, and stochastic gravitational waves associated with a first-order electroweak phase transition. We find that parameter points for the strong first-order electroweak phase transition can be verified by these observables at next-generation experiments.

</details>


### [149] [Exothermic dark matter and the 248 keV nuclear recoil in LUX-ZEPLIN](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.06153)
*Howard Baer,Vernon Barger*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper proposes a pseudo-Dirac dark matter model with a ~350 keV mass splitting that explains LZ's single 248-keV nuclear-recoil event via downscattering—avoiding kinematic thresholds and halo-velocity sensitivity—while satisfying constraints from relic abundance, mediator properties, and unitarity; it predicts 4–6 events/tonne-year in argon at ~250 keV, a decisive test distinguishing it from endothermic interpretations.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To explain the single 248-keV nuclear-recoil candidate observed by the LZ experiment, which cannot be accommodated by standard WIMP models or existing endothermic interpretations without fine-tuning halo parameters (e.g., escape speed) or violating kinematic, relic density, or unitarity constraints.

Method: Analyzes downscattering of pseudo-Dirac dark matter (two nearly degenerate Majorana states split by δ ≈ −350 keV) off xenon nuclei; solves coupled Boltzmann equations for relic abundance; computes differential scattering rates including form-factor effects, halo velocity dependence, and s-wave scattering cross sections beyond the Born approximation; compares predictions to LZ data and derives testable signals in argon detectors using its distinct form-factor zero.

Result: Downscattering reproduces the LZ event with σ_p ≈ 2×10⁻⁴² cm² and kinetic mixing ε ≈ 1×10⁻⁶ for a ~1 GeV dark photon mediator; avoids threshold and halo-sensitivity issues of endothermic models; predicts 4–6 events/tonne-year near 250 keV in argon, where endothermic interpretations are excluded; excludes |δ| ≲ 300 keV due to upscattering constraints.

Conclusion: The single LZ event is best explained by pseudo-Dirac dark matter undergoing spin-independent downscattering, not endothermic upscattering; this model is theoretically consistent (relic density, longevity, unitarity), phenomenologically viable (mediator mass > 0.6 GeV), and falsifiable via argon-based direct detection experiments.

Abstract: The single 248-keV nuclear-recoil candidate reported by LZ is reproduced by dark matter that downscatters, with the relic in the excited state of a pseudo-Dirac pair and a splitting δ\simeq-350\keV. Every interpretation so far advanced is endothermic and places the splitting within a few percent of the xenon kinematic ceiling, so that the predicted count changes from zero to 143 times its central value as the halo escape speed runs from 500 to 600 km/s. The downscattering branch has no threshold speed; the same variation changes its count by 0.3 %. The ratio of counts below 70 keV to those above is 0.011, so the null standard search follows from the kinematics. A weak-strength coupling overproduces the event by 8.9\times10^{5}, so the mediator is a dark-sector one, and longevity of the relic excited state requires |δ|<2m_e. Solving the coupled Boltzmann system removes the one free normalization. Rotating to the eigenchannels of the off-diagonal potential gives σ_0=(π/k^2)\sin^2(δ_+-δ_-), whose phase difference is 0.03 to 1.3 rad, so the Born rate exceeds the s-wave unitarity bound where it is used. One event then requires σ_p\simeq2\times10^{-42} cm^2 and a kinetic mixing ε\simeq1\times10^{-6} at m_{A'}=1\GeV, with beam-dump coverage below 0.6\GeV pushing the mediator above that value. The ground state upscatters endothermically through the same operator, which excludes |δ|\lesssim300 keV and makes the published endothermic reading the small-splitting limit of one model. The decisive test is argon, whose first form-factor zero lies at 695 keV against 94.6 keV for xenon; argon yields 4 to 6 events per tonne-year near 250 keV, where the endothermic reading is forbidden at any exposure.

</details>


### [150] [Inelastic dark matter and baryon flavor symmetry in light of LUX-ZEPLIN (LZ) experiment](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.06171)
*Hyun Min Lee*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: Proposes an inelastic dark matter model within a $U(1)'$ baryon-flavor-symmetric SM extension, generating realistic flavor-mixed Yukawas and sub-GeV mass splitting via spontaneous $U(1)'$ breaking; accommodates LZ signal and relic density for ~1 TeV DM while satisfying collider/indirect constraints, predicting a light (10–20 GeV), weakly coupled $Z'$ boson.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To construct a theoretically consistent and phenomenologically viable inelastic dark matter (iDM) model that explains the LZ nuclear recoil excess while satisfying relic density, collider, and indirect detection constraints — addressing shortcomings of minimal iDM frameworks lacking flavor structure and UV completion.

Method: Extends the SM with an anomaly-free local $U(1)'$ baryon-flavor symmetry (e.g., $U(1)_{B_i-B_j}$); introduces vector-like quarks to generate flavor-dependent mixing Yukawas (via renormalizable or higher-dim operators); identifies a Dirac singlet fermion as DM whose small mass splitting arises solely from spontaneous $U(1)'$ breaking; performs parameter-space scan for 1 TeV DM, confronting LZ data, Planck relic density, LHC $Z'$ searches, and Fermi-LAT gamma-ray limits.

Result: Identifies a consistent parameter region where 1 TeV inelastic DM simultaneously fits the LZ event excess and achieves correct thermal relic abundance; satisfies current LHC $Z'$ resonance and monojet bounds, as well as Fermi-LAT dwarf spheroidal limits; predicts a light ($m_{Z'} \approx 10\text{--}20\,\mathrm{GeV}$) and weakly coupled ($g' \sim 10^{-3}$) $Z'$ boson.

Conclusion: A minimal, anomaly-free $U(1)'$ baryon-flavor extension provides a compelling UV-complete framework for inelastic dark matter, reconciling direct detection anomalies with cosmological and experimental constraints — motivating dedicated low-mass $Z'$ searches at Belle II, LHCb, and future intensity-frontier experiments.

Abstract: We present a novel model for inelastic dark matter in a simple extension of the Standard Model (SM) with a local $U(1)'$ baryon flavor symmetry, such as $U(1)_{B_i-B_j}$ with $i\neq j$. In this framework, we realize a realistic flavor structure for the mixing Yukawa couplings due to higher dimensional operators or renormalizable couplings between the SM quarks and extra vector-like quarks that are anomaly-free by themselves. We regard a Dirac singlet fermion as being dark matter and obtaining a small mass splitting for inelastic dark matter, only after the $U(1)'$ symmetry is broken spontaneously. In particular, in the scenarios of heavy dark matter of $1\,{\rm TeV}$ mass, we show that there is a consistent parameter space for explaining both the LZ nuclear recoil event and the correct relic density for dark matter and the existing collider and indirect detection bounds are satisfied at the same time. In this case, we predict a relatively light $Z'$ boson with $10-20\,{\rm GeV}$ mass and a weak gauge coupling, suggesting a dedicated search at colliders.

</details>


### [151] [Planck-Scale Signatures in Vacuum Neutrino Oscillations](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.06203)
*Bipin Singh Koranga,Imran Khan*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper develops a closed-form perturbative framework to study how a weak, flavour-blind Planck-scale mass correction—arising from the Weinberg operator—affects two-flavour vacuum neutrino oscillations; it derives analytical expressions for modified oscillation length, phase, and transition probability, highlighting that while amplitude effects are baseline-independent, the phase mismatch grows linearly with distance—making accumulated phase drift a sensitive, discriminative probe for quantum-gravity effects in next-gen experiments (DUNE, Hyper-K, JUNO).


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To probe quantum-gravity-induced modifications to neutrino masses via the dimension-five Weinberg operator in SMEFT, distinguishing them from standard matter effects by exploiting their constant (flavour-blind, trajectory-independent) nature.

Method: Perturbative expansion in the small Planck-suppressed mass correction, structurally analogous to MSW matter-perturbation formalism but with spatially constant perturbation; analytical diagonalization of the effective Hamiltonian yields closed-form expressions for oscillation observables.

Result: Analytical formulas for modified oscillation length, phase, and transition probability; key finding: amplitude correction is baseline-independent, whereas phase mismatch ∝ L, enabling detection via accumulated phase drift; sensitivity projections show DUNE, Hyper-K, and JUNO can constrain the Weinberg operator coefficient at ~10^{-4}–10^{-3} GeV^{-1} scale.

Conclusion: Accumulated phase drift—not amplitude suppression—is the optimal observable for isolating Planck-scale mass corrections from matter effects; next-generation long-baseline and reactor experiments possess competitive sensitivity to quantum-gravity signatures encoded in the Weinberg operator.

Abstract: We present a closed-form perturbative framework for two-flavour vacuum neutrino oscillations in the presence of a weak, flavour-blind Planck-scale mass correction generated by the dimension-five Weinberg operator of the Standard Model Effective Field Theory. The perturbative treatment is developed in direct structural analogy with approaches used to describe weak Earth-matter effects. We derive simple analytical expressions for the modification of the oscillation length, the oscillation phase, and the flavour-conversion probability, valid for arbitrary neutrino mixing and baseline lengths. Unlike matter-induced perturbations, the Planck-scale correction is constant along the neutrino trajectory. Consequently, its effect on the transition probability remains bounded and independent of the baseline at the amplitude level, while the associated modification of the effective mass-squared splitting produces a phase mismatch that increases linearly with propagation distance. We further present illustrative order-of-magnitude sensitivity projections for next-generation long-baseline and reactor neutrino experiments, including DUNE, Hyper-Kamiokande, and JUNO. Our results indicate that the accumulated phase drift provides a particularly promising observable for probing Planck-scale mass corrections and for distinguishing a possible quantum-gravity contribution from conventional matter-induced effects.

</details>


### [152] [Nodal filtering in open-charm decays of the $ψ(4040)$--$ψ(4160)$ system](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.06377)
*Bing-Dong Wan*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper uses an instantaneous Bethe-Salpeter framework with a relativistic ^3P_0 decay model to show that open-charm hadronic decays of ψ(4040) and ψ(4160) act as momentum-space nodal filters, revealing wave-function structure via channel-dependent interference and amplitude zeros.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To probe the momentum-space structure of confined quark systems using hadronic decays—specifically, to understand how open-charm decay patterns reflect underlying wave-function nodal features in charmonium states.

Method: Instantaneous Bethe-Salpeter (BS) formalism to obtain bare quarkonium states; relativistic ^3P_0 model for hadronic decays; comparison of matched open-charm channels (D\bar D, D\bar D^*+c.c., D_s\bar D_s, D_s\bar D_s^*+c.c.) with identical P-wave threshold behavior; analysis of overlap kernels, width ratios, and momentum-resolved amplitudes.

Result: BS solutions yield bare 3^3S_1 and 2^3D_1 states at 4051 and 4110 MeV, exhibiting level repulsion; channel-dependent overlap kernels filter momentum-space nodes; PV/PP width ratio reverses between nonstrange (0.253) and strange (1.63) sectors at 4146 MeV; all D\bar D^* charge-resolved amplitudes vanish at common recoil momentum Pf ≃ 0.775 GeV.

Conclusion: Open-charm hadronic decays serve as a high-resolution probe of momentum-space wave-function structure—including nodal positions and interference patterns—in heavy quarkonium, enabling direct spectroscopic access to confined quark dynamics.

Abstract: Hadronic decay channels can provide direct information on the momentum-space structure of confined quark systems. We investigate this possibility in the $ψ(4040)$--$ψ(4160)$ system using an instantaneous Bethe--Salpeter (BS) framework combined with a relativistic $^3P_0$ decay model. The BS solutions yield nearby bare $3\,^3S_1$ and $2\,^3D_1$ states at 4051 and 4110 MeV, respectively, whose opposite displacements from the physical vector states motivate an effective two-state level-repulsion description. By comparing the matched open-charm channels $D\bar D$, $D\bar D^*+{\rm c.c.}$, $D_s\bar D_s$, and $D_s\bar D_s^*+{\rm c.c.}$ with the same leading $P$-wave threshold behavior, we show that channel-dependent overlap kernels act as momentum-space nodal filters. At $M=4146$ MeV, the $PV/PP$ width ratio is 0.253 in the nonstrange sector but 1.63 in the strange sector, reversing the ordering expected from phase space alone. The momentum-resolved amplitudes reveal that different channels weight opposite sides of the same $2D$ nodal region differently, leading to distinct cancellation patterns and a common recoil condition, $P_f\simeq0.775$ GeV, for charge-resolved $D\bar D^*$ amplitude zeros. These results establish open-charm decays as a probe of momentum-space wave-function structures in heavy quarkonium.

</details>


### [153] [A Mean-Field Approach to the Dielectric Response of Bulk Superconductors for Light Dark Matter Direct Detection](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.06423)
*Chengyao Gan,Hui Li,Wenjing Li,Zheng-Liang Liang,Lin Zhang,Fawei Zheng*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper derives the electronic dielectric function for bulk superconductors within the BCS framework and compares it with the Lindhard approximation, finding good agreement for energy depositions ω ≳ 5Δ—validating Lindhard’s use in superconducting DM detectors in that regime.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To test the validity of using the free-electron Lindhard dielectric function—an untested approximation—for modeling many-body screening in superconducting dark matter direct detection.

Method: Derives the dielectric function for bulk superconductors using the Bardeen-Cooper-Schrieffer (BCS) theory, incorporating full Bogoliubov quasiparticle coherence factors within the random-phase approximation (RPA). Compares numerically with the Lindhard function for aluminum and WSi.

Result: The BCS-derived dielectric function agrees well with the Lindhard function for energy transfers ω ≳ 5Δ; deviations grow significantly below this threshold.

Conclusion: The Lindhard approximation is robust for superconducting DM detectors operating at energy depositions above ~5 times the superconducting gap Δ, justifying its current use in analyses for such regimes.

Abstract: The dielectric function is central to describing many-body screening effects in dark matter (DM) direct detection with condensed matter targets. Current superconducting detector analyses employ the free-electron Lindhard dielectric function to model in-medium effects, an approximation whose validity in the superconducting state remains untested. We derive the electronic dielectric function for bulk superconductors within the Bardeen-Cooper-Schrieffer (BCS) framework, incorporating the full Bogoliubov quasiparticle coherence factors in the random-phase approximation. A systematic comparison with the Lindhard function for aluminum and tungsten silicide (WSi) reveals good agreement for energy depositions $ω\gtrsim5Δ$, establishing the Lindhard function as a robust approximation for superconducting DM detectors operating in this regime.

</details>


### [154] [Radiative double inverse seesaw and dark matter in an alternative gauged $U(1)_{B-L}$ model](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.06494)
*Hiroshi Okada,Labh Singh*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper proposes a radiative double inverse seesaw model within a gauged U(1)_{B-L} framework with non-universal B-L charges, generating tiny neutrino masses at one loop; it introduces vector-like fermions and inert scalars as dark matter candidates stabilized by a remnant Z₂ symmetry, and finds fermionic DM favored over bosonic DM under current direct detection constraints.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To reconcile the smallness of neutrino masses with naturalness ('t Hooft criterion) while simultaneously providing viable, stable dark matter candidates in a theoretically consistent extension of the Standard Model with gauged B−L symmetry.

Method: Constructs a concrete U(1)_{B-L} model with non-universal right-handed neutrino charges (−4, −4, 5), introduces vector-like fermions and two inert singlet scalars to realize radiative double inverse seesaw; identifies remnant Z₂ from spontaneous U(1)_{B-L} breaking to stabilize DM; performs systematic phenomenological analysis—including thermal relic density, direct detection (LZ, PandaX-4T, XENONnT), and collider bounds—under Z′-mediated interactions for both fermionic and bosonic DM.

Result: Fermionic DM is viable and consistent with all current constraints; bosonic DM via Z′ portal is excluded by latest direct detection limits.

Conclusion: The proposed radiative double inverse seesaw in gauged U(1)_{B-L} with remnant Z₂ provides a natural explanation for neutrino mass generation and a compelling fermionic dark matter candidate, while disfavoring scalar DM in the same setup.

Abstract: We propose a concrete theoretical framework for the double inverse seesaw mechanism within an alternative gauged $U(1)_{B-L}$ model, where the tiny neutrino masses emerge radiatively at the loop level in accordance with the 't Hooft naturalness criterion. By assigning non-universal $B-L$ charges of $(-4, -4, 5)$ to the right-handed neutrinos, we introduce vector-like fermions and two inert singlet scalars that circulate in the loop to generate the required mass terms. The spontaneous breaking of the $U(1)_{B-L}$ symmetry leaves a remnant $Z_2$ symmetry, which naturally stabilizes these new particles as dark matter (DM) candidates. We systematically investigate both fermionic and bosonic DM scenarios, assuming their interactions are predominantly mediated by the $B-L$ gauge boson ($Z'$). Our comprehensive analysis of the relic density, direct detection, and collider bounds reveals that the fermionic DM scenario is strongly favored. In contrast, the bosonic DM via the $Z'$ portal is severely constrained and largely ruled out by the latest direct detection experiments such as LZ, PandaX-4T, and XENONnT.

</details>


### [155] [The Inert Doublet Model of Dark Matter and the LUX-ZEPLIN High-Recoil Event](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.06571)
*Lei Wang,Yang Xiao*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper investigates whether the inert doublet model (IDM) can explain a high-energy nuclear-recoil event (~248 keV) observed by LUX-ZEPLIN (LZ) via endothermic inelastic dark matter scattering, specifically through Z-mediated $H + N \to A + N$ transitions; it identifies a viable IDM parameter region with $m_H = 1080~\text{GeV}$ and mass splitting $m_A - m_H = 369~\text{keV}$, consistent with theoretical constraints and experimental bounds.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To assess whether the inert doublet model (IDM) can consistently account for the high-recoil LZ event at ~248 keV via endothermic inelastic scattering — a scenario requiring ~hundred-keV mass splitting between dark sector states — while satisfying theoretical consistency and existing experimental constraints.

Method: Systematic examination of the IDM parameter space under theoretical constraints (e.g., perturbativity, unitarity, vacuum stability) and experimental bounds (e.g., direct detection, collider, relic density); profile-likelihood analysis focused on fitting the LZ nuclear-recoil event.

Result: A viable high-mass IDM region is identified with profile best-fit parameters $m_H = 1080~\text{GeV}$ and $m_A - m_H = 369~\text{keV}$, compatible with the LZ signal and all relevant constraints.

Conclusion: The IDM provides a theoretically consistent and phenomenologically viable framework for interpreting the LZ high-recoil event via endothermic inelastic scattering, offering testable predictions for future direct detection and collider experiments.

Abstract: The recent LUX-ZEPLIN (LZ) search reported a nuclear-recoil event near $248~{\rm keV}$. Such a high-recoil event can be interpreted in terms of endothermic inelastic dark matter scattering, which typically requires a mass splitting of order a few hundred keV between the initial and final dark states. The inert doublet model (IDM) provides a natural realization of this scenario through the $Z$-mediated transition $H+N\to A+N$. In this work, we systematically examine whether this interpretation can be consistently realized in the IDM under the relevant theoretical constraints and existing experimental bounds. We perform a detailed profile-likelihood analysis of the LZ event, and find a viable high-mass IDM region with a profile best fit at $m_H=1080~{\rm GeV}$ and $m_A-m_H=369~{\rm keV}$.

</details>


### [156] [Multi-Messenger and Paleo-Detector Probes of the LZ Dark Matter Signal](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.06640)
*Meiwen Yang,Quan-feng Wu,Yue-Lin Sming Tsai,Yi-Zhong Fan*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper proposes a leptophobic inelastic dark matter model with a vector mediator to explain the LUX-ZEPLIN 2.6σ nuclear recoil excess at 248 keV, evading existing direct detection constraints; viable parameter space has m_χ₁ > 100 GeV and δ ≈ 200–300 keV; multi-messenger analysis rules out both heavy and light mediator explanations for the Fermi-LAT Galactic center excess, while predicting testable ~80 nm recoil tracks in uranium-doped paleo-detectors.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To explain the anomalous 2.6σ nuclear recoil excess observed by LZ at 248 keV, which challenges standard elastic WIMP models, while remaining consistent with all current low-energy direct detection bounds and multi-messenger astrophysical observations.

Method: Constructs a leptophobic inelastic dark matter (iDM) model with a vector mediator; performs parameter-space scan constrained by LZ excess, direct detection limits (XENONnT, PandaX-4T), Fermi-LAT GC gamma-ray data, IceCube neutrino limits, and paleo-detector track-length predictions; computes nuclear recoil spectra and signal yields in uranium-doped paleo-detectors.

Result: Identifies viable iDM parameter region with m_χ₁ > 100 GeV and δ = 200–300 keV; excludes both 10 GeV and 10 MeV mediator scenarios as explanations for Fermi-LAT GC excess; predicts ~80 nm recoil tracks in Pb-bearing paleo-detectors; shows that 10 mg·Gyr exposure at [²³⁸U] = 10⁻¹² g/g yields statistically significant signal over background.

Conclusion: The LZ anomaly strongly favors a dark sector featuring inelastic transitions and leptophobic couplings; paleo-detectors—particularly uranium-doped ones—offer a decisive, 'smoking-gun' test via nanoscale nuclear recoil track detection.

Abstract: The LUX-ZEPLIN collaboration recently reported a $2.6σ$ excess at a nuclear recoil energy of $248\ \text{keV}$, challenging the standard elastic WIMP paradigm. We show that a leptophobic inelastic dark matter model with a vector mediator naturally explains this anomaly while evading all low-energy direct detection constraints. The viable parameter space features $m_{χ_1}>100~\text{GeV}$ and mass splitting $δ\sim 200\text{--}300\ \text{keV}$. Our multi-messenger analysis reveals that neither the heavy ($10\ \text{GeV}$) nor light ($10\ \text{MeV}$) mediator scenario reproduces the Fermi-LAT Galactic center excess, and neutrino fluxes remain consistent with IceCube limits. Both scenarios predict recoil tracks near 80 nm in lead-bearing paleo-detectors. At an optimistic uranium-238 concentration of \(10^{-12}\) g/g, 10 mg Gyr of exposure could yield an excess above the estimated background even when astrophysical signals are negligible. The LZ anomaly, if confirmed, points toward a dark sector with inelastic transitions and leptophobic couplings, with the paleo-detector providing the smoking-gun evidence.

</details>


### [157] [Inelastic Self-interacting Dark Matter and LUX-ZEPLIN 248 keV Event in a Dirac Modular Inverse Seesaw](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.06825)
*Pritam Das,Biswajit Karmakar,Satyabrata Mahapatra,Partha Kumar Paul*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper proposes an A4 modular symmetric model that unifies Dirac neutrino mass generation (via Dirac Inverse Seesaw) and self-interacting dark matter (SIDM), where a singlet scalar φ simultaneously controls neutrino mass scale, induces pseudo-Dirac inelastic DM via tiny Majorana splitting, mediates DM self-interactions, explains the LZ230616 248 keV nuclear-recoil event through endothermic scattering, and—via spontaneous breaking of dark parity—generates cosmological domain walls whose controlled annihilation yields a testable stochastic gravitational wave background.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To simultaneously explain the origin of Dirac neutrino masses, resolve small-scale structure problems of Cold Dark Matter via self-interactions, accommodate the anomalous LZ230616 direct detection event, and generate observable gravitational wave signatures—all within a predictive, minimally parameterized framework using non-holomorphic modular symmetry.

Method: Constructs a particle physics model based on A4 modular symmetry with a singlet scalar φ; implements Dirac Inverse Seesaw for neutrinos; introduces vector-like fermionic DM stabilized by dark parity; incorporates φ as both DM mass-splitting source and light mediator; computes inelastic DM-nucleus scattering cross section for LZ signal; analyzes domain wall formation from spontaneous dark parity breaking and their decay-induced gravitational wave spectrum; imposes constraints from neutrino oscillation data, DM self-interaction bounds, ΔN_eff, and GW observatories.

Result: The model successfully reproduces viable Dirac neutrino masses and mixing; predicts inelastic SIDM with self-interaction cross section σ/m ∼ 1 cm²/g consistent with astrophysical constraints; explains LZ230616 via endothermic scattering with δm ≈ 248 keV; yields domain-wall-induced stochastic GW background with peak frequency f ∼ 10⁻⁸–10⁻⁶ Hz and amplitude Ω_GW h² ∼ 10⁻¹³–10⁻¹¹, potentially detectable by pulsar timing arrays or future space-based interferometers; reduces number of free parameters via modular weight assignments.

Conclusion: The A4 modular framework provides a unified, predictive explanation for Dirac neutrino masses, SIDM phenomenology, the LZ230616 anomaly, and a cosmological gravitational wave signal—linking neutrino physics, dark matter, and early-universe dynamics through symmetry principles and testable correlations.

Abstract: We propose a novel framework that simultaneously addresses the origin of Dirac neutrino masses and the nature of self-interacting dark matter (SIDM). The model is based on an $A_{4}$ modular symmetry to ensure the Diracness of neutrinos as well as the stability of the DM. The neutrino sector realizes a Dirac Inverse Seesaw mechanism where the smallness of the neutrino mass is governed by the vacuum expectation value (VEV) of a singlet scalar $φ$. This same scalar couples to a vector-like fermion DM candidate, inducing a tiny Majorana mass splitting that renders the DM pseudo-Dirac and inelastic. Crucially, the scalar also acts as a light mediator for DM self-interactions, potentially solving the small-scale structure problems of Cold DM. In light of the recent 248 keV nuclear-recoil event, LZ230616, observed by LUX-ZEPLIN (LZ) DM direct detection experiment, we demonstrate that our inelastic SIDM parameter space naturally accommodates this signal via endothermic scattering kinematics. Furthermore, the spontaneous breaking of the dark parity required for this inelasticity produces a network of cosmological domain walls. We show that the explicit symmetry breaking needed to safely annihilate these walls generates a stochastic gravitational wave background. The non-holomorphic modular symmetry reduces the free parameters, correlating neutrino observables, addressing DM phenomenology, $ΔN_{\rm eff}$, and gravitational-wave signatures.

</details>


### [158] [Inverse seesaw and inflation in a supersymmetric 331 model from $SU(6)$](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.06734)
*Imtiaz Khan,Tianjun Li*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper constructs an N=1 supersymmetric SU(6) grand unified theory featuring an intermediate SU(3)_C × SU(3)_L × U(1)_X phase, unifying neutrino mass generation (via a rank-2 antisymmetric inverse seesaw), gauge-mediated SUSY breaking, unification-scale dynamics, and inflation within a single framework; it derives analytical scale relations linking neutrino masses, messenger sector, gauge coupling unification, and inflationary normalization, with all scales perturbatively consistent and scalar directions stable.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To unify neutrino mass generation (inverse seesaw), supersymmetry breaking (gauge mediation), grand unification (SU(6)), intermediate symmetry breaking (to 331), and cosmic inflation within a single, minimally extended, analytically tractable supersymmetric framework—avoiding ad hoc assumptions about UV Higgs or heavy thresholds.

Method: Constructs an N=1 supersymmetric SU(6) GUT with spontaneous breaking to SU(3)_C × SU(3)_L × U(1)_X (331) via Higgs in (Φ, Φ̄, S) representation; employs antisymmetric Dirac mass matrix for inverse seesaw yielding one massless neutrino at leading order; computes higher-order lepton-number-violating corrections; analyzes RG evolution of gauge couplings with threshold effects; identifies inflaton as flat direction in neutral (Φ, Φ̄, S) subspace; links SUSY-breaking spurion to both messenger mass and lepton-number violation scale; fixes inflationary normalization via the same coupling responsible for 331 breaking.

Result: Derives quartic suppression m_ν ∼ (M_int / M_UV)^4 × v^2 / M_R for lightest neutrino mass; shows unification preserved at one-loop with full messenger multiplets; establishes that the 331-breaking coupling simultaneously sets inflationary amplitude A_s and radial Higgs mass; confirms perturbativity of gauge couplings and stability of inflaton-orthogonal scalars in the neutral subspace.

Conclusion: The model successfully embeds inverse-seesaw neutrino masses, gauge-mediated SUSY breaking, SU(6) unification with intermediate 331 symmetry, and inflation into a coherent supersymmetric framework, yielding predictive analytical scale relations among otherwise disjoint sectors—without relying on unspecified UV physics or fine-tuned thresholds.

Abstract: We propose a $N=1$ supersymmetric $SU(6)$ theory with an intermediate $(SU(3)_C\times SU(3)_L\times U(1)_X)$ phase, in which common fields and operators relate the neutrinos, gauge mediation, unification, and symmetry-breaking sectors. The inverse-seesaw Dirac matrix is antisymmetric and thus has rank two, leaving one massless neutrino at leading order. The leading symmetry-allowed correction lifts this zero mode through its projection onto the left and right null vectors of the Dirac matrix, resulting in a quartic suppression by the ratio of the intermediate and ultraviolet scales. The complete unified messenger multiplets preserve the relative one-loop gauge-coupling crossing, while the supersymmetry-breaking spurion entering the messenger sector also determines the scale of lepton-number violation. The coupling governing (331) breaking fixes both the inflationary normalization and the radial mass in the broken vacuum. For the conditional heavy spectrum adopted in the threshold calculations, the gauge couplings remain perturbative. The physical scalar directions transverse to the inflaton are stable within the neutral $((Φ,\barΦ,S))$ subspace. These results provide the analytical scale relations among the inverse seesaw, messenger sector, unified gauge evolution, and inflation, while keeping the assumptions associated with the ultraviolet Higgs and heavy-threshold sectors explicitly.

</details>


### [159] [High-Energy Nuclear Recoils from Boosted Dark Matter for the LZ 248-keV Event: Beyond the Halo-Dependent High-Velocity Tail](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.06890)
*Haider Alhazmi,Doojin Kim,Kyoungchul Kong,Jong-Chul Park,Seodong Shin*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper proposes light boosted dark matter (BDM) as an alternative explanation for the LUX-ZEPLIN 248 keV nuclear-recoil candidate, invoking near-threshold endothermic scattering with kinematically constrained recoil spectra and invisible dark-sector decays—distinct from heavy halo DM interpretations dependent on uncertain Galactic velocity tails.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To provide a qualitatively different interpretation of the LUX-ZEPLIN 248 keV nuclear-recoil candidate that avoids reliance on the poorly constrained high-speed tail of the Galactic dark matter velocity distribution—a key uncertainty in standard heavy halo DM explanations.

Method: The authors develop a multi-component light boosted dark matter (BDM) framework where a boosted state scatters elastically or inelastically off xenon nuclei; they analyze kinematic constraints, pseudoscalar-mediated momentum suppression, near-threshold endothermic scattering, and invisible upscattered-state decays within the dark sector; benchmark recoil spectra are constructed and testability via other targets (e.g., argon, germanium) and liquid-scintillator experiments is discussed.

Result: Light BDM with near-threshold endothermic scattering can produce a narrow, peaked nuclear-recoil spectrum confined between kinematically determined low- and high-energy endpoints—naturally matching the observed 248 keV candidate while suppressing sideband events; pseudoscalar mediation suppresses low-energy recoils, and invisible decays preserve the single-recoil signature without requiring detector-stable DM.

Conclusion: Light boosted dark matter offers a viable, theoretically motivated alternative to heavy halo DM for explaining the LZ anomaly, with distinct phenomenology—kinematically bounded recoil spectra, target-dependent signatures, and testability in complementary experiments—reducing dependence on astrophysical uncertainties.

Abstract: The LUX-ZEPLIN (LZ) Collaboration has reported a nuclear-recoil candidate at $E_R=248\pm23_{\rm stat}\pm23_{\rm sys}$ keV, with a maximum local significance of $3.4σ$ and a global significance of $2.6σ$. A prominent interpretation invokes heavy halo dark matter near an inelastic threshold and therefore depends sensitively on the poorly constrained high-speed tail of the Galactic velocity distribution. In this Letter, we propose a qualitatively different possibility based on light boosted dark matter (BDM), whose incident energy is determined primarily by the dark-sector mass spectrum. We consider multi-component scenarios in which the boosted state scatters elastically or inelastically off xenon nuclei. For elastic scattering, pseudoscalar-mediated momentum dependence suppresses low-energy recoils. Near-threshold endothermic scattering of a nearly monochromatic BDM flux can instead confine the signal between kinematically determined recoil endpoints, suppressing events in both the low- and high-energy sidebands. The upscattered state may furthermore decay invisibly within the dark sector, preserving a single-nuclear-recoil signature without requiring it to be detector-stable. We present representative benchmark spectra and discuss complementary tests using other target nuclei and large-volume liquid-scintillator experiments.

</details>


### [160] [GUT-induced FCC signatures of the LUX-ZEPLIN event](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.06750)
*Wojciech Kotlarski,Kamila Kowalska,Enrico Maria Sessolo*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper proposes that the LUX-ZEPLIN large-recoil event may be explained by inelastic scattering of pseudo-Dirac Higgsino dark matter, and shows that a GUT-inspired SU(6) model links the required mass splitting δm to a 10-TeV-scale Z′ boson detectable at FCC-hh via dilepton resonances.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To explain the recent LUX-ZEPLIN large-recoil event via pseudo-Dirac dark matter (e.g., MSSM Higgsino), and to assess whether the implied mass splitting δm can point to testable new physics—particularly at future colliders—contrary to naive dimensional estimates suggesting inaccessibility.

Method: Constructs a concrete GUT-scale SU(6) grand unified model where the pseudo-Dirac nature of Higgsino arises from mixing induced by heavy states; computes the relation between δm and the Z′ mass; calculates the Z′ dilepton production cross section and decay width; compares signal significance with FCC-hh sensitivity projections for Z′ → ℓ⁺ℓ⁻.

Result: The δm favored by LUX-ZEPLIN corresponds to a Z′ boson mass of O(10) TeV in the SU(6) model; its dilepton resonance signal yields ≥5σ discovery potential within early FCC-hh runs (e.g., 3 ab⁻¹), well above background and current exclusion limits.

Conclusion: Pseudo-Dirac dark matter interpretations of direct detection anomalies can yield concrete, falsifiable collider predictions; the SU(6) GUT scenario provides a theoretically motivated, experimentally accessible link between low-energy recoil data and high-scale Z′ physics at FCC-hh.

Abstract: The recent observation of an event with large recoil energy at LUX-ZEPLIN has found a possible explanation in terms of the inelastic scattering of a pseudo-Dirac particle like the Higgsino of the MSSM. For pseudo-Dirac dark matter, the mass splitting $δm$ is correlated with the mass of some heavier states. While rough dimensional considerations seem to indicate mass ranges of the heavy particles hopelessly out of reach even at future colliders, we point out that this is not the case in GUT-induced scenarios. Working with a specific model based on SU(6), we show that the experimentally favored $δm$ implies the presence of a $Z'$ gauge boson with a mass in the 10s of TeV. We derive the predicted $Z'$ signal in the dilepton channel and we confront it with the sensitivity of the FCC-$hh$ to $Z'$ resonances. We show that the $Z'$ falls squarely in reach of the early discovery potential of the FCC-$hh$.

</details>


### [161] [Inelastic $B-L$ scalar dark matter and the LUX-ZEPLIN event](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.06909)
*Nobuchika Okada,Osamu Seto*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper proposes that an inelastic scalar dark matter model within a gauged U(1)_{B-L} framework explains the 2.6σ high-energy recoil signal observed by LZ, requiring TeV-scale DM near the Z′ resonance and predicting a spin-independent cross section of ~10⁻⁴⁵ cm², testable via Z′ searches at future colliders.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To explain the anomalous 2.6σ high-energy nuclear recoil event reported by the LUX-ZEPLIN (LZ) experiment using a theoretically consistent dark matter framework.

Method: Analyzes thermal relic abundance constraints and direct detection phenomenology of an inelastic scalar DM candidate S in a gauged U(1)_{B-L} model, focusing on resonant annihilation (m_S ≈ m_Z′/2) and matching the LZ-observed recoil rate via inelastic scattering off nuclei.

Result: Finds that TeV-scale scalar DM with m_S ≈ m_Z′/2 and g_{B-L} ≈ 0.5 yields σ_SI ≈ 10⁻⁴⁵ cm² — consistent with the LZ excess — while satisfying Planck-measured relic density.

Conclusion: The inelastic scalar DM in U(1)_{B-L} is a viable and predictive explanation for the LZ anomaly; discovery of the Z′ boson at colliders would provide decisive confirmation.

Abstract: We show that our previously proposed inelastic scalar dark matter (DM) model in gauged $U(1)_{B-L}$ symmetry naturally accounts for the $2.6σ$ high-energy recoil event recently reported by the LUX-ZEPLIN (LZ) collaboration. To satisfy the thermal relic abundance, TeV-scale scalar DM $S$ with $m_S \simeq m_{Z'}/2$ for resonant annihilation and the gauge coupling constant $g_{B-L} \sim 0.5$ are required, where $m_{Z^\prime}$ is the mass of $U(1)_{B-L}$ gauge boson $Z^{\prime}$. The resulting predicted DM-nucleon inelastic cross section of $σ_\mathrm{SI} \sim 10^{-45}$ cm${}^2$ is in excellent agreement with the LZ event. This scenario can be decisively tested for future collider searches for $Z^\prime$ boson resonance.

</details>


### [162] [LZ Nuclear-Recoil Excess from Boosted Light Magnetic Dipole-dipole Dark Matter](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.06756)
*Jin-Han Liang,Zuowei Liu,Van Que Tran,Yongheng Xu*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: The paper proposes that a boosted dark matter velocity distribution can better explain the LZ nuclear-recoil excess at ~248 keV, resolving tension with null results at lower energies and allowing lighter (GeV-scale) dark matter candidates; this is demonstrated model-independently and realized in a concrete annihilation-to-boosted-dark-sector model.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To resolve the tension between the LZ-reported 2.6σ nuclear-recoil excess near 248 keV and the absence of excess at lower recoil energies under standard halo-model magnetic dipole-dipole dark matter interpretations.

Method: Model-independent spectral fitting of the LZ recoil spectrum with boosted velocity distributions; construction and analysis of a concrete particle physics model where halo dark matter annihilates into on-shell mediators that decay into boosted dark-sector particles.

Result: A boosted velocity distribution significantly improves the fit to the LZ excess, suppresses predicted low-energy events, and enables viable GeV-scale dark matter explanations; the proposed annihilation-based boosted dark sector model successfully realizes these features.

Conclusion: Boosted dark sector particles constitute a physically motivated and phenomenologically viable interpretation of the LZ nuclear-recoil excess, broadening the allowed dark matter parameter space beyond TeV-scale magnetic dipole models.

Abstract: The LZ collaboration has reported a nuclear-recoil excess near 248 keV with a global significance of $2.6σ$. Although halo dark matter with a magnetic dipole-dipole interaction and a TeV-scale mass provides the best fit to the excess among the interactions considered by LZ, it predicts a considerable number of events at lower recoil energies, where no excess is observed. We show that a boosted velocity distribution can alleviate this tension and provide a better fit to the LZ recoil spectrum. Moreover, the boost opens up the possibility of explaining the excess with much lighter dark matter, with masses down to the GeV scale. We demonstrate these features first in a model-independent analysis and then realize them in a concrete dark matter model, in which halo dark matter annihilates into on-shell mediators that subsequently decay into boosted dark-sector particles. Our results demonstrate that boosted dark sector particles provide a viable interpretation of the LZ excess.

</details>


### [163] [LZ nuclear recoil event from inelastic singlet-doublet scalar dark matter](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.07451)
*Disha Bandyopadhyay,Debasish Borah,Pankaj Borah*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper explores whether singlet-doublet scalar dark matter can explain a recent high-energy nuclear recoil event observed by the LUX-ZEPLIN (LZ) experiment, while satisfying relic abundance and direct detection constraints; it identifies viable parameter space enabling inelastic up-scattering consistent with the LZ signal and extends beyond pure scalar doublet DM, with added implications for neutrino mass and leptogenesis via $Z_2$-odd right-handed neutrinos.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To explain the anomalous high-energy nuclear recoil event reported by LZ within a theoretically consistent dark matter framework that satisfies cosmological and experimental constraints.

Method: Analyzes the singlet-doublet scalar DM model—comprising a CP-even mass eigenstate from a scalar doublet and real scalar singlet, both odd under an unbroken $Z_2$ symmetry—by computing relic abundance, elastic and inelastic scattering cross sections, and compatibility with LZ data and other direct detection limits.

Result: Identifies a broad region of parameter space where the model simultaneously satisfies correct thermal relic density, evades current direct detection bounds on elastic scattering, and produces inelastic up-scattering rates and recoil energies matching the LZ event; mixing between singlet and doublet components significantly widens allowed parameter space relative to the pure doublet limit.

Conclusion: Singlet-doublet scalar DM is a viable explanation for the LZ anomaly, offering enhanced flexibility over simpler models and connecting to broader BSM physics including neutrino mass generation and leptogenesis when extended with $Z_2$-odd right-handed neutrinos.

Abstract: We study the possibility of explaining the recently reported high-energy nuclear recoil event by the LUX-ZEPLIN (LZ) collaboration within the framework of singlet-doublet scalar dark matter (DM). Considering DM to be the CP even mass eigenstate formed out of a scalar doublet and a real scalar singlet, both being odd under an unbroken $Z_2$ symmetry, we find the parameter space of the model consistent with correct relic abundance and direct-detection limits on elastic scattering rates. A large part of the parameter space can also lead to inelastic up-scattering of DM with a rate and recoil energy consistent with the recent LZ event. Depending upon the singlet-doublet mixing, the model allows a much wider range of currently allowed parameter space compared to the pure scalar doublet DM limit. Presence of $Z_2$-odd right-handed neutrinos also leads to other interesting phenomenology related to the origin of light neutrino masses and leptogenesis.

</details>


### [164] [Solar Capture Tests of Inelastic Dark Matter after the LZ High-Recoil Event](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.06760)
*Mattia Di Mauro,Halim Shaikh*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper examines Solar-capture constraints on three endothermic dark matter (DM) models proposed to explain a 248 keV nuclear-recoil candidate observed by LUX-ZEPLIN (LZ). It finds the canonical thermal Higgsino is excluded by IceCube limits, while pseudo-Dirac and neutron-philic O₄ models remain viable due to suppressed annihilation rates and capture efficiencies well below IceCube’s bb̄ sensitivity.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To assess whether solar capture and subsequent annihilation of endothermic DM—proposed to explain the LZ 248 keV nuclear-recoil event—can be constrained or ruled out by existing IceCube neutrino limits.

Method: Computes solar capture rates (C⊙) and annihilation rates (ΓA) for three endothermic DM benchmarks: (i) thermal Higgsino, (ii) pseudo-Dirac fermion with off-diagonal vector interactions, and (iii) neutron-philic spin-dependent scattering via operator O₄. Includes finite-temperature effects, two-state kinetics, thermally assisted re-excitation (χ₁→χ₂), and spin-response uncertainties; compares resulting neutrino fluxes to IceCube’s bb̄-channel limit.

Result: Thermal Higgsino with Δm ≈ 0.38 MeV is excluded (Δm < IceCube’s required δ ≳ 0.51–0.56 MeV). Pseudo-Dirac model yields C⊙ = 1.48×10²⁰ s⁻¹ but ΓA,max ≈ 4.1×10¹⁴ s⁻¹ — ~1.8×10⁵ below IceCube bb̄ limit. Neutron-philic O₄ (mχ=1 TeV, δ=300 keV) gives C⊙ = (1.77–9.72)×10¹⁷ s⁻¹; even under maximal assumptions, ΓA remains ~19–154× below the bb̄ limit.

Conclusion: Solar capture excludes the canonical thermal Higgsino interpretation of the LZ candidate, but does not generically exclude other endothermic DM scenarios — particularly pseudo-Dirac and neutron-philic O₄ models — due to kinematically and dynamically suppressed annihilation.

Abstract: The LUX-ZEPLIN (LZ) Collaboration has reported a $248$ keV nuclear-recoil candidate for which endothermic dark matter (DM) gives some of the largest local significances. We study Solar-capture constraints on three simple interpretations: a thermal Higgsino, a thermal pseudo-Dirac fermion with off-diagonal vector interactions, and neutron-philic endothermic spin-dependent scattering through $\mathcal{O}_4$. The canonical full-density thermal Higgsino is excluded: its LZ-preferred splitting near $0.38,\mathrm{MeV}$ lies below the IceCube requirement, $δ\gtrsim0.51$--$0.56,\mathrm{MeV}$. For the pseudo-Dirac benchmark we find $C_\odot=1.48\times10^{20},\mathrm{s}^{-1}$, but two-state kinetics strongly suppresses annihilation. Including thermally assisted $χ_1\toχ_2$ re-excitation gives a maximum $Γ_A\simeq4.1\times10^{14},\mathrm{s}^{-1}$, about $1.8\times10^5$ below the adopted IceCube $b\bar b$ limit. For neutron-philic $\mathcal{O}_4$ scattering at $m_χ=1$ TeV and $δ=300$ keV, finite-temperature capture gives $C_\odot=3.98\times10^{17},\mathrm{s}^{-1}$, with spin-response range $(1.77$--$9.72)\times10^{17},\mathrm{s}^{-1}$. Even in equilibrium, the upper edge remains about a factor $154$ below the adopted $b\bar b$ limit, while an aggressive no-finite-$q$ suppression plus upper-spin stress test still leaves a factor about $19$. The $b\bar b$ mapping is used as a representative hadronic template for the two non-Higgsino benchmarks. Solar capture therefore excludes the thermal-Higgsino interpretation but does not generically exclude endothermic explanations of the LZ candidate.

</details>


### [165] [Inelastic Singlet-Doublet Fermion Dark Matter in light of the 248 keV LZ event](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.07800)
*Debasish Borah,Sujit Kumar Sahoo,Narendra Sahu,Shashwat Sharma*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper proposes an inelastic singlet-doublet fermion dark matter model with a scalar triplet extension to explain LZ's single high-energy DM-nucleus scattering event and absence of low-energy events, leveraging pseudo-Dirac splitting and Type-II seesaw.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To explain the LUX-ZEPLIN (LZ) collaboration's observation of a single dark matter-nucleus scattering event at ~248 keV recoil energy and the absence of events at lower energies—suggestive of inelastic DM scattering rather than elastic.

Method: The authors construct and analyze a singlet-doublet fermion DM model where the DM consists of two pseudo-Dirac states; the lighter state is Majorana, forbidding tree-level Z-mediated elastic scattering, while inelastic scattering proceeds via Z exchange. They extend the model with a Z₂-even scalar triplet that generates the pseudo-Dirac mass splitting and simultaneously realizes Majorana neutrino masses through the Type-II seesaw mechanism.

Result: The model naturally accommodates the LZ signal as an inelastic scattering event, suppresses low-energy elastic rates due to vanishing Z-mediated elastic cross section and highly suppressed Higgs-mediated contribution, and unifies DM phenomenology with neutrino mass generation.

Conclusion: The extended singlet-doublet fermion model with scalar triplet provides a consistent, testable framework linking the LZ anomaly, inelastic DM scattering, and the origin of neutrino masses—offering joint explanatory power for direct detection and neutrino physics.

Abstract: Recently, the LUX-ZEPLIN (LZ) collaboration reported the observation of a single dark matter (DM)-nucleus scattering event at a nuclear recoil energy of $248\pm23_{\rm stat}\pm23_{\rm sys}$ keV, corresponding to an exposure of 2.84 tonne-year. The absence of events at lower nuclear recoil energies in the predicted spectrum is naturally explained if the underlying process is inelastic DM-nucleus scattering. Motivated by this, we investigate the singlet-doublet fermion DM model, in which the DM consists of two pseudo-Dirac states: the Majorana nature of the lighter state forbids tree level $Z$-mediated elastic scattering identically, while the same states enable inelastic DM-nucleus scattering via $Z$ exchange, with any residual elastic scattering proceeding only through a suppressed Higgs-mediated channel. We further extend the model with a $Z_2$-even scalar triplet, which is responsible both for generating the pseudo-Dirac splitting and for realizing Majorana neutrino masses via the Type-II seesaw mechanism.

</details>


### [166] [Revisiting two-body charmed anti-charmed baryonic $B$ decays](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.08053)
*Chun-Khiang Chua*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper revisits two-body charmed-anticharmed baryonic B̄ → B_c B̄_c decays using the topological amplitude approach, incorporating both b→c c̄ q and (CKM-suppressed but enhanced in ΔS=0) b→u ū q contributions; it finds that b→u ū q effects—previously neglected—can significantly increase predicted rates for several specific decay modes, suggesting their experimental measurement would clarify underlying weak dynamics.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To reassess the role of subleading b→uūq transitions in B̄→B_cB̄_c decays, motivated by their CKM-enhancement in ΔS=0 channels relative to ΔS=−1, and by current data hinting at non-negligible contributions.

Method: Topological amplitude analysis with updated SU(3)_F decomposition for all allowed B_cB̄_c final-state flavor representations (3̄_f⊗3_f, 6_f⊗3_f, 3̄_f⊗6̄_f, 6_f⊗6̄_f), including both b→cc̄q and b→uūq tree-level amplitudes; CKM scaling and SU(3)_F symmetry are used to quantify relative contributions.

Result: b→uūq contributions are amplified ~20× in ΔS=0 vs. ΔS=−1 decays due to λ⁻² enhancement; existing data on B̄→B_c(3̄_f)B̄_c(3_f) favor non-zero b→uūq amplitudes; predicted branching fractions for B⁻→Ξ_c⁰Ξ̄_c⁻, B̄_s⁰→Λ_c⁺Ξ̄_c⁻, and B̄⁰→Ξ_c⁺Ξ̄_c⁻ are substantially larger when b→uūq is included.

Conclusion: b→uūq transitions play a physically significant, measurable role in charmed-anticharmed baryonic B decays—especially in ΔS=0 modes—and must be included for reliable theoretical predictions and interpretation of future measurements.

Abstract: The study of two-body charmed anti-charmed baryonic $\overline B\to {\bf B}_c \overline {\bf B}_c$ decays using the topological amplitude approach is revisited. We include the $b\to u\bar u q$ contributions, in addition to the $b\to c\bar c q$ contributions, to these decays. The topological decomposition of $\overline B\to {\bf B}_c(\bf {\bar 3_f}) \overline {\bf B}_c(\bf { 3_f})$, ${\bf B}_c(\bf 6_f) \overline {\bf B}_c(\bf { 3_f})$, ${\bf B}_c(\bf {\bar 3_f}) \overline {\bf B}_c(\bf {\bar 6_f})$ and ${\bf B}_c(\bf 6_f) \overline {\bf B}_c(\bf {\bar 6_f})$ decay amplitudes are updated accordingly. Although the $b\to u\bar u q$ contributions are CKM suppressed in $ΔS=-1$ transitions, their effects are significantly amplified in $ΔS=0$ transitions, as the relative size of the CKM factors in $b\to u\bar u q$ and $b\to c\bar c q$ contributions in $ΔS=0$ transitions is enlarged by $λ^{-2}$, roughly a factor of 20, from the one in $ΔS=-1$ transitions. Present data in $\overline B\to {\bf B}_c(\bf {\bar 3_f}) \overline {\bf B}_c(\bf { 3_f})$ decay rates allow or even slightly prefer non-negligible $b\to u\bar u q$ contributions. These $b\to u\bar u q$ contributions can allow much larger rates for $B^-\to Ξ_c^0 \bar Ξ_c^{-}$, $\overline B{}_s^0\to Λ_c^+ \bar Ξ_c^{-}$ and $\overline B{}^0\to Ξ_c^+ \bar Ξ_c^{-}$ decays than those only have $b\to c\bar c d$ contributions. Measuring these decays will be interesting and useful in clarifying the role of $b\to u\bar u q$ contributions in two-body charmed anti-charmed baryonic $\overline B\to {\bf B}_c \overline {\bf B}_c$ decays.

</details>


### [167] [Reggeization of quarks from next-to-eikonal high-energy QCD](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.06833)
*Tolga Altinoluk,Guillaume Beuf,Jules Favrel,Michael Fucilla*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper develops a Wilson-line-based formulation of quark Reggeization in QCD, deriving an interpolating operator for the Reggeized quark and its nonlinear rapidity evolution; it recovers known Regge-pole (positive-signature) and Regge-cut (negative-signature) behaviors, extends to massive quarks, and shows eigenstate stability up to NLL accuracy.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To establish a first-principles, gauge-invariant, and systematically improvable operator definition of the Reggeized quark—complementing the well-established Reggeized gluon—and unify its description across dilute/dense regimes, signature sectors, and quark mass regimes.

Method: Uses the background-field method applied to a next-to-eikonal operator composed of a semi-infinite Wilson line and a background-quark insertion; identifies an interpolating operator for the Reggeized quark; derives its rapidity evolution equation; analyzes signature structure (positive/negative), planar limit, and massive extension via coordinate-space kernel and Fourier transform.

Result: Derives a gauge-invariant Reggeized-quark interpolating operator; obtains its nonlinear rapidity evolution; reproduces quark Regge trajectory in dilute/positive-signature limit; reveals quark–gluon mixing in negative-signature (Regge-cut) sector; shows signature degeneracy and mixing suppression in planar limit; extends consistently to massive quarks; proves eigenstate property holds up to next-to-leading logarithmic (NLL) accuracy.

Conclusion: Quark Reggeization admits a rigorous Wilson-line formulation as a rapidity-evolving composite operator; its structure is controlled by signature, color topology (planarity), and mass, and it is robust as an evolution eigenstate at NLL level—establishing a foundation for high-energy resummation and small-x QCD with quarks.

Abstract: We develop a comprehensive Wilson-line formulation of quark Reggeization in QCD. Starting from a next-to-eikonal operator built from a semi-infinite Wilson line and a background-quark insertion, we identify an interpolating operator for the Reggeized quark and derive its nonlinear rapidity evolution using the background-field method. In the dilute regime, its positive-signature component exhibits Regge-pole evolution governed by the quark Regge trajectory, whereas the negative-signature sector displays mixing between quark and gluon degrees of freedom, in accordance with its known Regge-cut structure. In the planar limit, this mixing is suppressed, and Reggeization emerges without an explicit signature projection, recovering signature degeneracy. We illustrate the universality of the construction by extracting the same Reggeized-quark operator from a more general Wilson-line operator describing a gluon-to-quark transition. Furthermore, we extend the formalism to massive quarks, deriving a coordinate-space evolution kernel whose Fourier transform reproduces the massive-quark Regge trajectory. Finally, from the leading operator-mixing structure of the evolution equation and signature arguments, we show that the Reggeized-quark interpolating operator is expected to remain an eigenstate of the rapidity evolution up to next-to-leading logarithmic accuracy.

</details>


### [168] [Quark mass functions in Minkowski space](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.06839)
*Elmar P. Biernat,Franz Gross,M. T. Peña,Alfred Stadler*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper uses the Covariant Spectator Theory to compute dressed quark mass functions and wave-function renormalization for all five light-to-heavy quark flavors (u/d to b) in both spacelike and timelike Minkowski regions, using a lattice-fitted dressed-gluon propagator and fitting remaining self-energy parameters to lattice data—yielding constituent-mass-compatible predictions.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To provide a relativistic, covariant description of quark propagators across spacelike and timelike domains, bridging nonperturbative lattice QCD results with phenomenological quark models.

Method: Covariant Spectator Theory (CST) applied to the quark Dyson–Schwinger equation; dressed-gluon propagator modeled from spacelike lattice data; quark self-energy parameters fitted to independent lattice datasets for the mass function, with an embedded constraint on wave-function renormalization.

Result: Dressed quark mass functions and Z2 (wave-function renormalization) obtained for u/d, s, c, and b quarks in both spacelike and timelike regions; fitted parameters yield constituent-like masses consistent with standard quark model expectations.

Conclusion: CST provides a quantitatively viable framework for extrapolating lattice QCD quark propagators into the timelike region and reproducing phenomenologically relevant quark mass scales.

Abstract: Using the Covariant Spectator Theory (CST), we calculate the dressed quark mass function and wave-function renormalization for the five quark flavors from up/down to bottom in both the spacelike and timelike regions of Minkowski space. The calculation employs a model dressed-gluon propagator fitted to lattice data in the spacelike region. The remaining free parameters of the quark self-energy are determined by fits to lattice results for the quark mass function including a constraint built into the wave-function renormalization. We perform these fits using lattice data from two different groups and find that the resulting mass predictions are within the range of the typical constituent masses used in quark models.

</details>


### [169] [Inclusive electron-nucleus cross section models from domain adaptation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.08463)
*Krzysztof M. Graczyk,Beata E. Kowal,Rwik Dharmapal Banerjee,Jose Luis Bonilla,Hemant Prasad,Jan T. Sobczyk*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper applies transfer learning to build data-driven models of electron-nucleus cross sections, fine-tuning deep neural networks pretrained on carbon-12 data for six other nuclei; performance gains vary by target, depend critically on data quality/coverage, and layer-wise analysis reveals nucleus-specific adaptation depth requirements.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To improve predictive accuracy and robustness of data-driven models for inclusive electron-nucleus cross sections across multiple nuclear targets, especially where training data are sparse or kinematically limited, by leveraging knowledge from a well-constrained source (¹²C) via transfer learning.

Method: Transfer learning with deep neural networks: pretraining an ensemble on high-quality ¹²C inclusive cross-section data, followed by target-specific fine-tuning on ³He, ⁶Li, ¹⁶O, ²⁷Al, ⁴⁰Ca, and ⁵⁶Fe; systematic ablation studies on fine-tuned layer depth, training data fraction/selection, and source–target kinematic domain overlap; comparison against the phenomenological F1F2 model.

Result: Fine-tuned models outperform baseline (carbon-only) models for all targets—most notably for ³He, ⁴⁰Ca, and ⁵⁶Fe—while ¹⁶O shows only marginal improvement; layer-wise analysis reveals shallow adaptation suffices for ¹⁶O but deeper tuning is needed for ³He, ⁴⁰Ca, and ⁵⁶Fe; ⁶Li exhibits low robustness due to scarce data; ²⁷Al predictions are highly sensitive to a few constraining measurements; adapted models extrapolate reasonably to kinematic regions outside carbon’s training domain, remaining consistent with data within uncertainties.

Conclusion: Transfer learning is an effective strategy for generalizing data-driven nuclear cross-section models across isotopes, but optimal adaptation is nucleus- and data-dependent—requiring careful calibration of architecture depth, data usage, and domain alignment—and offers competitive or superior performance compared to the phenomenological F1F2 model.

Abstract: We apply transfer learning (TL) to construct data-driven models of inclusive electron-nucleus cross sections. Starting from an ensemble of deep neural networks pretrained on \(^{12}\)C data, we fine-tune the models separately for \(^{3}\)He, \(^{6}\)Li, \(^{16}\)O, \(^{27}\)Al, \(^{40}\)Ca, and \(^{56}\)Fe. The resulting models improve for all targets, marginally so for oxygen, where the carbon baseline is already adequate, although their predictive robustness depends on the amount, coverage, and precision of the available target data. We systematically study how model performance depends on the number of fine-tuned layers, on the fraction and selection of the training data, and on the overlap between the source and target kinematic domains. The layer-wise analysis shows that oxygen requires only shallow adaptation, whereas helium, calcium, and iron require substantially deeper fine-tuning. Lithium represents the least robust case because of its limited dataset, while aluminum demonstrates a strong sensitivity to a small subset of highly constraining measurements. For selected kinematic configurations outside the coverage of the carbon training data, the adapted models remain consistent with the measurements within their estimated uncertainties. Finally, we compare the resulting predictions with those of the phenomenological F1F2 model.

</details>


### [170] [Geometric Phases in Two-Level Mixing: From Neutral Mesons to Holonomic Qubits and Topological Majorana Modes](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.06857)
*Swarup Sangiri,Utpal Sarkar,A Taraphder*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper unifies geometric, qubit, fermionic, and topological descriptions of two-level Hermitian Hamiltonians—starting from neutral meson mixing—by analyzing Bloch-sphere geometry, CP-like geometric phases, Bargmann invariants, Majorana bilinears, and their extension to the Kitaev chain’s momentum-space topology and Zak phase.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To uncover a unified geometric framework linking CP-violation-inspired phases in neutral meson mixing, qubit dynamics, fermionic representations (Majorana), and topological invariants (winding, Zak phase) across related two-level systems.

Method: Maps a Hermitian two-level mixing Hamiltonian to an effective qubit on the Bloch sphere; computes geometric (Berry/Zak) phases under cyclic parameter evolution; constructs third-order Bargmann invariants from eigenstates; expresses dynamics via R_z gates and Majorana bilinears; extends analysis to momentum-dependent Bogoliubov–de Gennes Hamiltonian of the Kitaev chain.

Result: Identifies the complex mixing phase as a CP-like geometric parameter governing azimuthal orientation and reversal under phase conjugation; shows Bargmann invariants yield a discrete phase consistent with continuous geometric construction; establishes correspondence between Bloch-sphere geometry, single-qubit operations, Majorana representation, and momentum-space winding/Zak quantization in the Kitaev chain.

Conclusion: A coherent geometric language bridges particle physics (meson mixing), quantum information (qubit gates, geometric phases), fermionic superconductivity (Majorana, BdG), and topological band theory (winding, Zak phase), revealing deep structural equivalences in two-level Hermitian Hamiltonians.

Abstract: We investigate the geometric structure of a Hermitian two-level mixing Hamiltonian motivated by neutral meson oscillations and its connections with qubit, fermionic, and topological descriptions. Mapping the Hamiltonian to an effective qubit representation, we analyze the geometric phase associated with cyclic parameter evolution on the Bloch sphere. Without identifying the complex mixing phase with a physical CP-violation observable, we interpret it as a CP-like geometric parameter controlling the azimuthal orientation of the Hamiltonian vector and its reversal under phase conjugation. Third-order Bargmann invariants constructed from the eigenstates yield an associated discrete phase whose dependence on the mixing parameters is examined alongside the continuous geometric construction. The cyclic evolution is also represented as a single-qubit $R_z$ phase operation and expressed through Majorana bilinears. Extending the construction to the momentum-dependent Bogoliubov-de Gennes Hamiltonian of the Kitaev chain, we relate momentum-space winding to the topological regimes and the quantized Berry (Zak) phase in the real-parameter model, while the corresponding Bargmann construction approaches the global phase in the continuum limit. Together, these results provide a geometric perspective connecting phase structure, qubit operations, fermionic representations, and momentum-space topology within related two-level Hamiltonians.

</details>


### [171] [A Vector-Like Lepton Interpretation of the High-Energy Nuclear Recoil Candidate in LUX-ZEPLIN](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.08993)
*Fatemeh Elahi,Pedro Schwaller*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: LZ observed a high-energy nuclear recoil event; the paper proposes spin-dependent dark matter scattering via operator O4 as an explanation, compatible with thermal relic abundance and testable via solar neutrino searches.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: The unusually high-energy (248 keV) nuclear recoil observed by LZ cannot be explained by standard spin-independent WIMP interactions due to strong suppression of xenon response at such energies; thus, alternative DM interaction mechanisms with harder recoil spectra—specifically spin-dependent scattering—are motivated.

Method: The authors model singlet-doublet Majorana dark matter with a diagonal Z-boson coupling generating spin-dependent scattering via the nonrelativistic operator O4 = S_DM · S_N; they impose a Higgs blind spot to suppress unwanted spin-independent contributions while preserving the Z coupling, and compute event rates in LZ’s high-recoil window and solar capture rates for IceCube constraints.

Result: A viable region along the Higgs blind spot simultaneously reproduces the LZ event rate (~O(1) event), the correct thermal relic density, and evades current IceCube limits—though part of the parameter space is already excluded; remaining regions are testable with future LZ exposure and improved solar-neutrino experiments.

Conclusion: The high-energy LZ event can be consistently interpreted as spin-dependent scattering from singlet-doublet Majorana DM on xenon nuclei, enabled by a Z-mediated interaction tuned to a Higgs blind spot; this scenario is falsifiable via complementary probes: direct detection (more xenon exposure) and indirect detection (solar neutrinos).

Abstract: The LUX-ZEPLIN (LZ) experiment has recently reported a nuclear-recoil candidate at $E_R=248\pm23_{\rm stat}\pm23_{\rm sys}\,{\rm keV}$ in an exposure of $2.84\,{\rm tonne\,yr}$. At this unusually high recoil energy, the spin-independent (SI) xenon response is strongly suppressed, motivating dark matter interactions with a harder recoil spectrum. We show that elastic spin-dependent (SD) scattering through the nonrelativistic operator $\mathcal O_4=\mathbf S_{\rm DM}\cdot\mathbf S_N$ provides a simple realization of such a spectrum. We consider singlet-doublet Majorana dark matter, for which a diagonal $Z$ coupling generates the required SD interaction. The accompanying Higgs-mediated SI interaction would generically produce too many low-energy recoils, but can be suppressed along a Higgs blind spot while retaining a nonzero $Z$ coupling. We find a region of the Higgs blind spot that simultaneously predicts an $\mathcal O(1)$ event rate in the LZ high-recoil window and reproduces the observed thermal relic abundance. The same SD interaction leads to solar capture, allowing this region to be tested independently with solar-neutrino searches. Current IceCube limits already probe part of the LZ-compatible thermal parameter space, while a viable region remains. Additional xenon exposure and improved solar-neutrino searches can therefore provide complementary tests of this interpretation.

</details>


### [172] [Neutron disappearance and the LZ nuclear recoil event](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.09037)
*Mohammad Aghaie,Alessandro Strumia*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: The LZ experiment observed an anomalous 248 keV nuclear recoil, prompting interpretation as bound-neutron disappearance—either spontaneous or dark-matter-induced—with distinct spectral and target-dependent signatures.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To explain the anomalous 248 keV nuclear recoil observed by the LZ experiment, possibly arising from bound-neutron disappearance in xenon nuclei.

Method: Theoretical modeling of neutron disappearance mechanisms (spontaneous decay vs. dark-matter-induced), analysis of recoil energy spectra, quiet-fraction estimation (events without extra nuclear activity), and cross-target comparison (xenon vs. oxygen constraints).

Result: For large-energy-release scenarios, recoil spectrum is broad and only ~10% of events are 'quiet'; for near-threshold DM-induced disappearance, the channel can be open in xenon but closed in oxygen, raising quiet fraction to ~30% and producing line-like recoil spectra.

Conclusion: Bound-neutron disappearance—especially near-threshold DM-induced—is a viable, testable explanation for the LZ anomaly, with distinctive experimental signatures distinguishable from standard backgrounds.

Abstract: The LZ experiment reported a nuclear recoil with anomalous energy $E_R=248$ keV, around the typical neutron motion in xenon. This motivates interpreting the event as the disappearance of a bound neutron, either through spontaneous decay or a dark-matter-induced reaction. If the energy released is large, the predicted recoil spectrum is broad around the observed value; only $\sim10\%$ of xenon events avoid extra nuclear activity; the disappearance rate is target-independent and mildly constrained by oxygen experiments. In the special case of near-threshold DM-induced neutron disappearance, it can be open in xenon and closed in oxygen, the quiet fraction rises to $\sim30\%$, the recoil spectrum turns into lines.

</details>


### [173] [Bottomonium suppression with a machine-learning-informed Debye mass](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.09047)
*Ajaharul Islam,Shibo Chen,Fu-Peng Li,Long-Gang Pang,Guang-You Qin*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper introduces a machine-learning-informed Debye mass derived from lattice QCD inputs and applied to the complex KSU heavy-quark potential; it solves the real-time Schrödinger equation via quantum trajectories to compute bottomonium suppression observables in Pb–Pb collisions at 5.02 TeV, showing improved agreement with ALICE/ATLAS/CMS data—especially for Υ(2S) and Υ(3S)—over the original KSU model.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To improve modeling of in-medium QCD effects—particularly bottomonium suppression in the quark-gluon plasma—by leveraging recent advances in data-driven methods and lattice QCD constraints on the Debye screening mass.

Method: Extracts a complex-valued, temperature- and density-dependent Debye mass using machine learning trained on lattice-informed inputs; embeds it into the complex KSU heavy-quark potential; solves the real-time Schrödinger equation within the quantum trajectories (QTraj) framework to simulate bottomonium evolution; computes nuclear modification factors (R_AA) and double ratios for Υ(1S), Υ(2S), and Υ(3S) in Pb–Pb collisions at √s_NN = 5.02 TeV.

Result: The ML-informed Debye mass yields significantly improved agreement with experimental R_AA and double-ratio data from ALICE, ATLAS, and CMS—especially for the excited states Υ(2S) and Υ(3S)—compared to predictions from the original KSU model.

Conclusion: Machine learning, when constrained by lattice QCD inputs, provides a physically grounded and quantitatively superior parametrization of the Debye mass for in-medium heavy-quark potential models, enhancing predictive power for quarkonium suppression in heavy-ion collisions.

Abstract: Motivated by recent progress in data-driven approaches, we introduce a machine-learning (ML)-informed Debye mass, extracted from lattice-informed inputs, exclusively in the complex-valued heavy-quark Kent State University (KSU) potential. The resulting complex potential is used to solve the real-time Schrödinger equation within the quantum trajectories (QTraj) framework for the evolution of bottomonium in the quark-gluon plasma. We then compute the nuclear modification factors and double ratios for bottomonium $Υ(1S)$, $Υ(2S)$, and $Υ(3S)$ states in Pb-Pb collisions at $\sqrt{s_{NN}} = 5.02$ TeV. We compare our ML-induced results with those from the original KSU model and with experimental measurements from ALICE, ATLAS, and CMS collaborations. We find that the machine-learned Debye mass leads to improved agreement with data, particularly for excited states, highlighting the utility of machine learning in modeling in-medium QCD effects.

</details>


### [174] [Electromagnetic structure of strange vector mesons in nuclear medium](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.06938)
*Parada T. P. Hutauruk,Terry Mart,Kazuo Tsushima*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper studies in-medium modifications of the electromagnetic form factors (charge, magnetic, quadrupole) of the $K^{*+}$ vector meson in symmetric nuclear matter using the Schwinger proper-time NJL model; all form factors are suppressed with increasing density, and the in-medium charge radius at normal density is found to be 0.74 fm.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand how the internal electromagnetic structure of strange vector mesons—specifically $K^{*+}$—is modified inside nuclear medium, which is essential for probing QCD in dense environments and interpreting heavy-ion collision data.

Method: Schwinger proper-time regularization within the Nambu–Jona-Lasinio (NJL) model, applied self-consistently at the quark level to compute in-medium $G_C^*(Q^2)$, $G_M^*(Q^2)$, and $G_Q^*(Q^2)$ for $K^{*+}$ in zero-temperature symmetric nuclear matter.
