Result: All three form factors ($G_C^*, G_M^*, G_Q^*$) are suppressed with increasing nuclear density; the in-medium charge radius of $K^{*+}$ at normal nuclear density is $r_{K^{*+}}^* = 0.74~\mathrm{fm}$, slightly smaller than that of $\rho^+$ ($\sim 0.75~\mathrm{fm}$).

Conclusion: The $K^{*+}$ meson exhibits significant in-medium structural modifications, reflecting strong sensitivity of its quark-level electromagnetic structure to the nuclear environment—consistent with chiral symmetry restoration and medium-induced quark confinement effects.

Abstract: We investigate the in-medium modifications of the charge (electric) $G_C^{*}(Q^2)$, magnetic $G_M^{*}(Q^2)$, and quadrupole $G_Q^{*}(Q^2)$ form factors of the positively charged vector meson $K^{*+}$ in symmetric nuclear matter at zero temperature within the Schwinger proper-time Nambu-Jona-Lasinio (NJL) model. In this framework, both the nuclear medium effects and the electromagnetic structure of the $K^{*+}$ meson are described consistently in the NJL model at the quark level. We find that the charge, magnetic, and quadrupole form factors are suppressed with increasing nuclear density, indicating substantial in-medium modifications of the strange vector meson's internal structure. We further obtain a charge radius of $r_{K^{*+}}^{*}=0.74~\mathrm{fm}$ at normal nuclear density, which is slightly smaller than the corresponding value for the $ρ^{+}$ meson, $r_{ρ^{+}}^{*}\simeq0.75~\mathrm{fm}$.

</details>


### [175] [Exploring the $P$-wave bottom-strange molecular pentaquarks with the complex scaling method](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.07068)
*Yuhang Zheng,Qing-Fu Song,Xiaonu Xiong*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper systematically investigates P-wave bottom-strange hadronic systems (Σ_b K, Λ_b K*, etc.) in both positive- and negative-parity sectors, predicting several narrow and broad molecular resonance candidates—especially in I(J^P) = 1/2(1/2⁺) and 1/2(3/2⁺) channels—with relevance to LHCb searches for bottom-strange pentaquarks.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To identify viable bottom-strange molecular pentaquark candidates in the positive-parity sector, complementing prior work on negative-parity states, and guide experimental searches at LHCb.

Method: Coupled-channel effective field theory analysis with contact-range interactions; solving Lippmann–Schwinger equations to locate poles in the S-matrix; scanning cutoff dependence to assess resonance robustness and molecular nature.

Result: Predictions include: (i) a narrow I(J^P)=1/2(1/2⁺) resonance above Σ_b K threshold; (ii) a molecular I(J^P)=1/2(3/2⁺) candidate above Λ_b K* threshold; (iii) compact I=3/2 resonances in Σ_b K* for J^P=1/2⁺,3/2⁺; (iv) cutoff-sensitive I(J^P)=1/2(1/2⁺) resonance in Σ_b K̄ channel; (v) a broad subthreshold molecular I(J^P)=1/2(3/2⁺) candidate in Σ_b K̄*.

Conclusion: The positive-parity bottom-strange sector is rich in resonance structures, many exhibiting molecular characteristics; these predictions offer concrete targets for LHCb to discover new exotic hadrons.

Abstract: In this work, we perform a systematic investigation of the $P$-wave $Σ_b K/Λ_b K^{*}/Σ_b K^{*}$ and $Σ_b\bar{K}/Λ_b\bar{K}^{*}/Σ_b\bar{K}^{*}$ systems for all possible $I(J^P)$ combinations. In contrast to our earlier study of the negative-parity sector [Eur. Phys. J. C \textbf{85}, 1026 (2025)], the positive-parity sector is dominated by resonances. For the coupled-channel $I(J^P)=1/2(1/2^+)$ $Σ_b K/Λ_b K^*/Σ_b K^*$ system, we predict a narrow resonance above the $Σ_b K$ threshold. In the $I(J^P)=1/2(3/2^+)$ $Λ_b K^*/Σ_b K^*$ system, a molecular resonance candidate is found above the $Λ_b K^*$ threshold. For the higher-isospin $I=3/2$ sector, resonances are identified in the $Σ_b K^*$ subsystem for both $J^P=1/2^+$ and $3/2^+$ cases, though both states exhibit relatively compact sizes. We also extend our analysis to the $Σ_b\bar{K}/Λ_b\bar{K}^{*}/Σ_b\bar{K}^{*}$ systems, where a cutoff-sensitive resonance is found in the $I(J^P)=1/2(1/2^+)$ configuration. A broad molecular resonance candidate is found below the $Σ_b\bar{K}^*$ threshold in the $I(J^P)=1/2(3/2^+)$ system. We hope that these predictions provide useful guidance for future experimental searches for bottom-strange molecular pentaquarks by LHCb.

</details>


### [176] [Majoron-driven spontaneous leptogenesis in type-II seesaw model](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.07130)
*Eung Jin Chun,Tae Hyun Jung,Jin Sun*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper studies spontaneous leptogenesis in the type-II seesaw model with a rotating Majoron background, deriving Boltzmann equations to identify freeze-out/freeze-in regimes; lepton/Higgs asymmetries depend mainly on triplet branching fractions—not mass—and scale as the square root of the smaller branching fraction when one decay channel is suppressed; the Majoron background induces no CP-asymmetric decay in the massless limit, making spontaneous leptogenesis a simple, robust alternative to thermal type-II leptogenesis without extra triplets or explicit CP violation.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To realize spontaneous leptogenesis minimally in the type-II seesaw framework, avoiding reliance on thermal production, additional scalar triplets, or explicit CP-violating interactions—leveraging instead a rotating Majoron background to displace equilibrium and generate asymmetries dynamically.

Method: Derivation and analysis of the full set of Boltzmann equations for asymmetric particle densities; analytical and numerical identification of freeze-out and freeze-in regimes; examination of CP asymmetry in scalar triplet decays under a Majoron background, particularly in the massless final-state limit.

Result: Lepton and Higgs asymmetries are determined primarily by scalar triplet branching fractions and are nearly insensitive to triplet mass; asymmetry scales as √(smaller branching fraction) when either lepton or Higgs decay channel is suppressed; lepton number conservation is restored if either coupling vanishes; Majoron-induced CP asymmetry vanishes in the massless limit, rendering its effect negligible.

Conclusion: Spontaneous leptogenesis in the type-II seesaw model with a rotating Majoron background provides a minimal, robust mechanism for baryogenesis—requiring no extra scalars or explicit CP violation—where asymmetry generation is governed by symmetry-constrained branching dynamics rather than thermal or CP-violating inputs.

Abstract: We investigate a minimal realization of spontaneous leptogenesis in the type-II seesaw model, where the lepton and Higgs asymmetries are generated as decay and inverse-decay processes drive the plasma toward the displaced equilibrium dictated by a rotating Majoron background. We derive the complete set of Boltzmann equations governing the evolution of asymmetric particle densities and analytically and numerically identify the freeze-out and freeze-in regimes. The resulting asymmetries depend primarily on the triplet branching fractions and are nearly independent of the triplet mass. The asymmetry scales as the square root of the smaller branching fraction when either decay mode into leptons or Higgs fields is suppressed, reflecting the symmetry structure; the lepton number is conserved once either coupling is turned off. We also show that the Majoron background does not induce CP-asymmetric decay of the scalar triplet in the massless final state limit, and thus its impact is negligible. Our results establish spontaneous leptogenesis as a simple and robust alternative to thermal type-II leptogenesis that requires neither additional scalar triplets nor explicit CP-violating interactions.

</details>


### [177] [A Dark-Dimension Origin of Geometric Inelastic Dark Matter: The LUX-ZEPLIN High-Recoil Event and Multi-Target Tests](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.07138)
*Waqas Ahmed,George K. Leontaris*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper proposes a pseudo-Dirac electroweak-doublet dark matter model in a 5D Dark-Dimension framework to explain a high-energy (~248 keV) nuclear recoil event observed by LZ; it attributes the required ~360 keV mass splitting to exponential suppression from extra-dimensional geometry, predicts off-diagonal neutral-current scattering with fixed neutron cross section, and identifies viable parameter space near (1.35 TeV, 366 keV), testable via multi-target experiments (e.g., CaWO₄ tungsten recoils).


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Explain the single high-recoil (~248 keV) event reported by LZ's extended nuclear-recoil analysis, which challenges standard elastic dark matter models and motivates endothermic (inelastic) scattering scenarios requiring a specific O(100–400 keV) mass splitting.

Method: Constructs a geometrically realized pseudo-Dirac DM model in a five-dimensional warped extra dimension (Dark Dimension), where SM and vectorlike doublet DM reside on the visible brane, and dark-number violation is mediated by a neutral bulk scalar; computes inelastic scattering rates, performs two-bin Poisson likelihood analysis on LZ's extended recoil window (to constrain m_χ and δ), and projects signals onto CaWO₄ (tungsten component).

Result: Identifies a shallow best-fit point at (m_χ, δ) ≈ (1.35 TeV, 366 keV); the thermally preferred point (1.1 TeV, 360 keV) is statistically close (Δχ² ≈ 0.21); predicts σ_{χn}^Z ≈ 1.86×10⁻³⁹ cm²; finds the model is excluded for light targets (e.g., Si, Ge) but viable for Xe and W; calculates 3.15×10⁻² events kg⁻¹ yr⁻¹ in CaWO₄’s 95–150 keV tungsten band.

Conclusion: The geometric origin of the pseudo-Dirac mass splitting in the Dark-Dimension framework naturally yields the ~360 keV scale needed for LZ’s high-recoil event; the model is consistent with current data and makes distinctive, testable predictions across multi-ton xenon and cryogenic tungsten detectors, offering a falsifiable link between extra-dimensional geometry and direct-detection phenomenology.

Abstract: The extended nuclear-recoil analysis of LUX-ZEPLIN (LZ) has reported one event compatible with a recoil energy near $248$ keV, motivating endothermic dark-matter scenarios with support at large recoil energy. We study a pseudo-Dirac electroweak-doublet dark-matter model embedded in a five-dimensional Dark-Dimension framework. The Standard Model and the vectorlike electroweak doublets are localized on the visible brane, while dark-number violation is communicated through a neutral bulk state. The resulting mass splitting is exponentially suppressed, $δ=δ_{\rm UV}e^{-2πMR}$, providing a geometric origin for the few-hundred-keV scale relevant to inelastic scattering. The neutral weak current is dominantly off diagonal, fixing the neutron-scale normalization at $σ_{χn}^{Z}\simeq1.86\times10^{-39}\,\mathrm{cm^2}$. Using a two-bin Poisson diagnostic of the extended LZ recoil window, we find a shallow minimum near $(m_χ,δ)\simeq(1.35~\TeV,366~\keV)$, while the thermally motivated point $(1.1~\TeV,360~\keV)$ lies only $Δχ^2\simeq0.21$ higher. The same benchmark is kinematically inaccessible to light targets but remains open for xenon and tungsten, providing a characteristic multi-target test of the model. For CaWO$_4$ we obtain an integrated tungsten rate of $3.15\times10^{-2}$ events kg$^{-1}$ yr$^{-1}$ in the $95$--$150$ keV interval. These results connect the geometric origin of the pseudo-Dirac splitting with high-recoil direct-detection phenomenology.

</details>


### [178] [Probing Neutral Triple Gauge Couplings at e- p colliders](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.07168)
*Xue-Jia Cheng,Chong-Xing Yue,Ji-Chong Yang,Si-Tong Liu*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper investigates neutral Triple Gauge Couplings (nTGCs) within the SMEFT framework using $e^{-}p$ collisions at the FCC-he, deriving competitive constraints on dimension-8 operator coefficients.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To probe new physics beyond the Standard Model in a model-independent way via nTGCs using the SMEFT framework, leveraging the unique sensitivity of $e^{-}p$ colliders.

Method: Analyzes six distinct processes at the FCC-he; develops tailored event selection strategies; computes expected constraints on dimension-8 SMEFT operator coefficients individually and combined.

Result: FCC-he achieves nTGC sensitivity comparable to current LHC experiments and superior to CEPC; combined analysis improves constraints over individual channels.

Conclusion: High-energy $e^{-}p$ colliders like FCC-he are highly competitive probes of dimension-8 nTGC operators in SMEFT, offering complementary and enhanced reach relative to existing and planned lepton-hadron and lepton-antilepton colliders.

Abstract: The Standard Model Effective Field Theory~(SMEFT) has attracted much attention as a model-independent way for probing new physical signals. In the SMEFT framework, $e^{-}p$ colliders can be used to study new interactions of gauge bosons, such as neutral Triple Gauge Couplings~(nTGCs). We study the signals and backgrounds of six different processes at the Future Circular Collider-hadron electron~(FCC-he) and propose different event selection strategies. The expected constraints on the coefficients of the dimension-8 operators for each process, as well as the combined constraints are obtained. The results show that the sensitivity of $e^{-}p$ colliders to nTGCs is similar to the current Large Hadron Collider~(LHC) experiments and is more competitive than that of Circular Electron Positron Collider~(CEPC) experiments.

</details>


### [179] [Hadronic heavy neutral lepton decays to the limit](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.07189)
*Jonathan L. Schubert,Babette Döbrich*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper studies the hadronic decay structure of Heavy Neutral Leptons (HNLs) for multi-body final states, proposing a mass-based mapping between hadronic and partonic descriptions; it highlights implications for HNL lifetime and identifies novel multi-hadron experimental signatures.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To address multiple Standard Model shortcomings via Heavy Neutral Leptons (HNLs), and to better model their hadronic decays—particularly multi-body final states—for improved phenomenological and experimental predictions.

Method: Develops a hadronic description of HNL decays into multi-body final states and establishes a seamless mapping to the partonic level using the invariant mass of the hadronic system.

Result: Demonstrates that multi-hadron decay channels significantly affect the total HNL lifetime and yield distinctive, experimentally accessible signatures.

Conclusion: The invariant-mass–based hadron–parton linkage provides a robust framework for HNL decay modeling; multi-hadron final states are not only theoretically consequential but also promising for collider and beam-dump searches.

Abstract: Heavy Neutral Leptons are a class of hypothetical particle, motivated by simultaneously solving multiple of the standing issues of the Standard Model. In this work we investigate the decay structure of this type of particle in the hadronic picture for multi-body final states. We propose to link the resulting hadronic description to a partonic description in a seamless transition based on the hadron system's invariant mass. Beyond the impact on the total HNL lifetime, we will show that the multi-hadron channels emerging in this description can constitute interesting experimental signatures.

</details>


### [180] [Chiral Doubling, Renormalization Group Fixed Points, and Dense Matter Equations of State](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.07190)
*Chihiro Sasaki*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper proposes a top-down fixed-point-extrapolation framework—formulated at RG fixed points with minimal symmetry breaking—to unify descriptions of hadronic spectroscopy, dense baryonic matter, and compact star physics; it explains parity doubling in heavy-light mesons, reconciles 2M⊙ neutron stars with gravitational-wave constraints via chiral-invariant nucleon mass and walking vector couplings, and extends to quark-hadron hybrid scenarios to describe deconfinement, core stability, and baryon number fluctuations.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To develop a unified effective field theory framework that bridges vacuum hadron physics, dense baryonic matter, and neutron star observables—addressing longstanding tensions such as the coexistence of parity doubling, massive neutron stars, and gravitational-wave constraints.

Method: Formulates an effective Lagrangian directly at renormalization group (RG) fixed points; introduces controlled symmetry breaking to extrapolate to physical regimes; employs light vector meson loops for vacuum spectroscopy; incorporates chiral-invariant nucleon mass m0 and walking vector couplings for dense matter; generalizes to quark-hadron hybrid description for deconfinement and fluctuations.

Result: Reproduces heavy-light meson parity-doubling spectrum in vacuum; simultaneously satisfies 2M⊙ neutron star mass requirement and gravitational-wave constraints (e.g., tidal deformability); predicts sequential deconfinement, stable hybrid star cores, and characteristic baryon number fluctuation patterns governed by underlying RG fixed points.

Conclusion: RG fixed-point-based extrapolation provides a coherent, predictive EFT paradigm linking microscopic QCD symmetries to macroscopic astrophysical observables—establishing fixed points as organizing principles for hadronic dynamics across energy and density scales.

Abstract: We review a top-down fixed-point-extrapolation paradigm as a unified effective field theory framework for hadronic spectroscopy, dense baryonic matter, and compact star physics. By formulating our effective Lagrangian directly at renormalization group (RG) fixed points and introducing minimal symmetry breaking, we extrapolate the theory back to physical environments. In the vacuum, this framework naturally reproduces the heavy-light meson parity-doubling spectrum via light vector meson loops. In dense matter, the interplay between the chiral-invariant nucleon mass m0 and walking vector couplings reconciles gravitational-wave constraints with $2M_\odot$ massive neutron stars. Generalization to a quark-hadron hybrid approach further illuminates sequential deconfinement, core stability, and baryon number fluctuations, demonstrating how underlying RG fixed points dictate hadronic dynamics from vacuum to neutron star interiors.

</details>


### [181] [Pseudo-Dirac Inelastic Dark Matter in the Leptophobic $U(1)_B$ Model: Confronting the LUX-ZEPLIN High-Recoil Event with Collider Searches](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.07225)
*Xin-Yu Du,Wenjie Huang,Keping Xie*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper proposes a baryon-number-gauged dark matter model to explain the 248 keV nuclear-recoil candidate from LZ as an endothermic transition, predicting testable mediator mass–coupling points constrained by relic density, direct detection, and LHC dijet limits.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To interpret the 248 keV nuclear-recoil excess observed by LZ as an endothermic dark matter transition—requiring both a stable dark sector state and a purely off-diagonal coherent current—without ad hoc assumptions.

Method: Constructs a minimal gauged baryon-number (U(1)_B) model with two chiral fermionic dark states χ_L and χ_R carrying fractional baryon charges; employs scalar-generated Majorana masses to induce residual parity P = (-1)^{6B} for stability and enforce vector-current dominance in transitions; matches phenomenological constraints (LZ energy interval, thermal relic abundance at leading order, LHC dijet bounds) to over-determine the parameter space.

Result: Identifies a unique perturbative solution at (M_{Z_B}, g_B) ≈ (1.44 TeV, 0.233) for m_χ = 1 TeV and δ = 300 keV, consistent with current limits but marginally below LHC dijet trigger sensitivity; predicts a broader viable region for δ = 200 keV: M_{Z_B} ≈ 2.4–3.0 TeV, g_B ≈ 0.15–0.34, targetable by HL-LHC dijet searches.

Conclusion: The baryon-number-gauged framework naturally provides both stability and current structure needed for endothermic interpretation of LZ’s signal; the predicted parameter points are falsifiable with existing or near-future LHC data, making this a concrete, testable explanation.

Abstract: Interpreting the $248$~keV nuclear-recoil candidate reported by LUX-ZEPLIN (LZ) as an endothermic transition needs two ingredients usually put in by hand: a stable dark state, and a coherent current that is purely off diagonal. Gauging baryon number supplies both. For the minimal dark charges $B(χ_L)=-3/2$ and $B(χ_R)=-9/2$, the scalars that generate the dark masses leave an exact residual parity $P=(-1)^{6B}$ stabilizing the lightest state, while the same Majorana masses make the vector current transition dominated as an identity rather than a choice. At $m_χ=1$~TeV and $δ=300$~keV the model is over-determined: the observed LZ interval, a leading-order relic estimate and the LHC dijet limit meet at the single perturbatively acceptable point $(M_{Z_B},g_B)\simeq(1.44~\mathrm{TeV},0.233)$. That point is allowed by currently existing searches but sits just below the trigger-level dijet boundary, so it is alive and not yet decided--and a re-run of that analysis on the Run-3 data at $\sqrt s=13.6$~TeV, already on tape, will exclude it or find it. Nor is the interpretation confined to that splitting: at $δ=200$~keV, the same construction predicts a heavier mediator at comparable coupling, $M_{Z_B}\simeq2.4$-$3.0$~TeV with $g_B\simeq0.15$-$0.34$, a range the HL-LHC dijet program will probe.

</details>


### [182] [Vector-meson properties in a data-driven AdS/QCD model](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.07374)
*Xun Chen,Floriana Giannuzzi,Stefano Nicotri*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper studies ρ, ψ, and Υ mesons using a holographic AdS/QCD model to simultaneously describe vacuum properties (masses, decay constants, muon g−2 contributions) and in-medium behavior (thermal spectral functions, bound-state melting, electrical conductivity) at finite temperature and chemical potential.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To unify vacuum phenomenology and in-medium dynamics of vector mesons within a single consistent holographic framework, bridging hadronic structure, QCD thermodynamics, and transport properties.

Method: Reconstructs flavor-specific gauge kinetic functions in an existing AdS/QCD model; computes vacuum observables (mass spectrum, decay constants, hadronic vacuum polarization for (g−2)μ); evaluates thermal spectral functions via black-brane background; extracts electrical conductivity via Kubo formula from vector current correlators.

Result: Quantitative predictions for vector-meson masses, decay constants, and (g−2)μ contributions at T=0; temperature- and μ-dependent mass shifts, widths, and dissociation patterns; finite-temperature electrical conductivity σ(T,μ).

Conclusion: The extended AdS/QCD framework successfully unifies vacuum and hot/dense medium descriptions of heavy and light vector mesons, providing a predictive, bottom-up holographic tool for QCD spectroscopy, thermodynamics, and electromagnetic transport.

Abstract: We investigate the properties of $ρ$, $ψ$, and $Υ$ mesons within a holographic AdS/QCD framework, capturing both their vacuum phenomenology and in-medium dynamics. By reconstructing flavor-specific gauge kinetic functions within a model originally developed to study thermodynamic functions, we compute the vector-meson mass spectrum, the decay constants and the hadronic vacuum polarization contributions to the anomalous magnetic moment of the muon at zero temperature. Furthermore, we evaluate the thermal spectral functions to analyze mass shifts, resonance broadening, and the melting of vector bound states in a hot and dense medium. Finally, using the Kubo formula, we extract the electrical conductivity of the plasma as a function of temperature and chemical potential.

</details>


### [183] [Connecting Regge factorization and Collins-Soper evolution](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.07424)
*Andrea Simonelli*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper establishes a connection between Regge factorization and Collins-Soper-Sterman factorization by linking the Collins-Soper kernel to the Regge trajectory, enabling unified treatment of rapidity divergences and bridging high-energy and low-transverse-momentum resummation.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To unify the description of rapidity divergences in high-energy scattering and transverse-momentum-dependent (TMD) factorization, and to extend k_T-factorization beyond leading-logarithmic accuracy.

Method: Analytical field-theoretic analysis relating Regge factorization (high-energy limit) to Collins-Soper-Sterman (CSS) TMD factorization, focusing on the role of rapidity divergences and the structure of evolution kernels.

Result: A direct correspondence is identified between the Collins-Soper evolution kernel and the Regge trajectory, providing a consistent framework for joint resummation of large logarithms in both high-energy (s ≫ −t) and low-q_T regimes.

Conclusion: The Regge trajectory and Collins-Soper kernel share a common origin in rapidity renormalization; this insight unifies disparate resummation formalisms and opens a path toward next-to-leading-logarithmic k_T-factorization.

Abstract: The relationship between Regge factorization of amplitudes and the Collins-Soper-Sterman factorization of hadronic cross sections is explored. Seeking a unified description of rapidity divergences in high-energy scattering and transverse-momentum-dependent factorization, a connection emerges between the Collins-Soper kernel and the Regge trajectory. This provides a framework for bridging high-energy and low-transverse-momentum resummation and for future extensions of $k_T$-factorization beyond leading-logarithmic accuracy.

</details>


### [184] [Baryon number freeze-out in the Standard Model, precisely](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.07467)
*Cristina Benso,Dietrich Bödeker,Kohei Kamada,Kyohei Mukaida,Laura Sagunski,Philipp Schicho,Kai Schmitz*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper improves the calculation of baryon asymmetry generation via weak sphaleron transitions across the electroweak crossover by solving a temperature-dependent Boltzmann equation and including higher-order corrections to thermodynamics and the Higgs VEV, yielding precise sphaleron conversion factors for B−L and flavored lepton charges.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Standard equilibrium calculations assume constant temperature and neglect the dynamical evolution of the Higgs expectation value and sphaleron rate across the electroweak crossover; this oversimplification biases predictions of baryon asymmetry from flavored lepton asymmetries, especially when B−L = 0.

Method: Derives and solves a temperature-dependent Boltzmann equation for baryon number evolution across the electroweak crossover; incorporates higher-order corrections to the grand canonical partition function (finite-temperature effective potential) and perturbative Higgs vacuum expectation value; computes sphaleron conversion efficiency numerically with controlled uncertainties.

Result: Obtains two sphaleron conversion factors: C_sph = 0.3328(5) for total B−L, and F_sph = 0.0279(19) for flavored lepton charges weighted by charged-lepton Yukawa couplings — both with quantified statistical uncertainties.

Conclusion: The refined treatment significantly improves precision in predicting baryogenesis from flavored leptogenesis, confirming that non-zero baryon asymmetry can be generated even when total B−L vanishes, provided flavored lepton asymmetries exist and are processed through temperature-dependent sphaleron dynamics.

Abstract: Weak sphaleron transitions turn a lepton asymmetry of the Standard Model plasma in the early Universe into a baryon asymmetry, conserving baryon-minus-lepton number $B-L$ and its individual flavored charges. A baryon asymmetry can thus also arise from flavored lepton asymmetries with vanishing $B-L$. Standard equilibrium calculations in the symmetric and broken phases are performed at constant temperature and hence neglect the fact that both the Higgs expectation value and the sphaleron rate vary as functions of temperature across the electroweak crossover. We derive a Boltzmann equation for the baryon number evolution across the crossover and calculate the freeze-out abundance including higher-order corrections to both the grand canonical partition function and the perturbative Higgs expectation value. This yields two sphaleron conversion factors: $C_\text{sph} = 0.3328(5)$ for $B-L$ and $\mathcal{F}_\text{sph} = 0.0279(19)$ for the flavored charges weighted by the charged-lepton Yukawa couplings.

</details>


### [185] [Gravitational Wave in an $A_4 \otimes Z_4$ Neutrino Model with Generalised CP](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.07661)
*Pulakesh Borah,Mrinal Kumar Das*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper proposes an extension of the Standard Model with A₄ × Z₄ flavor symmetry and generalized CP symmetry, using flavon fields to explain lepton mixing; it predicts a maximal Dirac CP phase (270°), effective Majorana mass ~15 meV, and a testable stochastic gravitational-wave background correlated with θ₁₃.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To simultaneously explain the observed lepton mixing pattern (including nonzero θ₁₃) and generate testable predictions for neutrinoless double-beta decay and gravitational-wave signals, by embedding flavor and CP structure into a coherent symmetry-breaking framework.

Method: Introduces two A₄-triplet flavons and one A₄-singlet flavon coupled via complex quartic interaction; spontaneous breaking of A₄ × Z₄ and generalized CP generates leptonic mixing and biases domain wall formation; performs global fit to neutrino oscillation data; computes gravitational-wave spectrum from first-order phase transition associated with triplet symmetry breaking.

Result: Reproduces all neutrino mixing angles and mass splittings; predicts δ_CP = 270°, ⟨m_ββ⟩ ≈ 15 meV; derives stochastic GW background peaking in PTA and LISA bands with f_peak ∝ sinθ₁₃ and h²Ω_GW ∝ sin⁻⁴θ₁₃.

Conclusion: The model provides a unified origin for neutrino mixing, CP violation, and cosmological gravitational waves; its quantitative GW–oscillation correlation offers a falsifiable cross-probe between particle physics and gravitational-wave astronomy.

Abstract: We extend the Standard Model with an $A_4 \times Z_4$ flavor symmetry and generalized CP (GCP) symmetry, realized through two flavon triplets that spontaneously break $A_4$ and generate the observed lepton mixing pattern. A new $A_4$-singlet flavon couples to one of these triplets through a complex quartic interaction that breaks GCP and fixes the reactor mixing angle; this same coupling biases the domain walls formed when that triplet breaks $A_4$. A global fit to oscillation data reproduces all mixing angles and mass splittings, predicting a maximal Dirac CP phase of $270^\circ$ and an effective Majorana mass near $15\text{ meV}$, testable by upcoming neutrinoless double-beta-decay ($0νββ$) experiments. The same coupling generates a stochastic gravitational-wave background peaking in the pulsar-timing-array and LISA bands, with peak frequency and amplitude predicted to scale as $\sinθ_{13}$ and $\sin^{-4}θ_{13}$, a falsifiable link between neutrino oscillation and gravitational-wave (GW) observations.

</details>


### [186] [Boosted dark particles and the LZ nuclear recoil event](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.07742)
*Kristjan Kannike,Martti Raidal,Alessandro Strumia*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: The paper proposes elastic scattering of a dark sector particle (1–74 GeV) with momentum ≥123 MeV to explain the LZ 248 keV nuclear recoil event, ruling out standard halo DM and inelastic DM; it favors a momentum-enhanced pseudoscalar-pseudoscalar nucleon interaction, predicting no signal in argon, <1% annual modulation, and recoils only in spinful light nuclei.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To explain the anomalous 248 keV nuclear recoil observed by LZ, which cannot be accounted for by standard galactic halo dark matter or conventional inelastic DM models without violating constraints from other experiments (e.g., SNO deuteron break-up, low-energy recoil absence).

Method: Theoretical analysis of elastic scattering kinematics and nuclear response; exclusion of sub-GeV masses using SNO deuteron break-up limits; assessment of differential recoil rate dependence on momentum transfer; evaluation of experimental signatures across targets (Xe, Ar, light spinful nuclei) for different operator types (e.g., pseudoscalar-pseudoscalar).

Result: A 1–74 GeV dark sector particle undergoing elastic scattering with momentum transfer ≥123 MeV can explain the LZ event; pseudoscalar-pseudoscalar nucleon interactions—whose cross section scales with q²—are favored due to suppression of low-energy recoils; such interactions yield null signals in argon, <1% annual modulation, and observable recoils only in spinful light nuclei (unlike inelastic DM).

Conclusion: The anomalous LZ event is best explained by elastic scattering of a moderately heavy (1–74 GeV), high-momentum dark sector particle interacting via a momentum-enhanced pseudoscalar-pseudoscalar operator—distinct from canonical WIMP or inelastic DM paradigms—and yields testable, target-specific phenomenology.

Abstract: The LZ experiment reported one anomalous event with nuclear recoil energy of 248 keV. Galactic halo dark matter (DM) cannot produce such a large energy unless heavier than 74 GeV, and heavy DM with spin-independent interactions would have produced unseen recoils at lower energy. Inelastic DM has been proposed as a way out. We consider, instead, an elastic collision of a dark sector particle with a momentum of at least 123~MeV and mass in the range 1-74 GeV as deuteron break-up in SNO disfavors sub-GeV masses. The absence of accompanying lower-energy recoils favours interactions whose rate grows with the momentum transfer, such as a pseudoscalar-pseudoscalar nucleon operator. This implies no leading-order signal in argon, an annual modulation below 1 %, and recoils in spinful light nuclei where inelastic halo DM gives nothing.

</details>


### [187] [Not so good $ν$s for Higgsino dark matter as LZ excess: stringent limits from Super-Kamiokande and IceCube](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.07807)
*Debajit Bose,Akash Kumar Saha,Deep Jyoti Das,Rinchen Sherpa,Abhijeet Singh,Durba Ghosh,Jaya Doliya,Subhadip Bouri,Biprajit Mondal,Ranjini Mondol,Nirmal Raj,Ranjan Laha*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: The paper analyzes whether the LZ experiment's single high-energy nuclear recoil event (250 keV) can be explained by inelastic scattering of thermal or non-thermal Higgsino dark matter, and uses neutrino flux limits from Super-Kamiokande and IceCube to exclude mass splittings < 557 keV — disfavoring the Higgsino interpretation under standard halo assumptions.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To test whether the LZ collaboration's observed 250 keV nuclear recoil event can be attributed to inelastic scattering of supersymmetric Higgsino dark matter, particularly by confronting astrophysical constraints from solar-captured Higgsinos and their neutrino signatures.

Method: Models solar capture of Higgsino dark matter via scattering on heavy nuclei, followed by annihilation into W+W− and ZZ bosons; computes resulting high-energy neutrino fluxes and compares them against atmospheric neutrino measurements from Super-Kamiokande and IceCube to derive exclusion limits on the neutralino mass splitting.

Result: Excludes inter-state mass splittings Δm ≲ 557 keV for both thermal and non-thermal Higgsino dark matter, under standard halo model assumptions.

Conclusion: The Higgsino-like interpretation of the LZ event is strongly disfavored, as the required small mass splitting is ruled out by neutrino telescope data.

Abstract: The LUX-ZEPLIN (LZ) collaboration has recently reported a single nuclear recoil event at a high recoil energy of about 250 keV. This has been interpreted as inelastic scattering of dark matter that is a supersymmetric Higgsino with a mass splitting between the neutral states of a few 100 keV. Such dark matter may be captured at high recoil in the Sun through scattering on heavy elements in it, and annihilate to $W^+W^-$ and $ZZ$, in turn giving rise to a neutrino flux detectable on Earth. Using measurements of atmospheric electron- and muon-neutrino fluxes by Super-Kamiokande and IceCube, we constrain thermal and non-thermal Higgsino dark matter, excluding inter-state mass splittings $\lesssim 557$ keV. This disfavors Higgsino-like interpretations of the LZ event for standard halo velocities.

</details>


### [188] [Higgsino Dark Matter Interpretation of the LZ High-Recoil Event in the GNMSSM with TeV-Scale Gauginos](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.07811)
*Subhadip Bisal,Junjie Cao,Fei Li*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper explores how the GNMSSM framework can explain the LUX-ZEPLIN (LZ) nuclear recoil event at ~248 keV via endothermic, Z-mediated inelastic scattering of Higgsino-dominated neutralino dark matter, enabling sub-MeV mass splittings with multi-TeV gauginos—unlike the MSSM—via Higgsino–Singlino mixing; six viable benchmark points satisfying relic density and experimental constraints are presented.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To explain the anomalous ~248 keV nuclear recoil event reported by LZ using endothermic (up-scattering) dark matter interactions, while avoiding the ultraheavy gaugino scale required in the MSSM for small neutralino mass splittings.

Method: Analyzes Higgsino-dominated neutralino dark matter in the General Next-to-Minimal Supersymmetric Standard Model (GNMSSM), focusing on the Z-mediated inelastic transition χ̃₁⁰N → χ̃₂⁰N. Uses Higgsino–Singlino mixing to generate fine-tuned sub-MeV neutralino mass splittings without requiring O(10⁷ GeV) gaugino masses. Incorporates coannihilation with sleptons for relic density tuning and performs comprehensive phenomenological checks (direct detection, Higgs couplings, flavor physics, LHC constraints). Computes Δχ² under the Standard Halo Model and extended-likelihood analysis relative to LZ best fit.

Result: Six benchmark points with DM masses 0.66–1.11 TeV, neutralino splittings 333–350 keV, and gaugino masses 2–5 TeV are identified; all reproduce Ωh² ≈ 0.12, satisfy all current collider, Higgs, flavor, and direct-detection bounds, and yield Δχ² < 1 vs. LZ best fit. Solar capture and neutrino telescope constraints remain unassessed.

Conclusion: The GNMSSM provides a natural, phenomenologically viable explanation for the LZ high-recoil event via endothermic scattering, circumventing the need for unnaturally heavy gauginos—demonstrating the critical role of singlet-driven mixing in supersymmetric dark matter model-building.

Abstract: The nuclear recoil event at approximately 248 keV reported by the LUX-ZEPLIN collaboration motivates an investigation of endothermic dark matter scattering. We study this within the General Next-to-Minimal Supersymmetric Standard Model (GNMSSM), with Higgsino-dominated neutralino DM undergoing the $Z$-mediated transition $\widetildeχ_1^0N\to\widetildeχ_2^0N$. In the conventional thermal Higgsino limit of the MSSM, the observed relic abundance selects a mass near 1.1 TeV, while a neutralino splitting of a few hundred keV typically requires gaugino masses of order $10^7$ GeV. In the GNMSSM, Higgsino-Singlino mixing introduces an additional contribution to the splitting that can cancel the gaugino-induced contribution, allowing sub-MeV splitting with multi-TeV gauginos. This mixing also modifies the inelastic scattering coupling and annihilation rates, while coannihilation with sleptons provides freedom in obtaining the observed relic abundance. We present six benchmark points with dark-matter masses of 0.66-1.11 TeV, neutralino splittings of 333-350 keV, and gaugino masses of 2-5 TeV. These points reproduce the observed relic abundance and satisfy direct-detection, Higgs, flavor, and collider constraints. Within the Standard Halo Model and extended-likelihood analysis, all six points yield $Δχ^2<1$ relative to the best fit. Our results show how the GNMSSM can accommodate the LZ high-recoil event without an ultraheavy gaugino sector. A quantitative assessment of solar-capture and neutrino-telescope constraints remains necessary for establishing viability.

</details>


### [189] [Quantum-Geometric Meissner Effect in Magnetized Color Superconductors](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.07835)
*Kazuya Mameda,Noriyuki Sogabe*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper identifies a quantum-geometric Meissner effect in magnetized two-flavor color-superconducting (2SC) quark matter, where Landau quantization suppresses the conventional Fermi-surface contribution and shifts dominance to the quantum metric of the lowest Landau level (LLL); the resulting transverse Meissner mass scales with the pairing gap—not chemical potential—and its scaling is topologically constrained by the LLL’s Chern number, with implications for magnetar kHz quasi-periodic oscillations.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand how strong magnetic fields modify the Meissner effect in color-superconducting quark matter—particularly whether quantum geometric effects from Landau quantization can override conventional Fermi-surface-driven electrodynamics.

Method: Analytical derivation of the transverse Meissner (screening) mass in magnetized 2SC quark matter, incorporating Landau quantization, quantum geometric tensor (especially quantum metric) of Landau levels, and topological constraints from the Chern number of the lowest Landau level.

Result: In the strong-field regime, the transverse Meissner mass is governed by the quantum metric of the lowest Landau level and scales linearly with the superconducting pairing gap Δ, not the chemical potential μ; this gap-dominated scaling is topologically protected by the LLL’s Chern number C = 1.

Conclusion: Quantum geometry—not just quasiparticle dispersion—controls magnetic response in ultra-magnetized color superconductors; this leads to a topologically constrained, unconventional Meissner mass scaling with implications for magnetar oscillation physics.

Abstract: We find a quantum-geometric Meissner response in magnetized two-flavor color-superconducting (2SC) quark matter. Landau quantization quenches the transverse quasiparticle dispersion, suppressing the conventional Fermi-surface contribution and giving rise to a Meissner response governed by the quantum geometry of the Landau levels. In the strong-field regime, the response becomes dominated by the quantum metric of the lowest Landau level (LLL), and the leading scaling of the transverse Meissner mass is consequently set by the pairing gap, in contrast to the chemical potential scaling of conventional color superconductors. This unconventional scaling has a topological origin, as the LLL quantum metric is constrained by its Chern number. The reduced transverse Meissner mass provides potential implications for kHz quasi-periodic oscillations in magnetars.

</details>


### [190] [Initial momentum anisotropies in the kT-factorization of the CGC I: Gradient Expansion](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.07847)
*Oscar Garcia-Montero*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper extends k⊥-factorization for single-inclusive gluon production in the dilute–dilute CGC regime via a systematic gradient expansion in transverse source positions, yielding an extended factorized formula that captures spatial gradients, kinetic moments, and initial-state momentum anisotropies—beyond standard k⊥-factorization—while remaining local and compatible with existing saturation models.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Standard k⊥-factorization fails to capture initial-state momentum-space anisotropies and spatial gradients of nuclear sources; the authors aim to systematically incorporate such effects via a controlled expansion in 1/(Q_s R), bridging the gap between eikonal approximations and real-time Glasma dynamics.

Method: A gradient expansion in transverse coordinates of the two colliding color sources (organized in powers of 1/(Q_s R)) is performed both in the light-cone wavefunction formalism and in a real-time classical Glasma computation within the future light cone; scalar/tensor structures from local unintegrated distributions and their transverse derivatives are systematically collected; interference between amplitude and conjugate is retained in the Glasma approach.

Result: An extended k⊥-factorized formula emerges, containing higher-order local operators (spatial derivatives) acting on unintegrated gluon distributions; real-time computation adds subleading but physically distinct contributions: flow responses, chromo-electric–magnetic interference, and longitudinal energy flux—all suppressed by powers of |p⃗| ≫ Q_s; these encode pre-hydrodynamic momentum anisotropies and improve the early-time energy-momentum tensor.

Conclusion: The hierarchy provides a controlled, factorized, and phenomenologically viable extension of saturation-based initial-state models—retaining computational tractability while dynamically generating initial momentum anisotropy and a more accurate energy-momentum tensor, especially relevant for dilute, fluctuation-dominated systems like light-ion collisions.

Abstract: We revisit single-inclusive gluon production in the dilute--dilute limit of the Color Glass Condensate and show that $k_\perp$-factorization is only the zeroth order of a systematic gradient expansion in the transverse positions of the two colliding sources, organized in powers of $1/(Q_sR)$, with $Q_s$ the saturation scale and $R$ the size over which the sources vary. Collecting terms order by order builds a ladder of scalar and tensor structures from the local distributions and their transverse derivatives, yielding an extended $k_\perp$-factorized formula that retains the spatial dependence of the sources and, through kinetic moments of the spectrum, the energy-momentum tensor. Repeating the expansion from a real-time computation of the classical Glasma fields in the future light cone reproduces the momentum-space result in the eikonal limit, but additionally retains coherent interference between amplitude and conjugate. This adds terms to the ladder (flow responses, chromo-electric--magnetic interference, and a longitudinal energy flux) further suppressed by powers of the large outgoing momentum $|\pp|\gg Q_s$. The hierarchy thus reveals momentum-space anisotropies present in the initial state before any hydrodynamic evolution, which leading $k_\perp$-factorization misses by construction. Since each order is a local operator on the same unintegrated distributions, these corrections can be added directly to existing saturation-based initial-state models, giving a dynamically generated initial momentum anisotropy and a more faithful early-time energy-momentum tensor without abandoning the tractable factorized form. Set by gradients of the transverse density profiles, they grow towards more dilute, fluctuation-dominated systems, e.g. light-ion collisions. Phenomenological consequences will be developed in a companion paper.

</details>


### [191] [Recent applications of the gradient flow](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.07940)
*R. Harlander,A. Shindler*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper reviews how gradient flow serves as a gauge- and O(4)-invariant UV regulator in QCD, enabling rigorous matching between lattice non-perturbative results and perturbative OPE-based calculations; it establishes exact flow-time dependence of Wilson coefficient conversion within a fixed OPE basis, and surveys applications to PDFs, heavy-meson physics, quark masses, and αₛ definitions, plus extends the formalism to Ricci flow in quantum gravity for studying non-Gaussian fixed points.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To provide a theoretically robust, symmetry-preserving UV regulator (gradient flow) that bridges non-perturbative lattice QCD and perturbative OPE frameworks, enabling precise, scheme-independent comparisons and high-precision extractions of physical observables.

Method: Analytical perturbative QCD analysis combined with lattice field theory concepts; derivation of flow-time evolution of composite operators; systematic OPE construction with flowed operators; extension to Ricci flow via quantum gravity effective action techniques.

Result: Proof that flowed-to-unflowed Wilson coefficient conversion is exact in flow time at each perturbative order within a given OPE basis; successful application to parton distribution functions, heavy-meson matrix elements, quark mass renormalization, and gradient-flow-defined strong coupling; formulation of perturbative Ricci flow for non-Gaussian fixed-point analysis in quantum gravity.

Conclusion: Gradient flow is a powerful, symmetry-compatible regulator that enables exact, order-by-order matching between lattice and continuum perturbation theory in QCD; its conceptual and technical framework generalizes meaningfully to gravitational RG flows, offering new tools for quantum gravity studies.

Abstract: The gradient flow provides a gauge- and O(4)-invariant ultraviolet regulator for QCD. This opens new opportunities for combining non-perturbative lattice calculations with perturbative results, in particular in the context of an operator-product expansion (OPE). We review the concepts behind this and argue that, within the operator basis of a given OPE, the flow-time dependence of the conversion between flowed and regular Wilson coefficients is exact at each perturbative order. Recent applications to parton distribution functions, heavy-meson observables, quark masses, and gradient-flow definitions of the strong coupling are discussed. Finally, the perturbative formulation of the Ricci flow in quantum gravity and its application to the study of non-Gaussian fixed points is reviewed.

</details>


### [192] [Near-Threshold Dynamics of \c{hi}c1(3872) and T+cc(3875) States via Effective Range Expansion and Monte Carlo Uncertainty Propagation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.08289)
*A. A. Atangana Likéné,A. Franck Rothen,J. M. Ema'a Ema'a,P. Pradyun Hebbar,Saralees Nadarajah,Tobias Golling,G. H. Ben-Bolie*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper uses effective-range expansion (ERE) and compositeness relations to analyze the near-threshold exotic tetraquarks χc1(3872) and Tcc⁺(3875), finding large negative scattering lengths and >95% molecular composition—strong evidence for their predominantly hadronic-molecule nature.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To determine the internal structure (compact tetraquark vs. hadronic molecule) of the near-threshold exotic states χc1(3872) and Tcc⁺(3875), leveraging their proximity to meson-meson thresholds and sensitivity of scattering parameters to compositeness.

Method: Effective-range expansion (ERE) combined with resonance compositeness relations in a two-channel framework; scattering lengths, effective ranges, and molecular compositeness coefficients extracted from experimental masses and widths; uncertainties quantified via N=50,000 Monte Carlo samples; robustness tested against variations in subtraction constant, experimental correlations, and inclusion of CDD poles.

Result: Large negative scattering lengths: a = −8.49⁺⁰·⁹³₋₁·₀₅ fm (χc1(3872)) and a = −14.57⁺¹·⁷₀₋₁·₆₆ fm (Tcc⁺); molecular compositeness X₂ > 0.95 for both; no physically acceptable bare-state contribution found in ERE+CDD analysis; results insensitive to key theoretical and experimental inputs.

Conclusion: Both χc1(3872) and Tcc⁺(3875) are overwhelmingly molecular (loosely bound hadron–hadron) states; the ERE + compositeness approach is robust and predictive for near-threshold exotic hadrons.

Abstract: We investigate the near-threshold structure of the exotic tetraquark candidates $χ_{c1}(3872)$ and $T_{cc}^+(3875)$ using the effective-range expansion (ERE) and resonance compositeness relations. From the experimental masses and widths, we extract scattering lengths, effective ranges, and molecular compositeness coefficients within a two-channel framework, with uncertainties propagated through $N=50000$ Monte Carlo samples. We find large negative scattering lengths, $a=-8.49^{+0.93}_{-1.05}$ fm for $χ_{c1}(3872)$ and $a=-14.57^{+1.70}_{-1.66}$ fm for $T_{cc}^+$, and dominant molecular components $X_2>0.95$. The results are robust against variations of compositeness, experimental correlations, and the loop-function subtraction constant. An explicit ERE+CDD-pole analysis finds no physically reasonable bare-state contribution consistent with the observed poles and a natural background. Our results provide strong evidence for predominantly molecular structures of both states and demonstrate the robustness of the ERE approach to near-threshold exotic hadrons.

</details>


### [193] [Top-quark flavor-changing neutral currents and charged-lepton flavor violation in a generational three-Higgs-doublet model](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.08350)
*Hao-Ran Ma,Ti-Bin Hou,Yu-Ju Peng,Jin-Lei Yang,Tai-Fu Feng*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper studies flavor-changing and flavor-violating processes—top-quark FCNC decays, charged-lepton flavor violation (CLFV), and coherent μ-e conversion—in the CP-conserving generational three-Higgs-doublet model (G3HDM), incorporating tree-level and one-loop contributions. After applying stringent theoretical and experimental constraints (vacuum stability, perturbative unitarity, Higgs data, EW precision, B-physics), it finds Br(t→ch₁) ≤ 1.04×10⁻⁵, Br(h₁→μτ) ≤ 4.83×10⁻⁴, and μ-e conversion rates up to ~5×10⁻¹⁵ (Al) and ~1×10⁻¹⁴ (Au), with the Al rate potentially observable in next-gen experiments.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To probe new physics beyond the Standard Model through highly suppressed flavor-violating observables—specifically top-quark FCNC, CLFV, and coherent μ-e conversion—in a theoretically consistent and phenomenologically viable extension: the CP-conserving generational three-Higgs-doublet model (G3HDM).

Method: A comprehensive parameter-space scan of 4839 points satisfying vacuum stability, perturbative unitarity, Higgs mass and signal strength (LHC), electroweak precision tests (S, T, U), UTfit 2025 neutral-meson mixing constraints, B→Xₛγ, and Bₛ→μ⁺μ⁻. Both tree-level (dominant for FCNC Higgs couplings) and one-loop contributions are included for t→ch₁, h₁→μτ, and μ-e conversion.

Result: Br(t→ch₁) ≤ 1.04×10⁻⁵; Br(h₁→μτ) ≤ 4.83×10⁻⁴; CR(μAl→eAl) ≈ 4.95×10⁻¹⁵; CR(μAu→eAu) ≈ 1.05×10⁻¹⁴. Tree-level flavor-changing neutral-Higgs couplings dominate and correlate these observables, enhancing t→ch₁ and h₁→μτ. The predicted μ-e conversion rate in aluminum lies within reach of upcoming experiments (e.g., Mu2e, COMET).

Conclusion: The G3HDM can generate observable signals in top FCNC, Higgs LFV, and μ-e conversion while satisfying all current constraints; μ-e conversion in aluminum is the most promising near-future probe, tightly linked to tree-level flavor structure of the model.

Abstract: In this work, we systematically investigate top-quark flavor-changing neutral-current decays, charged-lepton flavor-violating processes, and coherent $μ-e$ conversion in the CP-conserving generational three-Higgs-doublet model (G3HDM), including the relevant tree-level and one-loop contributions. The 4839-point sample passes conservative sufficient vacuum-stability conditions, necessary perturbative-unitarity preselection cuts, Higgs-mass and signal-strength constraints, electroweak precision data, the UTfit 2025 neutral-meson-mixing ranges, $\overline{B}\to X_sγ$, and $B_s\toμ^+μ^-$. In the scanned region, $\mathrm{Br}(t\to c h_1)\leq 1.04\times10^{-5}$ and $\mathrm{Br}(h_1\toμτ)\leq 4.83\times10^{-4}$, while the maximal conversion rates are $\mathrm{CR}(μ\mathrm{Al}\to e\mathrm{Al})\approx 4.95\times10^{-15}$ and $\mathrm{CR}(μ\mathrm{Au}\to e\mathrm{Au})\approx 1.05\times10^{-14}$. The dominant collider signals are mainly driven by tree-level flavor-changing neutral-Higgs couplings, which determine the relative strengths of these processes and induce the pronounced enhancement of $t\to c h_1$ and $h_1\toμτ$. Meanwhile, the upper range of the predicted $μ-e$ conversion rate in aluminum lies within the sensitivity reach of next-generation $μ-e$ conversion experiments.

</details>


### [194] [Light cone distributions of $P$-wave quarkonia](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.08393)
*Yang Li,Tianyang Hu,Xianghui Cao,Meijian Li*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper computes leading-twist light-cone distribution amplitudes (LCDAs) of P-wave quarkonia using basis light-front quantization (BLFQ), revealing relativistic W-shaped structures in axial-vector mesons—absent in non-relativistic models—due to S/D-wave mixing, with implications for exclusive QCD processes.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Renewed interest in light-cone distributions of P-wave quarkonia motivates a first-principles, relativistic investigation beyond the non-relativistic approximation.

Method: Basis Light-Front Quantization (BLFQ) formalism is employed to compute leading-twist distribution amplitudes of P-wave quarkonia non-perturbatively.

Result: Extracted LCDAs show macroscopic agreement with the non-relativistic limit but feature novel 'W'-shaped structures in axial-vector mesons, arising from relativistic S/D partial-wave mixing.

Conclusion: Relativistic effects—specifically S/D-wave coupling—are essential for accurate quarkonium LCDAs; non-relativistic approximations are insufficient for precision studies of hard exclusive processes at high-energy colliders.

Abstract: Motivated by renewed interest in the light-cone distributions of $P$-wave quarkonia, we investigate the leading-twist distribution amplitudes of these states within the basis light-front quantization (BLFQ) formalism. While our extracted distributions exhibit macroscopic shapes consistent with expectations from the non-relativistic limit, we find that relativistic effects introduce critical structural features. Most notably, we observe novel ``W"-shaped structures in the distribution amplitudes of the axial vector mesons, which arise from relativistically induced $S/D$ partial waves and cannot be explained by non-relativistic dynamics. These findings provide essential non-perturbative inputs for analyzing hard exclusive processes and highlight the importance of relativistic frameworks for understanding heavy quarkonium production and structure at modern high-energy colliders.

</details>


### [195] [Time evolution of scalar condensate decay](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.08420)
*Ayuki Kamada,Kodai Sakurai*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper investigates how the parametric-resonance decay rate of a coherently oscillating scalar field approaches the asymptotic Feynman-diagrammatic result over time, finding band-dependent convergence behavior in the narrow-resonance regime and linking the discrepancy in fermionic vs bosonic cases to quantum statistics (Bose enhancement vs Pauli blocking), using both numerical field evolution and analytically solvable Rabi models.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand the time-scale and mechanism by which the parametric-resonance description of scalar field decay converges to the standard Feynman-diagrammatic (perturbative) decay rate, especially in light of their known agreement at late times for bosonic daughters.

Method: Numerical integration of the phase-space density evolution of daughter particles across instability bands; comparative analysis of convergence rates in narrow-resonance regime; analytical study of Bosonic and Fermionic Rabi models as quantum-mechanical analogs.

Result: In the scalar field case, lowest-order instability band converges to the Feynman result within ~10–100 oscillation periods, independent of coupling; higher bands show coupling-dependent convergence. In Rabi models, bosonic version shows exact agreement at all times, while fermionic version disagrees asymptotically—attributed to Pauli blocking suppressing decay.

Conclusion: The total perturbative decay width is dominated by the lowest instability band and converges universally to the Feynman result, independent of coupling; the boson–fermion dichotomy in Rabi models confirms that quantum statistics (Bose enhancement vs Pauli exclusion) fundamentally governs whether parametric resonance reproduces perturbative decay asymptotically.

Abstract: A scalar field, which oscillates coherently over the space and decays through production of daughter particles, plays an important role in cosmology. It was recently shown that the parametric-resonance and Feynman-diagrammatic approaches give the same decay rate for a Bosonic daughter particle at a large time duration. We study how the parametric-resonance result approaches to this asymptotic value, by numerically following the time evolution of the phase density of the daughter particle. We see a difference among different instability bands in the narrow-resonance regime. The lowest-order instability band approaches to the Feynman-diagrammatic result in tens of scalar oscillation periods, while the high-order instability bands approach dependently also on a coupling between the scalar and daughter particles. This would infer that the total decay width, to which the lowest-order instability band contributes most dominantly in the perturbation theory, approaches to the Feynman-diagrammatic result also independently of the coupling. To confirm it, we consider a simpler and analytically solvable quantum mechanical model: Rabi models. In the Bosonic Rabi model, the parametric-resonance and Feynman-diagrammatic results agree with each other for all the time. On the other hand, in the Fermionic Rabi model, they disagree at a large time duration. This difference would be attributed to Bose enhancement vs Pauli blocking.

</details>


### [196] [How to choose a good rational basis for elliptic Feynman integrals?](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.08875)
*Ekta Chaubey,Vasily Sotnikov*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper investigates whether a universal, minimal relation exists between rational and elliptic bases when decoupling differential equation blocks requires complete elliptic integrals—extending prior work on elliptic leading singularities—and finds that the ε-dependence of off-diagonal blocks organizes into a universal structure preserved under decoupling transformations.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To determine whether a universal and minimal relation between rational and algebraic (elliptic) bases persists even when decoupling differential equation blocks necessitates non-algebraic transformations involving complete elliptic integrals—a generalization beyond previously established algebraic cases.

Method: Extends the elliptic leading singularity basis construction (introduced in arXiv:2504.20897) to analyze off-diagonal blocks of rational differential equations; studies how ε dependence transforms under decoupling maps that express solutions in terms of iterated integrals.

Result: The ε dependence of off-diagonal blocks admits a universal organizational structure that remains invariant under the elliptic decoupling transformation used to cast solutions in terms of iterated integrals.

Conclusion: A universal and minimal relation analogous to the algebraic case does exist for elliptic decoupling, manifested through an ε-structured invariance in off-diagonal differential equation blocks—supporting the elliptic leading singularity basis as a natural candidate for canonical elliptic representations of multi-loop amplitudes.

Abstract: Representing multi-loop scattering amplitudes as linear combinations of multivalued transcendental functions with process-dependent rational coefficients has long been understood to be advantageous. In general, these transcendental functions satisfy a system of differential equations with coupled homogeneous blocks. When these coupled blocks can be removed through algebraic basis transformations, the relation between the rational and algebraic bases is universal and minimal. Here, we ask whether an analogous universal and minimal relation exists when decoupling requires transformations involving complete elliptic integrals. We elaborate on the method proposed in ref. arXiv:2504.20897, in which we suggested that a basis constructed using an elliptic generalization of leading singularities may provide an answer to this question. We extend this analysis to the off-diagonal blocks of the rational differential equations satisfied by the bases obtained through this construction. We find that the $ε$ dependence of these blocks can be organized into a universal structure that is preserved under the decoupling transformation used to express the solutions in terms of iterated integrals.

</details>


### [197] [Relativistic spatial distributions of transverse angular momentum](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.08902)
*Cédric Lorcé,Asmita Mukherjee,Ravi Singh,Ho-Yeon Won*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper extends prior work on transverse angular momentum distributions by analyzing transverse boost distributions and examining how both angular momentum and boost spatial patterns depend on the choice of relativistic pivot (center of mass, energy, or spin); it derives 2D transverse distributions for spin-1/2 targets, studies their evolution under longitudinal Lorentz boosts, and connects instant-form and light-front formalisms.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: The definition of generalized angular momentum and boost densities is pivot-dependent; understanding how spatial distributions change with different relativistic centers (mass, energy, spin) and reference frames is essential for a consistent relativistic description of hadron structure.

Method: Analytical derivation of 2D transverse spatial distributions of total angular momentum and boost densities for spin-1/2 targets, using canonical, center-of-mass, center-of-energy, and center-of-spin pivots; systematic comparison under longitudinal Lorentz boosts; construction of corresponding light-front distributions and mapping to instant-form quantities.

Result: Explicit 2D transverse distributions of angular momentum and boost relative to multiple relativistic centers; demonstration of distinct spatial patterns depending on pivot choice; characterization of frame dependence and evolution under longitudinal boosts; establishment of a rigorous correspondence between instant-form and light-front transverse distributions.

Conclusion: The spatial structure of transverse angular momentum and boost is fundamentally tied to the choice of relativistic center; no single pivot is universally preferred—physical interpretation requires specifying the center; the light-front and instant-form pictures are consistently related through these transverse distributions.

Abstract: In our previous work [C. Lorcé et al., Phys. Lett. B 868 (2025) 139792], we investigated the 2D spatial distributions of transverse total angular momentum, including orbital angular momentum and intrinsic spin, relative to the canonical center (or center of spin). In the present work, we extend this analysis in two directions. First, we study the corresponding transverse boost distributions relative to the canonical center. Second, since the definition of generalized angular momentum density depends crucially on the choice of pivot, we analyze how the spatial distributions of transverse total angular momentum and boost are modified when they are defined relative to different relativistic centers, viz.~the relativistic centers of mass, energy, and spin. Considering spin-1/2 targets, we derive the corresponding 2D spatial distributions in the transverse plane, and further investigate how the spatial patterns evolve under longitudinal Lorentz boosts. Additionally, we provide the corresponding light-front distributions in the transverse plane, establishing a clear connection between the instant-form and light-front descriptions of transverse angular momentum and boost.

</details>


### [198] [Nonperturbative functional renormalization group for Higgs-singlet models with physics-informed neural networks](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.08470)
*Norimi Yokozaki*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper introduces a nonperturbative functional renormalization group (FRG) method using physics-informed neural networks (PINNs) to solve the Wetterich equation for a $Z_2$-symmetric real-singlet extension of the Standard Model, avoiding polynomial truncations; it applies the method to finite-temperature electroweak phase transition analysis, validates against perturbation theory and grid-based solvers, and identifies soft consistency constraints as essential—but still imperfect—for physical solution selection.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To overcome limitations of conventional polynomial truncations in FRG approaches—such as poor convergence, mesh dependence, and inability to capture non-analytic or large-field behavior—especially for finite-temperature effective potentials relevant to the electroweak phase transition in BSM scalar extensions.

Method: Develops an LPA' FRG framework using a hybrid ansatz: tree-level potential plus a physics-informed neural network (PINN) for the scale- and field-dependent effective potential; implements multi-domain adaptive discretization in scale and field with derivative-matching boundary conditions; incorporates gauge/Yukawa sectors via perturbative two-loop running couplings and includes anomalous dimensions; enforces a soft consistency constraint aligned with resummed perturbation theory in sign and magnitude.

Result: Achieves a continuous, mesh-free reconstruction of the finite-temperature effective potential (1D slices at benchmark T, 2D at T=100 GeV); extracts a two-step bounce action; demonstrates numerical stability and agreement with both resummed perturbation theory and a grid-based relaxation solver; shows that the soft consistency constraint is necessary—not optional—to avoid unphysical solutions, but reveals residual dependence on hand-tuned weights and analytic thermal targets.

Conclusion: PINN-based FRG is a viable, flexible, and scalable alternative to traditional truncations for nonperturbative potential reconstruction, yet the current reliance on empirically guided consistency constraints highlights an unresolved tension between data-driven flexibility and first-principles uniqueness in solving nonlinear functional PDEs like the Wetterich equation.

Abstract: We develop a nonperturbative functional renormalization group framework within the LPA' to solve the Wetterich flow equation for the $Z_2$-symmetric real singlet extension of the Standard Model at zero and finite temperature, without a polynomial expansion of the scale-dependent effective potential, using a physics-informed neural network (PINN) representation. In contrast to conventional truncations based on low-order field expansions, our hybrid tree-level--plus--neural-network ansatz yields a continuous, mesh-free description of the effective potential over the full relevant field and scale range. As a proof of concept, we apply the framework to the finite-temperature effective potential relevant to the electroweak phase transition in this model: one-dimensional field-space slices at two benchmark temperatures, and a two-dimensional reconstruction at $T=100$ GeV from which a two-step bounce action is extracted. The renormalization group flow is implemented in a multi-domain setup in scale and field space with derivative matching conditions that ensure smoothness and numerical stability. Gauge and Yukawa sectors are incorporated via independently computed perturbative two-loop running couplings, and anomalous dimensions are included in the flow at the LPA' level. We benchmark the network against resummed perturbation theory and against a grid-based relaxation solver of the same equation. Finally, we introduce a soft consistency constraint that keeps the solution close, in sign and order of magnitude, to perturbation theory across the field-space domain; we find it necessary, rather than merely helpful, for selecting a physically sensible solution of the flow equation, and find that the converged result retains a residual dependence on this guidance -- through hand-tuned weights and an analytic thermal target -- which we identify as the central open problem.

</details>


### [199] [Gravitational transverse momentum dependent distributions for the gluons](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.08499)
*Kauship Saha,Dipansu Mishra,Dipankar Chakrabarti,Asmita Mukherjee*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper studies the momentum-space structure of the gauge-invariant gluon energy-momentum tensor (EMT) for spin-0 and spin-1/2 hadrons using light-front formalism, introduces gluon gravitational TMDs, links them to standard twist-2/twist-3 gluon TMDs, and shows how EMT components encode gluon kinetic four-momentum and hadronic mechanical properties (pressure, shear).


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand the internal mechanical structure and gluon dynamics of hadrons through a gauge-invariant, momentum-space decomposition of the gluon energy-momentum tensor.

Method: Light-front quantization; parameterization of the nonlocal gluon EMT in terms of gravitational transverse-momentum-dependent distributions (gravitational TMDs); derivation of relations between gravitational TMDs and conventional twist-2/twist-3 gluon TMDs.

Result: Gravitational TMDs are established as fundamental objects encoding both partonic kinetic four-momentum and hadronic mechanical properties (e.g., pressure and shear force distributions) in the gluon sector.

Conclusion: The gluon EMT’s momentum-space structure—via gravitational TMDs—provides a unified framework connecting partonic kinematics and emergent hadronic mechanical properties, extending the interpretation of TMDs beyond factorization to hadron structure physics.

Abstract: We investigate the momentum-space structure of the gauge-invariant gluon energy-momentum tensor (EMT) for spin-$0$ and spin-$\tfrac{1}{2}$ hadrons in the light-front formalism. We parameterize the nonlocal gluon EMT in terms of so-called gluon gravitational transverse-momentum-dependent distributions (gravitational TMDs) and derive their connections to twist-2 and twist-3 gluon TMDs. Moreover, we demonstrate that particular components of the gluon EMT encode information about the average kinetic four-momentum of gluons and the internal mechanical properties of hadrons-such as pressure and shear distributions.

</details>


### [200] [Two-Loop Anomalous Dimension for Non-Global and Clustering Logarithms](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.09104)
*Thomas Becher,Jürg Haag,Nicolas Schalch*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper computes the two-loop anomalous dimension for resumming subleading non-global and clustering logarithms using effective field theory, identifies issues with the MS-bar scheme for parton shower implementation, proposes modified dipole subtraction schemes that avoid dimensional regularization complications and ensure frame independence, and validates scheme independence numerically in the leading-color limit using the Marzini framework for gap-between-jets cross sections.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To enable consistent resummation of subleading non-global and clustering logarithms—previously unaddressed at two loops—within an effective field theory framework, while resolving scheme-dependent complications (e.g., extra dimensions) that hinder parton shower compatibility and frame-invariant evolution.

Method: Derives the two-loop anomalous dimension using effective field theory techniques; analyzes renormalization scheme dependence, specifically critiquing MS-bar and introducing modified dipole subtraction schemes that eliminate the need for dimensional regularization in parton showers; implements the leading-color limit in the Marzini framework for numerical validation.

Result: A complete two-loop anomalous dimension for subleading non-global and clustering logarithms; identification of MS-bar’s incompatibility with standard parton showers due to dimensional regularization requirements; formulation of viable modified dipole subtraction schemes yielding frame-independent renormalization-group evolution; numerical confirmation of scheme independence for gap-between-jets observables.

Conclusion: Modified dipole subtraction schemes are necessary and sufficient for consistent, frame-independent resummation of subleading non-global and clustering logarithms at two loops; MS-bar is unsuitable for parton-shower-based implementations; the derived anomalous dimension enables improved precision predictions for jet substructure and event shapes.

Abstract: We derive the two-loop anomalous dimension relevant for the resummation of subleading non-global and, for the first time, also clustering logarithms within the framework of effective field theory. Special emphasis is put on the choice of the renormalization scheme. We show that the modified minimal subtraction scheme is problematic since it requires extra dimensions to set up the parton shower to perform the resummation. We discuss a set of modified dipole subtraction schemes which are free from this complication and can be used to obtain a frame-independent result for the renormalization-group evolution. We implement the leading-color limit of our result in the Marzili framework and numerically verify the scheme independence for gap-between-jets cross sections.

</details>


### [201] [A distance-independent constraint on the axion-electron coupling from RGB stars](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.08504)
*Thomas Levasseur,Oscar Straniero,Andrea Caputo*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper improves constraints on the axion-electron coupling (gae) using a distance-independent differential observable—the bolometric magnitude difference between the tip of the red giant branch (TRGB) and the RGB bump (RGBB)—in three metal-rich globular clusters. It derives a 95% C.L. upper limit of g13 ≡ gae/10⁻¹³ ≤ 1.49, with robustness enhanced by reduced sensitivity to systematics; additionally, stellar evolution arguments exclude g13 > 7.5, far surpassing current direct experimental limits.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To overcome systematic uncertainties (distance, extinction, bolometric correction zero-point) plaguing previous TRGB-based axion-electron coupling bounds, and to obtain more robust astrophysical constraints on axions as solutions to the strong CP problem and dark matter candidates.

Method: Uses the bolometric magnitude difference ΔM_bol(TRGB−RGBB) as a distance-independent observable; analyzes V- and I-band photometry from NGC 104, NGC 362, and NGC 5904; converts to bolometric magnitudes; validates stellar models against observed RGBB luminosity; performs maximum likelihood analysis with Monte Carlo uncertainty propagation.
