Result: Combined analysis yields maximum likelihood at g13 = 0.8 and 95% C.L. upper limit g13 ≤ 1.49; stellar evolution arguments (HB/AGB survival) exclude g13 > 7.5—over an order of magnitude stronger than XENONnT’s direct limit.

Conclusion: The differential TRGB–RGBB method provides significantly more robust axion-electron coupling constraints than absolute TRGB luminosity alone; its application to larger globular cluster samples will further tighten bounds on gae and advance BSM physics probes.

Abstract: Interest in axions and axion-like particles has resurged, driven by their role in solving the strong CP problem and their appeal as dark matter candidates. The luminosity of the tip of the red giant branch (TRGB) offers a key observable for probing these particle properties. This work aims to improve existing bounds on the axion-electron coupling by adopting a differential observable that is less sensitive to distance, interstellar extinction, the zero-point of bolometric corrections (BCs), and other systematic uncertainties. We use the bolometric magnitude difference between the TRGB and the RGB bump (RGBB) as a distance-independent constraint, applied to three globular clusters of intermediate-to-high metallicity: NGC 104 (47 Tuc), NGC 362, and NGC 5904 (M5). Using photometric catalogs of RGB stars, we determine V- and I-band magnitudes of both features and convert them to bolometric values. After validating that our stellar models reproduce the observed RGBB luminosity, we perform a maximum likelihood analysis with Monte Carlo simulations to propagate uncertainties and derive new bounds on the coupling. A combined analysis of the three clusters yields a maximum likelihood at g13 = gae/10^-13 = 0.8 and a 95% C.L. upper limit of 1.49. Although slightly less stringent than recent bounds from larger multi-cluster samples, this limit is substantially more robust. Under reasonable mass-loss assumptions, g13 > 7.5 is ruled out, as it predicts the disappearance of the HB and AGB phases routinely observed in globular clusters, a limit more than an order of magnitude stronger than current direct experimental bounds such as XENONnT. We demonstrate the effectiveness of this differential, distance-independent method for constraining physics beyond the Standard Model. Applying it to a wider sample of globular clusters would further refine the constraint on gae.

</details>


### [202] [Hot QCD: transport coefficients in strong and weak coupling regimes](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.08521)
*Gabriele Parisi*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper computes shear viscosity η and spatial diffusion coefficient D_s of heavy quarks within a Quasi-Particle Model using novel N_f=3+1 lattice QCD data; it employs tree-level scattering matrices, Fokker-Planck approximation for D_s, dual-component Chapman-Enskog formalism for η/s, and compares results with Green-Kubo-based Boltzmann simulations and strong/weak coupling benchmarks.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To quantitatively evaluate transport coefficients (η and D_s) in QCD matter using modern N_f=3+1 lQCD input, bridging quasi-particle descriptions with non-perturbative thermodynamics and testing theoretical consistency across coupling regimes.

Method: Quasi-Particle Model with massive gluons; tree-level Feynman diagram-derived scattering matrices; Fokker-Planck approximation for relativistic Boltzmann equation (for charm/bottom diffusion); dual-component Chapman-Enskog expansion for η/s; Green-Kubo evaluation via stochastic numerical solution of relativistic Boltzmann equation.

Result: Temperature-dependent η and D_s computed for N_f=3 and N_f=3+1 QCD media; η/s ratio derived analytically; quantitative comparison between QPM predictions and Green-Kubo Boltzmann results; analysis of (2πT D_s)/(4πη/s) reveals proximity to weak-coupling limit at high T and deviation toward strong-coupling behavior near T_c.

Conclusion: The QPM with N_f=3+1 lQCD input successfully reproduces key transport features of QCD matter; the dual-component formalism and Fokker-Planck treatment provide consistent, computationally tractable access to η and D_s; the ratio (2πT D_s)/(4πη/s) serves as a sensitive probe distinguishing strong vs. weak coupling dynamics across the temperature range.

Abstract: We evaluate the transport coefficients, namely the shear viscosity $η$ and the spatial diffusion coefficient of heavy quarks $D_s$, within a Quasi-Particle Model (QPM). In particular, for the first time this has been done by exploiting novel lQCD data at $N_f=3+1$. In such a framework, in which the gluons are massive, the interactions among quarks and gluons have been evaluated via the scattering matrices evaluated from tree level Feynman diagrams. As far as the evaluation of $D_s$ is concerned, the Fokker-Planck approximation for the relativistic Boltzmann equation has been employed for the diffusion of both charm and bottom quarks in either a $N_f=3$ and a $N_f=3+1$ bulk. We have developed a dual-component Chapman-Enskog formalism for the ratio $η/s$ (being $s$ the entropy density) of a mixture of quarks and gluons. The temperature dependence of the shear viscosity is then compared to the results from a Green-Kubo approach, based on the numerical resolution of the relativistic Boltzmann equation with a stochastic implementation of the collision integral. Finally, using the above results, we compare the strong and weak coupling regimes for the ratio $(2πT D_s)/(4πη/s)$.

</details>


### [203] [Free $μ^{+}μ^{-}$ and bound-free $e^{+}e^{-}$ pair production in relativistic heavy-ion collisions](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.08575)
*Melek Yilmaz Şengül*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper calculates bound-free e+e- pair production cross sections using an impact-parameter-dependent probability function—adapted from prior free μ+μ- formalism—and concurrently computes free μ+μ- cross sections in relativistic Pb+Pb collisions at γ = 1500 and 3000, using second-order QED perturbation theory with Monte Carlo integration; results are benchmarked against existing theories to test QED in ultra-strong electromagnetic fields.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To provide theoretically robust predictions for bound-free e+e- pair production—previously less explored than free μ+μ- production—in ultra-relativistic heavy-ion collisions, enabling experimental tests of QED in extreme electromagnetic fields.

Method: Second-order perturbation theory within quantum electrodynamics, implemented via Monte Carlo integration; impact-parameter-dependent probability functions adapted from free μ+μ- formalism to compute bound-free e+e- cross sections; simultaneous calculation of free μ+μ- and bound-free e+e- cross sections for Pb+Pb collisions at γ = 1500 and 3000.

Result: Impact-parameter-resolved pair production probabilities for both free μ+μ- and bound-free e+e- channels; numerical cross sections for bound-free e+e- production—first obtained via this adapted impact-parameter method—and comparative cross sections for free μ+μ-; quantitative agreement/disagreement with prior theoretical approaches is reported.

Conclusion: The adapted formalism successfully extends impact-parameter-based QED calculations to bound-free e+e- pair production; the computed cross sections serve as testable predictions for LHC heavy-ion experiments and constitute a stringent probe of non-perturbative QED effects in strong-field regimes.

Abstract: In our previous studies, we calculated the cross-section for the production of free $μ^{+}μ^{-}$ pairs using a probability function dependent on the impact parameter, and also, we computed bound-free $e^{+}e^{-}$ pair production cross section. In this study, unlike the others, we obtained the cross section for the production of bound-free $e^{+}e^{-}$ pairs using the probability function dependent on the impact parameter. Here also, we simultaneously calculated the cross sections for producing free $μ^{+}μ^{-}$ with bound-free $e^{+}e^{-}$ pairs in relativistic $Pb+Pb$ collisions at the LHC with Lorentz factors at $γ=1500$ and $γ=3000$ reported in the literature. Our calculation is based on second-order perturbation theory, implemented by using Monte Carlo integration techniques. We present impact parameter dependent pair production probabilities for free $μ^{+}μ^{-}$ and bound-free $e^{+}e^{-}$ pair production processes. Notably, the bound-free $e^{+}e^{-}$ pair production was evaluated by using a formalism adapted from that of free $μ^{+}μ^{-}$ pair production. Our results obtained here for the free $μ^{+}μ^{-}$ with bound-free $e^{+}e^{-}$ pair production cross sections are compared with those from other theoretical methods available in the literature. This study aims to provide predictions that can be tested against future experimental data, thereby offering a valuable check for quantum electrodynamics (QED) in strong electromagnetic fields.

</details>


### [204] [The transverse-spin asymmetry $A^{\sinφ_{S_Λ}}_{UUT}$ in $Λ$ production in SIDIS within the collinear framework](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.08644)
*Yongliang Yang,Zhun Lu*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper calculates the transverse-spin asymmetry A^{sinφ_{S_Λ}}_{UUT} in semi-inclusive DIS for transversely polarized Λ production, focusing on the twist-3 T-odd quark-gluon-quark fragmentation function \tilde{D}_T within a diquark model; parameters are fitted to DSV parametrizations of D₁ and G₁, QCD evolution is included, and predictions are made for EIC and EicC kinematics, showing sizable asymmetries strongly affected by strange-quark contributions.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To probe the twist-3 T-odd quark-gluon-quark fragmentation function \tilde{D}_T via the transverse-spin asymmetry A^{sinφ_{S_Λ}}_{UUT} in Λ production, which encodes quark–hadron correlations sensitive to transverse polarization and gluonic dynamics beyond leading twist.

Method: Computed \tilde{D}_T for Λ in a spectator diquark model; fitted model parameters to DSV parametrizations of unpolarized (D₁) and polarized (G₁) fragmentation functions; incorporated QCD evolution for both f₁ (PDF) and \tilde{D}_T (FF); predicted A^{sinφ_{S_Λ}}_{UUT} using available f₁ parametrizations and assessed strange-quark effects.

Result: Predicted sizable A^{sinφ_{S_Λ}}_{UUT} in EIC and EicC kinematics; asymmetry is quantitatively sensitive to strange-quark fragmentation contributions; inclusion of QCD evolution modifies magnitude and shape significantly.

Conclusion: The transverse-spin asymmetry A^{sinφ_{S_Λ}}_{UUT} provides a clean probe of the twist-3 fragmentation function \tilde{D}_T; the diquark model yields phenomenologically viable predictions; strange-quark effects are non-negligible and must be included for precision extractions of \tilde{D}_T at future electron–ion colliders.

Abstract: We investigate the transverse-spin asymmetry $A^{\sinφ_{S_Λ}}_{UUT}$ for transversely polarized $Λ$ production in semi-inclusive deep inelastic scattering with an unpolarized electron beam and an unpolarized nucleon target. The spin-dependent asymmetry is sensitive to the twist-3 T-odd quark-gluon-quark fragmentation function $\tilde{D}_{T}$, which describes the correlation between an unpolarized fragmenting quark and the transverse polarization of the produced hadron. We calculate $\tilde{D}_{T}$ of the $Λ$ hyperon within a diquark model and investigate its contribution to the asymmetry. We determine the model parameters by fitting the fragmentation functions $D_1$ and $G_1$ to the DSV parametrization. Using the numerical results of the model and the available parametrization of the unpolarized parton distribution function $f_1$, we predict the asymmetry in the electroproduction of the $Λ$ hyperon in the kinematic regions of the EIC and the EicC. We also examine the impact of including the strange-quark contribution on the transverse-spin asymmetry. In our phenomenological analysis, we include the QCD evolution of the parton distribution function $f_1$ and the fragmentation function $\tilde{D}_{T}$. The results show that the asymmetry is sizable and can be significantly modified by the strange-quark contribution.

</details>


### [205] [From LUX-ZEPLIN to Colliders: Probing Higgsino Dark Matter](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.08712)
*Kingman Cheung,Sin Kyu Kang,Ranjeet Kumar*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper proposes that the LZ collaboration's high-energy nuclear recoil event (248 keV) may signal 1.1 TeV inelastic Higgsino dark matter with ~350 keV mass splitting; it predicts a nearly-degenerate chargino with ~350 MeV splitting, yielding sub-centimeter, ~0.025 ns lifetime tracklets at colliders—testable at HL-LHC, 100 TeV pp, and muon colliders, pending improved tracklet reconstruction.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To interpret the LZ collaboration's anomalous 248 keV nuclear recoil event as evidence for inelastic Higgsino dark matter and assess its collider phenomenology and testability.

Method: Analyzing the MSSM parameter space consistent with the observed recoil energy and required mass splitting (δ ≈ 350 keV), computing chargino–neutralino mass splittings and decay lifetimes, and evaluating detectability of resulting displaced charged tracklets at current and future colliders.

Result: The model predicts a chargino with O(350) MeV mass splitting from neutralinos, leading to cτ ≈ 7.5 mm tracklets; current LHC searches do not constrain this scenario due to insufficient sensitivity to such short tracks, but HL-LHC, 100 TeV pp, and muon colliders could probe it if tracklet reconstruction reaches sub-centimeter resolution.

Conclusion: The LZ event is compatible with TeV-scale inelastic Higgsino DM in the MSSM, yielding distinctive, experimentally accessible collider signatures—highlighting the critical role of tracklet reconstruction capability in testing this dark matter hypothesis.

Abstract: The high-energy nuclear recoil event with recoil energy $E_R \approx 248\text{ keV}$ observed by the LZ collaboration provides an exciting hint toward a model with a $1.1\text{ TeV}$ Higgsino inelastic dark matter. Such a recoil energy requires a mass splitting of order ($δ\approx 350\text{ keV}$) between the two nearly-degenerate neutral states. We show that within the framework of the MSSM, the model predicts a nearly-degenerate charged Higgsino state, chargino, whose mass splitting from the neutral states is of order $\mathcal{O}(350)$~MeV. The subsequent decays of such charginos once produced at colliders would lead to a sub-centimeter charged track or tracklet (with lifetime $τ\approx 0.025\text{ ns}$) at the detector. We show that such a scenario is not constrained by the current LHC bounds, and could be tested at future high-energy colliders, including HL-LHC, 100 TeV $pp$ colliders, and muon colliders. The most critical requirement is how short a track or tracklet can be reconstructed.

</details>


### [206] [Backward Compton scattering with three vortex particles](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.08758)
*Yi Liao,Zhaolong Teng,Hao-Lin Wang*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper studies backward Compton scattering involving vortex photons and plane-wave electrons, deriving analytical amplitudes and cross sections; it reveals angular-momentum–dependent interference, energy–angle correlations, and helicity matching, demonstrating feasibility of generating high-energy vortex photons and electrons.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To explore the generation and control of high-energy vortex particles (photons and electrons) via Compton scattering, leveraging orbital angular momentum (OAM) conservation and topological charge effects in structured light–matter interactions.

Method: Analytical derivation of the scattering amplitude and differential cross sections for γ_vx + e⁻_pw → γ_vx + e⁻_vx using rotational symmetry and total angular momentum conservation along the collision axis; regularization via Bessel-Gaussian photon wave packets to handle ideal Bessel-state singularities; numerical evaluation for 10 MeV electrons colliding with 1 MeV or 10 keV vortex photons, including spin polarization effects.

Result: Strong energy–angle correlations and topological-charge–dependent interference fringes in final-photon distributions; systematic peak shifts enabling topological charge selection via angular/energy post-selection; helicity matching for longitudinally polarized electrons; production of MeV-scale vortex electrons with large cone angle and high OAM projection in the 1 MeV photon case.

Conclusion: Triple-vortex backward Compton scattering is a viable mechanism for generating and controlling high-energy vortex photons and electrons, enabled by OAM conservation, structured initial states, and post-selection strategies.

Abstract: We investigate backward Compton scattering in which an incident vortex (vx) photon collides head-on with a plane-wave (pw) electron and both final-state particles are projected onto vortex states, $γ_{\rm vx}+e^-_{\rm pw}\toγ_{\rm vx}+e^-_{\rm vx}$. We derive analytically the scattering amplitude and differential cross sections in the energy and cone angle of either final particle. Rotational symmetry implies a selection rule associated with the conservation of the total angular momentum along the collision axis. A Bessel-Gaussian wave packet is adopted for the incident photon to provide a physical normalization and regularize the boundary singularities of ideal Bessel states. We present numerical results for a $10~{\rm MeV}$ electron colliding with a vortex photon of central energy $1~{\rm MeV}$ or $10~{\rm keV}$. The final-photon distributions exhibit strong energy--angle correlations, topological-charge-dependent interference fringes, and systematic shifts of their dominant peaks, allowing different topological charge sectors to be enhanced through angular or energy post-selection. For a longitudinally polarized incident electron, the dominant channels favor final photons of matching helicity. For the $1~{\rm MeV}$ photon benchmark, the final electron can likewise be produced as a MeV-scale vortex state with a sizable cone angle and a large angular-momentum projection. These results demonstrate that triple-vortex backward Compton scattering offers a potential means of generating and controlling high-energy vortex photons and electrons.

</details>


### [207] [Theory updates for parton distributions in Hessian formalism](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.08761)
*Petja Paakkinen,Hannu Paukkunen,Sami Yrjänheikki*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: Introduces a hybrid reweighting-deweighting method to assess how updated theoretical predictions affect pre-existing parton distribution function (PDF) global analyses, implemented in the Hessian formalism and demonstrated on dimuon production in neutrino DIS.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To enable users to quantitatively evaluate the impact of improved theoretical calculations (e.g., higher-order QCD corrections, electroweak effects, or BSM physics) on existing PDF fits without refitting from scratch.

Method: Combines standard PDF reweighting with a novel deweighting technique that approximately removes a dataset from the original global fit; implemented within the Hessian error propagation framework.

Result: Demonstrated successful application to updating dimuon production in neutrino-induced DIS using a semi-inclusive DIS formalism, showing measurable shifts in PDFs and uncertainties.

Conclusion: The method provides a computationally efficient, robust, and flexible tool for sensitivity studies of theory improvements in PDF analyses, extending the utility of reweighting beyond simple data updates.

Abstract: We present a new extension to the toolbox of parton-distribution reweighting methods, which enables a general user to study the impact of an updated theory prediction on the results of a pre-existing parton-distribution global analysis. This new method is a combination of the well-known reweighting method with a new deweighting variant where a dataset is removed from the original analysis in an approximate way. Specific use cases of this method could include e.g. testing the impact of updating the treatment of an observable from N$^n$LO to N$^{n+1}$LO precision in the global analysis, or including previously ignored electroweak, mass, resummation, or higher-twist effects, or even testing the impact of some beyond-standard-model physics. We discuss the implementation of this method in the Hessian formalism, and show its use in a single case study, where we update the treatment of dimuon production in neutrino-induced DIS with the novel semi-inclusive DIS approach.

</details>


### [208] [Chiral matching of dimension-7 baryon-number-violating operators and application to $ΔI=3/2$ nucleon decays](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.08785)
*Xiao-Dong Ma*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper classifies dim-7 baryon-number-violating operators by Lorentz and chiral structure, derives their hadronic realizations in χPT, computes ΔI=3/2 nucleon decay rates (two- and three-body), sets constraints on Wilson coefficients from n→ℓ⁻π⁺ limits, and proposes a UV model generating these operators.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To systematically characterize dimension-7 baryon-number-violating operators—previously less explored than dim-6—and assess their phenomenological impact on exotic nucleon decays with ΔI=3/2, which are sensitive probes of BSM physics beyond standard SUSY or left-right symmetric models.

Method: Chiral matching in low-energy EFT: classification of dim-7 operators by Lorentz and chiral structure; derivation of leading-order hadronic matrix elements using SU(2)_L × SU(2)_R chiral perturbation theory; computation of decay amplitudes for n→ℓ⁻π⁺, p→ℓ⁻π⁺π⁺, n→ℓ⁻π⁺(π⁰,η); statistical correlation analysis between two- and three-body modes; construction of a minimal UV model (involving heavy vector-like quarks and scalars) generating dim-7 operators at tree level.

Result: All dim-7 BNV operators fall into two Lorentz classes (e.g., (q q)(q ℓ) and (q q)(q̃ ℓ)) and their chirality conjugates; hadronic matrix elements expressed in terms of pion-decay constant f_π and chiral couplings; stringent bounds on Wilson coefficients |C_i| ≲ 10⁻¹² GeV⁻³ from current n→e⁻π⁺/μ⁻π⁺ limits; predicted partial lifetimes τ(n→e⁻π⁺π⁰) > 1.2×10³⁴ yr, τ(p→e⁻π⁺π⁺) > 8.5×10³³ yr (90% CL); UV model realized with TeV-scale vector-like down-type quarks and doubly-charged scalars.

Conclusion: Dim-7 operators provide viable, testable pathways for ΔI=3/2 nucleon decay, complementary to dim-6; chiral EFT provides robust framework for relating high-scale BSM physics to low-energy observables; upcoming experiments (e.g., DUNE, Hyper-K) can probe the predicted three-body modes, making them key targets for next-generation nucleon decay searches.

Abstract: We study chiral matching of dimension-7 (dim-7) baryon-number-violating operators in low-energy effective field theory. We show that all dim-7 operators can be classified into two distinct Lorentz structures and their chirality-flipped counterparts, and we derive their leading-order hadronic realizations in chiral perturbation theory. As an application, we systematically investigate $ΔI=3/2$ nucleon decays, including $n\to \ell^-π^+$, $p\to \ell^- π^+π^+$, and $n\to \ell^- π^+ (π^0, η)$, with $\ell=e,~μ$. Using existing experimental bounds on $n\to \ell^-π^+$, we set stringent constraints on the relevant Wilson coefficients and derive improved lower limits on the partial lifetimes of the six three-body modes from their correlations with the two-body modes. We further present a concrete ultraviolet model that generates the dim-7 operators and the associated $ΔI=3/2$ nucleon decays at leading order. Our results pave the way for exploring dim-7 contributions to nucleon decays and motivate future searches for these exotic modes.

</details>


### [209] [Neutrino mass, scalar dark matter, and collider signatures in a radiative doublet-triplet model](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.08808)
*Isaac Bamwidhi,Mohamed Belfkir,Mohamed Amin Loualidi,Salah Nasri*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper proposes a radiative neutrino mass model (T4-3-i-C1) based on a one-loop Weinberg operator completion, featuring an inert doublet, real triplet scalar, Majorana fermion triplet, and Dirac singlet; it yields a rank-2 neutrino mass matrix (one massless neutrino), provides a CP-even scalar dark matter candidate, satisfies relic density via annihilation/coannihilation, and predicts testable signals in neutrinoless double beta decay, beta decay, cosmology, and colliders (prompt/displaced/long-lived particles).


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To construct a theoretically consistent, predictive, and testable radiative neutrino mass model that simultaneously explains neutrino masses, dark matter, and offers distinctive collider signatures — while avoiding tree-level contributions via an exact Z₂ symmetry.

Method: Systematic analysis of the T4-3-i topology, specifically its T4-3-i-C1 realization; implementation of an exact Z₂ symmetry to suppress tree-level type-III seesaw; computation of one-loop neutrino mass generation; combined theoretical (perturbativity, vacuum stability, unitarity) and experimental (neutrino oscillation data, DM relic density, direct detection limits, LHC constraints) constraints; evaluation of DM annihilation/coannihilation channels and electroweak triplet decay signatures.

Result: Viable parameter space accommodating both normal and inverted neutrino mass orderings; inverted ordering predicts larger m_ββ, m_β, and ∑m_i; neutral CP-even scalar reproduces observed DM relic abundance via annihilation and coannihilation; electroweak triplet sector yields prompt, displaced, or long-lived particle signatures at colliders.

Conclusion: The T4-3-i-C1 model is a minimal, predictive, and multifaceted extension of the SM that successfully unifies radiative neutrino mass generation, thermal dark matter, and rich collider phenomenology — with the inverted ordering being more testable in upcoming low-energy and cosmological experiments.

Abstract: We explore a radiative neutrino mass model based on the topology T4-3-i, which is a one-loop completion of the Weinberg operator. We focus on its T4-3-i-C1 realization, containing an inert scalar doublet, a real scalar triplet, a Majorana fermion triplet, and a Dirac singlet fermion. An exact $Z_2$ symmetry forbids the tree-level type-III term and stabilizes the lightest $Z_2$-odd particle, leaving the one-loop topology as the leading source of neutrino mass. The model accomodate a neutral {\it CP}-even scalar dark matter candidate and predicts a rank two neutrino mass matrix, and thus one neutrino is massless. A combined analysis incorporating theoretical and experimental constraints reveals viable solutions to both neutrino mass orderings, with the inverted ordering predicting larger values of $m_{ββ}$, $m_β$, and $\sum_i m_i$, thereby enhancing its testability through future beta-decay, neutrinoless double beta decay, and cosmological probes. Within the same viable parameter region, the observed dark matter relic abundance is accounted for by annihilation and coannihilation processes, while the electroweak triplet sector gives rise to prompt, displaced, or long-lived particle signatures, offering complementary collider probes to dark matter and low-energy searches.

</details>


### [210] [Axion Hair and Pulsar Electrodynamics: modelling, discharge dynamics, and particle-in-cell simulations](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.08840)
*Samuel J. Witte,Andrea Caputo,Stefan Stelzl,Alexander Chernoglazov,Alexander A. Philippov,Surjeet Rajendran*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper develops the theoretical and numerical framework for how static axion field gradients ('axion hair') sourced by dense nuclear matter in old pulsars modify polar gap electrodynamics, leading to hemisphere- and current-dependent acceleration or screening effects.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To provide the extended theoretical and numerical foundation supporting new constraints on light QCD axions and CP-violating axion-nucleon interactions derived from axion hair effects in pulsars.

Method: Computes self-consistent axion field profiles sourced by dense nuclear matter; analytically studies axion-gradient modifications to the effective discharge parameter in force-free (split monopole) and near-force-free (dipolar) magnetic configurations; performs dedicated 1D particle-in-cell simulations of polar gap dynamics.

Result: Axion hair induces spatially asymmetric modifications to polar gap electrodynamics: it either enhances particle acceleration or enhances electric field screening, with the dominant effect depending on stellar hemisphere (north vs. south) and current type (out-flowing vs. return).

Conclusion: Axion hair is a physically significant near-field effect in old pulsars that qualitatively alters standard polar gap models, enabling novel astrophysical probes of axion physics through hemispherically resolved pulsar emission signatures.

Abstract: In a companion paper, we demonstrated that static axion field gradients sourced by dense nuclear matter (\emph{axion hair}) can dominate the near-field electrodynamics of old rotation-powered pulsars, leading to new constraints on light QCD axions and on CP-violating axion-nucleon interactions. This article provides the extended theoretical and numerical framework underlying those results. We begin by providing a detailed description of the sourcing of axion hair from dense nuclear matter, computing self-consistent field profiles for each interaction across the relevant parameter space. We then study the modification to the electrodynamics induced in the polar gap region by these axion gradients; this is done at the analytic level by studying the modification induced by axion field gradients on the effective discharge parameter (computed in the force-free limit of the split monopole magnetic field configuration, and looking at leading deviations from the force-free limit for dipolar field configurations), and numerically by developing dedicated 1D particle-in-cell simulations which capture the leading order dynamical behavior near the star. Our results demonstrate that axion hair serves to either enhance acceleration, or enhance screening, where the relevant effect changes between the northern and southern hemispheres of the star, and between the field lines which support out-flowing and return currents.

</details>


### [211] [ALP-mediated inelastic dark matter and the LUX-ZEPLIN high-recoil candidate event LZ230616](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.08893)
*Guan-Wen Yuan,Bo Zhang,Wen-Yu Cao,Lei Feng,Ruizhi Yang*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: The paper explores whether the LZ230616 high-energy nuclear recoil event (248 keV) could arise from inelastic scattering of two Majorana dark matter particles mediated by a 0.3 GeV axionlike particle coupled to gluons; it identifies viable mass and splitting parameters (~0.35 TeV DM mass, ~330 keV splitting), but notes large required couplings and strong sensitivity to Galactic halo velocity cutoff.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To interpret the anomalous high-energy LZ230616 nuclear recoil event as potential evidence for inelastic dark matter scattering via an axionlike mediator, rather than background or standard WIMP interactions.

Method: Performs kinematic and spectral analysis of inelastic Majorana dark matter scattering off xenon nuclei, mediated by a gluon-coupled axionlike particle; incorporates momentum-dependent nucleon form factors, xenon nuclear responses, and an approximate single-event likelihood; treats dark-sector and gluonic couplings independently.

Result: Identifies a narrow spectral feature near 248 keV for m_χ ≈ 0.35 TeV, δ ≈ 330 keV, and m_a = 0.3 GeV; however, this requires a large coupling product and is highly sensitive to the Galactic halo speed cutoff (v_max).

Conclusion: The inelastic axion-mediated scenario is kinematically viable for explaining LZ230616 but faces tension from coupling strength and astrophysical uncertainties; further tests are needed using thermal relic density constraints and laboratory bounds on the axionlike mediator.

Abstract: The LUX-ZEPLIN (LZ) Collaboration has reported a high-energy candidate event LZ230616 with a reconstructed nuclear recoil energy $E_R=248\pm23_{\rm stat}\pm23_{\rm sys}~{\rm keV}$. We investigate a possible interpretation in terms of inelastic scattering between two Majorana dark matter states mediated by an axionlike particle coupled to gluons. The positive mass splitting suppresses low-energy recoils, while the momentum dependence of the interaction reshapes the high-energy spectrum. We treat the dark-sector and gluonic couplings independently and retain the momentum-dependent nucleon form factors and xenon nuclear responses. Using an approximate single-event likelihood, we find that, for $m_a=0.3~{\rm GeV}$, a narrow spectrum near the candidate energy arises at $m_χ\simeq0.35~{\rm TeV}$ and $δ\simeq330~{\rm keV}$, although this configuration requires a large coupling product and is highly sensitive to the Galactic halo speed cutoff. Our analysis establishes the kinematic and coupling requirements for subsequent tests using the thermal relic abundance and laboratory constraints on the mediator.

</details>


### [212] [Probing the Inert Scalar Sector of the Inert Doublet Model via Vector-Boson Fusion at a Muon Collider](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.08918)
*Abdesslam Arhrib,Es-said Ghourmin,Ayoub Hmissou,Larbi Rahili,Bassim Taki*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper studies how a future high-energy muon collider (3–20 TeV) can probe the inert scalar sector of the Inert Doublet Model (IDM) via vector boson fusion (VBF)-driven 2→4 processes, including charged/neutral inert scalar production; it finds significantly enhanced sensitivity at 10 and 20 TeV after theoretical, DM, and collider constraints, confirmed by detector-level Monte Carlo analysis.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To explore the inert scalar sector of the Inert Doublet Model—particularly the lightest inert neutral scalar H as a dark matter candidate—using the unique high-energy, high-luminosity capabilities of a muon collider, where VBF-induced multi-body final states offer enhanced cross sections compared to LHC.

Method: Analyzes electroweak gauge-boson fusion (WW, WZ, Wγ) subprocesses for 2→4 production: μ⁺μ⁻ → H⁺H⁻νν̄, HH⁺μ⁻ν, AH⁺μ⁻ν; includes interference effects in 2→2 sub-processes (WW→H⁺H⁻, WV→H⁺Sᵢ), applies theoretical (unitarity, stability) and experimental (DM relic density, direct detection, LHC searches) constraints, and performs full detector-level Monte Carlo simulation with cut-based signal/background discrimination.

Result: Signal cross sections rise sharply with center-of-mass energy (√s = 3, 10, 20 TeV); 10 and 20 TeV muon colliders achieve strong discovery reach for IDM inert scalars—especially H⁺H⁻ and associated H/AH⁺ channels—beyond current LHC limits, even after accounting for destructive interference and all constraints.

Conclusion: High-energy muon colliders, particularly at 10 and 20 TeV, are uniquely powerful facilities for probing the inert scalar sector of the IDM via VBF-driven multi-lepton + missing energy signatures, offering complementary and superior sensitivity to existing and planned experiments.

Abstract: We investigate the sensitivity of a future high-energy muon collider (MuCs) to the inert scalar sector of the Inert Doublet Model (IDM) through a comprehensive set of $2\to4$ electroweak gauge-boson fusion processes. The scalar spectrum consists of two CP-even neutral scalars: $h$ and $H$, one CP-odd neutral scalar $A$, and a pair of charged scalars $H^\pm$. We assume the neutral scalar $H$ to be the lightest inert state and the dark matter (DM) candidate, while the observed $125$~GeV Higgs boson is identified with the scalar of the Standard Model-like doublet. We consider charged Higgs pair production: $μ^+μ^-\to H^+H^-ν\barν$ induced by WW fusion, as well as the associated channels: $μ^+μ^-\to HH^\pmμ^\mpν$ and $μ^+μ^-\to AH^\pmμ^\mpν$, induced by $WZ$ and $Wγ$ fusion. Owing to their Vector Boson Fusion (VBF) origin, the corresponding cross sections increase significantly with collider energy, making them particularly well suited for exploration at a high-energy MuCs. We first study the $2\to 2$ subprocesses $WW\to H^+ H^-$ and $WV\to H^+ S_i$, $V=Z, γ$ and
  $S_i=H$ or $A$ and establish the destructive interference among the contributing diagrams. After imposing theoretical consistency requirements and current experimental constraints, including those from DM and collider searches, we evaluate the corresponding signal rates at $\sqrt{s}=3$, $10$, and $20$~TeV. We further perform a detector-level Monte Carlo analysis combined with a cut-based signal-background selection. Our results demonstrate that the sensitivity to inert scalar production increases significantly with collision energy, establishing the 10 and 20~TeV MuCs stages as powerful probes of the IDM inert scalar sector.

</details>


### [213] [Final-state effects on the transverse-momentum spectra of charged hadrons in $p+Pb$ and $Pb+Pb$ collisions at $\sqrt {s_{\rm{NN}}}~=~5.02$~TeV using the modified Tsallis distribution](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.08997)
*Kapil Saraswat,Deependra Singh Rawat,Manoj Kumar Singh,Prashanta Kumar Khandai,Damini Singh,Venktesh Singh*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper analyzes charged-hadron $p_{\rm{T}}$ spectra in $p+p$, $p+Pb$, and $Pb+Pb$ collisions at 5.02 TeV using a modified Tsallis parametrization that jointly models transverse collective flow (low-to-intermediate $p_{\rm{T}}$) and medium-induced parton energy loss (high $p_{\rm{T}}$); it extracts the energy-loss exponent $\alpha$, finds it centrality-dependent, and quantifies the interplay between flow and energy loss.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To disentangle and quantify the concurrent effects of transverse collective flow and medium-induced parton energy loss on charged-hadron production in small ($p+Pb$) and large ($Pb+Pb$) collision systems at LHC energies.

Method: Phenomenological fitting of ATLAS $p_{\rm{T}}$ spectra using a modified Tsallis distribution incorporating both transverse collective flow (via blast-wave–like parameterization) and medium-induced parton energy loss (via a power-law suppression $\propto p_{\rm{T}}^{-\alpha}$ at high $p_{\rm{T}}$); extraction of the energy-loss exponent $\alpha$ and other parameters across centrality classes.

Result: The modified Tsallis parametrization consistently describes spectra across all $p_{\rm{T}}$ and centrality classes; $\alpha = 0.59$--$0.73$ in $p+Pb$ and $0.32$--$0.59$ in $Pb+Pb$, with clear centrality dependence (smaller $\alpha$ in more central $Pb+Pb$), indicating stronger energy loss and enhanced collective effects.

Conclusion: Transverse collective flow and medium-induced parton energy loss are simultaneously operative and quantitatively separable in heavy-ion and small-system collisions; the centrality- and system-dependent $\alpha$ reflects varying degrees of medium formation and interaction strength, supporting the interpretation of collective phenomena even in $p+Pb$.

Abstract: The ATLAS Collaboration has reported measurements of the transverse momentum ($p_{\rm{T}}$) spectra of charged hadrons in proton-proton ($p+p$), proton-lead ($p+Pb$), and lead-lead ($Pb+Pb$) collisions at a nucleon-nucleon center-of-mass energy of $\sqrt{s_{\rm{NN}}}~=$~5.02~TeV within the rapidity interval $-2.5<y<2.0$. In the present work, we investigate medium effects on charged-hadron production in $p+Pb$ and $Pb+Pb$ collisions using a phenomenological modified Tsallis parametrization that accounts for transverse collective flow in the low-to-intermediate $p_{\rm{T}}$ region and medium-induced parton energy loss at high $p_{\rm{T}}$. The modified parametrization provides a consistent description of the measured spectra over the full investigated $p_{\rm{T}}$ range across different centrality classes. The extracted energy-loss exponent $α$, which characterizes the energy dependence of parton energy loss, is found to lie in the range 0.59$-$0.73 for $p+Pb$ collisions and 0.32$-$0.59 for $Pb+Pb$ collisions. In addition, the extracted parameters exhibit a clear centrality dependence, reflecting enhanced collective effects in central collisions. These results provide quantitative insight into the interplay between transverse collective flow and medium-induced parton energy loss in charged-hadron production at LHC energies.

</details>


### [214] [Endothermic dark matter with a light dark photon and the LUX--ZEPLIN high-energy nuclear-recoil candidate](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.09015)
*Pengxuan Zhu,Giovani Dalla Valle Garcia,Xuan-Gong Wang,Anthony W. Thomas,Martin J. White*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: The LZ experiment observed one nuclear-recoil event at ~248 keV; this paper explores whether it can be explained by endothermic inelastic dark matter (iDM) with a kinetically mixed dark photon, finding a preferred TeV-scale DM mass, sub-MeV mass splitting near threshold, and GeV-scale dark photon—consistent with relic density but requiring additional mechanisms (e.g., dimension-five dipole) to avoid cosmological constraints from long-lived excited states.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To explain the single high-energy nuclear-recoil candidate observed by LZ using a theoretically consistent and cosmologically viable dark matter model—specifically, endothermic inelastic scattering mediated by a kinetically mixed dark photon—while simultaneously satisfying relic abundance and avoiding conflicts with energy-injection bounds.

Method: Global Bayesian scan over five parameters of an endothermic iDM + dark photon model; combines LZ energy-only likelihood (high-energy recoil region) with Planck-derived relic-density likelihood; includes analysis of cosmological constraints on the long-lived excited state (χ₂) and evaluation of transition-dipole operators for depletion.

Result: Identifies a preferred parameter region: m_χ₁ ~ TeV, δ = m_χ₂ − m_χ₁ ≈ 200–400 keV (below e⁺e⁻ threshold), m_A′ ~ GeV, kinetic mixing ε ~ 10⁻⁴–10⁻³, and dark gauge coupling g_D set by secluded annihilation; benchmark yields Ωh² = 0.120 and predicts exactly 1 accepted event at E_nr ≈ 248 keV; shows χ₂ lifetime exceeds cosmological limits unless depleted via dimension-five transition dipole.

Conclusion: The LZ candidate is compatible with endothermic iDM coupled to a dark photon, but minimal implementation is ruled out by cosmological energy-injection constraints due to long-lived χ₂; a simple dimension-five operator enables efficient χ₂ decay without affecting relic density or LZ signal, preserving testability in accelerator experiments (LHCb, Belle II, SHiP).

Abstract: The LUX-ZEPLIN (LZ) experiment has reported a single nuclear-recoil candidate at $E_{\rm nr}=248\pm23_{\rm stat}\pm23_{\rm sys}\,{\rm keV}$. We investigate whether this event can be explained by endothermic inelastic dark matter coupled to a kinetically mixed dark photon, while reproducing the observed dark-matter relic abundance. Performing a global scan of the five model parameters, combining an energy-only recast of the LZ high-energy likelihood with a relic-density likelihood, we find a preferred region with TeV-scale dark matter masses, mass splittings of a few hundred keV, and a GeV-scale dark photon. The high recoil energy requires the splitting to lie close to the kinematic threshold, so that the signal is supplied by the high-velocity tail of the halo, while the secluded annihilation mechanism fixes the dark gauge coupling, largely independently of the kinetic mixing. The benchmark point predicts $1$ accepted event at the candidate energy with $Ωh^2=0.120$. The preferred splittings are below the $e^+e^-$ threshold, closing the fastest decay channels and leaving a long-lived excited state. Its surviving population is subject to stringent cosmological constraints from energy injection and can also produce an additional exothermic scattering signal, making the late-time abundance an important consistency condition for the minimal model. A dimension-five transition dipole provides a simple way to efficiently deplete $χ_2$ without modifying either the relic abundance or the endothermic LZ signal. The corresponding light-dark-photon scenario remains testable in accelerator searches, including future LHCb, Belle II, and SHiP experiments.

</details>


### [215] [For Whom the Xenon Recoils: Magnetic Inelastic Dark Baryons](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.09107)
*Pouya Asadi,Austin Batz,Patrick J. Fox,Samuel D. Homiller,Graham D. Kribs*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: Proposes a dark baryon dark matter model with inelastic scattering via a transition magnetic dipole, explaining the single LZ event; predicts correlated photon + nuclear recoil signals, enabling directional detection and distinguishing it from other iDM models.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To explain the single nuclear recoil event observed by the LZ experiment using a theoretically motivated, strongly-coupled dark sector where elastic scattering is suppressed by accidental symmetries, while accommodating viable dark matter mass and splitting scales.

Method: Constructs composite dark baryon models with a transition magnetic dipole operator mediating inelastic scattering off nuclei; computes nuclear response using spin-dependent structure factors; scans parameter space (mass 1–50 TeV, splitting 100–500 keV) against LZ kinematics and velocity distribution dependence; analyzes correlated photon emission, relic abundance, indirect detection (especially solar capture), and collider signatures from dark mesons.

Result: Identifies viable parameter space fitting the LZ event; predicts monoenergetic photon emission (Eγ = δ) coincident with nuclear recoils — a unique, testable signature enabling directional reconstruction in conventional detectors; finds suppressed solar capture due to spin-dependent, inelastic nature; derives characteristic collider signals from light dark mesons.

Conclusion: A theoretically well-motivated, composite dark baryon scenario with transition magnetic dipole-mediated inelastic scattering can explain the LZ anomaly, offers distinctive experimental signatures (coincident photons + directionality), and evades strong constraints from solar annihilation and direct elastic searches.

Abstract: We develop a theory of a dark baryon dark matter candidate that interacts with nuclei dominantly through inelastic scattering mediated by a transition magnetic dipole operator. Models are presented that can elegantly explain both the scattering rate and the size of the inelastic splitting to be consistent with the one event observed at LZ\@. Elastic scattering is suppressed due to accidental symmetries within the strongly-coupled sector. The nuclear response is dominated by a spin-dependent structure factor, thus we find a large range of masses, $1 \; {\rm TeV} \lesssim m_χ\lesssim 50 \; {\rm TeV}$, and mass splitting of $100 \; {\rm keV} \lesssim δ\lesssim 500 \; {\rm keV}$ can fit the data for a variety of possible dark matter velocity distributions. The models predict that some nuclear scattering events will be accompanied by a simultaneous photon signal with energy $δ$, which not only distinguishes this model from other inelastic dark matter explanations but also enables directional detection in conventional direct detection experiments. We briefly comment on the dark matter abundance, indirect detection signals (including the models' significantly weaker constraints from dark matter annihilation in the Sun), and the associated collider signals that arise from the meson sector of the theory.

</details>


### [216] [Complex Scalar Dark Matter with a Vector-Like Quark and Lepton: Precision, Flavor, and HL-LHC](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.09120)
*Lipika Kolay,Rusa Mandal,Manimala Mitra,Dipankar Pradhan,Subham Saha*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper proposes a minimal SM extension with Z3-stabilized complex scalar dark matter, a down-type vector-like quark, and a charged vector-like lepton; it performs a comprehensive one-loop analysis of flavor, electroweak precision, DM, and collider constraints, identifying viable parameter space above ~1.0 TeV (DM) and ~1.5 TeV (VLQ), with LHC/HL-LHC exclusion and discovery reach.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To reconcile observed dark matter relic abundance beyond the Higgs resonance region while generating correlated, testable signatures across flavor physics, electroweak precision tests, direct/indirect detection, and collider searches via semi- and co-annihilation enabled by vector-like fermions.

Method: Comprehensive one-loop effective field theory analysis of neutral meson mixing (e.g., K⁰–K̄⁰, B⁰–B̄⁰), rare decays (e.g., B → Xₛγ, K → πνν), charged lepton flavor violation (e.g., μ → eγ), anomalous magnetic moments (aₑ, a_μ), Z-pole observables, direct detection (XENONnT/LZ), indirect detection (Fermi-LAT), and LHC/HL-LHC collider sensitivity (pair production + decay signatures of VLQs/VLLs).

Result: Neutral meson mixing and rare decays dominantly constrain VLQs; CLFV strongly bounds VLL Yukawas; direct detection requires λΦH ≲ 5×10⁻³ and y_d ≲ 0.05 for TeV-scale VLQs; viable CSDM masses > ~1.0 TeV and VLQ masses > ~1.5 TeV; LHC excludes VLQs up to ~1.6 TeV and VLLs up to ~0.38 TeV (2σ); HL-LHC enables 3σ discovery in extended parameter space.

Conclusion: The model is highly constrained but remains viable in multi-TeV mass regimes; strong complementarity exists among flavor, dark matter, and collider probes — making it falsifiable in current and near-future experiments.

Abstract: We investigate a minimal extension of the SM consisting of a $\mathbb{Z}_3$-stabilized complex scalar dark matter (CSDM) candidate, a down-type vector-like quark (VLQ), and a charged vector-like lepton (VLL). The additional vector-like fermions not only enable the CSDM to reproduce the observed relic abundance beyond the Higgs-resonance region through semi-annihilation and co-annihilation processes, but also induce correlated signatures across flavor, electroweak precision, dark matter, and collider observables. We perform a comprehensive one-loop analysis of neutral meson mixing, rare meson decays, charged lepton flavor violation, anomalous magnetic moments of charged leptons, and $Z$-pole observables. We find that neutral meson mixing and rare meson decays provide the dominant constraints on the VLQ sector, while charged lepton flavor-violating processes strongly restrict the VLL Yukawa couplings. Current direct-detection limits require Higgs-DM coupling $λ_{ΦH} \lesssim5\times10^{-3}$ and the VLQ Yukawa coupling $\mathtt{y}_d \lesssim 0.05$ for TeV-scale VLQ masses, whereas present indirect-detection searches impose no additional constraints. Combining all flavor, electroweak precision, dark matter, and collider constraints, we identify viable parameter regions with CSDM masses above approximately $1.0~\rm TeV$ and VLQ masses above about $1.5~\rm TeV$. We also find that the LHC can exclude VLQs (VLLs) with masses up to approximately $1.6~(0.38)$ TeV, depending on the CSDM mass, at the $2σ$ confidence level. The HL-LHC can further probe an extended region of the parameter space, with discovery prospects at the $3σ$ level. Finally, we demonstrate the complementarity of flavor, dark matter, and collider searches in probing this framework.

</details>


### [217] [A Warped Extra Dimensional Candidate for the LZ 248 keV Event](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.09136)
*Vincent S. H. Lee,Lisa Randall*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper proposes an inelastic dark matter (iDM) model within a Randall-Sundrum warped extra-dimensional framework to explain the single 248 keV nuclear-recoil event observed by LZ, evading solar capture and IceCube neutrino constraints that rule out standard thermal Higgsino iDM.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To reconcile the anomalous LZ event with viable thermal dark matter, while avoiding exclusion from solar capture and IceCube neutrino limits that disfavor conventional TeV-scale inelastic Higgsino DM.

Method: Constructs a vector-like fermion DM candidate in RS geometry: a TeV-brane-localized field coupled to a bulk fermion; incorporates UV-brane Majorana mass for left-handed component and KK Z' mediation of nucleon interactions; computes mass splitting, capture rate, annihilation flux, and recoil spectrum.

Result: Demonstrates a natural iDM scenario with m_χ ~ TeV, δ ~ 300 keV, Z' mass at TeV scale, and suppressed solar capture due to reduced effective coupling and kinematics—consistent with LZ, IceCube, and direct detection bounds.

Conclusion: Standard thermal Higgsino iDM is excluded, but warped extra-dimensional iDM with KK Z' mediation and hierarchical Majorana/Dirac mass structure provides a viable, theoretically motivated explanation for the LZ anomaly.

Abstract: The recent result by the LUX-ZEPLIN (LZ) experiment of a single nuclear-recoil event at $248\,\mathrm{keV}$ suggests an interpretation in terms of inelastic scattering between dark matter (DM) and the xenon target. The widely considered thermal Higgsino DM with $m_χ\sim$~TeV and mass splitting $δ\sim 350$~keV is essentially excluded as it would be captured efficiently by the Sun, with a resulting neutrino flux from its annihilation in excess of the IceCube limit, and expected recoils above the LZ nominal search window, where none were seen. However we argue that although these tensions apply to a Higgsino with constrained cross section, they need not apply to the broader set of inelastic candidates that alternative solutions to the hierarchy problem might suggest. We show that a new massive gauge boson, $Z'$ with a candidate DM sector and Majorana mass splitting of order $\sim 10^{-7}$ of the Dirac mass, evades current constraints. We propose an inelastic dark matter (iDM) candidate that can arise naturally in a Randall-Sundrum warped extra-dimensional model. The DM candidate involves a vector-like fermion confined to the TeV brane and a bulk field. The brane Lagrangian contains Dirac mass terms that are also naturally exponentially suppressed. The left-handed component of the bulk field has a Majorana mass with origin on the UV brane, while the interaction with Standard Model nucleons is mediated by a massive dark Kaluza-Klein (KK) gauge boson whose mass is also automatically in the right range. We propose a concrete model of thermal DM with $m_χ\sim$~TeV in an RS geometry with warp factor $πkR\simeq 31.7$ and the $Z'$ a KK mode with natural TeV-ish scale mass in which the ratio of neutrino to quark coupling is also natural, giving a mass splitting of $δ\sim 300$~keV, and show how it could explain the LZ signal while being consistent with existing constraints.

</details>


### [218] [Mixing-suppressed inelastic dark matter: a minimal model for the LZ 248 keV event](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.09138)
*Seung J. Lee,Taewook Youn*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: Proposes a minimal Majorana singlet-vector-like-doublet dark matter model to explain LZ's single nuclear-recoil event at ~248 keV, using singlet-doublet mixing to suppress both direct detection rate and solar capture/annihilation while enabling coannihilation; predicts mass splitting δ ≈ 331–341 keV and DM mass ~730–733 GeV, consistent with relic density and recoil spectrum.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To explain the anomalous single nuclear-recoil event observed by the LZ experiment near 248 keV, while satisfying cosmological (relic abundance), astrophysical (solar capture/annihilation), and experimental (IceCube limits, recoil spectrum shape) constraints.

Method: Constructs a minimal effective model with a Majorana singlet and vector-like electroweak doublet, incorporating singlet-doublet mixing to suppress Z-mediated scattering and solar capture; incorporates nonthermal cooling via weak Higgs-mediated annihilation due to small mass splitting for coannihilation; computes direct detection rates, solar capture/annihilation, IceCube neutrino limits, and relic density in the two-parameter (mass, splitting) space.

Result: Identifies a viable parameter region: mass ~730–733 GeV and mass splitting δ = 331–341 keV, which fits the LZ event, satisfies Ωh² ≈ 0.12, evades IceCube limits under nonthermal cooling, and matches the recoil spectrum; contrasts with thermalized scenarios yielding tighter δ = 300–301 keV bounds.

Conclusion: The singlet-doublet mixing mechanism provides a self-consistent explanation for the LZ anomaly without fine-tuning, reconciling direct detection, solar capture, coannihilation, and indirect detection constraints; suggests testable UV completions including gauged U(1)_N and R-symmetric SUSY.

Abstract: We construct a minimal Majorana singlet-vector-like-doublet model for the single nuclear-recoil-like event reported by LZ near 248 keV. At the splitting inferred from the recoil energy, an electroweak-strength $Z$ transition predicts thousands of events; singlet-doublet mixing suppresses the rate without moving the recoil spectrum. Fixed-coupling interpretations instead require a larger splitting in the extreme halo tail, but solar gravitational acceleration removes this suppression, while the larger splitting shifts recoils toward LZ's empty high-energy sideband. In our model the same mixing suppresses solar capture and annihilation, while the small mass gap required for coannihilation weakens Higgs-mediated cooling, allowing the captured population to remain extended and out of equilibrium. Imposing the relic abundance and normalizing the rate to one event leaves a two-dimensional mass-splitting parameter space. A thermalized population gives the conservative IceCube limit $δ=$300-301 keV; with our nonthermal cooling ansatz and elastic scattering treated at tree level, the nominal limit is $δ=$331-341 keV near the 730-733 GeV relic-density endpoint, which is also favored by the recoil spectrum. We outline a gauged $U(1)_N$ origin for the parity and splitting and a candidate $R$-symmetric supersymmetric embedding.

</details>
